# Changelog

All notable changes to the plugins in this repository will be documented here.

## shipyard

### 4.11.8 — 2026-08-08

Two open PRs can each fix the exact required check the OTHER is failing — neither can go green alone, stalling the whole merge train — and the drain had no concept of this state: it applied `blocked:ci` to both independently, with the real explanation ("these two unblock each other") recorded nowhere. Adds detection + loud surfacing for this "mutually-blocking pair" shape. Detection and automated combining are separable and carry very different risk (combining mutates PR branches and, per the issue's own guardrail language, "changes what a reviewer is approving" — and the reporter's manual repro entangled a PR that was human-gated for an unrelated reason); this PR ships detection + surfacing as the complete deliverable and leaves automated combining as a documented follow-up rather than a silent default. closes #1140

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/detect-mutually-blocking-prs.sh` — new single-executable-source-of-truth detector, following the `detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh` / `detect-ci-gate-narrowing.sh` / `detect-stale-node-modules.sh` precedent: a pure `--decide-pair` mode (unit-testable without a live `gh`) plus a live mode that reads each candidate PR's required-check rollup (latest-run-per-name, per issue #333's dedup rule) and reports every mutually-blocking pair found, along with whether the two diffs are file-disjoint.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/detect-mutually-blocking-prs.test.sh` — new regression suite: the pairwise decision truth table, the live signal-extraction helpers against mocked `gh` output, an end-to-end scan reproducing the #1140 repro shape, and doc-wiring assertions against `drain.md` / `cleanup-summary.md` / `do-work-RATIONALE.md`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — new "Mutually-blocking PR detection" per-poll step (runs before the existing dispatch actions): scans `R_new ∪ B`, posts an idempotent diagnosis comment on both PRs (sentinel-guarded so it never re-posts every 60s poll) naming the specific checks each blocks/is blocked by and whether the diffs are file-disjoint, and logs a `[drain]` line. Explicitly does NOT change `R_new` / fix-checks-only / `blocked:ci` routing — a detected pair still settles through the exact same path as before.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new `Mutually-blocking PRs (#1140)` end-of-session summary line, omitted entirely when no pair was detected.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — new section documenting why automated combining was deliberately left out of this PR's scope, and what a future opt-in implementation would need to guarantee.

### 4.11.7 — 2026-08-08

Follow-up to #1115's declined `verifying` non-terminal return token: `worker-preamble`'s `SKILL.md` already told a worker that genuinely exhausts its budget mid-verification to bail with `blocked:` rather than narrate, but named no canonical phrase — so `steady-state.md`'s Reason → class table had nothing reliable to substring-match, and such a bail fell through to the table's conservative `refuse` default (`needs-human-review`) instead of the `soft` (`blocked:agent-soft`, auto-retried next session) treatment an environmental/transient condition actually deserves. Standardizes the canonical phrase `verification did not complete within budget` and adds one row to the routing table classifying it as `soft` — the one-row fix #1115's decision record already named as the chosen alternative to the heavier structured token. closes #1135

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — extends the existing "Auto-backgrounded verification must be awaited" sentence to name the canonical bail phrase (no new heading — the file was already 22 bytes under its byte ceiling).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — new `did not complete within budget` → `soft` row in the Reason → class table, plus the matching `case` branch in the bail-handler implementation.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — cross-references the new row in the existing "Blocked-reason routing table rationale (#521/#628)" section.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/spec-size-budget.test.sh` — raises the `SKILL.md` ceiling 59000 -> 60000 bytes (documented, reviewed growth per the file's own convention).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/commit-before-yield-1054.test.sh`, `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/detect-ci-gate-narrowing.test.sh` — two other suites hardcoding the same `SKILL.md` byte ceiling, kept in sync with the raised value.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — new regression coverage: the canonical phrase, the new table row, the new `case` branch, and the RATIONALE.md cross-reference.

### 4.11.6 — 2026-08-08

Closes the recycled-worktree-slot `node_modules` staleness gap reported against `mattsears18/lightwork`: `/shipyard:do-work` reuses worker worktree slots across dispatches, moving a slot's checkout onto a new branch/commit via `git checkout -B` without ever re-installing `node_modules/` — it's whatever the slot's previous occupant left behind. When the newly-checked-out `package.json` changed a dependency since that install, the worktree's `node_modules` predates the commit, but the existing missing-`node_modules` presence check (`[ ! -d node_modules ]`) passes cleanly because the directory still exists — the gap stayed invisible until a pre-push hook failed with a confusing "cannot resolve module" error, in the reported session misdiagnosed as a lint false positive and worked around with `SKIP_PRE_PUSH=1`, bypassing the exact net that would have caught the real problem. Takes the validate-on-slot-reuse approach (option 1 of the issue's three, and what the downstream repo's own `lightwork#3627` fix already did): a new single-executable-source-of-truth detector script decides `noop` (no `package.json` — ecosystem-tolerant no-op) / `missing` (pre-existing gap, unchanged remediation) / `fresh` / `stale` (installed tree doesn't satisfy `package.json` — the recycled-slot case), and `worker-preamble`'s node-bootstrap.md fragment reinstalls for real and clears the ESLint cache only after a genuine reinstall, since that cache keys on file content/mtime rather than dependency resolution and survives a correct `npm ci` otherwise. closes #1138

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/detect-stale-node-modules.sh` — new detector: `--decide <package_json_exists> <node_modules_exists> <npm_ls_ok>` pure/hermetic mode for tests, `<dir>` live mode that runs `npm ls --depth=0 --silent` against the target directory. Verdict word on stdout (`noop`/`missing`/`fresh`/`stale`), diagnostics on stderr — same shape as `detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh` / `detect-missing-workflow-scope.sh`.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/node-bootstrap.md` — new "Stale `node_modules` on a recycled worktree slot" section documenting the failure mode, the detector invocation, and the per-verdict remediation (reinstall + clear `.eslintcache` / `.expo/cache/eslint` only on a `stale` verdict). `SKILL.md`'s on-demand-fragments table row is left as-is — the mandatory-load spec is already at its byte ceiling (issue #980) with no headroom for this addition.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/stale-node-modules-1138.test.sh` — new suite: the `--decide` truth table, live-mode fixtures (real `npm ls` against scratch `package.json`/`node_modules` combinations, no network required), and a doc-contract check that node-bootstrap.md invokes the script rather than re-deriving the freshness condition inline.

### 4.11.5 — 2026-08-08

Closes [#1139](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/1139): a `fix-main-ci` worker had restored green `main` by adding a self-authored allowlist that permanently exempted three high-severity `npm audit` advisories from ever failing the gate, then armed auto-merge on that PR — the engineering was sound, but nothing in the spec required a human to accept the risk of a permanent, repo-wide weakening of a security gate before it landed. This is a different risk class from the existing "never disable a committed security control" rule (#1088): that rule catches a PR that had to *disable/bypass* an existing control; this one catches a PR that leaves the control nominally in place but *narrows* what it considers a failure (a new allowlist/exception file, a raised severity threshold, `continue-on-error: true`, a deleted gate step, a narrowed trigger filter) — the risk-acceptance decision belongs to a human either way, but the mechanism itself is legitimate work and still ships as a PR. Adds `scripts/detect-ci-gate-narrowing.sh` — a diff-based heuristic, single-executable-source-of-truth detector following the `detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh` precedent — wired into the shared `auto-merge.md` fragment (step 0.34, before any merge call) so every mode that opens a PR gets the check for free, with explicit callouts in `fix-main-ci` and `fix-failing-prs-batch` (the two modes most likely to reach for a suppress-rather-than-fix remedy). A match routes to `needs-human-review` via a new `auto_merge: "unarmed-gate-narrowing"` structured outcome (and its free-text `auto-merge: unarmed — gate-narrowing: <signal>` suffix) instead of a refusal to do the work.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/detect-ci-gate-narrowing.sh` — new detector: six diff-derived signals (workflow-file touch, new allowlist/exception file, `continue-on-error: true` added, raised `--audit-level`, deleted gate step, narrowed `paths-ignore` filter), a pure `--decide` mode for tests, and a live mode reading `gh pr diff`. Fails toward `unknown` (treated as `narrowing`) when the diff can't be read at all.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` — new step 0.34, between the #1088 policy-override check (0.3) and the ungated-admin-direct-merge check (0.5), running the detector and gating auto-merge on its verdict.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — extends the PR-creation-contract sentence to name the new gate-narrowing pre-check.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`, `fix-main-ci.md`, `fix-failing-prs-batch.md` — step 6/7 preambles reference the check; step 8 return vocabulary gains the `unarmed — gate-narrowing` line; fix-main-ci and fix-failing-prs-batch also gain a `Don't` bullet.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/worker-return.schema.json` and `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.core.js` (regenerating `do-work-dispatch.workflow.js`) — new `unarmed-gate-narrowing` enum value, `gate_narrowing` field, and required-when conditional, kept in parity per #856's checker.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/detect-ci-gate-narrowing.test.sh` — new suite: the detector's decision truth table, signal extraction against representative synthetic diffs (including a negative control), and doc-wiring assertions across every touched file.

### 4.11.4 — 2026-08-08

Resolves the open design question [#1115](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/1115) left from [#1113](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/1113)'s deferred P3 slice: whether to add a structured, non-terminal `verifying` return token a worker could hand back instead of `blocked:` when it genuinely cannot finish verification within budget. Decision: not now — [#1127](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/1127) (closing [#1111](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/1111)) shipped in the same session and further shrinks the case `verifying` would address (a resumed worker is now told not to arm *any* new background process, not just to stop waiting on the one it was resumed for); the token's own design question 1 (re-dispatching to await risks recreating the #529/#813 backgrounding trap on the *orchestrator* side) has no safe answer yet; and the implementation cost, sized against #1101's `unarmed-policy-override` precedent, touches roughly seven files (schema, steady-state reconcile, dispatch-rules, both `Workflow`-substrate builders, worker-preamble, issue-work.md, the parity checker) for a case the two prior hardenings already made rare. Ships a cheaper alternative instead: auditing the existing blocked-reason routing table while deciding this found that a genuine budget-exhaustion bail has no matching row and falls through to the table's conservative `refuse` default (`needs-human-review`) — the wrong bucket for a transient/environmental condition that belongs with the table's existing soft (retryable) rows. Files that fix as its own small, targeted follow-up (#1135) rather than building it inline. closes #1115

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — new "`verifying` non-terminal return token — declined for now" decision record: full context, the four reasons against building it now, and the concrete two-part trigger (soft-bail fix proves insufficient AND a safe orchestrator-side wait/resume primitive exists) that would revisit it.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — cross-reference from the "`blocked` outcome is always available" bullet to the new decision record.
- Filed [#1135](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/1135) — classify a genuine within-budget verification-exhaustion bail as `soft` (not `refuse`) in the routing table, the cheaper alternative this decision record names.

### 4.11.3 — 2026-08-08

Consumer-repo counterpart to #1131/#1142: `/shipyard:init` step 6 seeded every newly-bootstrapped repo's `.gitignore` with the same inert-negation `.shipyard/` (bare directory-exclusion) pattern that #1131 found broken in shipyard's own repo — git's rule is that a negation (`!path`) cannot re-include a file whose *parent directory* is excluded, only a content-level exclusion like `.shipyard/*` lets a later `!.shipyard/trusted-authors.txt` line actually work. Changes the seed value to `.shipyard/*`, verified behavior-preserving via `git check-ignore -v` / `git status --porcelain -uall --ignored` against `.shipyard/config.local.json` (stays ignored) and a nested `.shipyard/audits/foo.json` path (stays ignored) — only a file with an explicit negation line changes behavior. The idempotency check (`grep -qx`) now recognizes **both** the new `.shipyard/*` form and the legacy bare `.shipyard/` form as "already seeded," so re-running `/shipyard:init` never appends a duplicate/conflicting line either way. A repo already bootstrapped with the old bare `.shipyard/` line is deliberately left on that form — `/shipyard:init` never rewrites an existing `.gitignore` line (the "never modifies any other line" invariant); the old pattern still correctly ignores everything under `.shipyard/`, it only forgoes negation-safety until a maintainer manually migrates the one line. closes #1137

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/init.md` — step 6's seed value and idempotency check, plus the surrounding prose (summary line, step 2's "detect existing usage" bullet, step 5).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-init-config.test.sh` — new content assertions plus a functional idempotency check (fresh repo, already-migrated repo, legacy-form repo) mirroring the seeding logic against a scratch directory.

### 4.11.2 — 2026-08-08

Fixes the gap #1065's audit filed separately as #1131: `.shipyard/trusted-authors.txt` is designed to be a git-tracked, committed security-boundary file (CODEOWNERS names it, `label-event-audit.yml` reads it from a fresh checkout of the default branch, and `setup/01-repo-recovery.md` step 1.7's bare relative read only works against tracked content) — but the repo's `.gitignore` carried a single blanket `.shipyard/` directory-exclusion entry with no negation exception, so `git add .shipyard/trusted-authors.txt` was refused. The issue's own suggested fix (`.shipyard/` + `!.shipyard/trusted-authors.txt`) does not work — verified empirically with `git check-ignore -v` and `git add --dry-run`: git's rule is that a negation cannot re-include a file whose *parent directory* is excluded, only its *contents*. Changes the entry to `.shipyard/*` (content-level exclusion) instead, confirmed via the same empirical checks to correctly un-ignore `trusted-authors.txt` while `config.local.json` and `flake-suspects.txt` stay ignored. Also clarifies `setup/01-repo-recovery.md` step 1.7's prose that the file must be committed, not merely present in the orchestrator worktree. The identical inert-negation pattern is also seeded into every consumer repo by `/shipyard:init`'s `.gitignore`-append step — filed separately as #1137 rather than fixed inline here, since it's a different file/mechanism than what this issue asked for. closes #1131

- `.gitignore` — `.shipyard/` → `.shipyard/*` so a later `!.shipyard/trusted-authors.txt` negation actually works.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — step 1.7's per-repo override file bullet now states the file must be git-tracked/committed, distinguishing it from the gitignored `config.local.json` / `flake-suspects.txt` siblings under `.shipyard/`.

### 4.11.0 — 2026-08-08

`/my-turn` and `/do-work` previously communicated through exactly one channel — GitHub labels — even though `/do-work` maintains a much richer session-state file at `~/.shipyard/sessions/<id>.json` that already answers questions `/my-turn` was re-deriving by hand (or getting wrong): whether an issue/PR has a live agent working it right now, and whether the default branch's CI is red. Adds a new, strictly optional `/my-turn` setup step that reads the newest session file for the resolved repo and uses it for two things only — **suppression** (drop an issue/PR from the human-only queue when a live session's `.in_flight` shows an agent working it this instant) and **enrichment** (a one-line default-branch-CI banner sourced from the cached `.main_ci` field instead of re-deriving it via several `gh` calls). `.in_flight` is gated on the session's PID being alive (`session-state.sh is-active`, the same two-gate liveness check `/do-work`'s own orphan-sweep uses) so a dead session's stale in-flight list is never mistaken for live work; `.main_ci` is gated on its own 30-minute `checked_at` freshness window instead, since it's a cached fact rather than a claim about a running process. A missing, unreadable, malformed, wrong-repo, or dead session file degrades silently to today's exact behavior — `/my-turn` still produces a correct queue on a machine that has never run `/do-work`, and the new signal never adds a queue item or urgency, only suppresses or annotates. `deferred_issues`, `divert_queue`, `session_prs`, `rebase_blocked_prs`, and `rebase_success_counts` are deliberately left unconsumed as a narrower, independently-verifiable slice of the six fields the originating issue named — see the new setup step's own rationale for why. closes #1080

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — new Setup step 9 (best-effort newest-session-file discovery, the `is-active` liveness gate, the two consumed fields); a new in-flight suppression bullet in the Human-only queue filter; new `in-flight-pointer-line` and `default-branch-ci-line` output affordances wired into list-snapshot mode, the termination contract, and the empty state; a new `Don't` rule keeping the session-state read to suppression/enrichment only.

### 4.10.30 — 2026-08-08

Decides the open design question #1059 deferred: whether to keep re-deriving `SHIPYARD_REPO_ROOT` per post-relocation `shipyard-config.sh` call site (option (a), the shape #1064 swept and regression-tested) or to snapshot the fully merged config once at step 0.4 and have every later read consume that snapshot instead (option (b)). Keeps (a) — see the recorded rationale for the full weighing, including a live coverage gap this PR found and fixed as evidence for the decision: `setup/01b-backlog-overview.md`'s `triage.investigate_dispatch` read called `shipyard-config.sh` post-relocation with no `SHIPYARD_REPO_ROOT` pin, and had never been added to `shipyard-repo-root-preamble.test.sh`'s hardcoded `FILES` array. Fixes that call site and converts the regression test's file discovery from a hardcoded array to the same mechanical `find`-based glob `claude-plugin-root-preamble.test.sh` already uses for the analogous `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` preamble, so a future new file under `commands/do-work/` is covered automatically instead of requiring a remembered array edit. closes #1105

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01b-backlog-overview.md` — adds the missing `SHIPYARD_REPO_ROOT` re-derivation to the `triage.investigate_dispatch` read.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-repo-root-preamble.test.sh` — `FILES` discovery converted from a hardcoded six-file array to a mechanical `find` over `commands/do-work/` (minus `setup/00-config-worktree.md`); adds canary-file + exclude-file sanity checks mirroring `claude-plugin-root-preamble.test.sh`'s #910 discovery-regression guards.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — new decision record weighing option (a) vs (b), naming the rejected alternative and why, plus the ordered follow-up chain a future revisit of (b) should take if it's ever pursued.

### 4.10.29 — 2026-08-08

Audit follow-up to #1059 (closes #1065): grepped the do-work orchestrator + worker spec tree for gitignored, repo-relative session state resolved via a bare `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` instead of an explicit pinned root. Confirmed the audit's flagged wrinkle is a real, currently-vacuous bug: `fix-checks-only.md`'s pre-rerun flake-suspects check ran `flake-enforce.sh is-suspect --repo-root "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"` from inside the **worker's own** freshly-provisioned `agent-*` worktree — which, like every worktree, never contains `.shipyard/flake-suspects.txt` (gitignored, written only to the primary checkout by the orchestrator's setup-time enforce pass). `is-suspect` treats a missing file as "not a suspect," so the check could never see a chronic-flake entry the orchestrator wrote, silently defeating the `stop-auto-rerunning` enforcement action since it shipped. Fixes it **without** propagating the orchestrator's `SHIPYARD_REPO_ROOT` pin into the worker (that pin stays deliberately scoped to the orchestrator's own session per #1059) — instead the worker now resolves the primary checkout itself via `git worktree list --porcelain`, which reads the shared `.git` common-dir worktree registry (not cwd-scoped like `--show-toplevel`) and always lists the main checkout first. The other two `.shipyard/*` repo-relative state files were also audited: `config.local.json` (shipyard-config.sh) was already fixed by #1059/#1063's `SHIPYARD_REPO_ROOT` pin; `trusted-authors.txt` is confirmed to be a *different* design (a git-tracked, CODEOWNERS-protected committed file, not a personal local override) and so isn't an instance of this bug class — its own gap (the blanket `.shipyard/` gitignore entry has no negation exception for it) is filed separately as #1131 rather than fixed inline here. closes #1065

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — pre-rerun flake-suspects check (fix-loop step 1.5) now resolves `--repo-root` via `git worktree list --porcelain` instead of `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)`.

### 4.10.28 — 2026-08-08

Dispositions the data-cleanup half of a follow-up from #1090 (tracked at https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/1120, which stays open): a live re-measurement (2026-08-08) found 0 open `needs-triage` issues in both `mattsears18/lightwork` (47/47 all-time applications already closed) and `mattsears18/shipyard` (1/1 closed), confirming there is no pre-existing human-applied corpus left to disposition and that #1090's recommended option 3 — treat a vague issue as an ordinary `/refine-issues` candidate, no new label — requires no code change. Documents this finding, plus the concrete, mechanically-checkable retirement gate for the `needs-triage` label itself (both an earliest date, `2026-09-20`, derived by mirroring the 43-day gap between the #520 `needs-refinement` code-change close and the #859 label-object deletion, AND a live-usage condition that must also hold), in [`04d-investigate-routing.md`](plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/04d-investigate-routing.md)'s "Migration window" section so a future session can evaluate readiness mechanically instead of re-deriving it. The label itself, its `gh label create` calls, and its acceptance as a live-detection signal are deliberately left untouched — the window has not elapsed — so the label-retirement half of the follow-up remains outstanding.

### 4.10.27 — 2026-08-08

A label audit across `mattsears18/shipyard` and `mattsears18/lightwork` found three dead label objects (`blocked:agent`, `blocked:agent-hard`, `needs-operator` — each superseded and zero all-time usage in either repo), three dead spec branches referencing them, a dead `needs-human` reference that never had a corresponding label object, and two labels (`blocked:agent-soft`, `discussion`) whose behavior had never been formally decided or fully provisioned. Removes the dead `needs-refinement` Pass B bucket and P1 tier entry from `/my-turn` (the label was eliminated #520 and its GitHub object deleted #859, so the bucket could never match a live issue again), closes the `needs-operator` → `agent-console` legacy-name back-compat window across every matching site (zero issues in either repo carried the old name), provisions the `discussion` gate label that four spec files already gated dispatch on without a corresponding `gh label create` line, records decisions on `blocked:agent-soft` (kept — changes no routing, description updated to say so) and the bare `blocked` label (deprecated, migration to `needs-human-review` tracked in #1128 before the shipyard-side bail checks can be dropped), removes a dead reference to a `needs-human` label that never existed in `investigate.md`'s bail list, fixes `do-work-supervisor.sh`'s `GATE_LABELS` default (still matched on the two eliminated `blocked:agent*` labels), and adds a canonical "Retired labels" table to CLAUDE.md as the single source of truth so none of the above can be silently reintroduced. Adds a `blocked:ci` work-exists-gate test case to `do-work-supervisor.test.sh`, documenting that the 3-attempt fix-checks circuit breaker itself (the trigger, as opposed to the label-matching consumers) is orchestrator prose rather than deterministic script and so isn't mechanically unit-testable the way the label-matching code is. Manual deletion of the three dead label objects is left as a documented operator follow-up — that's an irreversible repo-admin mutation, out of scope for a spec-only PR. closes #1082

- `CLAUDE.md` — new "Retired labels — do not reintroduce" table; `blocked:agent-soft` and `blocked:ci` state-label entries record their #1082 decisions; new `discussion` and bare-`blocked` gate-label entries; the `needs-operator` → `agent-console` migration section documents the closed window.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — removes the dead `needs-refinement` Pass B bucket and P1 tier entry; closes `needs-operator` legacy-name recognition in the human-only-queue filter and the chrome-prompt inclusion rule.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/04-backlog-divert.md`, `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/04-steady-state-hooks.md`, `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01b-backlog-overview.md`, `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/backlog-ownership.md` — close the `needs-operator` legacy-name back-compat window at every remaining matching site.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01c-label-recovery-refine.md` — provisions `discussion`; updates `blocked:agent-soft`'s description to state it changes no routing; consolidates `blocked:agent`, `blocked:agent-hard`, `needs-operator`, and the dead `needs-human` reference into the file's `# RETIRED` comment block.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/investigate.md` — removes the dead `needs-human` bail-list reference.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/do-work-supervisor.sh` — `GATE_LABELS` default drops the eliminated `blocked:agent` / `blocked:agent-hard`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-supervisor.test.sh` — new `blocked:ci` work-exists-gate test case.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/agent-console-rename-995.test.sh`, `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh` — updated to assert the closed migration window and the removed dead reference, respectively, instead of the now-retired behavior they previously guarded.

### 4.10.26 — 2026-08-08

Workers routinely ended their turn waiting on a background job THEY THEMSELVES launched — a Monitor-tracked commit, a backgrounded CI-verification wait — and returned narrative prose instead of a terminal string, costing the orchestrator a full round-trip per occurrence. Five instances in one `/shipyard:do-work` session across `issue-work` and `fix-checks-only`, including the SAME worker doing it twice on the SAME PR: once, then again immediately after an A.0.5 resume that had already told it to stop waiting on the specific thing it was resumed for. That repeat is the gap #1054's and #1113's earlier fixes to this same failure family didn't close — the canned resume message stopped the worker re-subscribing to its OLD wait but said nothing about arming a NEW one for a later step in the same resumed dispatch. Strengthens the canned resume-message template in `steady-state.md` (shared across all seven modes' A.0.5 resume path, so the fix reaches every mode rather than one) to explicitly forbid backgrounding anything — a new `run_in_background` Bash call, a Monitor, a backgrounded commit or pre-commit hook — for the rest of the dispatch, not only the thing being resumed from. Also reinforces `fix-checks-only.md`'s `pending` return as a success-shaped terminal on par with `green`, not a lesser outcome worth camping on after it's been honestly earned — waiting for CI to fully settle is explicitly the orchestrator's job, not the worker's. closes #1111

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — the A.0.5 resume-message prose gains a new bullet, and the canned resume-message template a new paragraph, both forbidding a NEW background arm for any later step in a resumed dispatch, citing the #1111 same-worker-twice-in-a-row finding.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — `pending`'s documentation gains a reinforcement: treat it as success-shaped, not partial; return it and stop rather than continuing to watch after you've earned it.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — 5 new assertions covering both file changes and their #1111 citations.

### 4.10.25 — 2026-08-08

Two `/my-turn` survey signals — Pass B's "last comment authored by someone other than `$ME`" and Pass C's per-PR unanswered-review-comment follow-up — key on comment *authorship* to detect "a human is waiting on a reply." In a shipyard-driven repo every comment, human or agent, is authored by the same `$ME` login (agents comment/file/act through the maintainer's own authenticated `gh`), so both predicates are structurally dead rather than merely rare: Pass B's identity test can never fire, and Pass C spends its per-PR `gh api` follow-ups (the largest single line item in the survey's performance budget) on a guaranteed-empty result. Replaces Pass B's identity test with an **intent** test on the newest comment (any author): an explicit `@$ME` mention, an un-labeled `<!-- shipyard-* -->` handoff sentinel (the concrete residue case — a worker's reaped-mid-investigation progress comment that never got a gate label), or a bare `?` only when the issue carries no gate label already (avoiding double-surfacing an item its own labeled bucket already covers). Gates Pass C on repo shape instead: skip it outright, zero `gh api` calls, when Pass A's already-fetched open-PR authors contain no one but `$ME`. Both specs now state explicitly, at the point `$ME` is resolved, that it names the repo's automation identity rather than "the human." Checked whether the `Co-Authored-By: Claude` commit trailer (PR #1108's inherited-draft-PR classifier) applied here — it doesn't: that's a *commit*-level signal, and this fix is about *comment*-level attribution, where no such trailer exists. closes #1089

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — Setup step 2 states `$ME` is the automation identity; Pass B's last-comment signal rewritten from an identity filter to three intent markers; Pass C gains a zero-extra-call skip gate on Pass A's already-fetched author set; the Ranking P1 bullet and the Performance budget's Pass C line updated to match.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh` — 9 new assertions covering the automation-identity statement, the rewritten Pass B signal (identity-filter disclaimer, the shipyard-handoff-sentinel marker, the gate-label-gated `?` branch), the Pass C skip-gate documentation, and a regression guard that the stale identity-keyed wording doesn't reappear.

### 4.10.24 — 2026-08-08

A dispatch prompt asserted a dependency PR "already merged, so `main` has the fix" as confirmed fact when the referenced PR was actually still `OPEN` — `mergeStateStatus: BLOCKED`, auto-merge only just armed, most checks `QUEUED`/`PENDING`. The worker independently re-verified and worked around it, but a less-orthogonal pair of dependent issues could implement against a `main` silently missing the dependency's integration point. This is the same defect family #781 already named for the issue's own blocked-status framing — an unverified assertion about live state handed to the worker as fact — so rather than add a competing rule, extends the existing "confidence/status framing composed into the dispatch prompt must be comment-thread-aware" rule to cover assertions about *another* PR's or issue's merge/close state: re-verify live at prompt-composition time (`gh pr view <N> --json state,mergedAt,mergeStateStatus`) before asserting merge state as fact, or downgrade to a conditional framing ("should have merged by the time you start; verify before assuming...") the worker knows to re-check. closes #1062

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the #781 rule gains a new paragraph generalizing it to cross-PR/cross-issue merge-state assertions, with the repro and the `gh pr view` re-verification mitigation.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — the #781 rationale entry gains a note on the #1062 repro and why it was folded into the existing rule instead of a new one.

### 4.10.23 — 2026-08-08

`/my-turn` surfaced any PR with `mergeStateStatus: DIRTY` for >24h without distinguishing author, but `/shipyard:do-work`'s drain-phase `fix-rebase` worker mode already adopts and rebases `@me`-authored DIRTY PRs across sessions — so the `@me` half was double-owned, surfaced by both loops. The obvious fix (drop `@me` from the signal entirely) would have repeated the exact mistake #1093 just fixed for `agent-console`: `fix-rebase`'s adoption is drain-phase-only and conditional on the existing `ci.skip_drain_rebase` knob, so an unconditional author-scoped exclusion would strand a `@me` DIRTY PR under a repo that's disabled drain-phase rebase dispatch (nothing adopts it, and now nothing surfaces it either). Adds a dedicated `/my-turn` setup step resolving `ci.skip_drain_rebase` (an existing `/do-work`-side knob — no new schema entry) and gates the DIRTY-PR signal, the Human-only queue filter, the P2 ranking tier, and the leverage-score bottom-of-tier bucket on it, mirroring #1093's config-conditional-ownership shape. Two carve-outs preserve visibility for a `@me` DIRTY PR even under the default: one already-observable (`blocked:ci`, caught by the existing signal) and one that isn't — `/do-work`'s two rebase-specific give-up states (`rebase_blocked_prs`, the 3-rebase rate-limit cap) are per-session, in-memory drain bookkeeping never persisted anywhere `gh` can read, so per the issue's own guidance a `@me` DIRTY PR still surfaces as a low-priority fallback (bottom of tier) rather than being dropped on an unverifiable assumption that `/do-work` is handling it. Outside-contributor DIRTY PRs are unaffected either way. closes #1075

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — new Setup step 8 resolving `ci.skip_drain_rebase`; the Pass A DIRTY-PR signal (split into an author-scoped primary bullet and a `@me` fallback bullet), the Human-only queue filter's new `/do-work`-owns-`@me`-DIRTY-PRs bullet, the P2 ranking tier, the leverage-score-1 bottom-of-tier bucket, and the relevant `Don't` bullet all gated on the knob with both branches stated explicitly.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh` — 13 new assertions covering the setup step, the config gate on every affected section, the fail-safe-to-surfacing degrade direction, the two carve-outs, and cross-checks confirming `ci.skip_drain_rebase` is already declared in `shipyard-config.sh` / the schema (no new knob needed).

### 4.10.22 — 2026-08-08

Setup step 0.5's `EnterWorktree` fallback only covered "the tool is unavailable" (an older Claude Code build, or a `Bash`-only harness variant) — it had no recovery for the case a repo-level `WorktreeCreate` hook rejects the harness's payload and the `EnterWorktree` call itself errors. Reading the spec literally in that case left the orchestrator stuck: the primary path errored, and the only documented fallback's precondition was false. Worse, the identical hook rejects every subsequently-dispatched worker's `Agent(isolation: "worktree")` the same way, since workers provision their worktree through the same harness mechanism — so an affected repo doesn't just block session startup, it silently breaks the entire dispatch model, once per worker, with nothing in the spec naming the target repo's hook as the cause. Adds a documented create-then-enter-by-path recovery (raw `git worktree add`, then `EnterWorktree` with `path:` rather than `name:` — entering an existing worktree doesn't invoke `WorktreeCreate`, so it sidesteps the hook while still registering harness isolation, unlike the raw-git-only fallback) plus an explicit callout naming `WorktreeCreate`/`WorktreeRemove` hooks as a target-repo-side failure source, and a pointer to the `Workflow`-substrate alternate dispatch shape as the session-level workaround once this signature is observed (that shape pre-provisions worker worktrees itself and never triggers the hook). A repo-wide preflight probe was considered and deliberately not built — the step-0.5 signal already surfaces the condition for free the first time it's hit. closes #1066

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — step 0.5's fallback intro re-gated off "EnterWorktree failed, for any reason" instead of "EnterWorktree unavailable"; points to the new fragment before the raw-git-only path.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00c-worktree-recovery.md` — new deep-link fragment: the two-case breakdown, the named-hook-failure callout with the verbatim error signature, the create-then-enter-by-path recovery, and the worker-dispatch implication (with the preflight-probe alternative explicitly declined and why).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md` — router table gains the `00c` entry.

### 4.10.21 — 2026-08-07

`/my-turn`'s ownership claims over `agent-console` items assumed `/shipyard:do-work`'s default config — the Human-only queue filter excluded them, and the operator pointer line told the maintainer to "run `/shipyard:do-work` to have Claude complete them," on the unstated premise that `/do-work`'s browser-operator phase was actually draining them. `--no-operate` / `--hands-off` turns that phase off entirely, and under that opt-out `agent-console` items are surfaced as hand-backs instead — gated exactly like `needs-human-review` with nothing draining them — so the pre-fix filter and pointer stranded those items, invisible to both loops (excluded from `/my-turn`'s queue, pointed at a command configured not to touch them). `--no-operate` / `--hands-off` is a per-invocation CLI flag, not persisted state, so `/my-turn` — a separate command run — cannot observe how the *last* `/do-work` session was actually started; reading `/do-work`'s live session state directly is a distinct capability tracked separately in #1080 and is explicitly NOT resolved here. Instead, adds a declared per-repo `my_turn.assume_operator_enabled` config knob (default `true`, matching `/do-work`'s own operator-inclusive-by-default posture) that a maintainer running `/do-work --no-operate` habitually can flip to `false` — `/my-turn` then stops filtering `agent-console` items to a pointer line and walks them in the human-only queue directly (P1 tier, leverage score 3) instead. A failed config resolution degrades to `false` (surfacing, not hiding) rather than silently reproducing the old unconditional-filter bug. This is the same config-conditional-ownership shape #1077 established for `needs-triage`'s `triage.investigate_dispatch` gate, applied here to a knob `/my-turn` alone reads since there's nothing for `/do-work` itself to condition. `backlog-ownership.md`'s bucket 5.6 row now states this config precondition inline, per the general principle that an Owner claim conditioned on a knob's default value must say so on the row, not just in prose. #1075's related DIRTY-PR-signal author-scoping fix (still unimplemented) needs the equivalent conditioning on `ci.skip_drain_rebase` when it lands — flagged on that issue rather than absorbed into this one. closes #1093

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — new Setup step 7 resolving `my_turn.assume_operator_enabled`; the Pass B `agent-console` bucket, the Human-only queue filter, the P0/P1 ranking tiers, the leverage-score-3 bucket, the operator pointer line, and the relevant `Don't` bullet all gated on the knob with both branches stated explicitly.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` — built-in default `my_turn.assume_operator_enabled: true`.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — schema entry for the new knob.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/backlog-ownership.md` — bucket 5.6's Owner cell states its operator-phase-enabled config precondition inline; new "Reading this table" principle about config-conditional Owner claims.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh` — 15 new assertions covering the setup step, the config gate on every affected section, the fail-safe-to-surfacing degrade, and the schema/defaults declarations; 2 more asserting `backlog-ownership.md`'s precondition annotation.

### 4.10.20 — 2026-08-07

`needs-triage` carried two incompatible meanings with one behavior: an inbound API from the Sentry integration (13/13 Sentry-authored issues in the measured sample) and a human convention meaning "I haven't thought this through" (34/47 applications) — but investigate mode's entry condition was solely the label, and the label's own GitHub description ("Needs refinement before /do-work picks it up") was false wherever it was created, since #556 already made `/do-work` auto-dispatch a trusted-author `needs-triage` issue via investigate mode. Mirrors #520's `needs-refinement` retirement: moves investigate-mode's entry to a **live detection scan** (bot-shaped trusted author, or a symptom-shaped body — a stack trace, a fingerprint, an error string) run first, with `needs-triage` kept as a *sufficient-but-not-required* trigger during a migration window so the inbound Sentry pipeline (and any consuming repo's existing labeling habit) doesn't break. The label itself is deliberately NOT retired yet — that, plus a disposition for the pre-existing corpus of human-applied `needs-triage` issues, is tracked in a follow-up (#1120) once the migration window has genuinely elapsed. Also fixes the label's GitHub description everywhere it's created (three call sites carried two different, both-false descriptions) and rewrites `filing-github-issues`'s guidance, which was actively instructing filers to apply `needs-triage` for genuine human-decision findings — exactly the mechanism generating the mislabeled 34. closes #1090

### 4.10.19 — 2026-08-07

`/my-turn`'s `--chrome-prompt` mode was running the exact same [Human-only queue filter](plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md#human-only-queue-filter) as every other mode — but that filter's whole point is to hand a *human* only the items an autonomous loop can't complete, which deliberately excludes `agent-console` items (a browser/console action an agent can drive). Chrome-prompt mode's consumer is the opposite of a human at a terminal: it's the Claude for Chrome browser extension, which can drive exactly the `agent-console` class the filter throws away, and can't make the `needs-human-review` judgment calls the filter kept. The mode inverted its own purpose — a live repro against `mattsears18/lightwork` found it excluding five browser-doable operator items while including a decision call and an on-device task, its worst possible output. Adds a dedicated **Chrome-completable queue filter**, the inverse of the Human-only queue filter: the prompt body is built from `agent-console` (or legacy `needs-operator`) items whose action is mechanical navigate/click/type, while genuine human-only items — plus any credential-/account-creation-shaped `agent-console` item (paste-secret / external-provisioning shaped, per #991's harness-level prohibition that binds the extension exactly as it binds `/do-work`'s own MCP-driven operator phase) — route to the trailing "can't be automated" section instead of the pasted prompt. closes #1092

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — new "Chrome-completable queue filter" subsection under Chrome-prompt mode; the "Survey, filter, ranking run identically" paragraph rewritten to state the inversion and why; the "can't be automated" section, empty-state handling, the operator-pointer-line bullet, the `--chrome-prompt` Args description, and the "Distinction from `/do-work`" paragraph all updated to match.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh` — 11 new assertions covering the Chrome-completable queue filter, the credential/provisioning exclusion, the same-item-set/different-driver relationship to `/do-work`, and a regression guard (`assert_not_contains`, new helper) against the exact pre-fix phrasing reappearing.

The binary-backlog fold ([#515](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/515), [#519](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/519), [#520](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/520), [#521](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/521)) collapsed every human gate into `needs-human-review`, which was right for `/do-work` — one label to exclude — but turned the label into a catch-all with at least 8 distinct provenances, and `/my-turn` had no reliable way to tell them apart short of inferring intent from comment prose. An absent marker still degrades to the prior generic render. closes #1091

### 4.10.17 — 2026-08-07

[#519](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/519) folded the `needs-decomposition` / `tracking` epic-decomposition label pair into `needs-human-review` + the `<!-- do-work-needs-decomposition -->` body marker, and dropped `tracking` from the dispatch-gate label set on the theory that the label object would eventually be deleted. It never was: `tracking` kept being applied to brand-new epics in at least one consuming repo (`mattsears18/lightwork`, confirmed live 2026-08-06 — two open issues, one filed the same day, neither carrying `needs-human-review` or the decomposition marker), and since `01c-label-recovery-refine.md`'s sub-sweep e migration only runs once per session at setup time, a `tracking`-labeled epic created mid-session (or by a human between sessions) reached bucket 8 (Workable) and was dispatch-eligible as an ordinary single-issue PR, relying solely on the scope pre-flight as a backstop. This repo's own `tracking` label object was never created and has zero all-time issue usage (confirmed via `gh label list` and `gh api /search/issues`), so no live-data migration was needed here — the fix is the dispatch-time gate that protects every consuming repo going forward. ### 4.10.16 — 2026-08-07

A worker whose local verification run got auto-backgrounded by the harness (this repo's suite has grown to 117 bash suites and now routinely exceeds the `Bash` tool's foreground timeout) ended its dispatch with a narrative status — "waiting on the suite, I'll resume once it reports back" — instead of a documented terminal return string, five times in one session. That belief is false: the worker's process has already stopped, and only an explicit orchestrator `SendMessage` restarts it, so the orchestrator's dispatch slot stays held on a dead process until a human or the reap-and-resume path recovers it. A prompt-level instruction telling one of those five workers not to do this had no effect — it did it anyway with the instruction in its own dispatch prompt — confirming the rule belongs in the always-loaded worker contract, not per-dispatch prose. Adds a hard rule to `skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`, sited directly next to the #1054 commit-before-yield invariant it's the same family as ("don't yield in a state the orchestrator can't act on"): poll an auto-backgrounded verification command to a terminal result and return one of your mode's documented strings — never narrate past it. Companion guidance in `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` §4 corrects a now-stale claim ("the shipyard bash suites complete in seconds" — no longer true at 117 suites) and steers workers toward targeted suites covering the change plus at most one full sweep, rather than defaulting to the whole battery for a small change. The issue's third, lower-priority slice — a structured non-terminal `verifying` return token — was filed separately as #1115 rather than built here: it needs cross-cutting wiring (schema, orchestrator reconcile, `Workflow`-substrate builders) that a correct P1 should make rarely necessary, so it's deferred pending evidence the first two slices aren't already sufficient (closes #1113).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — §4's suite-discovery paragraph split: the CI-mirroring minimum stays as-is, followed by a new "Prefer targeted suites over the full discovered set" paragraph correcting the stale "completes in seconds" claim and capping a full sweep at one per dispatch for small changes.

### 4.10.15 — 2026-08-07

`/my-turn`'s Phase 1 partitioned the human-only queue on a decision-gated trigger surface that required *enumerated* decisions — a numbered "Blocking decisions" list, an `## Open questions` heading, a `<!-- do-work-human-decision-required -->` marker, or a `design`-gated set of questions — and explicitly punted anything else to Phase 3's serial walk. That missed a common, high-leverage shape: a `needs-human-review` issue (or draft PR) whose gated work already landed, where the only remaining human action is a keep/close/split judgment call rather than answering a question the body poses — the decision arose from the thread, never got written down anywhere the trigger surface could see, and so the item was pushed to Phase 3's one-at-a-time walk instead of Phase 1's batched `AskUserQuestion` run, even though it's exactly the "one-line answer" shape Phase 1 exists to batch. Adds a fourth trigger class, **disposition call**, detected entirely from data the survey passes already fetch — no extra `gh` round-trip: for an issue, the newest of Pass B's already-trimmed last-3 comments asserting completion while the gate label is still on (the strongest signal — the label is provably older than the assertion, since nothing newer walked it back); for a draft PR, its `closingIssuesReferences` naming an issue absent from Pass B's already-fetched open-issue set (validated against this session's own PR #1100 / issue #1096, where #1096 closed `NOT_PLANNED` mid-dispatch leaving the PR gated with nothing left to decide about the underlying feature). Ships a templated three-option walkthrough (close as done / keep open naming the remainder / close and split into a narrow follow-up), keyed on whether the unfinished remainder is shared with an already-verified path or genuinely distinct unverified code, plus its own Phase 2 record shape (close / comment-and-conditionally-unblock / file-and-close) distinct from `/resolve-decisions`' dispatch-ready shape. The original "never invent a question for an issue that enumerates none" guard survives unchanged — extended to disposition calls, not bypassed by them — and a new `my_turn.disposition_call_detection` config knob (default `true`) lets a repo opt out if the completion-assertion heuristic misfires on its comment conventions (closes #1074).

### 4.10.14 — 2026-08-07

`/my-turn`'s spec had no bound on per-item investigation depth once an item was ranked: nothing told the command to stop at "surface the signal and the URL" instead of root-causing it. Ranking an item P0 is the strongest "invest here" signal the command produces, so an agent chasing a genuinely useful `→ Now:` line had every incentive to keep drilling — read CI logs, walk a run's jobs, trace a stack trace — with no bound telling it to stop. A live repro against `mattsears18/lightwork` (session `1fd0e344`, 2026-08-06) burned six `gh` calls diagnosing a red-main GitHub Actions infrastructure blip down to `Failed to resolve action download info` — nothing a human could act on — and displaced the session's one genuinely human-only item from the batched `AskUserQuestion` call. #1072/#1110 removed the one red-main instance that triggered the repro; this closes the general gap so the same pull applies to `blocked:ci`, `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, or any future signal. Adds a "router, not investigator" framing to the command's opening promise, a `Don't diagnose — surface` rule naming CI-log reading and root-causing as explicitly out of scope, and a new "Per-item investigation ceiling" subsection under Performance budget: deriving any item's action costs zero additional tool calls beyond the survey projection in the steady case and never more than a small, fixed, config-tunable ceiling (`my_turn.max_diagnostic_reads_per_item`, default 1) — with a specified degraded render (known signal + artifact URL + owning command) for anything that would exceed it. Phase 1's deliberate front-loading is explicitly re-scoped to grounding a decision, not diagnosing a Phase 3 item, so it can't be misread as license to go deep (closes #1073).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — new "router, not investigator" paragraph in the opening promise; new Setup step 5 resolving `my_turn.max_diagnostic_reads_per_item`; new "Per-item investigation ceiling" subsection (with degraded-render spec) under Performance budget; Phase 1's front-loading paragraph re-scoped to decision-grounding only; Phase 3's `blocked:ci`/epic/housekeeping walk bullet now points at the ceiling and the degraded render; new `Don't diagnose — surface` rule in the Don't section.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` — new `my_turn.max_diagnostic_reads_per_item` default (1) in `DEFAULTS_JQ`.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — `my_turn` object schema gains `max_diagnostic_reads_per_item` (integer, minimum 0, default 1).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh` — 16 new assertions covering the router framing, the Don't-diagnose rule, the per-item ceiling subsection and its generalization to future signals, the degraded render, the Phase 1 decision-grounding scoping, and the new config knob's declaration in both the built-in defaults and the schema.

### 4.10.12 — 2026-08-07

`/my-turn`'s Pass B signal for "issue authored by `$ME` with no linked PR and no `needs-human-review` label" matched `/do-work`'s entire bucket 8 (Workable) — on a solo-maintainer repo, `author == $ME` is a tautology, so the predicate degenerated to "every open issue without a PR," the normal steady state of a healthy backlog. A live measurement against `mattsears18/lightwork` found 8 of 15 open issues matching, all ordinary dispatch-eligible issues with 0% true positives, three of them mid-dispatch under a live `/do-work` session at that exact moment. `/do-work` already stamps the `shipyard` session-stamp label and self-assigns `@me` on every issue it dispatches before implementation begins (per `inline-trivial.md`'s soft-lock convention), so the absence of both — not the absence of a linked PR alone — is the actual "never claimed" evidence. The predicate now requires no `shipyard` label AND no self-assignment to `@me` AND age past a new `my_turn.stale_undispatched_days` config knob (default 7d) on top of the existing no-linked-PR / no-`needs-human-review` conjuncts, all four already riding the Pass B projection with no added `gh` call, and re-ranks the signal from P1 (decisions) to P2 (housekeeping) to match its now-narrow, non-blocking scope. Closes the third and last of #1076's three explicitly-tracked overlap/gap rows — bucket 8 — following #1077 (bucket 5) and #1079 (bucket 0.5) (closes #1078).

### 4.10.11 — 2026-08-07

An inherited PR left in **draft** was invisible to every `/do-work` sweep: the failed-PR scan (step 5) keys on a red check rollup, and step 5.7's inherited-DIRTY seed keys on `mergeStateStatus == "DIRTY"` — a draft PR is typically neither (checks read `SKIPPED` on any repo whose CI guards jobs with a `draft != true` condition, and `mergeStateStatus` reads `CLEAN`). A fully-resolved, agent-authored draft could sit stranded indefinitely, as it did for 2 days holding a P1 fix in `mattsears18/lightwork#3465`, recovered only by manual inspection. Adds a new setup step 5.75 that runs a new `scripts/draft-pr-recovery.sh enforce` — the single executable source of truth for both the classification decision and the recovery action, mirroring the `detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh` precedent. It classifies each inherited `@me` draft PR by seven independent signals (agent-authored via commit trailer — deliberately **not** the `do-work/*` branch prefix, since the repro PR's branch was `fix/android-sdk-root-3461`; an existing gate label; an open vs. already-CLOSED closing-issue reference; an empty/WIP/DO-NOT-MERGE/unchecked-task-list body) and either readies it + arms auto-merge (gated/ungated-aware, mirroring issue-work.md step 6.a) or hands it back with `needs-human-review` + a one-line reason, idempotently. Every inherited draft this step touches — readied or handed back — is recorded into a new `inherited_draft_prs` cold orchestrator-state structure and surfaced in the end-of-session summary regardless of disposition, closing the silent-and-unbounded-stall gap even for the conservative hand-back path (closes #1069).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/draft-pr-recovery.sh` — new script: the pure classification decision (`classify-one`), pure signal extractors (`agent-authored-check`, `gated-label-check`, `is-body-empty`, `has-wip-marker`, `has-unchecked-tasks`), and the live `enforce` subcommand.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/draft-pr-recovery.test.sh` — new test suite (55 assertions): the full decision matrix, both named test cases from the issue (a lightwork#3465-shaped PR auto-readies; a shipyard#1100-shaped PR — already labeled `needs-human-review` after its issue closed `NOT_PLANNED` — hands back and is never auto-readied), the ungated admin-direct-merge shape, and idempotency.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/04c-draft-pr-recovery.md` — new deep-link fragment documenting step 5.75, following the existing `00b`/`01c`/`04b`/`06b` split precedent (`04-backlog-divert.md` was already within ~450 bytes of the 60000-byte setup-file token-budget cap).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/04-backlog-divert.md` — new short step 5.75 pointer to the fragment above.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md` — router table gains a row for the new fragment; the `04-backlog-divert.md` row's step summary mentions the new seed.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/orchestrator-state-reference.md` — new `inherited_draft_prs` cold orchestrator-state structure; struct count corrected from eleven to thirteen (a pre-existing drift — `setup_reap_sweep_denial` had already been added without the count being updated).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — cold-structure list and total-structure count updated to match.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new `Inherited draft PRs (#1069):` end-of-session summary line, omitted when empty, per the `ci_session_counters` silence-on-quiet-sessions precedent.

### 4.10.10 — 2026-08-07

An issue filed by an author outside `trusted_authors` was correctly excluded from `/do-work` dispatch by bucket 0.5 (a security gate, not a triage hint — unchanged by this fix), but the exclusion applied no label and emitted no handoff, and `/my-turn` had no author-trust signal of its own. A stranger's bug report reached no human by any path: never dispatched, never labeled, never surfaced. Implements Option A from the issue body (preferred over having `/my-turn` read the trust allowlist directly, which would duplicate the trust computation in a second place and let the two commands drift): when bucket 0.5 drops an issue, `04-backlog-divert.md`'s client-side filter now applies `needs-human-review` plus a one-line comment naming the vouch path, so `/my-turn` picks it up via its already-working bucket-5.5 path with no new signal. The write is bounded to issues newer than a persisted cross-session high-water mark (`$SHIPYARD_HOME/untrusted-author-highwater.json`, keyed by repo) rather than a full-history sweep every session, and idempotent via a `needs-human-review`-already-present check plus a `<!-- do-work-untrusted-author-review -->` sentinel comment. The new mechanism outgrew `04-backlog-divert.md`'s 60000-byte setup-file token-budget cap, so it's split into a new deep-link fragment, `04b-untrusted-author-handoff.md`, following the existing `00b`/`01c`/`06b`/`06c` split precedent (closes #1079).

### 4.10.6 — 2026-08-07

`do-work-supervisor.test.sh`'s install test set a fake `$HOME` so the plist file landed in a throwaway directory, but `launchctl` is not scoped by `$HOME` — `cmd_install`'s `launchctl load` call registered a real job in the developer's actual launchd domain, pointing at a `mktemp -d` plist path teardown had already deleted. The suite's assertions only ever inspected the plist file's contents, so the side effect was invisible to the very suite that caused it, and the leaked `com.shipyard.do-work-supervisor.acme-widget` registration would have failed silently on every interval forever. This makes `launchctl` injectable the same way `gh` and `claude` already are, stubs it in the test harness so no test touches the real launchd domain, strengthens the install/uninstall assertions to check the stub's recorded invocations instead of only the plist file, and adds a suite-wide before/after assertion on real launchd *state* so a future regression that bypasses the injectable binary fails the suite instead of going unnoticed again (closes #1097).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/do-work-supervisor.sh` — new `LAUNCHCTL_BIN="${SHIPYARD_SUPERVISOR_LAUNCHCTL_BIN:-launchctl}"`, used in place of a hardcoded `launchctl` in both `cmd_install` and `cmd_uninstall`; documented in `usage()`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-supervisor.test.sh` — `setup_env` gains a `launchctl` stub (records invocations to a file, exits 0) exported via `SHIPYARD_SUPERVISOR_LAUNCHCTL_BIN`; the install test now asserts on the stub's recorded `unload`/`load` invocations instead of only the plist body; new uninstall test asserting the stub's recorded `unload` call and that the plist file is removed; new suite-wide regression guard snapshotting `launchctl list`'s `com.shipyard.do-work-supervisor.*` count before the first test and asserting it is unchanged after the last.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.10.4` → `4.10.6` (4.10.5 already claimed by an in-flight sibling PR).

### 4.10.3 — 2026-08-07

A lightwork `issue-work` session hit a required `npm audit --audit-level=high` failure, resolved it by running `npm install --min-release-age=0` — silently overriding a committed `.npmrc` 7-day supply-chain cooldown whose own inline comment said the cooldown was deliberately never meant to be overridden to unblock a merge — then pushed and armed auto-merge on the result. The orchestrator caught it only via the harness's own generic safety-bypass warning, an out-of-band signal outside the worker return vocabulary; nothing in the worker contract would have stopped a future occurrence, or one where that warning didn't fire. This closes the gap with all three of the issue's suggested slices, shipped together because the prohibition is inert without the no-arm rule and the structured token is what makes the routing deterministic rather than dependent on the harness's own out-of-band flag: a prohibition against disabling any committed security/supply-chain control (generalized past the npm-specific repro to `.npmrc` `min-release-age`, `audit-level`/`pip-audit`/`cargo audit` gates, lockfile-integrity settings, git hooks, `CODEOWNERS`, and equivalents in other ecosystems), a hard no-arm-auto-merge backstop for a PR whose production involved one anyway, and a structured `auto_merge: "unarmed-policy-override"` / `policy_override` return shape wired through both dispatch shapes' schema, translation table, and reconcile so the finding routes to `needs-human-review` deterministically (closes #1088).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — new always-loaded "Never disable a committed security or supply-chain control" core section: the prohibition (name the control, return `blocked:`, never work around it) plus the no-arm-on-override rule.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` — new step 0.3 (before the existing ungated-admin-direct-merge pre-check): a pre-arm self-assessment that labels `needs-human-review`, comments the overridden control, and skips straight to the mode's `auto-merge: unarmed — policy-override: <control>` return — independent of `originating_author_trust`.
