### 4.10.2 — 2026-08-04

Phase 1 of #1058: a worktree-isolated worker had no sanctioned scratch space at all. The Bash worktree-isolation guard refuses any command it deems "too complex to verify that it stays inside the worktree" — including read-only commands whose only complexity is a shell redirect, a `for`/`while` loop, or an unresolved `$PWD` — and `enforce-edit-scope.sh` independently blocks every `/tmp` write, so the only way to run a loop was to write a helper script into the repo working tree itself, exactly the phantom-dirty-state failure mode the edit-scope hook exists to prevent. This slice promotes `$WORKTREE_PATH/.shipyard-scratch/` from a narrow `--body-file`-payload-only convention (#979) into the single sanctioned general-purpose worker scratch dir for any ephemeral artifact — a `--body-file` payload, a helper script for a refused compound command, loop input, a redirect target, or captured output — makes the directory self-ignoring by seeding its own `.gitignore` (`*`) as the very first artifact so `git status` stays clean even if cleanup never runs, downgrades the mandated `rm -rf` cleanup to explicitly best-effort/non-blocking, documents the decompose-first-then-helper-script recovery pattern for a refused compound read-only command, and points `enforce-edit-scope.sh`'s BLOCK message at the sanctioned destination instead of leaving a `/tmp`-reaching worker at a dead end. Out of scope by design (not deferred): relaxing the harness's Bash complexity classifier, and any carve-out that would place scratch outside the worktree (closes #1058).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — new always-loaded "Scratch directory" hot-rule section documenting the general-purpose `.shipyard-scratch/` convention, the `.gitignore` self-ignoring seed step, and the best-effort cleanup downgrade; fragment-index rows for `body-file-convention.md` and `compound-command-refusal.md` broadened to reflect their generalized scope.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/body-file-convention.md` — retitled and rewritten as the general-purpose scratch-directory convention (the `--body-file` case is now one of two documented uses alongside the new #1058 helper-script case); adds the `.gitignore`-seed mechanism and the best-effort, non-blocking cleanup guidance.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/compound-command-refusal.md` — new section documenting the #1058 decomposition guidance: decompose a refused compound read-only command into plain, separate commands first; when a loop/redirect is genuinely unavoidable, `Write` a helper script into `.shipyard-scratch/` and invoke it as one plain command via a literal absolute or worktree-relative path, never `$PWD`.
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/enforce-edit-scope.sh` — BLOCK stderr message now names `<worktree>/.shipyard-scratch/` as the sanctioned scratch destination for a worker reaching for `/tmp`; the `/tmp` (out-of-worktree) block itself is unchanged.
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/tests/enforce-edit-scope.test.sh` — new assertion covering the updated BLOCK message text.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — extended the existing scratch-parity regression block with #1058 assertions across `SKILL.md`, `body-file-convention.md`, `compound-command-refusal.md`, and `enforce-edit-scope.sh`.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — both scratch-dir call sites (§5's PR-body write, §6's external-trust-gate comment) updated to seed `.shipyard-scratch/.gitignore` first and mark the `rm -rf` cleanup best-effort.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.10.1` → `4.10.2`.

### 4.10.1 — 2026-08-07

`do-work-supervisor.sh` shipped in 4.10.0 at mode `100644`, so the command the README documents fails on a fresh install:

```
$ plugins/shipyard/scripts/do-work-supervisor.sh reap
permission denied
```

Nothing in the pipeline could have caught it. shellcheck lints content, not permissions; CI and every documented workflow invoke suites as `bash <file>`, which works regardless of the exec bit; and the 113-suite run was green. The break surfaced only when a human followed the README's own instructions on the installed plugin. A sweep for the same shape found a second, older instance — `verify-new-dep-versions.sh`, documented for direct invocation in the `adding-dependencies` skill and committed `644` since #1046 — which is what makes this a class worth guarding rather than a one-off slip. Both are now `100755`, and a new test sweeps every production script under `plugins/shipyard/scripts/` for both halves of the "directly invocable" contract: committed-executable **and** carrying a shebang. `tests/` is deliberately out of scope — suites are genuinely mixed between `755` and `644` in this repo because nothing ever runs them by path, so there is no convention there to enforce (closes #1094).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/do-work-supervisor.sh` — mode `100644` → `100755`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/verify-new-dep-versions.sh` — mode `100644` → `100755`; same defect, predates 4.10.0.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/script-exec-bits.test.sh` — new. Asserts no production script under `scripts/` is committed non-executable, pins the two files that motivated the guard, and checks every one starts with a shebang. Carries a non-vacuity assertion (the sweep must match >20 files) so a broken glob fails loudly instead of passing green against nothing — the same failure shape the repo's rules warn about for narrowed sweeps. Verified to fail against the reverted mode before being trusted.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.10.0` → `4.10.1`.

### 4.10.0 — 2026-08-06

`/do-work` is specified as a terminating batch job but used as a continuous burndown loop, and nothing bridged the gap — when a session ended the backlog simply stalled until a human typed the command again. Months of hardening had gone into `drain.md`'s termination assertion on the theory that the orchestrator rationalizes premature exits; the session tombstones say otherwise. A clean termination runs `cleanup-summary.md`'s cleanup step, which **retires the session state file**, so a session file that still exists is by construction a session that died before cleanup — and four of the five files found in `~/.shipyard/sessions/` when #1083 was filed had `in_flight > 0` at their last write. Those sessions did not decide to stop; they died mid-flight with workers still dispatched, and the drain never ran at all. No amount of "don't stop early" prose fixes a session that runs out of context. The spec already assumes a next run exists — #662's bounded-exit concessions are "surfaced for the next run", setup step 3c triages orphan worktrees, and setup step 5.7 / #373 seeds inherited DIRTY PRs as an explicit cross-session hand-off — so restart-and-resume was already designed and built. The only missing piece was the thing that starts the next run.

New `do-work-supervisor.sh` supplies it: a launchd-driven tick that decides whether to launch a fresh headless `claude -p "/shipyard:do-work"`. Five guards must all pass before it spends a token — an atomic-mkdir single-instance lock (macOS ships no `flock`), a liveness check keyed on session-file mtime, a circuit breaker that trips after N consecutive unproductive sessions and stays tripped until `reset` is run by hand, a daily launch ceiling, and a work-exists gate that never boots a session just to discover an empty backlog. A `gh` failure is deliberately distinguished from an empty backlog so a network blip cannot silently park the loop or penalise the breaker. Every observed session death is classified (`abandoned` / `clean` / `failed-launch`) and appended to a per-repo ledger, turning "why do sessions die" into data rather than a guess — if deaths are predictable context exhaustion then restarting is right, and if it is a crash loop then restarting makes it worse; the ledger is what tells the difference. Abandoned session files are **archived, never deleted**: the stale file is the only evidence that a session died rather than exiting cleanly, so discarding it would destroy the signal the whole mechanism reads (closes #1083, closes #1084).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/do-work-supervisor.sh` — new. `tick` (the launchd entry point), `status`, `install`, `uninstall`, `reset`, and `reap` subcommands; the generated plist sets `AbandonProcessGroup`, without which launchd reaps the `claude` session seconds after the tick job exits. Defaults to `--permission-mode auto` rather than `bypassPermissions`, matching the posture `do-work-RATIONALE.md` #763 recommends — an unattended loop is exactly the context where silently escalating permissions is worst. Eleven `SHIPYARD_SUPERVISOR_*` env vars tune the thresholds.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/lib/common.sh` — gained `sessions_dir` and `iso_to_epoch`, both previously private to `status.sh`. The supervisor reads the same session files and needs identical parsing, so the helpers moved to the shared library rather than becoming a third copy (the #887 precedent). `iso_to_epoch`'s degrade-to-`0` behavior on malformed input is now documented as load-bearing for both callers.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/status.sh` — local `sessions_dir` / `iso_to_epoch` definitions deleted in favour of the `lib/common.sh` versions it already sources.
- **Productivity is measured in PRs, never duration.** The first cut excused a zero-PR session if it had run long enough, on the theory that a long session must have been doing something. Running the supervisor against the real tombstones killed that idea immediately: a 6-hour session with `session_prs: 0` came back classified `productive`, when it is precisely the outcome the breaker exists to catch — maximum tokens, nothing shipped. `SHIPYARD_SUPERVISOR_MIN_PRODUCTIVE_PRS` (default `1`) replaced the duration threshold; duration is still recorded in the ledger for forensics but takes no part in the verdict. Pinned by a regression test built from that exact session's shape.
- **The mtime probe must try GNU `stat` before BSD `stat`, not the other way round.** The first cut probed `stat -f %m` first and fell back to `stat -c %Y`, which is green on macOS and catastrophic on Linux: GNU's `-f` means `--file-system`, so it reads `%m` as a *file argument* and prints multi-line filesystem info **with exit 0** — the fallback never fires, and the garbage reaches `(( now - mtime ))`, where bash resolves the bare word `File` as a variable name and `set -u` aborts the whole tick. Every mtime-dependent assertion failed that way on CI while passing locally. Probe order is now GNU-first (BSD has no `-c` and errors cleanly), plus a `^[0-9]+$` guard so anything non-numeric degrades to `0` — "epoch zero, therefore very old" — rather than wedging the supervisor. Pinned by a test that stubs `stat` to expose only GNU semantics, verified to fail against the original ordering.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-supervisor.test.sh` — new, 61 assertions across every guard, both breaker directions, the three death classifications, the archive-don't-delete contract, lock takeover from a stale holder, the GNU-`stat` probe ordering, and the generated plist's `AbandonProcessGroup` key. Stubs `gh` and `claude` and runs against a throwaway `SHIPYARD_HOME`, so no test spends a token or touches the real ledger.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/common-lib.test.sh` — coverage for the two newly-shared helpers, including a hardcoded UTC epoch constant that a parse-as-local-time regression would fail.
- `README.md` — new "Keeping the loop running" section.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.9.0` → `4.10.0`.

### 4.9.0 — 2026-08-05

`/shipyard:my-turn`'s walkthrough interleaved *collecting* a human decision with *acting on* it, so the maintainer sat waiting between every answer. Three things landed in that gap: the per-issue record step (write scratch file → `gh issue comment` → `gh issue edit --remove-label`) fired before the next issue's first question rendered; each recommendation was grounded in codebase reads performed at ask-time; and the ranked queue interleaved decision-gated issues with PR reviews and `blocked:ci` investigations, so a walk step could turn into real work mid-queue. The maintainer's framing: *"simply collect any input that it needs from me — FOR ALL ISSUES — and then get to work."*

The default run is now three phases. **Phase 1 (Collect)** partitions the ranked human-only queue into decision-gated issues vs everything else, runs one bulk read-only context pass for all recommendations up front, then asks every decision back-to-back with zero GitHub round-trips in between — journaling each answer to `~/.shipyard/my-turn-<repo-slug>.json` as it's given, so a dead session loses nothing and a re-invocation can resume or commit. **Phase 2 (Commit)** replays the journal and fires every issue's record step in one batch, then stops. **Phase 3 (Walk)** runs the pre-existing one-at-a-time walkthrough for the non-decision items, unchanged.

Three invariants the batching could plausibly have broken are preserved explicitly: **carry-forward** (only *mutually independent* decisions are grouped into one `AskUserQuestion` call; a decision whose options depend on an earlier answer is still asked singly, in source order), the **partial-run rule** (generalized per-issue — a fully-answered issue's gate is cleared, any issue with a skipped decision keeps it), and **non-autonomy** (Phase 2 stops rather than chaining into `/do-work`). A halt during Phase 1 proceeds to Phase 2 rather than discarding answers already given. The no-agent rule narrowed from "no agents" to "no *mutating* agents" so Phase 1's read-only bulk context pass may fan out readers — that pass is where the remaining wait lives once mutations move to Phase 2 (closes #1070).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — § Walkthrough mode split into Phase 1 / Phase 2 / Phase 3 with the queue partition, bulk context pass, answer journal, and batched-commit contract; § Termination contract now branches on where a halt lands (Phase 1 halt still commits) and moves the survey re-derive point to after Phase 2; § Performance budget gained a separate front-loaded budget for the bulk pass whose metric is zero tool calls between answers; § Don't narrowed the no-agent rule to mutating agents, and gained rules against interleaving collect with act, batching dependent decisions, discarding journaled answers, and chaining into `/do-work`; frontmatter description, intro, division-of-labor table, and mode resolution updated.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/resolve-decisions.md` — § Record + unblock gained an explicit firing-mode contract (immediate for the standalone `--issue N` path, unchanged; deferred for a batch caller), stating that deferring changes timing but never content, the partial-run branch, or which labels come off; the § Don't "don't advance past an unanswered decision" rule now explicitly denies a batching caller any carry-forward exemption.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh` — the `dispatches no agents` assertion updated to the narrowed `dispatches no *mutating* agents` phrasing, plus assertions that read-only research agents are permitted and that code workers are still forbidden; new #1070 regression block pinning the three phase headings, Phase 1's mutation-free guarantee, the journal path, the no-interleaving rule, the mutually-independent batching constraint, the deferred firing mode, and the no-auto-chain rule.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.8.4` → `4.9.0`.

### 4.8.4 — 2026-08-04

Phase 1 of #1060 (both ends of the deadlock, plus deadlock-signature surfacing; out of scope: the 3-attempt fix-checks cap, the rebase rate-limit cap, the trivial-or-bail conflict policy). A PR that is simultaneously `mergeStateStatus: DIRTY` and carrying a hard-failing check couldn't be moved by either repair mode: `fix-checks-only`'s DIRTY short-circuit (#1015) bailed straight to `fix-rebase`, while `fix-rebase`'s own precondition bailed on any hard failure back to `fix-checks` — a cycle with no exit, and one the drain silently reported as "settled" via `rebase_blocked_prs` membership even though neither mode had ever actually touched the PR. The crux: while a PR is DIRTY, GitHub cannot compute a merge ref, so no check can ever queue or refresh — a red check observed on a DIRTY PR is a frozen fossil of the last base the PR could still build against, not live evidence about its current health. `fix-rebase.md` step 2's hard-failure bail is now gated on `mergeStateStatus != "DIRTY"`, so a DIRTY PR always proceeds with the rebase regardless of check state. `drain.md`'s `D_dirty_red` set (DIRTY-and-red PRs) is no longer a distinct dispatch target routed to `fix-checks` — it's an informational subset of `D_dirty`, dispatched to `fix-rebase` identically to any other DIRTY PR; `R_new` (which does route to `fix-checks`) gained an explicit `mergeStateStatus != "DIRTY"` exclusion so a DIRTY-and-red PR is never double-counted into it. This inverts the routing rule from #577, which was correct before #1015 removed the assumption it depended on (that `fix-checks` would rebase as part of getting green). A new `dirty_fix_checks_prs` / `deadlocked_prs` tracking pair records the narrower residual case — a PR that returned `dirty` from a fix-checks dispatch and later `blocked rebase` from a fix-rebase dispatch within the same session — and surfaces it as a distinct `deadlocked` entry in the end-of-session summary rather than an undifferentiated `rebase_blocked_prs` member (closes #1060).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md` — step 2's hard-failure bail gated on `mergeStateStatus != "DIRTY"`; new prose documenting that a DIRTY PR skips the bail and rebases regardless of check state; header description updated to state this mode now also handles DIRTY-and-red PRs.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — DIRTY-PR short-circuit section notes that drain no longer dispatches this mode against a DIRTY PR at all; the short-circuit is now a defensive net for the mid-session race (PR goes DIRTY between dispatch and worker start), not the primary DIRTY-detection path.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — `D_dirty_red` redefined as an informational subset of `D_dirty` (references #1060); `D_dirty` no longer excludes `D_dirty_red` members and no longer reads the check rollup for its own eligibility test; `R_new` gained an explicit `mergeStateStatus != "DIRTY"` exclusion; per-poll action 1 no longer dispatches `D_dirty_red` to fix-checks, action 2 dispatches it to fix-rebase with an inverted routing log line; the `dirty_red=` status-line gloss updated to describe the new informational role; new `dirty_fix_checks_prs` / `deadlocked_prs` per-session structures initialized alongside the existing three; the settled definition documents `deadlocked_prs` as a subset of `rebase_blocked_prs`, not a disjoint gate.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — the `dirty #<M>` reconcile (explicit return, narrative-synthesized, and the green→dirty trust-but-verify downgrade) now writes `dirty_fix_checks_prs`; the `blocked rebase #<M>` reconcile now runs the deadlock-signature check and writes `deadlocked_prs` when it fires, with an amended PR comment.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — `Drain-phase rebases:` summary line gained a `<deadlocked_count> deadlocked (...)` clause, counted and listed once (not double-counted into the generic `blocked` bucket).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — new rule documenting the inverted DIRTY+red routing, explicitly citing #1060 as superseding #577.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — new "#1060 supersedes #577" section recording the full history: why the old rule was correct pre-#1015, why #1015 invalidated its premise, why rebase (not a louder alarm) is the fix, and why both ends had to flip in the same PR.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/drain-dirty-red-routing.test.sh` — rewritten to assert the inverted routing (was: DIRTY-and-red → fix-checks per #577; now: DIRTY-and-red → fix-rebase per #1060, with `D_dirty_red` an informational subset of `D_dirty`).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — new #1060 regression block asserting the deadlock-signature plumbing across `fix-rebase.md`, `drain.md`, `steady-state.md`, `cleanup-summary.md`, `dont.md`, and `do-work-RATIONALE.md`.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.8.3` → `4.8.4`.

### 4.8.3 — 2026-08-04

Phase 1 of #1059: post-relocation config reads silently lost the `.shipyard/config.local.json` layer for the rest of the session. `/shipyard:do-work` reads its merged config once at step 0.4, in the primary checkout, then relocates every subsequent write (and, incidentally, most subsequent reads) into a fresh orchestrator worktree checked out from `origin/<default-branch>` at step 0.5 — which by construction has no gitignored files. `shipyard-config.sh`'s `repo_root()` already honored a `SHIPYARD_REPO_ROOT` override, but nothing pinned it, so every config read after relocation (a fresh `shipyard-config.sh get`, or a helper like `resolve-dispatch-model.sh` / `flake-enforce.sh`) silently fell back to the merged defaults+user+repo layers, dropping local overrides to `trust.authors`, `auto_merge.policy`, `concurrency.*`, `cost_tracking.*`, `ci.*`, `flake_registry.*`, and every `models.*` knob with no warning. This slice pins `SHIPYARD_REPO_ROOT` to the primary checkout at step 0.5 (stashed the same way the per-worktree session id already is), adds a drift warning for call sites the pin hasn't reached yet, and fixes the one concrete consumer already known to be affected: step 5.8's flake-registry enforcement was writing/reading `.shipyard/flake-suspects.txt` against the ephemeral orchestrator worktree, silently restarting flake tracking from empty every session instead of accumulating across them. Sweeping every remaining post-relocation config-reading call site, and auditing other gitignored session inputs for the same bug, are filed as follow-ups (closes #1059).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — step 0.4 now captures and echoes `SHIPYARD_PRIMARY_CHECKOUT_ROOT` as a literal while cwd is still the primary checkout; new step 0.56 stashes it into the orchestrator worktree, pins `SHIPYARD_REPO_ROOT` for the rest of the session (scoped explicitly to the orchestrator, never propagated into a dispatched worker), and adds a conservative drift warning that fires only when the primary checkout's local layer both exists and changes the merged config.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/04-backlog-divert.md` — step 5.8's `flake-enforce.sh enforce --repo-root` now re-derives the pinned primary-checkout root from the step-0.56 stash instead of `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (which resolved to the orchestrator worktree post-relocation); corrected the adjacent comment that had asserted the orchestrator worktree was "where … `.shipyard/config.local.json`" lives.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.8.2` → `4.8.3`.

### 4.8.2 — 2026-08-02

`/shipyard:refine-issues`' candidate scan treated the `user-feedback` label as a live "raw text needing classify+rewrite" signal, but CLAUDE.md § Origin labels defines it as permanent provenance — "applied at intake and never removed." The two readings collided for an issue filed already-refined but carrying the label: there's no `<!-- do-work-refinement-agent -->` sentinel until a refinement worker has actually run once, so such an issue matched the scan every session, forever. Live repro this session: #1045 (trusted author, fully-refined body, `user-feedback` label from a prior classification) bucketed as "awaiting refinement" and would have routed to a no-op-at-best Branch A rewrite. Narrowed the classify+rewrite source signal (option (a) from the issue's suggested-shape list, most faithful to the label's documented origin-not-signal semantics) to require BOTH the `user-feedback` label AND a body that still contains the raw-feedback fenced block (```` ```user-feedback ````) from the intake contract — the contract's only hard requirement, so every genuinely-unrefined issue still matches while an already-refined one (hand-written, or refined by a prior pass whose rewrite strips the block) does not. The rewrite step in Branch A bucket (c) is now documented as load-bearing for removing that block, mirroring how resolve-defaults' `## Open questions` heading removal already drops an issue from its own signal.

Also resolved the Branch A(c) / CLAUDE.md contradiction the issue flagged: `refine-issues.md`'s bucket (c) directive said "leave `user-feedback` and `needs-human-review` in place" — phrasing that implied both labels were already present and didn't add either — while CLAUDE.md's gate-label table (and this same command's own summary table) already documented the classify+rewrite branch as an applier of `needs-human-review`. Resolved toward "applies it," matching CLAUDE.md and the command's own table: the directive now explicitly runs `gh issue edit <N> --add-label needs-human-review` (a no-op if the intake backend already applied it), mirroring Branch C's explicit add. This isn't a `dont.md` attempt-then-escalate violation for trusted-author work — the #1055 body-shape narrowing above means a trusted-author, already-refined issue like #1045 never reaches Branch A at all; the human-review gate is deliberate scrutiny reserved for issues whose body is still genuinely unrefined end-user input (closes #1055).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/refine-issues.md` — the step-3 candidate scan's `$is_user_feedback` computation now ANDs the label with a new `$looks_like_raw_feedback` body-shape check; the source-signal table, Branch A's header/condition/worker-prompt table, the sentinel-filter walkthrough, the intake-contract prose, and the label-semantics table all updated to describe the conditional signal; Branch A bucket (c)'s directive now explicitly adds `needs-human-review` instead of "leave ... in place"; a new Idempotency-invariants bullet documents the body-shape signal.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01b-backlog-overview.md` — the `--fast` advisory-count jq (a duplicate of the canonical scan) and the bucket 5.4 criteria description updated to match.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01c-label-recovery-refine.md` — step 3.5's classify+rewrite branch description updated to match.
- `CLAUDE.md` — Origin labels section documents that the permanent `user-feedback` label can't double as a live refinement flag, and points at the intake contract's raw-feedback fenced block as the actual signal.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/refine-origin-label-1055.test.sh` — new per-issue regression test: doc-consistency assertions across the four touched files, plus a functional jq test pinning the scan's behavior against a synthetic #1045-shaped fixture (must not match), a genuine raw-feedback fixture (must match), a post-rewrite bucket-(c) fixture (must not match), and an open-questions fixture (must still match, unaffected by the narrowing).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.8.1` → `4.8.2`.

### 4.8.1 — 2026-08-02

Recurrence fix: a sixth confirmed instance of the "worker yields to a Monitor/background process before committing, stranding a complete diff" failure class already closed five times (#813, #529, #753, #707, #838). Staleness probe confirmed all five prior fixes are live and correctly scoped, but every one of them frames the prohibition around a specific trigger — a CI-watch loop (#753), a background test-waiter (#529/#813), gating the commit on a CI result (#707) — so an `issue-work` worker doing ordinary mid-implementation work (no CI watch, no backgrounded build) didn't recognize itself as covered when it opened a `Monitor` of its own accord and yielded before ever running `git commit`. Added a new, mode-agnostic "commit-before-yield" invariant to `shipyard:worker-preamble`'s always-loaded core (not a fragment, so every mode loads it unconditionally): commit locally-verified work before *any* operation that could end your turn — Monitor, backgrounded `Bash`, or otherwise — generalizing #707's CI-watch-specific statement to every yield point, and naming `issue-work` explicitly as covered. `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` was left untouched (115926 of a 116000-byte ceiling, ~74 bytes headroom — no room for growth) per its own size-budget test; it already carries #529/#707/#753 cross-references naming `issue-work` at its own step-8 return contract. The orchestrator-side recovery contract that caught this session's incident (`steady-state.md` A.0.5's inspect-before-reap + resume-the-same-agent path) is unchanged and un-weakened — the only touch there is a new, purely additive canned resume-message template so future A.0.5 recoveries fill in bracketed fields from the verified worktree reading instead of hand-writing recovery prose per incident (closes #1054).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — new bullet in "Return-contract discipline" (between the #529/#813 bullet and the #753 bullet): the commit-before-yield invariant, citing #1054, naming `issue-work` explicitly, generalizing #707. File grew 52379 → 53906 bytes, still under the 57000-byte ceiling.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — A.0.5's resume-message guidance gained a literal, fill-in-the-brackets canned template (verified state fields, stop-waiting instruction, re-run-foreground instruction, terminal-string instruction) immediately after the existing prose bullets it formalizes; no existing bullet, step, or test-covered behavior was changed.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/commit-before-yield-1054.test.sh` — new per-issue regression test: the new bullet exists, cites #1054, names `issue-work` explicitly, generalizes #707's "any yield point" framing, lives in SKILL.md's core section (not a fragment file), and both `SKILL.md` and `issue-work.md` stay under their respective size-budget ceilings.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.8.0` → `4.8.1`.

Extended the CLAUDE.md-emission mechanism `/shipyard:init` already has (step 9.5, the primary-checkout guard's paired hook + CLAUDE.md rule) with a new step 9.7, gated on a new `--dep-rule <off|pointer|inline>` flag (default `off`, opt-in per the parent issue's attribution requirement): `pointer` mode appends a one-liner pointing at `Skill("shipyard:adding-dependencies")`; `inline` mode appends a self-contained ~6-line summary for repos without shipyard installed. Idempotency is handled by a new `<!-- shipyard:adding-dependencies:start/end -->` sentinel-comment pair that replaces the block in place on a re-run — a markdown-level idempotency convention step 9.5 doesn't have (it only tracks idempotency on the `settings.json` hook entry) (closes #1048).

### 4.7.0 — 2026-08-02

Follow-up to #1045, explicitly deferred out of that PR's phase-1 slice (item 5 of its suggested shape). #1045 shipped the `shipyard:adding-dependencies` skill's introduction-time rule — look up the current stable version before adding a new dependency, across every manifest class from `package.json` to GitHub Actions `uses:` pins. That rule only fires at introduction; it can't catch drift that accumulates on dependencies already current when introduced, or that predate the rule entirely — exactly the pattern #1045's own measurement documented (46 of 117 Dependabot PRs on `mattsears18/lightwork` were major-version bumps, 30 opened after the original #694 rule landed). ### 4.6.0 — 2026-08-02

Follow-up to #1045, explicitly deferred out of that PR's phase-1 slice. #1045 shipped the `shipyard:adding-dependencies` skill — the rule to look up a new dependency's current stable version before writing it down, across every manifest class from `package.json` to GitHub Actions `uses:` pins. That rule was prose plus a PR-body convention ("record the resolved version"); nothing verified the claim, so a worker that silently wrote a stale/remembered version produced a PR indistinguishable from a compliant one. Shipping the phase-1 slice of the verification: a new `verify-new-dep-versions.sh` that parses `git diff <base>...HEAD` for newly-ADDED dependency lines and cross-checks the written version against the authoritative registry for the two ecosystems that have a CLI-driven, network-reachable lookup (npm/npx via `npm view`, GitHub Actions `uses:` pins via `gh api .../releases/latest`) — failing on an unexplained ≥1-major gap. Every other manifest class the skill names, and the npm/Actions path itself when the CLI or network is unavailable, falls back to an offline check (does the PR body record a resolved version?) that only skip-with-notes, never hard-fails. The peer/SDK carve-out (react/react-native/`@react-native-firebase/*`/`expo-*`) and a cooldown/carve-out note in the PR body are both honored as valid explanations for an otherwise-unexplained gap. Wired as a new step 4 in the skill itself — not a pointer added to `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`, which sits at 115926 of a 116000-byte hard ceiling with no headroom for it — since the worker already loads that skill whenever it introduces a dependency (closes #1046).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/verify-new-dep-versions.sh` — new script: parses a unified diff (from `git diff <base-ref>...HEAD` or a `--diff-file` fixture, for hermetic testing) for newly-added dependency lines across every manifest class `shipyard:adding-dependencies` names; npm (`package.json`, with a reserved-top-level-key guard so a package's own `"version"` bump is never mistaken for a new dependency) and inline `npx`/`pnpm dlx`/`yarn dlx` invocations resolve via `npm view <pkg> version`; GitHub Actions `uses:` pins resolve via `gh api repos/<owner>/<action>/releases/latest`; every other class (pip, Go, Cargo, Gemfile, Gradle, CocoaPods, Dockerfile `FROM`, `.nvmrc`/`.tool-versions`), or the hard-online path when the CLI/network is unavailable, falls back to an offline `--pr-body-file`-based check that only skip-with-notes. A version *bump* of an already-present dependency (removed old line + added new line for the same name) is distinguished from a genuine addition and is never flagged — that's Dependabot's job, not this check's. Written bash-3.2-compatible (macOS's default `/usr/bin/bash` has no `declare -A`) using newline-delimited membership sets instead of associative arrays.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/adding-dependencies/SKILL.md` — new "Step 4 — run the automated pre-PR-create check" section documenting the invocation (reusing the worker's own `.shipyard-scratch/pr-body.md` scratch file from `issue-work.md` step 5's `--body-file` convention) and the hard-online/offline-fallback split; the "Out of scope" list's pre-PR-automated-check bullet removed and replaced with a narrower phase-2 bullet naming the ecosystems still offline-only.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/dep-version-verification-1046.test.sh` — new per-issue regression test: hermetic (PATH-stubbed fake `npm`/`gh`, fixture diffs, no real git repo or network), 33 assertions covering same-major pass, unexplained-gap fail, both carve-out shapes, the PR-body cooldown-note explanation, npm/gh CLI unavailability falling back to the offline check, the offline-only classes never hard-failing, the newly-added-vs-bumped distinction, the reserved-npm-key guard, the no-newly-added-deps clean pass, and usage errors.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.5.1` → `4.6.0`.

The absolute rule was restated with the same contradiction in five more places beyond the named fragment, and the return-string contract had no field for the resulting incidental PR, which the orchestrator's reconcile would have silently dropped from `session_prs`. Added an explicit, narrow carve-out everywhere the rule was restated — scoped to a pure missing-test-coverage fix for an already-acknowledged untested path, never a real behavioral bug — and wired the incidental PR through the structured return schema so it's tracked and drained like any other session PR (closes #1044).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06b-scope-carveouts.md` — the QA-verification carve-out's dispatch-mechanics paragraph qualified from "opens no PR" to "opens no *resolving* PR," cross-referencing the new exception.

### 4.4.33 — 2026-08-01

`/shipyard:do-work`'s setup phase reaps stale agent worktrees from prior sessions inside a single fire-and-forget `(...) &` background Bash tool call (step 3b, wrapped alongside steps 1.6 / 1.6.5 / 3a / 3c). The permission classifier evaluates that whole compound command as one unit — a destructive verb anywhere inside it (`git worktree remove --force` deep in `reap-stale`) can trip a denial for the entire call, so none of the background group's steps run at all, including its own `unreaped:` bookkeeping. `dont.md`'s "Don't swallow a failed or denied reap" rule (#712) already requires surfacing this via `worktree-reap.sh report-unreaped` and a `Cleanup: <N> worktree(s) could not be reaped` line in the end-of-session summary, and that wiring exists in `cleanup-summary.md`'s step 5.5 — but it only runs at end of session, has no fallback for a whole-group denial at session *start*, and the step itself never echoed its computed count anywhere the orchestrator could read it back, so the line silently never fired even when the underlying scan ran correctly. A live session (`do-work-20260801T1015`) hit exactly this: 29 agent worktrees from prior sessions accumulated unreported, a recurrence of #836 with a different root cause (closes #1042).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00b-parallelization-cache.md` — new "Classifier-denial fallback" section: detect a whole-group Bash tool call denial directly from its tool result, run `worktree-reap.sh report-unreaped` as an independent read-only foreground call (not part of the risky compound command, so it can't share the same denial), and record the outcome in a new session-local `setup_reap_sweep_denial` state field rather than letting the denial go unreported until (or unless) end-of-session cleanup happens to catch it.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/orchestrator-state-reference.md` — documented the new `setup_reap_sweep_denial` session-local field (written by the setup-phase fallback above, read by cleanup-summary's step 5.5).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — step 5.5 now explicitly `echo`s its computed `unreaped_worktrees` count (and each leftover path) instead of leaving it trapped in a shell variable the Bash call discards on exit — the concrete mechanical reason the `Cleanup:` line never appeared even when worktrees were genuinely stranded; also folds in `setup_reap_sweep_denial`'s count via `max` so a setup-time whole-group denial is never lost even if this session's own later reap attempts happen to succeed on unrelated worktrees.
- Verified the bounded, oldest-first reap cap (`worktree_reap.max_per_session`, from #836 fix 2) already addresses this issue's third suggested option — no changes needed there. Left the second suggested option (a default `Bash` permission rule for `worktree-reap.sh reap` in `/shipyard:init`'s settings scaffolding) out of scope — it grants a destructive capability by default and is a security/permissions posture decision for the maintainer, not one to bake in autonomously.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.32` → `4.4.33`.

### 4.4.32 — 2026-08-01

`session-state.sh`'s `usage()` heredoc always ends with the full exit-code legend, whose own last row describes exit 66 (the #365 cross-repo write refusal) — the most alarming-sounding entry in the table. A caller that reads a failed invocation's stderr through `tail` (a normal thing to do against a chatty helper) saw that legend tail and misread a plain exit-64 usage error as a cross-repo contamination refusal — the exact misdiagnosis a real `/shipyard:do-work` session hit on 2026-08-01 when an `update` call with the wrong flags was read through `tail -2` and diagnosed as a #365 refusal, costing two follow-up investigation calls before the actual exit code (64, not 66) was noticed. Added a `usage_error` helper that prints the usage block and then a final, unambiguous stderr line naming the real outcome — `session-state.sh: <subcommand>: usage error (exit 64)` — on every usage-error exit path, so the last line of a `tail`'d read is always meaningful (closes #1039).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/session-state.sh` — new `usage_error [<subcommand>]` helper (calls `usage()`, then emits the named trailing line, then `exit 64`); every one of the 21 `usage; exit 64` call sites across `init`/`read`/`update`/`bump-tokens`/`read-tokens`/`set-progress`/`is-active`/`cleanup` and the top-level dispatcher now routes through it. The `-h|--help|help` path (exit 0) is untouched — it still ends on the plain exit-code legend, since it isn't an error.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/session-state.test.sh` — new "usage-error tail line names the actual outcome" section asserting the last stderr line on several usage-error paths (unknown subcommand, missing required flag, unknown flag, no subcommand at all) names the exit-64 outcome and never reads like the exit-66 legend row, plus a regression check that `--help` is unaffected.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.31` → `4.4.32`.

### 4.4.31 — 2026-08-01

The thin entry `commands/do-work.md`'s "Session state file" section states its own inclusion criterion — stay reachable inline only when consulted on the reconcile hot path every turn — and inlined the `bump-tokens` call shape on that basis, but left `session-state.sh update`'s call shape out, reachable only from the on-demand `session-state-file.md`. By the entry's own criterion `update` belongs inline too: every reconcile turn writes `.in_flight` (slot release) and `.session_prs` (shipped-return append) at the same frequency as `bump-tokens`. The full flag reference stays in `session-state-file.md`, unchanged.

### 4.4.30 — 2026-08-01

`dont.md`'s "Don't dispatch fix-rebase outside the end-of-session drain" rule rested on a premise — "steady-state dispatch never produces a DIRTY PR" — that's false on any repo whose release convention bumps a shared manifest/CHANGELOG row on every merge, which is exactly this repo's own "ALWAYS cut a release when a PR merges" rule. A session repro (`01K5AguWha8G2tPgnjMNKSd6`, 2026-07-31, `--concurrency 1`) showed the cost: PR #1013 went DIRTY ~17:33Z when a sibling merge advanced its version row, and sat with no merge ref (so no CI could even queue) until the end-of-session drain rebased it ~22:05Z — roughly four hours and ~24 intervening merges later, plus a whole `fix-checks-only` dispatch burned misdiagnosing the resulting silence as a CI infrastructure issue. At `--concurrency 1` this isn't a race the old rule's "would churn branches" / "race a fix-checks worker" concerns describe — there's only one slot, so neither applies. Replaced the unconditional ban with a three-condition permit: dispatch `fix-rebase` mid-session (not just at drain) when the PR is in `session_prs` and DIRTY, no in-flight worker already holds its head branch, and a `--concurrency` slot is free — safe to attempt speculatively because `fix-rebase.md`'s existing §4.6 trivial-conflict-or-bail policy already resolves a conflict confined to the coordinated manifest+CHANGELOG rows deterministically and bails identically to the drain-time case on anything wider (closes #1034).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — the fix-rebase bullet rewritten from an unconditional ban to the three-condition permit, with a `why` link to the new RATIONALE section.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — "Why fix-rebase is drain-only" corrected to acknowledge steady-state DOES produce DIRTY PRs on coordinated-manifest repos; new "Don't dispatch fix-rebase mid-session outside the three conditions" section with the session repro and the corrected rule.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — D-tail sub-sweep 2's DIRTY branch now dispatches a mid-session `fix-rebase` worker when the three conditions hold (falling back to the drain otherwise); the fix-rebase-work reconcile header updated to note it's now reached from either dispatch site.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.29` → `4.4.30`.

### 4.4.29 — 2026-08-01

`bump-tokens --degraded-total-only` (the harness-`<usage>`-gap fallback from #279) already recorded a per-session `.tokens.degraded_attribution_count` counter and a per-invocation `degraded: true` marker — but `cost-history.sh flush` never propagated either into the persistent cross-session ledger, and neither the end-of-session summary nor `/shipyard:cost report` labeled the affected spend accurately. Worse, where a label DID exist (the end-of-session banner), it claimed the degraded figure was a "lower bound, real spend ≈ 1.5× printed" — wrong in both directions: folding a dispatch's whole `total_tokens` into the `--input` bucket prices any real cache-read tokens (far cheaper than input) at the input rate, inflating the estimate, while pricing any real output tokens (several times pricier than input) at the same rate deflates it. The two errors don't cancel predictably, so the figure isn't reliable as a bound at all — the same reasoning #728 already applied to unpriced models ("`$0.00` is a legitimate value and must never double as the error sentinel") applies here too: a precise-looking `$23.70` with a fabricated zero-output profile must not pass as authoritative. Relabeled every surface **UNRELIABLE** (distinct from the genuine, directional `[LOWER BOUND]` #728 introduced for unpriced models) and closed the propagation gap so the label survives past session-file reap (closes #1035).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/cost-history.sh` — `degraded_attribution_count` now flows end-to-end: the `flush` session-record projection persists it from `.tokens.degraded_attribution_count`, `merge_session_records` merges it by max (same reconcile-safety contract as every other `tokens.*` counter, #745), `report`'s rollup sums it (`degraded_attribution_count`) and counts affected sessions (`degraded_sessions`), and `render_markdown_report` tags the `Spend:` line `[UNRELIABLE]` plus prints a new `DEGRADED COST ATTRIBUTION` advisory block (mirroring, and deliberately distinct from, the existing `UNPRICED MODELS` block) whenever the window contains a degraded session.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — both end-of-session banners (all-degraded and partial-degraded) and their explanatory prose rewritten from "lower bound, real spend ≈ 1.5×" to UNRELIABLE, with the direction-of-error reasoning spelled out; cross-references the now-propagated persistent-ledger labeling.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — corrected "degraded attribution under-counts cost" to reflect that it's not a clean under-count.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — "Degraded-path tradeoff" section corrected; the 1.5× multiplier claim is retired with an explanation of why it was wrong.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/cost.md` — new "Degraded cost attribution" doc section, parallel in structure to "Unpriced models" but explaining why the two get different labels.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/cost-history.test.sh` — new regression coverage: `flush` projects `degraded_attribution_count` (including the `0` no-degradation case), `--reconcile` merges it by max rather than summing, and `report` (markdown + json) tags `[UNRELIABLE]` / prints the advisory only when a degraded session is in range, with a companion "clean window prints neither" check.

### 4.4.28 — 2026-07-31

Two of shipyard's audit dimensions were structurally best suited to catching drift before anyone noticed it, but neither ran that way: `testing-auditor` (tests that lie, coverage holes, CI gate completeness) and `functional-qa-auditor` (signs in as a real user, exercises features end-to-end) — and every other `/audit` dimension — were invoke-on-demand only, via `/shipyard:audit <dimension>`. In practice that means they run once a maintainer already suspects a problem, which is exactly the case where they add the least value; a consuming repo (`mattsears18/lightwork`) had native E2E disabled for 58 days across 45 published releases, with `app/`/`components/` contributing zero coverage signal, before anyone looked. Closed by adding a recurring-cadence config surface and a thin cadence-gate command in front of `/shipyard:audit`'s existing dispatch machinery, rather than inventing a new execution engine: a new `audits.schedule` block in `shipyard.config.json` names dimensions + a `<int>[mhdw]` cadence, and `/shipyard:audit-scheduled` reads it, checks each entry's last-run timestamp against a small state file, and dispatches only the dimensions actually due — reusing `/shipyard:audit`'s own dispatch table, run-id marker, and reconciliation, plus the `audit-key` fingerprint dedup already built into `filing-github-issues`, so a scheduled run that finds nothing new produces zero new issues. Composes with the existing `/loop` skill for the actual periodic trigger, the same pattern `/shipyard:eas-watch` already established (closes #975).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/audit-schedule.sh` — new cadence-gating helper: `state-path`/`state-init`/`state-read`/`record` manage `~/.shipyard/audit-schedule-state.json` (last-run timestamp per `(owner/repo, dimension)`, atomic writes via `lib/common.sh`); `cadence-seconds` parses a `<int>[mhdw]` cadence string; `due` compares a schedule array against recorded state and emits one JSON line per dimension whose cadence has elapsed (or that has never run), honoring `"enabled": false` and treating an absent/null schedule as "nothing to do."
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/audit-scheduled.md` — new `/shipyard:audit-scheduled` command: reads the effective `audits.schedule` config, computes what's due via the helper above, dispatches each due dimension through `/shipyard:audit`'s own Dispatching table (no reimplementation), records completion only after each dimension's dispatch actually returns, and supports `--dry-run` to preview without running anything.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — new `audits.schedule` array (`dimension` enum matching `/audit`'s own type argument, `cadence` pattern-validated as `<int>[mhdw]`, optional `url` and `enabled`).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/audit.md` — cross-references `/shipyard:audit-scheduled` for the recurring-cadence case.
- `README.md` — new `/shipyard:audit-scheduled` command entry and a "Scheduled audits" section with a worked example (weekly `testing`/`functional-qa`, daily `security`, composed with `/loop 1h /shipyard:audit-scheduled`).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/audit-schedule.test.sh` — new regression suite: state-file round-trips, cadence-string parsing (including the invalid-cadence exit-64 path), and `due` semantics (never-run vs freshly-recorded vs elapsed-cadence, the `enabled: false` exclusion — a targeted regression test for jq's `//` operator treating `false` as falsy alongside `null`, which would otherwise silently re-include a disabled entry — absent/null schedule config, stdin vs file-path input parity, and the missing-file exit-5 path).

### 4.4.27 — 2026-07-31

`shipyard:verify-worker` adversarially judges whether a PR resolves its issue, but had no notion of platform parity — whether a change touching one platform variant of a module (Metro's `.web.`/`.native.`/`.ios.`/`.android.` resolution) also touches, or deliberately excludes, its siblings. A PR editing only one half of a sibling pair produces code that's correct on the platform the author tested and silently divergent on the other, invisible to CI because each variant typechecks and lints independently and a single-`testEnvironment` runner frequently never loads the untouched side at all. Added a new advisory check (2f) to `verify.md`'s adversarial-checks pass: it detects sibling sets from the PR's changed files by stripping a matched `.web`/`.native`/`.ios`/`.android` infix, confirms a sibling is a **real file in the repo tree** (never a guessed path) before treating it as a pair, and flags an asymmetric touch or a newly-added platform-split file with no discoverable test. Because a false positive that blocks a legitimate PR is worse than a miss on a check this heuristic, 2f never independently produces `not-verified` — it only folds a note onto whichever verdict 2a–2e already reached, and gives PR authors a documented way to declare a deliberate asymmetry (`Platform-parity: intentional — <reason>` in the PR body) so a correct single-sided change isn't permanently re-flagged. Fires only when `verify_gate: on` (opt-in, off by default) — no change to the common no-verify-gate path (closes #977).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/verify.md` — new §2c-style check **2f** (platform-variant parity, advisory-only); step 1's evidence-gathering `gh pr view` projection extended with `headRefOid` (needed for 2f's repo-tree query) and per-file `status` (needed for the new-file-no-test sub-check).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.26` → `4.4.27`.

### 4.4.26 — 2026-07-31

Every shipyard `*-auditor` is assertive — it checks a codebase against a rubric and files defects. None is generative — none produces a description of what the app actually does — so a maintainer running an AI-authored codebase has no readable whole to check their own understanding against, only a long series of individually-reviewed diffs. Added `comprehension-auditor`, a new `/audit comprehension` dimension that inverts the usual shape: its primary output is a document (feature inventory traced from routes/entrypoints, state machines for core domain objects, data flows with authorization-gate coverage, and confirmed cross-cutting invariants), not a list of issues. Filing "here's what the checkout flow does" as a bug report would be noise, so only the document's "Surprises" section — behavior that looks unintentional, inconsistent between platforms, or contradicted elsewhere in the codebase — files `audit:comprehension`-labeled issues; the descriptive sections never do. Because every auditor is hard-barred from `git add`/commit/push (`shipyard:filing-github-issues` § "Don't"), the document can't be committed by the auditor itself: instead it proposes the content via a single "living-doc" tracking issue (updated in place on repeat runs rather than re-filed, keyed by a stable `comprehension/living-doc` audit-key) for a later `/shipyard:do-work` dispatch to land at `docs/COMPREHENSION.md` inside its own isolated worktree — reusing the existing PR machinery rather than inventing a parallel one. Each run diffs the freshly-assembled document against the last committed version at `HEAD:docs/COMPREHENSION.md` and leads with a "Since the last comprehension audit" section, since the diff — not the document in isolation — is what actually shows semantic drift between runs. Registered in `/shipyard:audit`'s dispatch table and Args list; `/shipyard:audit-scheduled`'s cadence surface (issue #975) is still mid-flight on `origin/main` as of this PR, so no integration there yet. No new config keys or model pins were needed — `model: sonnet` already has a pricing row (closes #976).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/comprehension-auditor.md` — new agent: the artifact-vs-issues split, the entrypoint/state-machine/data-flow/invariant/surprises process passes, the living-doc tracking-issue handoff, and the size-discipline ceiling for the assembled document.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/audit.md` — new `comprehension` dispatch-table row and Args-list entry; a callout at the shared `audit:<dimension>` labeling paragraph noting the exception.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/filing-github-issues/SKILL.md` — new `audit:comprehension` row in the agent-identifying-label table; a narrow-exception note at the "REQUIRED" labeling rule for the living-doc tracking issue.
- `README.md` — `comprehension` called out explicitly as generative-not-assertive in the "Finds work" summary and given its own bullet in the slash-command list.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.25` → `4.4.26`.

### 4.4.24 — 2026-07-31

While triaging a CI failure, a session fetched a run's full log with `gh run view --log`, grepped it for every failure marker it could think of, got zero hits, and concluded the job died silently — filing a GitHub issue on that premise. The conclusion was wrong: `gh run view --log` had silently truncated its output (no warning, no non-zero exit, nothing distinguishing "complete" from "cut off"), and the real, fully-legible error (`fatal error: ... No space left on device`) lived past the cut. A subsequently-dispatched worker found it immediately by fetching the specific failing job's log instead of the whole run's. This is the general hazard of drawing a negative claim ("no error here," "X is not present") from any capped read whose truncation isn't self-reported — the same class of failure the orchestrator independently hit the same day reading a spec file against a token cap, where a printed truncation banner made the fix obvious (page before answering) precisely because the tool *did* announce the cut. `gh run view --log` doesn't. Closed with a new `ci-pitfalls.md` fragment section generalizing the rule past CI logs — prefer the narrowest fetch (drill to the job, not the run), check for a completion signal before concluding absence, and page to completion or state the limit explicitly when you can't — plus targeted cross-references at the two remaining diagnostic paths (`fix-main-ci.md`, `fix-failing-prs-batch.md`) that still read a whole-run `--log-failed` capture raw. Landed as an on-demand fragment addition rather than touching the always-loaded `issue-work.md`/`SKILL.md`, which have little-to-no remaining budget under `spec-size-budget.test.sh`'s ceilings (closes #988).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/ci-pitfalls.md` — new "A truncated read cannot support a negative claim" section (after the existing "An absence-assertion that observed nothing is not a pass" section); fragment header's cross-reference list extended.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-main-ci.md` — cross-reference at the `gh run view --log-failed` call: prefer drilling to the failing job's log, sanity-check the tail before concluding "no error."
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-failing-prs-batch.md` — cross-reference at the sampled `--log-failed | head -200` call: the excerpt is a sample, not proof of absence for any one PR.

### 4.4.23 — 2026-07-31

A `lightwork` `issue-work` dispatch ran into a session-scoped constraint no documented shape covered: its dispatch prompt's Context block declared `.github/workflows/ci.yml` off-limits (a sibling worker owned it this session), but the issue's own acceptance criteria spanned both the jest-config split it could ship and a `ci.yml` wiring change it couldn't touch this run. `issue-work.md`'s only existing "ship partial, leave issue open" shape was §6.5's operator-residual split — built for a residual that needs a *human*, so it always applies `agent-console`/`needs-human-review`. The deferred `ci.yml` work needed neither; gating it behind either label would have hidden ordinary, dispatch-ready follow-up work from future `/do-work` runs. The worker improvised a return string by analogy and filed this issue rather than guess wrong twice. Closed by adding a third disposition shape, §6.7 (`issue-work-deferred-slice-dispatch.md`), that a worker recognizes for itself mid-implementation — no scope-preflight carve-out predicts it the way §6.5/§6.6's Context paragraphs do — and hands the deferred remainder to a **fresh, ungated** follow-up issue instead of a gate label, since the constraint is transient to this session, not intrinsic to the issue. Weighed PR #1028's precedent (reuse the generic `blocked` machinery rather than add a schema value) against this case and reached the same conclusion for the same reason: this still rides the existing `outcome:"shipped"` schema shape (the `partial ... deferred to #<F>` qualifier is a documentation-only variant of the return-string suffix, exactly like §6.5's own `partial` marker already is), so no schema/JS wiring touched. `issue-work.md`'s per-file size ceiling (set by #980/#1011 earlier today) had almost no headroom left, so every touchpoint was trimmed to the terseness of the existing #851/#852 stubs before the ceiling was raised 112000 → 116000 bytes for the necessary remainder (closes #986).

### 4.4.22 — 2026-07-31

A long-running `issue-work` dispatch reads the issue's state exactly once, at step 0, then implements, tests, opens a PR, and — when the dispatch prompt says to override a `needs-human-review` gate and "make the design call autonomously" — posts a decision comment reflecting that call. Nothing re-reads the issue in between, so the worker has no way to notice a concurrent session (a `/shipyard:my-turn` walkthrough, a maintainer's own comment) resolved the exact same gate, in the opposite direction, while the worker was mid-implementation. A live `mattsears18/lightwork#3254` dispatch hit this: `/shipyard:my-turn` recorded a maintainer decision of `wontfix` ("no `patch-package` infra") and closed the issue at 11:21, and the worker's own decision comment — adopting exactly the rejected option, having already implemented and pushed it — landed ten minutes later; only a manual state check caught the auto-merge risk before it armed. Closed with a re-read, not a lock, per the design steer: `issue-work.md` gains a new §5.3 terminal-state re-read positioned immediately before §5.5's decision-comment write (a trip skips §5.5 through §6 entirely, so the same checkpoint structurally guards auto-merge too) — it re-reads `state`/`labels`/`comments` and trips on the issue closing, a new disposition-signal label (`wontfix`/`needs-human-review`/`duplicate`/`invalid`/`blocked:*`), or a new `<!-- shipyard-resolve-decisions -->`/`<!-- do-work-decision-resolved -->` sentinel comment; on trip it posts nothing resembling a decision, converts the PR to draft, labels it `needs-human-review`, and returns a distinct `blocked #<N> at terminal-state-recheck: …` string rather than risk contradicting a recorded human decision. Step 2's comment-thread classification also now recognizes both decision-resolved sentinels as outranking even the dispatch prompt's own override instruction, independent of the race — the sentinel is the human answer the gate was waiting for, whenever it lands. Reused PR #1017's precedent of documenting a new bail reason explicitly in `steady-state.md`'s reason→class table for auditability; no new schema/JS wiring was needed since the reason routes through the existing generic `blocked`/`needs-human-review` machinery (closes #997).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — new documented row in the bail reason→class table for `issue dispositioned mid-dispatch by a concurrent session`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — new assertion block locking in the §5.3 heading, its position ahead of §5.5, all three trip conditions, the recovery actions, the return string, the step 2 sentinel-precedence rule, both `Don't` bullets, and the steady-state.md table row.

### 4.4.21 — 2026-07-31

`operate.md` framed browser-completable operator work around a single backend — "preferring the `claude-in-chrome` extension" — with no line telling a worker it may reach for anything else, and stated the singleton/serialization constraint as though it applied to all browser work rather than specifically to the shared, authenticated real Chrome. A real `/do-work` session against `mattsears18/lightwork` reflected this literally: five `agent-console`-labeled issues, every browser attempt routed through `claude-in-chrome` only, no alternative considered because the spec never established one existed. Reframed the backend section as an explicit, preference-ordered ladder: gstack's headless `/browse` skill first for inspection/verification work (navigating, reading, confirming a value) — fast, no singleton contention, doesn't disturb the user's live Chrome — extended mid-filing with the correction that `/setup-browser-cookies` imports the user's real session cookies into the headless profile, so it covers *authenticated* surfaces too, not just logged-out ones; `claude-in-chrome` next for work that genuinely needs the live session directly (the mutating playbooks); `chrome-devtools-mcp --autoConnect` as the existing fallback when the extension is absent. Rescoped the serialization sentence in `operate.md`'s "How it fits the loop" to the authenticated shared browser specifically. Added an explicit three-way distinction — backend unavailable/unauthenticated-but-importable → fall back and continue; no live session anywhere for the site → hand back saying "sign in once, then automatable," not "impossible"; action denied by the harness permission classifier → hand back, never route around via a different backend — so "try the next tier" is never conflated with retrying a denial (`dont.md`'s existing classifier-denial prohibition is unchanged). Composes with, rather than duplicates, #998's exhaustion checklist: the checklist's new first item points at this ladder before the identity/zero-result/domain-form/public-mirror checks that already existed. `issue-work.md`'s external-provisioning bail now points a worker at the same ladder so a `blocked: external provisioning required` bail doesn't read as a permanent dead end (closes #996).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/03-error-handling-and-safety.md` — exhaustion checklist gained a new leading item ("try the next tier of the browser backend ladder") and renumbered the rest; "Companion, not a substitute" note now cross-references the ladder by name.

### 4.4.20 — 2026-07-31

`needs-operator` read as "needs a human operator" — the exact opposite of what it means: the label marks work an **agent** can drive outside the build (a browser, a cloud console), not work that requires a human. A real `/do-work` session against `mattsears18/lightwork` misread it this way, applying it to four genuinely agent-barred items (secret writes, an unauthenticated login) that should have carried `needs-human-review` instead. Worse, because `/do-work` drives `needs-operator` while `/my-turn` deliberately filters it OUT of the human-only queue, a genuinely-blocked issue wearing the old name was reachable by neither loop — stranded with no path to anyone. Renamed to **`agent-console`** (the issue's primary proposal, over the alternatives `agent-ops`/`agent-external`) — the `agent-` prefix makes ownership unmistakable and can't be misread as a person, while `console` still conveys where the work happens. `needs-human-review` is kept verbatim per this repo's existing convention; the operator-vs-decision split the two names preserve is unchanged, only the operator-side name moved. Paired with an explicit decision rule everywhere the label is defined — *can an agent perform this action? Yes, but outside the build → `agent-console`; no (barred, or genuine human judgment) → `needs-human-review`* — since the rename alone doesn't fix the catch-all misuse the issue also reported. Zero issues in this repo carried `needs-operator` at rename time, so there was no live-data migration to perform here; every label-creation site now creates only `agent-console`, while the load-bearing MATCHING sites (the dispatch-exclusion set, the operator proactive sweep, `/my-turn`'s human-only-queue filter) recognize both names during a migration window, per the sequence the issue specifies: ship dual-recognition first, then let a consuming repo rename its live GitHub label object, then drop legacy recognition in a later release. Renaming this repo's own live label object is left as a manual follow-up (it mutates the repo outside this PR's reviewable diff) — closes #995.

- `CLAUDE.md` — the `agent-console` gate-label entry (renamed from `needs-operator`) now carries the decision rule and a dedicated "Renamed: `needs-operator` → `agent-console`" migration subsection.
- 34 spec/doc files across `plugins/shipyard/commands/`, `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/`, `README.md`, and `CONTRIBUTING.md` — mechanical rename of every reference (203 occurrences), with the dispatch-exclusion set (`setup/04-backlog-divert.md`), the operator proactive sweep (`operate/04-steady-state-hooks.md`), and `/my-turn`'s human-only-queue filter (`my-turn.md`) additionally annotated to recognize the legacy `needs-operator` name during the migration window. `docs/images/shipyard-infographic.svg`'s label-callout text updated to match.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01c-label-recovery-refine.md`, `setup/06c-scope-handling-ui.md`, `steady-state.md` — the three `gh label create` sites now create only `agent-console`, with an updated description pointing at CLAUDE.md's decision rule; none re-create the legacy label.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/agent-console-rename-995.test.sh` — new regression suite (25 assertions) pinning the label-creation-vs-matching split: creation sites emit only `agent-console`, matching sites recognize both names, and CLAUDE.md carries the decision rule and migration note.

### 4.4.19 — 2026-07-31

Scope pre-flight dispatched a worker against a phase-1 slice whose gaps (a) and (b) had already shipped in a merged sibling PR two days earlier — the PR fixed part of the issue without a closing keyword, so nothing on the issue itself signaled the drift, and the existing comment-thread-awareness rule only catches staleness the thread *documents*, not staleness it's silent about. The worker recovered on its own initiative (its pre-implementation verification step re-derives the problem from the codebase before shipping), but only after burning a full dispatch on a prompt that asserted a false premise. Added a staleness probe to the scope-agent's own remit: before returning ready or deferred, check the issue's concrete, individually-checkable claims (a named file, a specific literal, a config key) against `origin/<default-branch>` via cheap bounded queries — `git log --grep`/`gh pr list --search` for a merged PR already touching the issue, targeted `git grep`/`gh api` reads for specific claims — never a broad re-read of the repo. A partial landing narrows `phase_1_scope` to the surviving claims (the existing field, unchanged shape); a full landing returns a new **already-landed** shape, closed by the orchestrator without a worker dispatch. The full-landing case requires a corroborating citation (a merged PR/commit reference, or a claim's confirmed absence at the location the body names) before firing — a false "already landed" silently drops real remaining work from the backlog, which is worse than the wasted-dispatch cost this probe exists to avoid (closes #992).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — new "Staleness probe" section (the three-outcome framing: unaffected / narrow `phase_1_scope` / already-landed) and the new **already-landed shape** documented alongside the existing ready/deferred shapes.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06b-scope-carveouts.md` — scoping-agent prompt instruction extended with the staleness-probe directive (bounded-query guidance, the already-landed shape, the partial-vs-full misclassification warning).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06c-scope-handling-ui.md` — new "Already-landed entries" handling bullet (validate → comment → `gh issue close --reason completed`, no `deferred_issues` append, no gate label); `raw_backlog` removal sweep and file-summary line updated to include the third shape.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — 5.a and 5.b pre-drain re-validation branches gained an already-landed handling case so a fresh scope-agent re-check during drain can also close a since-landed deferred entry rather than hitting an unhandled shape.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.18` → `4.4.19`.

### 4.4.18 — 2026-07-31

`operator_queue`'s `paste-secret` kind was enqueued and only handed back at drain time — a real session against `mattsears18/lightwork` classified "store a Vercel deploy-hook URL in Google Secret Manager" as an attemptable `console-action` under the new CLI-first preference (#972) and tried `curl ... | gcloud secrets create ... --data-file=-`, piping the value straight from an authenticated API read into Secret Manager so it never touched agent context. The Claude Code auto-mode permission classifier correctly denied it — the harness prohibition on entering a secret/credential value binds the *effect*, not the mechanism, so piping around agent-context exposure doesn't change the classification, and the browser path would have been denied identically. The spec gap wasn't the denial; it was that this class of action was ever enqueued as attemptable in the first place, and that "storing an already-known value into a new secret entry" wasn't clearly routed to the same categorically-undrainable bucket as *minting* a fresh credential. Fixed by classifying secret/credential-value writes at candidate time, in both `operator_queue` feeders, before either one would ever enqueue the item — recognizing the shape is enough to skip straight to the hand-back, with no attempt step to gate on a classifier denial and no retry/reframe machinery built around one. The productive half stays explicitly in scope: read-only verification (confirm the value is still absent) and composing the exact remediation command for the human is the complete, expected outcome for this class, not a consolation prize after a failed attempt — and it must never be routed through the `#998` exhaustion checklist, since a secret-value write is undrainable by policy, not by reachability (closes #991).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/04-steady-state-hooks.md` — both the A.1 reactive-enqueue hook and the D proactive-sweep hook now exclude secret/credential-value writes from `operator_queue` entirely, routing them to an inline classify-and-hand-back instead.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/01-queue-and-authorization.md` — new "Secret/credential-value writes are never enqueued" subsection; both feeder descriptions cross-reference it and drop the stale "needs the `EXPO_ASC_*` secret pasted" / "CI secrets flagged by a red run" enqueue examples.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/02-execution-and-playbooks.md` — `paste-secret` playbook reframed as classified-and-handled-inline, never popped from `operator_queue`; new note on the effect+direction classification table that a secret-value write isn't answered by that table at all; CLI-first section's credential-handling paragraph now explicitly names the pipe-straight-into-Secret-Manager shape as forbidden regardless of context exposure.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/03-error-handling-and-safety.md` — broadened the "never paste a secret from issue/PR text" safety bullet to cover any secret/credential-value write through any mechanism.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` / `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/orchestrator-state-reference.md` — dropped `paste-secret` from the enqueueable `operator_queue` kind enum; `operator_handbacks`/`operator_denials` descriptions note the tee-up is now appended at classification time, not from a popped queue item.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.17` → `4.4.18`.

#979 converted every `gh --body "$(cat <<EOF...EOF)"` heredoc-in-command-substitution shape (refused by the worktree-isolation `Bash` guard in a worktree-isolated session) to a `Write`-generated scratch file + `--body-file` across the `/shipyard:do-work` worker specs, but deliberately scoped that fix to the seven mode-shim worker files. #1004 was filed to check the handful of non-worker command files the fix left untouched. The three genuine `gh ... --body "$(cat <<EOF...)"` call sites — `commands/file-issue.md`, `commands/resolve-decisions.md`, `commands/eas-watch.md` — are standalone, human-invoked slash commands rather than worker dispatches, so they're not *guaranteed* worktree-isolated the way a mode-shim worker is; but `resolve-decisions.md`'s flow is explicitly reused inline by `/my-turn`, which pairs with `/shipyard:do-work` and can plausibly run from the same session after `/do-work` has relocated into its `orchestrator-<session-id>` worktree, and the fix has near-zero cost when the guard doesn't apply. Converted all three to the established `--body-file` + `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.shipyard-scratch/` scratch-file convention (closes #1004).
