### 4.4.16 — 2026-07-31

`agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` §5.8 has a complete add-and-verify path for a closing link (patch the PR body to prepend `Closes #<N>`, re-verify) but no remove path — and the naive fix doesn't reliably work: once GitHub registers `#<N>` as a closing reference, editing the body to strip the keyword is not guaranteed to retract it. A live dispatch hit this: a post-open validation gate disproved the issue's premise after the PR had already opened with a correct `Closes #<N>`, and the worker's body rewrite (to a "does NOT close it" phrasing) left `closingIssuesReferences` still populated several minutes later. Verified the mechanism empirically rather than assuming it, via three scratch PRs against a scratch issue on this repo (closed and deleted after the test): confirmed GitHub's GraphQL schema has no `unlinkPullRequestFromIssue`-shaped mutation — `closingIssuesReferences` is a derived, read-only field with no direct edge to unmutate; found a clean bare-URL body rewrite (no closing-keyword-shaped word sharing the reference's line) reliably cleared a live link within seconds in every trial, including the exact negated phrasing from the original repro — consistent with that repro tripping the already-documented #990 keyword-adjacency hazard rather than proof of a strictly one-way mechanism; and found a closing keyword confined to a commit message (never in the PR body) did not register in the pre-merge `closingIssuesReferences` read, though tier 2's commit-message rewrite stays in the escalation ladder as defense-in-depth for non-squash merge strategies the pre-merge check can't validate. Rather than invent a new removal mechanism, §5.85's existing three-tier remediation ladder (body → commit message → abandon-and-reopen-from-neutral-branch — built for the inverse "leak prevention" case) is generalized to a fourth trigger shape covering "retraction," since the script doesn't care where the protected issue number came from (closes #1001).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — §5.85's stub widens from three to four trigger shapes (shape (4): a disposition change on the SAME PR that opened as the dispatched issue's own resolving PR); §5.8 gains a pointer paragraph to §5.85 shape (4) instead of leaving a worker to improvise an untested body edit; new `Don't` bullet warning against trusting a single retraction edit and naming the confirmed-absent `unlinkPullRequestFromIssue` mutation.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work-parent-epic-leak.md` — "When this step applies" widens to four shapes; new "Shape (4) — retracting an already-live closing link" section documenting the empirical findings (no direct unlink mutation, the body-rewrite reliability window, the commit-message vector and why tier 2 still matters despite not showing up pre-merge).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/parent-epic-leak-guard.test.sh` — 9 new assertions covering the widened trigger-shape list, the §5.8→§5.85 pointer, the Don't-list warning, and the fragment's shape-(4) section, unlink-mutation confirmation, and both empirical findings (51 → 60 assertions).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.15` → `4.4.16`.

### 4.4.15 — 2026-07-31

A `fix-checks-only` worker dispatched against a DIRTY PR (its `mergeStateStatus` conflicts with the default branch — a sibling PR merged first and advanced `main` past this PR's claimed version row) has no merge ref for GitHub to run `pull_request`-triggered checks against, so no check ever queues at all. `gh pr checks <M>` reporting `no checks reported on the '<branch>' branch` is indistinguishable, from inside the worker, from "CI hasn't started yet" — so the worker polled to exhaustion and returned a misleading `blocked … CI workflows not triggering after push … workflow initialization delay/infrastructure issue`, burning a full dispatch (~135k tokens) diagnosing an infrastructure problem that didn't exist, on a PR whose real problem (a stale base) belongs to `fix-rebase`, not this mode. Fixed by adding a DIRTY-PR short-circuit: immediately after Setup, before any check-watching, the worker reads `mergeStateStatus` and — if DIRTY — returns a new first-class `dirty #<M>: PR conflicts with <default-branch>; no merge ref, so no checks will run` disposition instead of entering the fix-loop. `dirty` does not count toward the 3-attempt cap and does not earn `blocked:ci` (it isn't a CI failure); the orchestrator routes it to the drain's `fix-rebase` path instead, which re-derives DIRTY status live from `session_prs` and rebases the PR (carrying forward any fix the worker had already pushed) rather than replacing it. The same short-circuit also closes a related trap: a DIRTY PR's empty `statusCheckRollup` would otherwise vacuously satisfy the `green` evidence clause's "0 pending, 0 failing" condition, so the orchestrator's own trust-but-verify spot-check and narrative-return synthesis (steady-state.md's fix-checks reconcile) now check `mergeStateStatus` before their respective empty-rollup rules too, as defense in depth against a non-compliant worker claiming `green` anyway (closes #1015).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — new "DIRTY-PR short-circuit" section (checked immediately after Setup, before Hard rules and before the fix-loop's first `gh pr checks --watch`); new `dirty #<M>: ...` terminal disposition in the return contract (widened from five to six documented terminal strings); fix-loop step 6 re-checks `mergeStateStatus` if a post-push re-watch reports an empty rollup instead of settling; new `Don't` bullet naming the exact "CI workflows not triggering / infrastructure issue" misdiagnosis from the #1015 repro.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — step A's fix-checks reconcile gains a first-class `dirty #<M>: ...` branch (append to `session_prs` so the drain's `D_dirty` scan picks it up; no `blocked:ci`, no fix-attempt charge); both the trust-but-verify `green` spot-check and the unrecognized-return-string narrative synthesis now check `mergeStateStatus == "DIRTY"` before their empty-rollup `green` rule; the unrecognized-return-string prefix list now includes `dirty`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the `mode: fix-checks-only` prose dispatch template's return-values line and the Workflow-substrate structured-return translation table both gain the `dirty` disposition.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md`, `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — the `green`-without-verifying-the-rollup rationale entry describes the DIRTY-PR vacuous-empty-rollup trap and how the structural short-circuit closes it; both recognized-prefix lists now include `dirty`.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/worker-return.schema.json`, `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.core.js`, `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` (regenerated via `generate-dispatch-workflow.mjs`), `plugins/shipyard/workflows/prompt-templates/fix-checks-only.mjs` — `dirty` added to the structured-return `outcome` enum (canonical schema + its hand-copied `workerReturnSchema` literal, verified in parity via `check-worker-return-schema-parity.mjs`) and to the Workflow-substrate dispatch prompt's example return shapes.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-checks-infra-flake-classification.test.sh` — new assertions covering the DIRTY-PR short-circuit section, the `dirty` return string, the steady-state reconcile branch, the mergeStateStatus-before-empty-rollup ordering, and schema/workflow parity for the new outcome.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.14` → `4.4.15`.

### 4.4.14 — 2026-07-31

§5.85's non-close parent/epic leak verification (the guard that stops a "reference but don't close" PR from silently auto-closing its protected issue) checked `closingIssuesReferences` with a single `gh pr view --json` read right after `gh pr create`/`gh pr edit`. GitHub computes that field asynchronously — it parses the PR body and commits after the PR object itself exists — so a lone immediate read can come back falsely empty even though the closing link registers moments later. That's a false negative in the dangerous direction: a worker trusting one clean-looking read arms auto-merge on a leak that's still there, and the protected issue silently closes on merge with no second chance to catch it. A live dispatch hit exactly this and only caught it because it happened to re-check; the guidance to distrust `gh pr view`'s cache and use a direct GraphQL query as the authoritative check wasn't in the spec (distinct from #990's negation-blind-parser fix, which addressed a different leak vector in the same guard). Closed by adding a `check_closing_ref` helper to the fragment that queries `closingIssuesReferences` directly via `gh api graphql` (bypassing `gh pr view`'s own object-resolution layer) and requires two consecutive reads, a few seconds apart, to agree before trusting an empty result — every `LEAKED=` check in the verification-and-remediation script now routes through it instead of a bare `gh pr view --json closingIssuesReferences` call (closes #982).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work-parent-epic-leak.md` — new "Verification staleness" paragraph naming the false-negative hazard; new `check_closing_ref` bash function (direct GraphQL query, two-read agreement, retry-on-disagreement); all four `LEAKED=` assignments in the verification/remediation script now call the helper instead of `gh pr view --json closingIssuesReferences` directly; new paragraph explaining why the fix is scoped to §5.85 and not §5.8's own (lower-severity, self-correcting) dispatched-issue check.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — §5.85 stub gets a pointer sentence to the new staleness-resistant verification requirement.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/parent-epic-leak-guard.test.sh` — new assertions: the fragment links #982, defines `check_closing_ref`, queries via `gh api graphql`, names the false-negative failure mode, and every `LEAKED=` assignment routes through the helper (no bare `gh pr view --json closingIssuesReferences` reads survive).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.13` → `4.4.14`.

### 4.4.13 — 2026-07-31

`agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` §5's bare-URL guidance for referencing a "do NOT close" parent/epic issue is necessary but not sufficient: GitHub's closing-reference parser matches a closing-keyword-shaped word (`close`, `fix`, `resolve`, and inflections) sitting anywhere in the same sentence or line as the reference, with **no negation awareness** — a line like *"This PR does NOT close `<URL>`"* still registers a closing link and silently closes the protected issue on merge. A live `mode: issue-work` dispatch hit this: the worker's §5.85 escalation ran the full three-tier remediation ladder and landed on the tier-3 branch-name attribution as the cause, when the actual (and far cheaper to fix) cause was a keyword-adjacent sentence the tier-1 "clean body" check never looked for. Closed by naming the negation-blind-parser hazard explicitly in §5's prevention note, adding a keyword-adjacency check to §5.85's tier 1 remediation (a body already using the URL form is not "clean" just because it has no bare `#<E>` token), and softening tier 3's branch-name attribution so a worker re-checks for the keyword-adjacency hazard before paying tier 3's abandon-and-reopen cost (closes #990).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — §5's "inverse hazard" prevention note gets a new paragraph naming the negation-blind-parser hazard and prescribing a neutral-synonym workaround instead of negation.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work-parent-epic-leak.md` — new "Prevention" paragraph for the keyword-adjacency hazard; tier 1's remediation now greps the patched body for a closing-keyword-shaped word sharing a line with the reference and instructs a manual rewrite before re-verifying; tier 3's rationale (both the bash comment and the prose) now requires re-checking the keyword-adjacency hazard before attributing a surviving leak to the branch name.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/parent-epic-leak-guard.test.sh` — new assertions covering the #990 additions in both files.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.11` → `4.4.13` (`4.4.12` is claimed by PR #1013, still open at the time of this release; this PR was based on `origin/main` before #1013 merged).

### 4.4.11 — 2026-07-31

Every `mode: issue-work` dispatch unconditionally loaded 187 KB (~53k tokens) of spec before reading a single line of the repo it was working on — `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` alone had grown 2.3x since 1.9.4 with nothing in the release process budgeting for the drift, and a 14-dispatch session measured tokens-per-dispatch staying nearly flat (1.8x spread) regardless of how much work each dispatch actually did (a 2-line deletion cost 197k tokens). Re-measured against current `origin/main` rather than trusting the issue's numbers (they'd already drifted +3.2 KB / +2.1 KB since filing): the mandatory floor was 192,542 bytes across `issue-work.md` + `worker-preamble/SKILL.md` + the mode router. Applied shipyard's own thin-router pattern (already used for `commands/do-work.md` and the `worker-preamble` fragment split) to the hottest file: four sections that only apply to rare or opt-in code paths — the parent/epic leak-verification guard, the opt-in adversarial-verify gate, and the two scope-preflight split-dispatch carve-outs — moved into on-demand fragments loaded only when their trigger condition fires, leaving a compact trigger-check-and-pointer stub in their place. `issue-work.md` drops from 120,559 to 100,546 bytes (−16.6%); the mandatory floor drops to 172,529 bytes (−10.4%). Added a CI size-budget test so the next round of unbounded growth fails the build instead of silently compounding (closes #980).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — §5.85, §5.9, §6.5, and §6.6 replaced with compact stubs that preserve the exact trigger condition and point to the new fragment; every heading, anchor, and section-ordering relationship is unchanged so existing cross-references (from `06b-scope-carveouts.md`, `dispatch-rules.md`, `steady-state.md`, the worker-preamble fragments) keep resolving.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work-parent-epic-leak.md`, `issue-work-adversarial-verification.md`, `issue-work-split-dispatch.md`, `issue-work-verification-dispatch.md` — new on-demand fragments carrying §5.85's three-tier leak-remediation procedure, §5.9's verify-gate dispatch, §6.5's split-dispatch disposition, and §6.6's verification-only disposition respectively, verbatim from the sections they replaced (cross-references adjusted to resolve across files; verified with `scripts/check-anchor-links.mjs`, 0 broken links across 101 files).

### 4.4.10 — 2026-07-31

Four consecutive `/shipyard:do-work` operator attempts against the same lightwork issue concluded a Google Play Console was unreachable and handed the work back — the console was reachable the whole time (a fifth attempt reached it in about ten minutes). Root cause: the operator phase's `toggle-setting`/`console-action` and `verify` playbooks treated a single failed access attempt (a login wall, or the default account lacking access) as license to declare the console unreachable outright, with nothing requiring the agent to try an adjacent account index, distrust a zero-result helper, vary a domain-form, or check for a public mirror first — so a "couldn't reach it on this attempt" observation generalized into an "it is unreachable" claim, and each of the four wrong hand-back comments made the next session more likely to inherit the conclusion instead of retesting it. Closed by adding an exhaustion checklist the operator phase must walk before writing a hand-back that asserts a console/account is unreachable, and requiring the hand-back to name what was tried (closes #998).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/03-error-handling-and-safety.md` — new "Exhaustion checklist — before declaring a console unreachable" section: enumerate identities/account indices before trusting the default account's result, treat a zero-result helper as inconclusive rather than evidence of absence, try domain-form variants on host-matching helpers, and prefer a published/public mirror when the console form itself is gated; frames the hand-back as naming what was tried, and raises the bar before escalating an item off the agent queue (`needs-operator` → `needs-human-review`) on the strength of reachability alone.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/02-execution-and-playbooks.md` — the `toggle-setting`/`console-action` step-3 logged-out branch and the `verify` step-2 logged-out branch both now require walking the exhaustion checklist before treating a failed access attempt as terminal.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate.md` — router table + load-order note for `03-error-handling-and-safety.md` updated to mention the exhaustion checklist.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/05-dont.md` — new prohibition: don't declare a console/account/resource unreachable on one failed attempt.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.9` → `4.4.10`.

### 4.4.9 — 2026-07-31

The `setup/` split (#611) was sized against the 256KB single-file `Read` byte limit, but the limit that actually binds is the per-`Read` 25,000-token cap — three of the five setup sub-files still silently overflowed it. Reproduced live this session: reading `setup/00-config-worktree.md` returned a PARTIAL view (39,202 of the file's tokens, cap 25,000), so 62% of the file — including every step from 0.55's read-back convention onward — was invisible to a plain `Read`, with no loud failure signal. Re-measured all five setup sub-files against current `origin/main` rather than trusting the issue body's stale byte counts (several had been edited by other PRs earlier the same day): `00-config-worktree.md`, `01-repo-recovery.md`, and `06-scope-preflight.md` still overflowed; `04-backlog-divert.md` and `07-pool-fill.md` already fit. Closed by further splitting each of the three oversized files into 2–3 sub-cluster files at natural step boundaries (preferring a split point that keeps a heavily-cross-referenced heading's anchor in its original file, to minimize cross-reference churn), fixing every cross-file anchor reference the moves broke, and adding a token-budget-derived regression test scoped to `setup/` (closes #994).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — trimmed to steps 0.3 → 0.7 (intro); the rest of step 0.7 (canonical parallelization batch + background cleanup group) plus steps 0.8 → 0.9.1 move to new `00b-parallelization-cache.md`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — trimmed to steps 1 → 1.7; step 2 (backlog overview) moves to new `01b-backlog-overview.md`; steps 3 → 3.5 (label-ensure + prior-session recovery + refinement) move to new `01c-label-recovery-refine.md`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — trimmed to the start of step 6 (pre-scope detectors + freshness check); the design/arch/epic/spike carve-out, operator-slice carve-out, QA-verification carve-out, and per-class evidence-shape table move to new `06b-scope-carveouts.md`; per-returned-entry handling plus steps 6.5 → 6.8 move to new `06c-scope-handling-ui.md`.

### 4.4.8 — 2026-07-31

A merge train stalled permanently after the CI fix that was supposed to unblock it had already landed. Session against `mattsears18/lightwork`: `main` went red on a required `🧹 Lint & Typecheck` check; seven open session PRs each ran their required checks before the fix existed and recorded a stale `FAILURE`; a `fix-main-ci` dispatch shipped the remedy and `main` went green — but all seven PRs stayed `BLOCKED` indefinitely, because GitHub does not re-run an already-completed required check just because the base branch moved underneath it, and nothing in the drive-the-tail machinery ever called `gh pr update-branch`. The DIRTY-handling path didn't cover it (these PRs were never DIRTY), and the `ci.settled_minutes` settle-signal actively misreported the stranded PRs as "settled" rather than fixable. Closed by adding a post-main-CI-fix branch-refresh mechanism that fires on every genuine red/pending/unknown → green `main_ci.status` transition: for each OPEN, non-DIRTY `session_prs` entry carrying a required-check failure whose completion predates the fix's merge commit, run `gh pr update-branch` exactly once per PR per fix (never masking a genuinely-red PR behind a fresh run, and never looping) (closes #993).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/04-backlog-divert.md` — new "Post-main-CI-fix branch-refresh" subsection under 4.5a: detects the `main_ci.status` transition into `green`, computes the fix's merge-commit cutoff, and re-triggers checks on every stale-red `session_prs` entry via `gh pr update-branch`, gated by a `stale_check_refresh_done` per-PR-per-SHA dedup map. Fires at both call sites that run 4.5a (session setup, step D's periodic refresh) with no extra wiring needed.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — new "Post-main-CI-fix branch-refresh (drain phase)" per-poll action: runs the same main-CI health read every poll (read-only against `divert_queue`, never enqueues a new `fix-main-ci` diversion) so a fix that lands *during* drain still un-sticks the PRs it stranded rather than leaving them for the next session.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — the fix-main-ci `shipped` reconcile gains a cross-reference clarifying that the branch-refresh fires later (at the next `main_ci` transition check), not at this reconcile step.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/orchestrator-state-reference.md`, `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — new cold orchestrator-state structure `stale_check_refresh_done` registered (hot+cold count 22 → 23).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — new "Stale-red vs genuinely-red (#993)" entry recording the session repro.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.7` → `4.4.8`.

### 4.4.7 — 2026-07-31

An `issue-work` worker armed auto-merge with `--merge` instead of the repo's `--squash` convention — intermittently, not systematically: two workers in one lightwork session both mis-armed `mergeMethod: MERGE` while every sibling PR that session correctly armed `SQUASH`, and it reproduced live a third time during a `/shipyard:do-work` session against this repo (PR #1000 armed `MERGE` while PRs #1002/#1003/#1005/#1006 in the same session armed `SQUASH` correctly). Root cause: every `gh pr merge` code block across the worker specs and orchestrator call sites showed the literal flag `--merge` as a stand-in for "whatever the repo's configured method is" — a worker (or orchestrator-turn call site) that copies the block as written has no reason to substitute anything, so it faithfully arms `--merge` regardless of the surrounding prose, and the merge method is repo policy, not a worker's call to make. Closed two ways: (1) a new `auto_merge.method` config knob (default `squash`) resolved and substituted at every `gh pr merge` call site instead of the hardcoded literal; (2) a reconcile-time verification in the orchestrator's `shipped` handler that reads the PR's actual `autoMergeRequest.mergeMethod` and corrects a mismatch via the disable-then-rearm dance a bare re-arm against an already-armed PR is a silent no-op (closes #989).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh`, `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — new `auto_merge.method` config knob (enum `squash`/`merge`/`rebase`, default `squash`).
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` — the shared auto-merge fragment resolves `$AUTO_MERGE_METHOD` from config in a new step 0.9 and substitutes it into both the `--auto` arm and the manual green-checks merge, with a callout explaining why this is a named step rather than a parenthetical.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`, `fix-main-ci.md`, `fix-failing-prs-batch.md` — each mode's `gh pr merge` call sites resolve and use `$AUTO_MERGE_METHOD` instead of a hardcoded `--merge`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — the A.1 `shipped` handler gains a mandatory merge-method-drift check: reads the PR's actual armed method, compares to the configured value, and corrects via disable-then-rearm on a mismatch (with a PR comment if the correction doesn't take); the A.0.5 crash-recovery re-arm also resolves the method from config instead of hardcoding `--merge`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/inline-trivial.md`, `setup/00-config-worktree.md`, `drain.md` — the remaining orchestrator-direct `gh pr merge` call sites (inline-trivial's arm, the setup-3c orphan-recovery arm, the release-train arm, and the deferred-merge lander's direct merge) all resolve `auto_merge.method` instead of hardcoding `--merge`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — the workflow-scope-blocked remediation advisory now tells the operator to re-arm with their configured method rather than the literal `--merge`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-config.test.sh` — new assertions for the `auto_merge.method` default, `set --repo`/`get` round-trip, and enum-validation rejection.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/crash-recovery-A05.test.sh` — updated its merge-arm assertion to match the config-resolved-method form.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.6` → `4.4.7`.

### 4.4.6 — 2026-07-31

`fix-checks-only` workers repeatedly returned the terminal `green #<M>` while the PR's required checks were still `IN_PROGRESS` / `pending` — four times in one session across two PRs on both Haiku and Sonnet, three of them despite an explicit mid-session prompt-level prohibition, reasoning from local evidence ("my local tests pass, CI is running normally") to a remote conclusion that hadn't actually settled yet. A sharper variant of the same slip conflated "no failures in the rollup" with "all checks are green" — a true statement about the absence of a `FAILURE` conclusion laundered into a false claim about the presence of an all-`SUCCESS` rollup, while a required `Web E2E Tests` check sat `pending`. The orchestrator's independent `statusCheckRollup` spot-check caught every occurrence (nothing incorrect ever merged), but that's a backstop for a defect on the worker side, and the worker side had no honest exit for "I fixed it, CI hasn't finished" — only `green` (overclaims), `blocked` (overstates and burns a fix attempt), or a narrative (a contract violation). Closed by making the `green` claim mechanical rather than narrative and by adding the missing vocabulary: `fix-checks-only.md`'s return contract now requires a computed evidence clause (`green #<M> (rollup verified <ISO8601>: <n> passed, 0 pending, 0 failing)`) that can't be written without the pending count having actually been queried this turn, and a new first-class `pending #<M>: <n> check(s) still running (<names>)` disposition for the honest "nothing failing, rollup not yet settled" state — it doesn't count toward the 3-attempt cap and doesn't earn `blocked:ci`, mirroring how `flake` is already treated (closes #985, closes #987).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — return contract rewritten: `green` / `noop: already green` now require the computed passed/pending/failing evidence clause; new `pending #<M>: ...` terminal disposition documented with its two legitimate paths (pre-fix-attempt and post-push-and-watch) and its non-cap, non-`blocked:ci` bookkeeping; the fix-loop's watch steps cross-reference `ci-pitfalls.md`'s harness-auto-backgrounding re-block pattern so a long-running `--watch` call has an honest, sanctioned exit that isn't a guess; every "three documented strings" / "four strings" reference updated to the new five-string vocabulary; a new `Don't` bullet names the "no failures ≠ green" conflation directly.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the `mode: fix-checks-only` prose dispatch template's return-values line and the Workflow-substrate structured-return translation table both gain the `pending` disposition and the evidence-clause examples for `green`/`noop`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — step A's fix-checks reconcile gains a first-class `pending #<M>: ...` branch (same bookkeeping as a `green→pending` downgrade: append to `session_prs`, no `blocked:ci`, no fix-attempt charge); the unrecognized-return-string prefix list now includes `pending`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md`, `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — the `green`-without-verifying-the-rollup and narrative-status-string rationale entries updated to describe the worker-side evidence clause and the `pending` disposition as the complementary fix, and to include `pending` in the recognized-prefix lists.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/worker-return.schema.json`, `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.core.js`, `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` (regenerated), `plugins/shipyard/workflows/prompt-templates/fix-checks-only.mjs` — `pending` added to the structured-return `outcome` enum (canonical schema + its hand-copied `workerReturnSchema` literal, which must stay in parity) and to the Workflow-substrate dispatch prompt's example return shapes.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-checks-infra-flake-classification.test.sh` — updated its hardcoded "four terminal strings" / recognized-prefix assertions to match the widened five-string return contract.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.5` → `4.4.6`.

### 4.4.5 — 2026-07-31

`gh run view <run-id> --log-failed` returns **empty output whenever any sibling job in the same workflow run is still `in_progress`** — even when the specific job a `fix-checks-only` worker actually needs has already finished and failed. Two consecutive dispatches against `mattsears18/lightwork` PR #3245 read that emptiness as "logs not ready yet," polled for 15+ minutes waiting on a slow `Web E2E Tests` sibling, and both returned `blocked` — burning ~310k tokens combined — when the failing `Unit Tests` job's logs were retrievable via the per-job REST endpoint the entire time. Fixed by rewriting `fix-checks-only.md`'s log-pulling step (and the Infra-flake classification's Step A, which carried the same false "wait for the whole run to settle" premise) to resolve the failing job id and hit `/actions/jobs/<job-id>/logs` FIRST, naming the empty-`--log-failed`-while-siblings-run trap explicitly and demoting `gh run view --log-failed` to a fallback for once the entire run has finished (closes #984).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — Fix-loop step 2 now prescribes resolving the failing job id from `/actions/runs/<run-id>/jobs` and fetching its logs from `/actions/jobs/<job-id>/logs` as the primary path; Infra-flake classification's Step A reworded from "wait for the whole run to settle" to "wait only on the specific job you need, never a sibling"; two `Don't` bullets added/updated naming the empty-output trap and the #984 token-burn variant of the #654 premature-bail failure mode.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-checks-log-fetch-984.test.sh` — new regression test asserting the per-job endpoint is prescribed as primary, the empty-output trap is named, `--log-failed` is demoted to a fallback, and Step A no longer tells the worker to wait on the whole run.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-checks-infra-flake-classification.test.sh` — updated its Step A assertion to track the reworded heading (the underlying #654 invariant — a premature "logs unavailable" bail is still forbidden — is unchanged and still asserted).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.4` → `4.4.5`.

### 4.4.4 — 2026-07-31

`agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` §5 prescribed a `gh pr create --body "$(cat <<'EOF' ... EOF)"` heredoc-in-command-substitution shape that the worktree-isolation `Bash` guard refuses as "too complex to verify that it stays inside the worktree" — the spec's own documented command was unrunnable by the workers it's written for, reproduced twice against real dispatches (`mattsears18/lightwork` #3200 and #3333) and a third time live during this fix's own authoring. Every multi-line `--body`/`--comment` a worker mode writes (`gh pr create`, `gh issue create`, `gh pr comment`, `gh issue comment`) hit the identical refusal. Fixed by prescribing `--body-file` everywhere a heredoc body previously appeared: write the content with the `Write` tool to a scratch file, then pass the file path. The scratch location took two iterations to get right — an initial design used the per-worktree git directory (`$(git rev-parse --git-dir)`) on the theory that it's categorically outside the working tree, but that path is *also* rejected by the `Write` tool's own `enforce-edit-scope.sh` scope hook (confirmed by testing it directly), since that hook checks against the worktree ROOT directory specifically. The corrected design uses a worktree-root dotdir (`$WORKTREE_PATH/.shipyard-scratch/`, the same precedent `write-probe.md`'s `.shipyard-write-probe` already relies on) with an explicit `rm -rf` cleanup step after each use, since — unlike the git-dir — this location does show up in `git status --porcelain` until removed (closes #979).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/body-file-convention.md` — new on-demand fragment documenting the refusal, the `--body-file` + worktree-root-dotdir scratch-location convention, why `/tmp` and the per-worktree git directory are both rejected, and the one-line-body exception (`gh issue close` has no `--comment-file` flag at all).
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — PR-creation contract section gains a short pointer at the new fragment; fragment index table gains its row. Net line count kept under the #617 300-line budget by folding the pointer into an adjacent paragraph and merging the `gh` JSON discipline section's two intro paragraphs into one.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — every heredoc `--body`/`--comment` occurrence (§5's resolving PR, §5.9's verify-gate comment, §6's external-trust-gate comment, §6.5's split-dispatch disposition comment, §6.6's verification-status comment) converted to the `--body-file` + scratch-file pattern.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-main-ci.md`, `fix-failing-prs-batch.md`, `investigate.md`, `spike.md` — same conversion applied to each mode's PR-create / issue-create / issue-comment heredoc bodies (investigate.md's noise-close comment simplified to a plain one-line `--comment` instead, since `gh issue close` has no `--comment-file` flag and the message never needed to be multi-line).
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/reaped-escape-hatch.md` — the incremental-progress-posting example converted to the same pattern.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — new regression coverage: the fragment exists and documents the corrected worktree-root-dotdir location (not the rejected git-dir location), SKILL.md points at it, and every converted per-mode file both uses `--body-file` + the scratch location + cleanup and no longer contains the refused heredoc shape (checked via an end-of-line-anchored regex that distinguishes a real heredoc opener from this fix's own explanatory prose, which quotes the refused shape inline as documentation).

### 4.4.3 — 2026-07-31

A dispatch that omitted the `model` parameter (the orchestrator forgetting to attach a resolved `models.<mode>` override to an `Agent`-tool call) was completely undetectable after the fact: no warning fired anywhere, `in_flight[slot]` never recorded which model a dispatch was resolved to run on, and `bump-tokens` accepted the orchestrator's self-reported `--model` on faith. The repro (issue #978, retitled after an earlier version of the same issue misdiagnosed the root cause): a session self-reported `claude-sonnet-5` cost for eleven dispatches that had actually run on Opus, producing a confidently-wrong `$4.39` total for a session that burned 2.6M+ tokens. Closed the observability gap on three fronts: `in_flight[<slot>].model` now records the family alias (or `"default"`) the per-dispatch model-resolution rule resolved, written through unconditionally at dispatch time; `/shipyard:status` renders it as a new MODEL column (text and `--json`); and `session-state.sh bump-tokens` now warns on stderr when a dispatch's billed model family disagrees with what `models.<mode>` resolves to for that mode, with the same check surfaced cumulatively as a new end-of-session banner. None of these confirm which model a dispatch actually ran on (the harness exposes no such signal) — they make a policy mismatch or a silently-omitted override visible instead of invisible (closes #978).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/session-state.sh` — `cmd_bump_tokens` gains a mode/model policy-consistency check: when `--mode` and `--model` are both supplied, cross-references the billed model's family against `resolve-dispatch-model.sh <mode>`'s resolved value and warns on stderr (fail-open) on a mismatch.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/status.sh` — new `model` field in both the `--json` in_flight projection and the text-table renderer (new MODEL column), defaulting to `"default"` when the session-state slot carries no `model`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/status.md` — documents the new MODEL column and its `--json` shape, and the caveat that it records orchestrator intent, not confirmed ground truth.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — `in_flight` struct doc gains the `model?` field.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/session-state-file.md` — schema example and field docs updated with `in_flight.<slot>.model`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — the per-slot dispatch metadata write-through now includes `model` in the mandatory field list and the example `session-state.sh update` call, with the unconditional-write rationale spelled out.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/07-pool-fill.md` — the per-slot metadata prose mirrors the same `model` requirement.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new `Model/mode mismatch(es)` end-of-session banner (omitted when every dispatch's billed model family agreed with configured policy), documented alongside the existing `Unpriced model(s)` banner.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/session-state.test.sh` — new coverage for the mode/model mismatch warning (configured-override mismatch, matching-model silence, no-override silence via `spike`, no-`--mode` silence); existing bump-tokens fixtures that incidentally paired `issue-work`/`fix-checks-only` with an opus-tier model (purely for pricing-math tests, unrelated to mode/model policy) rebased onto `verify` — the mode whose actual configured tier is opus — to keep the suite hermetic against the new check; adds a default `SHIPYARD_REPO_ROOT` test-isolation fixture so the suite's bump-tokens calls don't accidentally read this repo's own committed `shipyard.config.json`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/status.test.sh` — new coverage for the MODEL column (explicit model, and the `"default"` fallback when the field is absent) in both text and `--json` output.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.2` → `4.4.3`.

### 4.4.2 — 2026-07-31

`git rebase` can report **"Auto-merging \<file\>"** — a clean 3-way merge, zero conflict markers — while still silently splicing content from the wrong hunk, when a file has high internal repetition (many structurally-identical blocks) combined with a large line-offset shift on the default-branch side. A `fix-rebase` dispatch hit exactly this: a CI workflow's deliberate, load-bearing per-job cache-action split (one job saves, three jobs restore-only) was silently collapsed to one variant across three jobs during a "clean" rebase, with the pinned regression test for that split corrupted in the same pass — no error signal, and none of the mode's existing post-rebase guards (conflict-marker grep, CHANGELOG monotonicity, empty-diff check) key off anything that would catch it, since the whole-PR diff stays non-empty and no marker survives. Added a new post-rebase guard: for every file the PR's own commit(s) touched, assert every line the PR added is still present verbatim somewhere in that file after the rebase — a generic, mechanical, merge-base-relative check that never inspects which file or what the content means, so it needs no hard-coded knowledge of any specific file to catch this corruption class (closes #983).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md` — new §5.8 post-rebase line-survival guard, placed after the empty-diff check and before the force-push: computes the merge-base between the pre-rebase PR head and the rebase target, enumerates every file the PR touched, and bails with `blocked rebase #<M>: auto-merge silently dropped/altered PR-added content in: ...` if any added line is missing verbatim from the rebased file.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-rebase-line-survival-guard.test.sh` — new regression test pinning the guard's bail string, its merge-base-relative extraction, its exact-line survival check, its content-agnostic (no per-file hard-coding) design, and its ordering after §5.7 / before §6.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.1` → `4.4.2`.

### 4.4.1 — 2026-07-31

The worker-preamble's sanctioned re-block pattern for a foreground call the harness auto-backgrounds past 600s (`until [ -s "<output-file>" ]; do sleep 15; done; cat "<output-file>"`) is itself a compound shell command, and Auto Mode's compound-command classifier can refuse it in a worktree-isolated session even though it's read-only — reproduced in three shapes against a live GitHub Actions polling loop in a real `issue-work` dispatch: a `for`/`sleep` loop, the same `until`/`sleep` shape with a live command-substitution condition instead of a file test, and even a standalone `sleep`. The refusal message's own framing ("git operations must target its own worktree") doesn't match any of the three commands, none of which touch git. The suggested fix in the originating issue — relaxing Claude Code's harness-level permission classifier — is an external dependency outside this repo's control; the shippable slice is the documentation half. Added a new `compound-command-refusal.md` fragment (kept out of the always-loaded `SKILL.md` core to preserve the #617/#808 line budget) documenting the three reproduced refusal shapes and the fallback that actually gets accepted: arming a `Monitor` with the identical polling command, since `Monitor`'s `command` argument is a single structured tool parameter rather than raw `Bash` text the classifier parses — while still never ending the turn on its notification, preserving the existing #529/#813/#753 never-wait-on-background-work rules (closes #981).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — the "A foreground call the harness auto-backgrounds past 600s" section now points at the new fragment when the re-block loop itself is refused; new fragment-index row.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/compound-command-refusal.md` — new on-demand fragment: the three reproduced refusal shapes, the `Monitor`-based fallback, and the narrower-than-#753 scoping of when it's sanctioned.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — new assertions pinning the SKILL.md pointer and the fragment's content.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.4.0` → `4.4.1`.

### 4.4.0 — 2026-07-26

The operator phase was framed as browser-driven throughout, but a meaningful subset of operator actions are strictly better served by an already-authenticated CLI: `gcloud` for GCP API-key restrictions and IAM/Secret Manager reads, `vercel` for Vercel env rows, `gh` for GitHub Actions secrets — scriptable, auditable (the exact invocation lands in the transcript), and independent of the browser profile being logged into the right account. Not hypothetical: in the originating session the browser path failed outright for two items a CLI would have sidestepped (an App Store Connect redirect-to-login, and a Play Console account closed years earlier). Added a CLI-first check ahead of the `toggle-setting`/`console-action` playbook and the `verify` playbook's console-read step: `command -v <cli>` plus an auth-state check (`gcloud auth list`, `vercel whoami`, `gh auth status`) decides whether to prefer the CLI over navigating a browser, falling back to the existing browser playbook whenever no CLI covers the action or it's absent/unauthenticated. This changes only *how* an attempt-class action is carried out — it composes with, and does not alter, #970's direction-based attempt/hand-back classification, and the harness-level credential-handling prohibitions (never type/paste a secret via a CLI flag or stdin, never create a new credential via CLI) bind the CLI path exactly as they bind the browser path (closes #972).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/02-execution-and-playbooks.md` — new "CLI-first: prefer an authenticated CLI over the browser" subsection (mechanism, classify-first composition with #970, the harness-credential-prohibition note, and a known-CLI-covered-actions table); a new step 0 on the `toggle-setting`/`console-action` playbook pointing at it; a CLI-first pointer added to the `verify` playbook's navigate step.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/03-error-handling-and-safety.md` — the third-party console deep-link section now points at the CLI-first check before deriving a deep link.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate.md` — the on-demand-bodies table's `02-execution-and-playbooks.md` row now names the CLI-first preference.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/05-dont.md` — new prohibition: a CLI must never loosen what the browser path wouldn't do (no secret-via-flag, no credential creation via CLI).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.3.7` → `4.4.0`.

### 4.3.7 — 2026-07-26

The operator layer's security/access-control carve-out (narrowed to an effect-based test by #936) still classified purely by *whether* an action touched who-can-access-what, not by *which direction* it moved exposure — so a mutation that genuinely tightens security (e.g. adding an HTTP-referrer restriction to an API key) was handed back exactly like one that loosens it. A real `needs-operator` queue from a `/do-work` session bore this out: 4 of 5 items were handed back under a plain reading, including a key-restriction task whose refusal left the key *more* exposed, not less. Split the carve-out by direction and reversibility: auto-drive (attempt, harness-classifier-gated) actions that narrow/revoke access or delete a value a merged change made redundant; hand back only actions that grant/widen access or create a new credential. Reaffirmed that read-only verification was always, and remains, unconditionally outside the boundary. Distinguished the separate harness-level prohibition on typing a password / entering a secret or API-key value / creating a new account from this spec's own access-control carve-out — a credential-rotation task stays partly a hand-back because of that harness rule, not because it's a "security setting" (closes #970).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/03-error-handling-and-safety.md` — the Safety § access-control bullet now splits the in-category bucket by direction (hand back only widen/grant/create-credential; attempt narrow/revoke/delete-redundant), states the harness-level credential-handling boundary as orthogonal to this carve-out, and reaffirms read-only verification is never a hand-back on its own.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/02-execution-and-playbooks.md` — the `toggle-setting`/`console-action` step-4 classification and its worked-example table now carry a third column (narrows/revokes/deletes — attempt) alongside the existing out-of-category-attempt and widens/grants/creates-credential-hand-back columns; names the credential-rotation split explicitly.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/01-queue-and-authorization.md` — the security/access-control-heavy-queue degradation note now explains the queue is smaller than it first looks once direction is applied.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — the thin entry's operator-layer summary restates the hand-back class as "widens/grants/creates a credential," not "touches a security setting."
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/operator-security-classification-970.test.sh` — new regression guard pinning the direction split, the harness-level-boundary distinction, and that the prior #936 effect-based test survives unchanged underneath it.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.3.6` → `4.3.7`.

### 4.3.6 — 2026-07-26

#965 (shipped in 4.3.4) made a "Resolved plugin root (orchestrator-supplied, literal path)" Context paragraph mandatory as the first paragraph of every dispatch prompt, scoped to `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/*.sh` invocation — but the paragraph said nothing about read/edit targets, and on the dogfooding repo (`/do-work` running against shipyard's own source) the orchestrator's resolved value IS the primary checkout, the one tree `dont.md` forbids writing to. A worker dispatched to edit `plugins/shipyard/...` spec files took the literal at face value and edited the primary checkout by mistake; `enforce-worktree-isolation.sh` caught it, but only after a wasted edit cycle. Implemented the preferred fix from the issue's stated preference order: make a worker's own worktree copy the default whenever `<worktree>/plugins/shipyard/scripts` exists, with the orchestrator-supplied literal demoted to an invocation-only fallback for consumer-install layouts with no worktree-local plugin copy (closes #969).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the canonical "Resolved plugin root" Context paragraph prepended to every `Agent`-tool dispatch prompt now instructs the worker to check for a worktree-local `plugins/shipyard/scripts` copy first (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel` + a plain `test -d`, never chained) and prefer it — for both script invocation and as the base for every spec file read-to-edit or written — before falling back to the orchestrator-supplied literal for invocation only.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — step-0's plugin-root-resolution guidance mirrors the same worktree-local-preferred default.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/prompt-templates/shared.mjs` — `worktreeAnchorCheckLines` now renders the same worktree-local-preferred `test -d` check (instead of an unconditional `export CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT="<pluginRoot>"`) for the `Workflow`-substrate alternate's `pluginRoot` work-unit field, closing the same ambiguity at the point a `Workflow`-dispatched worker hits it first. `workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` regenerated via `scripts/generate-dispatch-workflow.mjs`.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.3.5` → `4.3.6`.

### 4.3.5 — 2026-07-26

`agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` and `agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md` landed the PR's head branch via `git switch <branch>`, which git refuses whenever that branch is already checked out anywhere else. That refusal is the *normal* state for any PR the same session just opened — the originating issue-work worker's worktree deliberately keeps its branch checked out until end-of-session cleanup — so the bail fired on essentially every fix-checks/fix-rebase dispatch against a same-session PR, and the bail text ("needs end-of-session reap") pointed at a destructive remedy `dont.md` explicitly forbids (neither branch name nor lock PID can distinguish a live peer worktree from an abandoned one). Switched both modes to detached HEAD by default, which sidesteps git's one-worktree-per-branch exclusivity entirely since only a named-branch checkout is exclusive (closes #966).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — Setup now does `git checkout --detach "origin/$HEAD_REF"` instead of `git switch`; the locked-branch bail (and its "needs end-of-session reap" phrase) is removed. The fix-loop's push step now pushes explicitly (`git push origin "HEAD:refs/heads/$HEAD_REF"`) since a bare `git push` has no upstream to infer from detached HEAD. The "leave your worktree on the PR's head branch" return-state rule now says detached HEAD is expected.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md` — same `git checkout --detach` swap in step 1, with the locked-branch bail and its "needs manual rebase or end-of-session reap" phrasing removed. Step 6's force-push now targets `HEAD:refs/heads/$HEAD_REF` explicitly instead of relying on an upstream. Same return-state rule update as fix-checks-only.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.3.4` → `4.3.5`.

### 4.3.4 — 2026-07-26

`shipyard:worker-preamble`'s step-0 fail-fast instructed every dispatched worker to resolve `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` via a compound `export CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT="${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT:-$(...)}"` one-liner — but that exact compound shape (command substitutions plus an inline `if`) is refused by the harness's Auto Mode classifier as "too complex to verify that it stays inside the worktree" when it fires inside a worktree-isolated dispatch, and it fires as (or near) nearly every worker's first tool call. The refusal's own error text pointed at the wrong recovery ("break it into plain, separate commands" rather than the documented fallback), and the same compound block was repeated at four more worker-side call sites plus the `Workflow`-substrate dispatch-prompt builder. Per the maintainer's stated preference order on the consolidated issue thread (#964/#965), implemented the top-ranked fix: the orchestrator now passes its already-resolved plugin root into every worker's dispatch prompt as a literal path, so the worker never has to run the compound block at all (closes #965).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — new "Resolve the plugin root once and pass it to every worker as a literal path" step: the orchestrator reuses the `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` value it already resolved for the per-dispatch model-resolution call and prepends a `Resolved plugin root (orchestrator-supplied, literal path): …` Context paragraph to every mode's dispatch prompt, under both dispatch shapes. The `Workflow`-substrate per-mode JSON payload examples now also carry a `pluginRoot` field alongside `worktreePath`.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/prompt-templates/shared.mjs` — `worktreeAnchorCheckLines`/`worktreeAnchorLines` now accept a `pluginRoot` parameter: when the work unit carries one, the worktree-anchor block renders a literal `export CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT="<path>"` line instead of the compound resolution; when absent (a caller predating this fix), the compound block is emitted unchanged — purely additive, no regression for existing callers. `workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` regenerated from the updated source via `scripts/generate-dispatch-workflow.mjs`.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — step-0 fail-fast and mid-session cwd anchoring now instruct the worker to check its dispatch prompt for an orchestrator-supplied literal plugin root first, and to reuse whatever value it resolves (orchestrator-supplied or self-resolved) across every later `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`-referencing call rather than re-deriving it at each site. Added a decomposed, individually-verifiable fallback for when the compound export itself is refused, so the worker has a documented recovery in hand rather than the classifier's misrouting error text.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — the CHANGELOG monotonicity scan, the verify_gate model resolution, and §6.a's ungated-admin-direct-merge detector each now carry a pointer back to the already-resolved literal value instead of re-deriving it independently.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.3.3` → `4.3.4`.

### 4.3.3 — 2026-07-26

`/shipyard:do-work`'s scope pre-flight recognized only one of the two sentinels that mean "a human already answered this issue's blocking decisions": a maintainer's hand-written `<!-- do-work-decision-resolved -->` comment (#569), but not the `<!-- shipyard-resolve-decisions -->` comment `/shipyard:resolve-decisions` (and `/my-turn`'s reused decision-gated walkthrough) posts automatically after walking a maintainer through the same decisions. The result was a re-gate loop, reproduced twice on the same day against `mattsears18/lightwork`: `/my-turn` resolved the decisions and cleared `needs-human-review`, and hours later — with no new information — a fresh `/do-work` scope pass re-applied the label, citing verbatim the same policy question the resolution comment had already answered (closes #962).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — the freshness check's Signal B (and its "when NOT to apply" list) now recognizes `<!-- shipyard-resolve-decisions -->` alongside `<!-- do-work-decision-resolved -->` as an equally-dispositive gate-clear signal; the scope-agent prompt instruction likewise now names both sentinels when telling the agent to read the comment thread before judging actionability. Added a new mechanical Recording-path guard (step 3.5, orchestrator-side rather than prompt-side, matching the file's existing "prompts are not contracts" posture): before applying `needs-human-review` for a `human-decision-required` or `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` defer, check whether a decision-resolution comment (either sentinel) postdates the most recent removal of that label — if so, the fresh defer's evidence must name what changed since the resolution, or it's rejected (bounced back to `raw_backlog`, not silently re-gated) exactly like a malformed-evidence defer.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — added a regression block asserting both sentinels are recognized at both call sites and that the new step-3.5 guard exists with its timeline-comparison mechanism and its no-silent-re-gate rejection behavior.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.3.2` → `4.3.3`.

### 4.3.2 — 2026-07-25

Issue #888 posed a design fork about the native Dynamic Workflows dispatch substrate (`workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js`): its `agent()` calls are invoked once per work unit (mirroring the orchestrator's own `--concurrency N` rolling pool), so the script's `parallel()`/`pipeline()` fan-out primitives sat unused — carried as scaffolding for a hypothetical future batch-dispatch phase rather than exercised. A maintainer decision comment resolved the fork in favour of (B): keep the once-per-unit invocation and the orchestrator's rolling pool as the sole concurrency mechanism, and stop carrying the dormant fan-out branch as scaffolding — recovering it from git history later is cheaper than maintaining an untested one now, especially since `parallel()`'s option surface isn't fully public and an empty `parallel([])` call is exactly what made the #817 zero-unit regression invisible (closes #888).

- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.core.js` — the orchestration-logic source (post-#958 split) — deleted the `typeof parallel === 'function' ? await parallel(...) : await pipeline(...)` fan-out ternary; Stage 2 now dispatches each work unit with a plain sequential loop over `workUnits`, awaiting `dispatchWorker` in turn (a no-op change on the live single-unit path, and still exercises correctly for a manual multi-unit dry run). Rewrote the header's RUNTIME API list, "Concurrency model" section, and the "script's job" summary so they state plainly that the script calls `agent()` only, the orchestrator's rolling pool is the sole concurrency mechanism, and `pipeline()`/`parallel()` are available-on-the-platform-but-unused rather than reserved/in-use.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — regenerated from the updated `do-work-dispatch.core.js` + `prompt-templates/*.mjs` sources via `scripts/generate-dispatch-workflow.mjs`; not hand-edited.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/README.md` — corrected "Why this substrate" and the "Still out of scope" bullet (the line the issue cited) to stop presenting fan-out as part of the design, while keeping the rationale for why it was removed rather than kept dormant.
- Explicitly out of scope per the decision comment: whether the generated `do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` file's size is a problem — that concern was already addressed separately by #958/#961's core/prompt-templates split.
- Verified `scripts/generate-dispatch-workflow.mjs --check` reports no drift and `scripts/tests/dispatch-workflow-generated-958.test.sh` + `scripts/check-dispatch-prompt-parity.mjs` stay green.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.3.1` → `4.3.2`.

### 4.3.1 — 2026-07-25

`plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` was ~40KB / ~996 lines, but the maintainer decision that closed #888 (which only removed the file's unused `parallel()`/`pipeline()` fan-out branch) measured that the bulk of that size was the seven embedded `build<Mode>Prompt` worker-dispatch-prompt template functions (~469 lines), not orchestration logic — and directed that extracting those templates be filed and handled separately, without treating file size itself as evidence for or against the Workflow-substrate's shape. A source-level `import`/`require` split isn't possible for the file the Dynamic Workflows runtime actually executes — it runs in an isolated sandbox with no filesystem access of its own — so the fix is a generate-from-source split: the seven builders and their shared worktree-anchor helpers now live as independently-editable ES modules, and a small generator concatenates them with the orchestration logic into the runtime file, which stays checked into git as the generated artifact (closes #958).

- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.core.js` (new) — the orchestration-logic source of truth: `meta`, `args` handling, the select/dispatch/collect stages, `unitLabel`, `buildWorkerPrompt`'s mode-routing switch, and `workerReturnSchema`.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/prompt-templates/` (new) — `shared.mjs` (the `worktreeAnchorLines`/`worktreeAnchorCheckLines` helpers) plus one file per mode (`issue-work.mjs`, `fix-checks-only.mjs`, `fix-rebase.mjs`, `fix-main-ci.mjs`, `fix-failing-prs-batch.mjs`, `investigate.mjs`, `spike.mjs`) — each a normal, valid, independently `node --check`-able ES module.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/generate-dispatch-workflow.mjs` (new) — concatenates the core fragment + template modules (stripping `import`/`export` syntax) into `do-work-dispatch.workflow.js`; run with no args to regenerate, `--check` to verify without writing.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — now a generated artifact (byte-for-byte what the generator produces from the sources above); its function bodies are unchanged, so `scripts/check-dispatch-prompt-parity.mjs` (issue #880/#918) keeps working unmodified — it parses the generated file directly.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/dispatch-workflow-generated-958.test.sh` (new) — drift-checks the checked-in generated file against regenerating from source, validates every source fragment's syntax in isolation, exercises the generator's strip/concatenate logic against fixture fragments, and confirms the #880 parity checker still passes post-split.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/README.md` — documents the new core/prompt-templates/generator split and the "never hand-edit the generated file" contract.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.3.0` → `4.3.1`.

### 4.3.0 — 2026-07-25

A `/do-work` issue can be **undispatchable by construction** — the harness auto-mode permission classifier denies the dispatch itself, regardless of prompt wording, so the session discovers this only after burning both permitted attempts (the initial dispatch plus #718's one corrective re-dispatch) and handing back with no worker ever having run. #718's denial handling was already correct (record, one corrective re-dispatch, hard stop, `needs-human-review`); the gap was that the hand-back was indistinguishable at a glance from a worker that started and later bailed, and nothing marked the outcome for reuse by a later session (closes #953).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the two-denial hand-back now stamps a `<!-- do-work-classifier-undispatchable -->` marker as the literal first line of its `needs-human-review` comment, with an explanation of why the marker matters: no worker ever ran, so there is no bail reasoning to review, and the marker is what lets a later session's scope-preflight freshness check reuse the cached diagnosis instead of re-walking the same two-denial cycle.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — the scope-result freshness check now recognizes the new marker (mapped to the existing `human-decision-required` `defer_reason_class`, since a scope agent never returns this class itself — only `dispatch-rules.md`'s hand-back synthesizes it, the same pattern as the existing pre-scope detectors). Added the marker to the marker table and an accepted `evidence_pointer` prefix (`Classifier denied dispatch on both permitted attempts`) to the per-class evidence-shape table and validator, for the freshness check's mechanical shape-only re-validation.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — added a distinct bucket for the marker (surfaced as "no worker ran — do it by hand or make a permission-policy call," never conflated with an agent-refuse's bail reasoning), and excluded it from the decision-gated walkthrough (there's no enumerated decision to walk, and clearing `needs-human-review` alone is not itself a fix since nothing about the issue's content changed).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.2.8` → `4.3.0`.

### 4.2.8 — 2026-07-25

Issue #883 was originally scoped as "extract a `resolve-plugin-root.sh` helper and replace all 66 inlined `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` bootstrap copies with a 2-line `source` call" — a prior scoping pass rejected that route as circular (locating the helper requires the identical probe it would replace, and every occurrence runs in a fresh hermetic subshell with nothing persisting between calls) and a maintainer decision comment re-aimed the issue at shrinking the inlined probe in place instead: keep the repo-local dogfooding-override layer (governed separately by #907, which kept it), and collapse the remaining installed-path + marketplace-glob layers into the one layer (`installed_plugins.json`) that's authoritative and resolves identically across every install method (closes #883).

- The canonical `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` bootstrap preamble shrunk from ~696 to ~403 bytes per occurrence — dropped the marketplace-glob fallback layer (the `.bak`/`.old`/`.orig`/`.disabled`-excluding glob over `$HOME/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/*/plugins/shipyard`), which only ever mattered when `installed_plugins.json` was unreadable or missing the `shipyard@shipyard` entry — verified against a real install that this is not the steady state.
- Applied mechanically across all 80 occurrences of the old form: 9 files under `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/` (65 occurrences — the issue's original hot-path scope), plus 3 files under `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/` and 5 files under `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/` (15 occurrences) so the regression test's single `EXPECTED_PREAMBLE` stays consistent across every file it scans rather than tolerating two co-existing forms.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` §0.3 — rewrote the layer-by-layer semantics prose for the two-layer form, and added a permanent record of why helper-script extraction was rejected (so a future source-review pass doesn't re-propose it).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/claude-plugin-root-preamble.test.sh` — updated `EXPECTED_PREAMBLE` to the shrunk form, rewrote the header-comment rationale, and replaced the two marketplace-glob-specific sandbox checks (`.bak`-shadowing exclusion, authoritative-install-vs-marketplace preference) with two checks matched to the new two-layer shape: `installed_plugins.json`'s `installPath` is used when the repo has no local `plugins/shipyard` (issue #417, re-verified), and a malformed/partial `installed_plugins.json` entry (no `scripts/` dir) still falls through to the repo-local-anyway default rather than being trusted blindly.

### 4.2.7 — 2026-07-25

The `main` ruleset required **zero** status checks, so `gh pr merge --auto` did not queue behind CI — for an admin it fell through to an immediate direct merge, the ungated shape `scripts/detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh` exists to detect (issues #438 / #465 / #598 / #602 / #645 / #716). Observed live while landing 4.2.6: the PR merged the instant auto-merge was armed. Five checks are now required on the ruleset, and the reasoning is written down so the next person extending the list doesn't brick the repo with a check that never reports on a pull request.

- Ruleset `16669759` — added a `required_status_checks` rule for `conflict markers`, `gitleaks`, `bash test suites`, `shell tests`, and `shellcheck`; all five come from workflows triggered on every `pull_request` against `main`. `strict_required_status_checks_policy` deliberately left `false` (a `true` value would force a rebase on nearly every PR at ~97 merges/week). The detector now reports `verdict=gated`.
- `CLAUDE.md` — new § *Required status checks on `main` — and the two that must never be added* under Permissions, documenting the five required contexts and what each guards, plus an explicit warning that `External-author gate` (triggers on `issues` only) and `Audit label event` (triggers on label events only) must never be required, since neither runs on every PR and a required check that never reports is indistinguishable from one that never passes.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.2.6` → `4.2.7`.

### 4.2.6 — 2026-07-25

The operator layer's security/access-control carve-out classified by **keyword and provider-console location** ("any 'security' toggle") rather than by what an action actually does, and handed matching items back *before* the harness permission classifier — the layer that owns the permission decision — was ever consulted. Both halves stranded work: `vercel env rm` of six provably-dead, zero-consumer `EXPO_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` env vars was refused as a security mutation despite granting and revoking nobody's access, and because the hand-back pre-empted the attempt, the maintainer was never offered the scoped allow-rule that would have unblocked it. The carve-out is now defined by effect — *changes who can access what, or the strength of an authentication/authorization control* — and everything outside it is attempted, degrading to a hand-back only on a real classifier denial via the existing two-denials branch (closes #936).

Deliberately **not** changed: the untrusted-author block still applies in both columns (it is what stops a prompt-injected issue body from manufacturing apparent authorization, so the original request to drop the "outranks explicit user authorization" clause wholesale was declined and the clause scoped instead), and a genuine in-category access-control mutation remains an unconditional hand-back that outranks standing authorization.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/03-error-handling-and-safety.md` — the § *Safety — trust boundary* carve-out restated around the effect-based definition, with the attempt-then-degrade rule for out-of-category actions, the dead-config worked example, the retained conservative default on genuine ambiguity, and an explicit note that the clause does not scope down for untrusted-derived actions.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/02-execution-and-playbooks.md` — `toggle-setting` / `console-action` step-4 classification and the auto-drive-vs-hand-back table re-keyed to the same definition; the left column is now "attempt" rather than "auto-drive", names zero-consumer non-secret config removal, and routes refusals through the `denied-by-classifier` degrade path.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/01-queue-and-authorization.md` — the security-heavy-queue expectation note now sizes that bucket by the effect test, so console work merely filed under a provider's "Security" heading is no longer counted as a structural hand-back.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — `security-class` reason phrase reworded to name the effect rather than the keyword.
- New `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/operator-security-classification-936.test.sh` regression suite (20 assertions) pins the effect-based definition in both files, asserts the keyword catch-all is gone, and guards the two carve-outs #936 deliberately preserved. Verified to fail (15 assertions) against the pre-change spec.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.2.5` → `4.2.6`.

### 4.2.5 — 2026-07-25

Issue #867 was closed after `security-auditor` recommended enabling CODEOWNERS enforcement plus `required_approving_review_count: 1` on `main` — advice that would have deadlocked every PR on this repo (a single push-capable maintainer, and an empty `bypass_actors` list on the `main` ruleset means no admin bypass exists either). GitHub does not let a PR author approve their own PR, so that combination makes every future PR unmergeable, including every `/shipyard:do-work` auto-merge PR. The advice was only caught because a `/shipyard:my-turn` walkthrough verified the ruleset before handing over a ready-to-run `gh api` command (closes #938).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/security-auditor.md` — new pass 8, "Branch-ruleset / CODEOWNERS remediation gate": before recommending any change to `required_approving_review_count` or `require_code_owner_review`, the auditor must now read the target ruleset's `bypass_actors` and the repo's push-capable collaborator count live via `gh api`, refuse to recommend a review-count bump above 0 when either signal shows the repo would deadlock (fewer than 2 push-capable collaborators, or an empty `bypass_actors` list) — stating the prerequisite instead of the bump as the actionable step — flag a CODEOWNERS-review recommendation as a self-lock when the owner set for the affected path is a subset of the sole maintainer, and scope-check any proposed CODEOWNERS pattern against the file set touched by the repo's own routine automated PRs. A new `Don't` bullet cross-references the pass. The auditor remains read-only — it never calls `gh api -X PUT`/`PATCH`/`-X POST` to apply the change itself.
- New `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/security-auditor.test.sh` regression suite (15 assertions) pins the new pass's evidence-gathering commands, the deadlock guardrail, the CODEOWNERS self-lock check, and the CODEOWNERS-breadth scope-check.
- Deliberately did not extend `plugins/shipyard/scripts/detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh` to cover this — that script is the single source of truth for a distinct decision (whether `gh pr merge --auto` will silently direct-merge past CI) consumed by the issue-worker's merge step; the auditor's question (would a review-count bump deadlock the repo) is a different decision with a different evidence set, and folding it in would blur that script's pinned single responsibility.

### 4.1.42 — 2026-07-25

`check-dispatch-prompt-parity.mjs`'s augmentation-anchor check (CHECK 3, added by #880) compared against a hand-maintained `AUGMENTATION_ANCHORS` table inside the script — it caught a *known* anchor's text drifting, but was structurally blind to the *addition* of a brand-new augmentation to `dispatch-rules.md` that was never wired into the corresponding builder. That blind spot wasn't hypothetical: PR #917 (#851) added the operator-residual augmentation and the very next PR, #919 (#852), added the verification-scope augmentation — both landed with zero coverage in `buildIssueWorkPrompt`, and the static table never grew to notice either, so the parity suite stayed green for both (closes #918).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-dispatch-prompt-parity.mjs` — CHECK 3 now DERIVES its anchor set from `dispatch-rules.md`'s own structure instead of a static table: every bolded `**<Name> augmentation ...**` heading marks the start of an augmentation, and the checker extracts the Context-paragraph blockquote that follows it, using that blockquote's own leading bold span as the anchor phrase to assert present in the applicable builder(s) — mirroring `check-worker-return-schema-parity.mjs`'s #856 precedent of deriving the comparison set from the canonical source. Adding a new augmentation now extends coverage automatically, closing the property #880 actually wanted. An augmentation intentionally left unwired can carry an explicit `<!-- dispatch-prompt-parity: waived — <reason> -->` marker so a documented gap doesn't fail CI; the checker also fails if a waived anchor is later found present in the builder anyway (a stale waiver nobody removed).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the two augmentations that previously lacked a structural "augmentation" heading (user-feedback preamble, next-available-version) now carry one, so all six augmentations are uniformly derivable.
