### 4.1.41 — 2026-07-25

A dogfooding session read `dispatch-rules.md` via `$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT`, which for the dogfooding case (shipyard orchestrating its own repo) resolves repo-local to the **primary checkout** — and that checkout was 49 commits behind `origin/main`, so the session executed an arbitrarily-stale copy of its own spec for its entire run with no warning anywhere. The staleness is unbounded and silent: the orchestrator relocates into a fresh worktree off `origin/<default-branch>` for its *working tree* (step 0.5) but, until now, kept reading its *spec* from whatever commit the primary checkout happened to sit at (closes #907).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — step 0.4 now compares the resolved repo-local `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` against `origin/<default-branch>` and prints a loud one-line warning (plus the session-local `SHIPYARD_PLUGIN_ROOT_STALE` var) when the primary checkout is measurably behind; the check is a no-op for a consumer install (layers 2-4), where `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` points at a distinct install with its own update cadence. Also records the resolved plugin root's own commit sha (`SHIPYARD_PLUGIN_ROOT_SHA`, echoed at both step 0.4 and step 0.5) so "which version of the spec ran?" is answerable after the fact. Step 0.5 now re-echoes the freshly-relocated `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` + commit and documents that spec-file reads for the rest of the session should use that post-relocation resolution, not the pre-relocation one step 0.4 warned about — the orchestrator worktree is checked out fresh off `origin/<default-branch>`'s tip by construction, so a spec read from there can't be the stale copy.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new `Plugin root:` end-of-session line, printed only when `SHIPYARD_PLUGIN_ROOT_STALE` was set (silence is the default for a fresh checkout or a consumer install), re-surfacing the staleness warning for a user who scrolled past the startup output — same pattern as the existing `Config:` line.

### 4.1.40 — 2026-07-25

Three of the repo's four `check-*.mjs` gates (`check-workflow-meta-literal.mjs`, `check-dispatch-prompt-parity.mjs`, `check-worker-return-schema-parity.mjs`) guarded their CLI entry point with `process.argv[1] && import.meta.url === \`file://${process.argv[1]}\``. `import.meta.url` is always the fully-resolved real path while `process.argv[1]` is the path exactly as typed, so invoking any of the three through a symlinked path (macOS's `/tmp` -> `/private/tmp` is the everyday case) made the comparison false, skipped the entire CLI body, and exited 0 having checked nothing — a silent fail-open in the exact class of tooling whose only job is to fail closed. CI never observed it because GitHub Actions checks out to a real path with no symlink component; the incidental protection is why `main` stayed green while local runs got a false pass (closes #933).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-workflow-meta-literal.mjs`, `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-dispatch-prompt-parity.mjs`, `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-worker-return-schema-parity.mjs` — the `isMain` guard now compares `realpathSync(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url))` against `realpathSync(process.argv[1])` instead of a raw string compare, so it correctly resolves whether invoked via a real or symlinked path. `fileURLToPath` also fixes a second latent bug in the old hand-built `` `file://${...}` `` string, which was wrong for any path containing spaces or non-ASCII characters. Each checker's existing zero-files usage-error branch (`exit 2` with no positional args) already gives "validated zero files" a non-zero exit once `isMain` actually fires, so no additional defense-in-depth logic was needed there.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/symlinked-cli-invocation-933.test.sh` — new regression suite: builds a deliberate symlinked directory (so the repro doesn't depend on the host having an incidental `/tmp` symlink, which a CI runner may not) and drives all three checkers through it with known-bad, known-good, and zero-argument input; also re-runs the existing `workflow-meta-pure-literal-809.test.sh` suite through the same symlinked path and asserts it reports the identical `30 passed, 0 failed` as the real-path run.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.39` → `4.1.40`.

### 4.1.39 — 2026-07-25

`claude-plugin-root-preamble.test.sh`'s `FILES` array went stale after #611 split `setup.md` into a thin router + step-cluster sub-files — `setup.md` itself dropped to zero `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` occurrences (so its array entry passed vacuously) while `dispatch-rules.md`, the `setup/*.md` sub-files, `skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`, `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`, and `agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md` all grew their own preamble-carrying bash blocks without ever being added to the array. 41 of 69 preamble occurrences (59%) had zero regression coverage at the time this was found (closes #910). Rather than append the missing filenames (the same rot the array already suffered once), the test now discovers candidate files **mechanically** — every `*.md` under `commands/do-work/` (recursive), `skills/worker-preamble/` (the skill plus every on-demand fragment), and `agents/issue-worker/` (every per-mode worker spec) is a candidate; a candidate with zero `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`-referencing bash blocks is a no-op, so the glob stays safe to keep wide and a future file is swept in automatically the moment it grows a matching block. The wider scan also has to recognize the two-block idiom `SKILL.md` uses (a preamble-only block immediately followed by a bare script-invocation block) as a second passing shape, alongside the original single-block "preamble is the block's own first line" shape. Widening the scan to its true boundary surfaced real, previously-invisible instances of the #354 bug class — bash blocks that call a `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`-rooted script with no preamble anywhere above them, which would have silently exited 127 the first time the harness didn't already have the var set: `skills/worker-preamble/ci-pitfalls.md`, `skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md`, `agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` (×3), `agents/issue-worker/fix-main-ci.md` (×2), `agents/issue-worker/fix-failing-prs-batch.md`, `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` (one additional block beyond its already-covered one), and `commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md`. All ten got the canonical preamble added rather than being left for a follow-up PR, since the fix is the same one-line, well-understood boilerplate already used at 60+ other call sites in this tree. Coverage went from 28 checked occurrences across 5 files (with `setup.md` covering 0) to all 78 `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`-referencing bash blocks across the 39 files that actually reference it.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/claude-plugin-root-preamble.test.sh` — hardcoded `FILES` array replaced with `find`-based discovery over the three scope directories; discovery-sanity assertions (candidate-count floor + a canary-file check) guard against a silent zero-file regression; `walk_blocks` extended to recognize the two-block idiom; doc comments rewritten to describe the mechanical scope and its rationale.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/ci-pitfalls.md`, `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md`, `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md`, `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-main-ci.md`, `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-failing-prs-batch.md`, `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`, `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — added the canonical `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` re-export preamble to the ten bash blocks the widened scan found missing it.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md`, `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — canonicalized two pre-existing non-canonical preamble variants (missing `export`, extra leading comment) to the exact literal form, so the test's check logic didn't need a third fuzzy-matching mode to accept them.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.38` → `4.1.39`.

### 4.1.38 — 2026-07-25

Issue #878 (filed from a source-review session analyzing the native-Workflows migration) flagged `check-workflow-meta-literal.mjs` as a "never-wired CI gate" — a plain grep for the checker's name outside `.test.sh` files found no hit in `.github/workflows/`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, or `package.json` (closes #878). Investigation found the premise was actually stale: `scripts/tests/workflow-meta-pure-literal-809.test.sh` (added in bb31052, the day before #878 was filed) already invokes this checker against the real `plugins/shipyard/workflows/*.workflow.js` files in its section (A), and — matching every sibling checker's convention (`check-anchor-links.mjs`, `check-dispatch-prompt-parity.mjs`, `check-worker-return-schema-parity.mjs`) — that `.test.sh` suite IS this repo's CI wiring, auto-discovered by `.github/workflows/tests.yml`'s `find plugins -type f -name '*.test.sh'` with no separate workflow entry needed. Running the suite confirms it: 30/30 assertions pass, including section (A)'s live check against `do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` and section (F)'s self-pin that the suite's own `*.test.sh` filename keeps it inside `tests.yml`'s discovery. Rather than duplicate that already-working gate with a second one (or delete a checker guarding a real, currently-held invariant), this PR documents the wiring directly in the checker's own header comment and in the test suite's section (F), so a future grep-based review can't re-flag it — the wiring is dynamic (test-file discovery), not textual, so a static-reference grep will always come up empty even though the gate is fully live.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-workflow-meta-literal.mjs` — header comment gains an "ALREADY WIRED INTO CI (issue #878)" section explaining the test-file-discovery wiring mechanism and citing the exact suite + section that exercises it against the real workflow file.

### 4.1.37 — 2026-07-25

Issue #866's own anchor-link checker (`check-anchor-links.mjs`) explicitly excluded bare `[label](path)` links with no `#anchor` fragment from its scope; #929 found one — a stale 4-level-up relative path to `issue-work.md` in `06-scope-preflight.md:56`, one directory level too deep after a doc move (closes #929). Rather than fix only the one named link, this PR extends `check-anchor-links.mjs` itself to also validate that every bare relative-path link resolves to a real file or directory on disk (`broken-path`, alongside the existing `missing-file`/`missing-anchor` anchor-check kinds), reusing the same file-walking and link-extraction machinery so the two validations can't drift apart the way #866's own header comment warns duplicated logic tends to. Building the extension surfaced a real bug in the existing checker: its inline-code masking used a naive single-backtick regex that mis-pairs a multi-backtick CommonMark code span (e.g. a 4-backtick span wrapping a literal 3-backtick fence example) — on such a line the naive mask silently swallowed real markdown links that followed, which is exactly why #929's own repro line went undetected by any prior scan. Fixed with a proper backtick-run-aware `maskCodeSpans()`. With that fix in place, the mechanical resolver found **3 broken bare-path links** (not 1) across the same doc set — all fixed in this PR — while the pre-existing anchor check still reports 0 broken across the same 1036 anchor links, confirming no regression.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-anchor-links.mjs` — new `maskCodeSpans()` (CommonMark backtick-run matching, replacing the naive `` `[^`]*` `` mask used by link extraction); new bare relative-path validation branch (`broken-path` kind) for every link with no `#anchor`, gated by a generalized URI-scheme skip (`https:`/`mailto:`/`tel:`/any other scheme) so external links are never disk-checked; summary/diagnostic output now reports both link classes.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/relative-links-929.test.sh` — new regression suite (10 assertions): basic bare-path pass/fail, directory targets, the multi-backtick masking-fix regression (using the literal #929 repro shape), external-scheme skip, mixed anchor+bare-path diagnostics on one file, and a full real-tree run as the regression guard.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — the reported link: `../../../../agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` → `../../../agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — two more broken bare-path links found by the extended checker: `./scripts/detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh` → `../scripts/detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh`, and `../.github/CODEOWNERS` → `../../../.github/CODEOWNERS`.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.36` → `4.1.37`.

### 4.1.36 — 2026-07-25

A `docs` audit found 6 stale `#anchor` links in the do-work orchestrator docs — a regression of #411, which fixed 13 of the same class two months earlier (closes #866). The issue's own count of 6 turned out to be stale by the time this landed: roughly 20 PRs had merged into the same phase docs in the hours between the audit and dispatch, adding many new cross-file anchor links (notably #911/#912's `*-RATIONALE.md` splits). Rather than fix only the named 6, this PR commits a dependency-free `check-anchor-links.mjs` checker (following the `check-worker-return-schema-parity.mjs` / `check-dispatch-prompt-parity.mjs` convention — no `eval`/`new Function`) that walks every relative `[label](path#anchor)` link in `plugins/shipyard/**/*.md` and the root `CLAUDE.md`, resolves each against its target file's real headings using a GitHub-slug-accurate algorithm (calibrated against real headings in this repo, including duplicate-heading `-1`/`-2` suffixing, explicit `<a id="...">` anchors, and fenced-code-block exclusion), and reports every unresolvable one. The mechanical resolver found **33 genuinely broken anchors** (not 6) across the same doc set — every one fixed in this PR — plus two real bugs the checker itself caught while being built: an intraword-underscore false-positive (`skip_speculative_rerun` mangled by naive italic-stripping) and a placeholder-collision bug that spliced the literal string `"undefined"` into a slug whenever a heading contained a bare number surrounded by spaces (e.g. "used by step 7 and step C"). The checker is CI-run via `scripts/tests/anchor-links-866.test.sh` (auto-discovered by `tests.yml`'s `find plugins -name '*.test.sh'`), so this class of drift now fails a check instead of rotting silently between audits — the second time the same drift class has been filed, and the fix this time is a permanent guard rather than another one-off pass.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-anchor-links.mjs` — new dependency-free anchor-link checker: GitHub-slug algorithm, fence-aware heading/link extraction, `missing-file` vs `missing-anchor` diagnostics.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/anchor-links-866.test.sh` — new regression suite (17 assertions): the #866 case-2 worked example, the intraword-underscore and placeholder-collision bug regressions, bold-lead-in / explicit-`<a id>` / fenced-code edge cases, duplicate-heading suffixing, missing-file vs missing-anchor, and a full real-tree run as the actual regression guard.
- 18 markdown files across `plugins/shipyard/agents/`, `plugins/shipyard/commands/`, and `CLAUDE.md` — 33 broken anchor links repointed to their targets' current headings (or, for two bold-lead-ins that were never real headings, either promoted to a real `####` heading or repointed to the nearest enclosing real heading), plus a handful of adjacent stale relative-path bugs (`../issue-work-RATIONALE.md` → `issue-work-RATIONALE.md`, a 4-`../` `CLAUDE.md` reference off by one level) found while resolving the same links.

### 4.1.35 — 2026-07-25

A `data-lifecycle` audit found `~/.shipyard/flake-registry.jsonl` had a working `prune` subcommand with no scheduled caller and no discoverable manual path — unlike the sibling `cost-history.jsonl` ledgers, which document `/shipyard:cost reset` as an explicit escape hatch (closes #863). `flake-registry.sh` already shipped a correct, idempotent, atomic-rewrite `prune --window-days N` (default 90) as of #378 — this was a scheduling/discoverability gap, not a missing implementation, confirmed by reading the existing script before touching it. The fix schedules that existing `prune` from the one place already gated on `flake_registry.enabled` and invoked once per `/do-work` session: setup step 5.8's `flake-enforce.sh enforce` call. `flake-enforce.sh` now takes an opt-in `--prune-window-days N` flag — omitted, it's a pre-#863 no-op (existing callers and tests are unaffected); passed, it prunes the registry via `flake-registry.sh prune` *before* reading crossed rows, skips entirely under `--dry-run` (enforce's documented "no side effects" contract), and logs a `prune advisory` line and continues into the rest of enforcement on failure rather than blocking it. Setup wires `--prune-window-days` to the new `flake_registry.prune_window_days` config knob (default 90, `0` disables pruning) on every session that has `flake_registry.enabled: true`, satisfying the issue's acceptance criteria without touching the registry file's mutation logic or its local-only, off-by-default privacy posture.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/flake-enforce.sh` — `enforce` gains `--prune-window-days N`: opt-in, skipped under `--dry-run`, fire-and-forget (logs `enforce: pruned registry to last <N>d` or an `enforce: prune advisory: ...` failure line, never blocks the rest of enforcement).
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — `flake_registry.prune_window_days` (integer, default 90, `0` = keep everything).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` — built-in default `flake_registry.prune_window_days: 90` added alongside the existing `flake_registry` defaults.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/04-backlog-divert.md` — step 5.8's `flake-enforce.sh enforce` call now passes `--prune-window-days "$PRUNE_WINDOW_DAYS"` (read from `flake_registry.prune_window_days`) inside the existing `flake_registry.enabled` gate; prose updated to cite #863 alongside #385.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/flake-enforce.test.sh` — new `--prune-window-days` test block (10 assertions): omitted flag is a no-op, a real prune drops out-of-window events and keeps in-window ones, `--dry-run` skips the prune entirely, and a prune failure logs an advisory without blocking the rest of enforcement.

### 4.1.34 — 2026-07-25

A `data-lifecycle` audit found that orphaned atomic-write `.tmp` files under `$SHIPYARD_HOME` are never swept (closes #858). Every atomic-write helper in this plugin writes to a sibling tmp file then renames it into place — `session-state.sh`'s `atomic_write()` ("`<target>.tmp.$$`" for `sessions/<id>.json`), `flake-registry.sh`'s prune rewrite ("`<path>.tmp.$$`" for `flake-registry.jsonl`), and `cost-history.sh`'s reconcile-rewrite path (`mktemp "${session_target}.XXXXXX"` / `.err.XXXXXX` for `cost-history.jsonl`) — and each already cleans up its own tmp file on every code path it controls, but a hard kill (SIGKILL, an auto-mode permission denial, an OOM) between the tmp-file write and that cleanup leaves the file on disk forever: `session-state.sh`'s own `cleanup --session-id <id>` sweep only fires for a *known* session id, which a crash mid-`init` never produces, and the orchestrator's step-1.6 orphan sweep discovers stale sessions by globbing `*.json` — a bare `.tmp.<pid>` sibling never matches. `cost-history.sh` and `flake-registry.sh` had no sweep for their own tmp leftovers under any circumstance. The new sweep applies the same age-floor-plus-liveness safety shape issue #253 established for the existing `*.json` orphan sweep: a 30-minute mtime floor, plus a precise per-file `kill -0 <pid>` liveness check for the two pid-suffixed writers (the pid is embedded in the filename itself), and — for `cost-history.jsonl`'s pid-less `mktemp`-suffixed leftovers — skipping the whole category while `cost-history.jsonl.lock` is held and fresh, so an active flush is never raced. Every reap is logged, not silently deleted, since a `.tmp`/`.err` leftover under `cost-history.jsonl` can be evidence of a real failure (the deliberate abort-on-jq-failure path from #901) rather than garbage — consistent with the #871–#877 silent-failure-path sweep earlier this session. `reap-audit.jsonl` and `autoreport-failures.jsonl` are deliberately out of scope: both are plain `>>` appends with no tmp-file step, so neither has an atomic-write leftover to reap.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/sweep-orphan-tmp.sh` — new script; a single `sweep --shipyard-home <dir> [--stale-min <N>] [--dry-run]` subcommand covering all three tmp-leftover categories in one pass.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/sweep-orphan-tmp.test.sh` — new regression suite (32 assertions): the crash-mid-`atomic_write` repro (a `.tmp.<pid>` with no corresponding `.json`, reaped), its mirror-image safety cases (a fresh/in-progress `.tmp` left untouched by mtime, and separately by live-pid liveness), the `flake-registry.jsonl.tmp.<pid>` and `cost-history.jsonl.<rand>`/`.err.<rand>` categories, the fresh-vs-stale `cost-history.jsonl.lock` gate, `--dry-run`, `--stale-min` overrides, and bad-usage cases.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — step 0.7's background cleanup group now also runs the new sweep immediately after the existing `*.json` orphan-session sweep, in the same background subshell.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — step 1.6 documents the new sweep alongside the existing orphan-session-file writeup: why it's needed, the two-gate safety shape per category, and why the sweep logs every reap instead of deleting silently.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.33` → `4.1.34`.

### 4.1.31 — 2026-07-25

`report-plugin-error.sh` — the plugin's own opt-in (`SHIPYARD_AUTOREPORT=1`) error-tracking hook — discarded the result of its two terminal `gh` write calls (`gh issue comment` and `gh issue create`) with `>/dev/null 2>&1 || true` and no fallback of any kind recording that the filing attempt itself failed. Every other swallowed-failure spot in this codebase either had a documented reason the loss was bounded, or was already hardened after a prior incident (#357, #712); this one had neither — a persistently-misconfigured or rate-limited autoreport pipeline failed 100% of the time going forward with nothing anywhere to indicate it, defeating the entire point of the mechanism (surfacing failures the user would otherwise miss) (closes #877). This is the last of a seven-issue "swallowed failure" sweep this session (#871–#877). The fix has one hazard the other six didn't: this script IS the error-reporting pipeline, so its own failure handler must not attempt to report *that* failure through the same `gh issue create`/`comment` mechanism — doing so risks an infinite loop on a persistent failure (auth expired, rate-limited) or, at best, silently burying the original failure behind an identical second one. `record_autoreport_failure()` avoids the hazard by construction: it never calls `gh` at all, only appending a JSONL line to a local fallback log and printing a diagnostic to stderr — there is no code path from the handler back into the reporting pipeline. The hook's "never propagate a non-zero exit" contract is unchanged.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/report-plugin-error.sh` — both `gh` write call sites now capture the exit status explicitly (`|| gh_status=$?`, avoiding the script's own `trap 'exit 0' ERR`) and, on a nonzero status, call the new `record_autoreport_failure()` helper, which appends one JSONL record (timestamp, action, gh exit status, target, skill/agent name, dedupe signature) to `$SHIPYARD_HOME/autoreport-failures.jsonl` (default `~/.shipyard/`) and logs a matching stderr line — mirroring the `reap-audit.jsonl` / cost-history ledger pattern used elsewhere in this plugin. The helper calls no `gh` command, by design.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/report-plugin-error.test.sh` — new "Fallback log on gh failure (#877)" section: runs the helper for real (not dry-run) against a fake, always-failing `gh` on `PATH`, and asserts the filing failure never propagates a non-zero exit, lands exactly one fallback-log record (proving no recursive self-reporting), the record identifies the failing action/skill, and the failure is also surfaced on stderr.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.30` → `4.1.31`.

### 4.1.30 — 2026-07-25

`cmd_update`'s self-healing `setup-timing.sh flush` in `session-state.sh` (issue #283's opportunistic fallback for the orchestrator's own step-6.8 flush call) fired with `2>/dev/null || true` and no stderr line at all, win or lose — unlike the degraded-recovery branch a few dozen lines earlier in the same function, which deliberately logs its self-healing action. A persistently-failing flush (a permanently malformed `.timing.json` sidecar, or a `setup-timing.sh` bug) meant `.setup` stayed `null` forever for that session, the autoflush attempted silently on every single `cmd_update` call, and nothing anywhere recorded that it was failing — an operator debugging "why is setup timing always missing" got nothing to go on even in a verbose run (closes #876). ### 4.1.28 — 2026-07-25

`reap_orphan_branches` in `worktree-reap.sh` discarded `git branch -D`'s exit status (`|| true`) and unconditionally emitted both a `reaped-branch:` stdout line and a `"action":"reaped-orphan-branch"` audit-log line for every orphan `worktree-agent-*` branch, so a delete that actually failed (an unmerged commit, a permission error, a concurrent-delete race) was recorded as a successful reap — the same false-success-audit-log shape issue #712 fixed one function over in this file for `fast_worktree_remove`/`reap_action`, but `reap_orphan_branches` predates that fix and was never brought in line with it (closes #874). The delete's exit status is now checked: a successful `git branch -D` still emits the `reaped-branch:` stdout line and the `reaped-orphan-branch` audit line; a failed one emits neither, and instead writes a distinguishable `"action":"reaped-branch-failed"` audit line with `"reason":"branch-delete-failed"`, mirroring the `reaped`/`reaped-failed` split `reap_action` already uses.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/worktree-reap.sh` — `reap_orphan_branches` now branches on `git branch -D`'s exit status instead of swallowing it; header docstring and the `reap-orphan-branches` subcommand's own doc comment updated to describe the `reaped-branch-failed` variant and the now-conditional stdout line.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — step 4.5's description of the audit-log shape updated to cover the failure path.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-reap.test.sh` — new (57b)/(57c) cases: force a deterministic `git branch -D` failure via a read-only `.git/refs/heads` (mirroring the existing (81) `reaped-failed` permission-denial fixture) and assert no stdout `reaped-branch:` line, no `reaped-orphan-branch` audit line, a `reaped-branch-failed` audit line with `reason:"branch-delete-failed"`, the branch survives, and the sweep still exits 0.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.27` → `4.1.28`.

### 4.1.27 — 2026-07-25

`do_file_tracking_issue` in `flake-enforce.sh` deduped chronic-flake tracking issues by searching OPEN issues for the flake-key marker via `gh issue list --search ... 2>/dev/null || echo ""` — a transient `gh` failure (rate limit, network blip, auth hiccup) at that lookup and a genuine "no existing issue" result both collapsed to the same empty string, so the function silently fell through and filed a duplicate tracking issue with no diagnostic explaining why dedupe didn't catch it (closes #873). This is the same swallowed-failure shape fixed for `do_apply_blocked_ci` in the same file earlier this session (#872): capture the `gh` call's own exit status explicitly instead of discarding it with `|| echo ""`, and log a distinguishable stderr line (`file-tracking-issue dedupe-lookup-failed repo=... marker=... status=<rc> — proceeding to file anyway`) only when the lookup itself errored, leaving the genuine-empty-result skip path unchanged.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/flake-enforce.sh` — `do_file_tracking_issue`'s dedupe lookup now captures `dedupe_status` explicitly and logs the new diagnostic line on a nonzero status.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/flake-enforce.test.sh` — new "file-tracking-issue — a failing dedupe lookup must be logged, not fail open silently (#873)" case, mirroring the #872 `do_apply_blocked_ci` test style: mocks a failing `gh issue list`, asserts the failure is logged and distinguishable from a genuine skip, and asserts filing still proceeds.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.26` → `4.1.27`.

### 4.1.26 — 2026-07-25

QA-verification-shaped issues — "walk flow X, file bugs for anything broken, check the box" — don't fit `do-work`'s PR-shaped model, so they fell straight through scope-preflight to a whole-issue defer even when part of the verification surface was automatable (closes #852, `mattsears18/lightwork` — epic #2711 and child #2714 were deferred entirely as `external-dependency` for lacking native-device access, even though a web slice was runnable via `functional-qa-auditor` against the repo's own staged audit accounts). This mirrors the operator-slice carve-out shipped in 4.1.25 for `needs-operator`-bound issues with a shippable code slice, applied here to verification-bound issues with an automatable audit slice instead. A new **QA-verification carve-out** in scope-preflight tests whether any stated acceptance criteria are checkable by an existing shipyard auditor against a reachable surface before deferring the whole issue, returning the ready shape with a new `verification_slice` / `verification_residual` pair (which auditor to run against what surface, and what stays un-automated on the SAME issue) instead of a whole-issue defer. On the worker side, `issue-work.md` gains a new `§6.6` "Verification disposition" that dispatches the named auditor, lets it file its own `bug` issues for real findings, posts a verification-status comment, and dispositions the issue (label + leave open, or close as verified) — **without opening a resolving PR**, since there's no code slice on this path. The structured-return schema's `disposition` enum gains a `verified` token to carry this outcome.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — new `verification_slice` / `verification_residual` fields on the ready-shape return; new "QA-verification carve-out" subsection with a worked example (the lightwork repro); scoping-agent prompt instruction and the ready-entry caching step updated to reference it.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — new "Verification-scope augmentation" (sibling to "Operator-residual augmentation") that renders the verification-dispatch instructions into the dispatch prompt when `verification_slice` is present.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — new Inputs entry for the "Verification slice" Context paragraph; new §6.6 "Verification disposition"; step 8 gains a `verified #<N> (bugs filed: <count>, residual: ...)` return line; new "Don't open a PR on a verification-slice dispatch" bullet.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — new reconcile bullet for the `verified #<N>` return (no PR, no `session_prs` append; label-and-leave-open or already-closed handling).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — new bullet cross-referencing the carve-out.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/worker-return.schema.json` and `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — `disposition` enum gains `verified`, kept in parity (verified via `check-worker-return-schema-parity.mjs`).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.25` → `4.1.26`.

### 4.1.25 — 2026-07-25

`/do-work` used to hand back a `needs-operator`-bound (or security-flavored `needs-human-review`-bound) issue wholesale even when most of the deliverable was ordinary, independently-shippable in-repo code and only a narrow operator/console step actually needed a human or browser (closes #851). Real repro on `mattsears18/lightwork`: config scaffolding deferred entirely because only its secret *values* needed provisioning; a regression-guard test deferred entirely because only the actual env-var removal was an operator step. The phase-1-slice split already existed for design/architecture obstacles ([#767](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/767)) and for provisioning-blocked scaffolding ([#628](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/628)) but had no counterpart for the operator/security-obstacle shape — this session applied the split by hand for issue #867 (PR #900: doc-honesty code slice shipped immediately, the security-setting residual handed back on the same issue via a comment, re-labeled `needs-human-review`, left OPEN) and promotes that pattern to an automated scope-preflight rule. A new **operator-slice carve-out** in scope-preflight tests for a code slice before deferring a `needs-operator`/security-flavored obstacle wholesale, returning the ready shape with `phase_1_scope` (the code) plus a new `operator_residual` / `operator_residual_security_sensitive` pair (what stays on the SAME issue, and which label it gets) instead of a whole-issue defer. On the worker side, `issue-work.md` gains a third `§5.85` non-close trigger shape and a new `§6.5` that ships the code slice without closing the dispatched issue, posts a disposition comment naming what shipped and what's handed back, and applies `needs-operator` (default) or `needs-human-review` (security-sensitive residual, per [#848](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/848)'s existing relabel rule) to keep the issue open and correctly surfaced.

### 4.1.22 — 2026-07-25

Drops the Haiku default for `fix-rebase` — the drain-phase rebase mode requires real conflict-resolution judgment, not just git mechanics, and Haiku gets it wrong (closes #854). A drain-phase Haiku `fix-rebase` worker hit a conflict where main's release-notes strategy had already been settled by a merged sibling PR 36 seconds before the target PR's last commit, misread the stale-collateral conflict as a live design decision, and bailed to a human with an incorrect claim. Re-dispatching the identical task on Sonnet resolved it cleanly. `fix-rebase` now pins Sonnet 5 — the same tier as `issue-work` — in the built-in `models.fix_rebase` default, the `shipyard:fix-rebase-worker` shim's frontmatter, and every prose routing table that documented the old Haiku pin; `fix-checks-only` (log pattern-matching, no comparable judgment fork) is unaffected and stays on Haiku. Also fixes the issue's secondary finding: `models.*` couldn't be set at the user-global layer via its canonical dot-path — `shipyard-config.sh set models.fix_rebase <id> --global` failed schema validation because only the `default_models` alias was accepted there, even though the merge logic already anticipated a coexisting `models` key. The user-config schema now accepts `models` directly, alongside `default_models`, with an explicit `models.<mode>` winning on conflict.

### 4.1.21 — 2026-07-25

Closes the visibility gap where the orchestrator's own `gh` token lacking the `workflow` OAuth scope silently blocked auto-merge with no signal at all, distinct from #812's earlier fix which only covered the worker-side path (closes #850). #812 taught a dispatched worker to detect the missing-`workflow`-scope GraphQL block, name it distinctly (`auto-merge: unavailable — gh token lacks workflow scope`), and feed a session-local `workflow_scope_blocked_prs` list that the end-of-session summary reports once. But four call sites where the *orchestrator itself* arms `--auto` directly — inline-trivial's fast path, the A.0.5 crash-recovery re-arm, setup-3c's orphan-recovery re-arm, and the release-train sweep — discarded the merge-arm call's stderr unconditionally (`2>/dev/null || true`, or no capture at all), so an orchestrator-armed PR that hit the identical block surfaced nothing, even though a worker-armed PR hitting the same block was already fully reported. Investigated and rejected the issue's alternate "delegate the arm to a worker" suggestion (documented in RATIONALE): a dispatched worker shares the orchestrator's own `gh` credentials on this host, so delegating wouldn't route around a genuinely missing scope — the fix is visibility, not delegation. All four sites now capture stderr, match the same GraphQL signature the worker-side fragment already uses, and feed the identical `workflow_scope_blocked_prs` list on a match.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/inline-trivial.md` — §E's `gated`-branch `--auto` call now captures stderr and detects the workflow-scope block.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — A.0.5 crash-recovery's `gated`-branch re-arm does the same.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — setup-3c's orphan-recovery re-arm does the same.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — the release-train sweep's arm does the same; the canonical "four orchestrator-turn call sites" paragraph now cross-references the #850 wiring.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — `workflow_scope_blocked_prs`'s orchestrator-state description documents the extended, five-call-site population path.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — new `#850 — why not delegate the arm to a worker` section.

### 4.1.20 — 2026-07-25

Shrinks `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — the hottest per-dispatch context file, loaded on every `issue-work` worker's prompt on top of the 43KB worker-preamble skill, multiplied by `--concurrency` (closes #885). Follows the pattern PR #911 (closes #884) established for the sibling `do-work/**` files: a new sibling `issue-work-RATIONALE.md` carries the extracted "why" (repro write-ups, historical drift narrative, multi-sentence "why this is a script call" essays), while the hot file keeps a short rule statement plus a `See [RATIONALE → ...]` link. Every existing heading is confirmed untouched (diffed against `origin/main` — zero renames or removals, so issue #866's 6 pre-existing stale anchors gained no company), and every new RATIONALE link was mechanically verified to resolve to a real heading via a GitHub-slug-compatible checker. The file is unusually dense with regression-pinned verbatim strings — 13 bash test suites (`scripts/tests/*.test.sh`) pin exact phrases across nearly every section, most saliently `parent-epic-leak-guard.test.sh`'s 33 assertions against §5.85 (a section rewritten by PR #902 two commits before this one started, per #893's three-tier escalation) — so §5.85, §2's prompt-injection defenses, and most of §7/§8's return-string categorization were left untouched as normative content inseparable from its provenance, rather than force-extracted. The full `scripts/tests/*.test.sh` suite (85 files) was run after every edit batch, not just at the end, to catch a pinned-phrase break immediately rather than at the end of a large diff. Net effect: issue-work.md 107,602 → 102,960 bytes (-4,642 bytes, ~4.3%), with ~5.1KB landing in the new RATIONALE file. This is well short of the issue's own 40–50KB estimate: unlike the six `do-work/**` files PR #911 covered, `issue-work.md`'s bulk turned out to be predominantly load-bearing procedure and safety-critical prompt-injection guidance (§0–§2) or recently-hardened, heavily-regression-tested guard logic (§4.4, §5.7–§5.9, §6.a, §7–§8) rather than decorative provenance — the extractable narrative was a genuinely smaller fraction of the file than the issue estimated.

### 4.1.17 — 2026-07-24

Fixes a TOCTOU race in `cost-history.sh flush`'s dedupe check where two concurrent orphan-sweeps flushing the SAME dead session id could both observe "no existing record" before either had appended, landing two session records for that session_id and silently double-counting its tokens/USD in every subsequent `/shipyard:cost report` (closes #861). The read-check-append sequence — and the reconcile rewrite's analogous race, where a concurrent `>>` append landing between the rewrite's snapshot-read and its `mv` would be silently discarded — is now serialized behind a cross-process lock scoped to the session ledger file. The lock is `mkdir`-based rather than `flock`: `flock` is Linux util-linux and is not installed by default on macOS/Darwin, the primary local execution environment for `/shipyard:do-work`, so depending on it would have silently broken local locking on exactly the host class this race was found on while appearing to work in CI. A lock held past a 30s staleness floor is assumed abandoned by a crashed owner and stolen rather than deadlocking every future flush forever, mirroring `worktree-reap.sh`'s own peer-lock staleness posture; a lock that can't be acquired within 15s now fails loudly with a new exit code 66 instead of hanging.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/cost-history.sh` — new `acquire_flush_lock` / `_dir_mtime_epoch` helpers (mkdir-based mutex with stale-lock stealing and a script-level `EXIT` trap for release on every return/exit path); `cmd_flush` acquires the lock immediately before the dedupe-check-then-append sequence and holds it through the reconcile-rewrite path; new exit code 66 documented in the module header.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/cost-history.test.sh` — new regression section firing 8 concurrent `flush --session-id` calls at the same session id and ledger, asserting exactly one session record and one set of per-issue records land (verified this reliably reproduces 8 duplicate records against an unlocked variant before the fix) and that no lock directory leaks after all racers exit.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.16` → `4.1.17`.

### 4.1.16 — 2026-07-24

Fixes the per-mode `/shipyard:do-work` dispatch prompt existing in two hand-synced copies with nothing enforcing the sync (closes #880) — the prompt-template half of the same drift shape #856 fixed for the worker-return schema. The reviewable prose template in `commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` (passed verbatim as the `Agent` tool's `prompt` under the default Agent-tool dispatch shape, #825) and the executing builder in `workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` (used under the alternate Workflow-substrate shape) are not byte-identical by design — the Workflow-substrate builders additionally inject a worktree-anchor block and a structured-return-contract closing neither the Agent-tool prose nor the runtime's filesystem-less execution environment can share — but the mode inventory, `agents/issue-worker/<mode>.md` spec-file references, and the four conditional augmentation paragraphs (verify-gate / user-feedback / phase-1-slice / next-available-version) must stay in sync, and a real drift had already happened: `buildIssueWorkPrompt`'s user-feedback preamble was missing dispatch-rules.md's trailing "the refinement step may have misread the user" clause. Also folds `buildIssueWorkPrompt`'s duplicate worktree-anchor "no worktreePath" CALLER-BUG guard onto the shared `worktreeAnchorLines` helper the other six builders already use (the "Internal dup" evidence #880 cited), generalizing the helper to include the issue number in its diagnostic when `unit.number` is set.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-dispatch-prompt-parity.mjs` — new dependency-free checker: extracts each mode's dispatch-rules.md blockquote template and workflow.js builder-function body (brace-balance scanning, no `eval`/`new Function`), renders the builder's template-literal segments as flowing prose (so a cosmetic source line-wrap can't split an anchor phrase), and diffs mode inventory, spec-file references, and augmentation-anchor presence.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — restores the missing "misread the user" clause; folds `buildIssueWorkPrompt`'s CALLER-BUG guard onto `worktreeAnchorLines` (generalized to include `unit.number` in the diagnostic when present); header comments on `buildWorkerPrompt` and the worktree-anchor helpers updated to point at the new mechanical parity check.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the "keep this template and the script's builder in sync" note now points at the checker and cites #880's already-found drift.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/dispatch-prompt-parity-880.test.sh` — new regression suite: asserts the real repo files match today, that the checker fails on a fixture reproducing the exact pre-fix drift, that it also catches mode-inventory / spec-file-rename / augmentation-deletion / worktree-anchor-dedup drift shapes, and that a cosmetic mid-sentence line-wrap does NOT false-fail. Auto-discovered by `tests.yml`'s `find plugins -name '*.test.sh'` — no separate CI wiring needed.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.15` → `4.1.16`.

### 4.1.15 — 2026-07-24

Fixes the `Workflow`-dispatch runtime's hand-copied `workerReturnSchema` literal being weaker than the canonical `schemas/worker-return.schema.json` it's meant to mirror (closes #856). `do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` can't `require`/`import` the canonical JSON file — the Dynamic Workflows runtime executes the script with no filesystem access of its own — so it carries a literal hand-copied duplicate instead, previously kept in sync only by a code-comment instruction to "review both files together." That drift had already happened: the `allOf` conditional-required block (a `blocked` outcome must carry `blocked_reason`; a `disposition` outcome must carry `disposition`) was missing entirely from the copy, and `issue`/`pr` were missing `minimum: 1`. Both gaps meant the workflow's embedded validator would silently accept malformed worker returns the canonical schema would reject — undermining the "fail loudly at the stage boundary" guarantee the schema-validation layer exists to provide. Both fields are now added to the `.js` copy, and a new dependency-free checker mechanically diffs the two schemas on every validation-relevant facet (`type`/`enum`/`minimum`/`required`/`additionalProperties`/`allOf` — `description` text is deliberately excluded, since the `.js` copy omits it) so a future drift fails CI instead of relying on a human noticing.

- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — `workerReturnSchema` gains the `allOf` block (mirroring the canonical schema's two conditional-required rules) and `minimum: 1` on `issue`/`pr`; header comment updated to point at the new mechanical parity check instead of "review both files together."
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-worker-return-schema-parity.mjs` — new dependency-free checker: parses the JS literal with a hand-rolled recursive-descent parser (no `eval`/`new Function`) and diffs it against the canonical JSON schema, reporting every field-level mismatch.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-return-schema-parity-856.test.sh` — new regression suite: asserts the real repo files match today, that the checker fails on a byte-for-byte reproduction of the pre-fix drift (naming the missing `allOf`/`minimum` in its diagnostic), and that it also catches enum/required/additionalProperties/missing-property/allOf-content drift shapes beyond the two #856 named — plus that `description`-only differences are correctly NOT flagged.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/README.md` — the schema-file table row now documents the mechanical parity check alongside the "keep in sync by hand" note.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.14` → `4.1.15`.

### 4.1.14 — 2026-07-24

Fixes `flake-enforce.sh`'s `apply-blocked-ci` action logging `apply-blocked-ci labeled` even when the `gh pr edit` (label) or `gh pr comment` call silently fails (closes #872). `do_apply_blocked_ci` — the per-PR side effect that labels a PR `blocked:ci` and posts an explanatory comment when a chronic flake is detected — discarded both `gh` calls' exit statuses with `|| true` and then printed the `apply-blocked-ci labeled ...` success line unconditionally as its last statement, so a rate limit, permission error, or network blip on either call left the PR silently unlabeled/uncommented while the stderr log actively claimed success — actively misleading a human scanning the log mid-incident ("why isn't this PR blocked yet, the log says it was labeled"), and with no per-row/per-PR counter anywhere in `cmd_enforce`'s calling loop to catch the drift either. `do_apply_blocked_ci` now captures each `gh` call's own exit status explicitly and only prints the `labeled` success line when both succeed; on any failure it prints a distinguishable `apply-blocked-ci partial-failure repo=... pr=... (label=<rc> comment=<rc>)` line instead, mirroring the exit-status-capture pattern #871/PR #903 applied to `shipyard-config.sh`'s `cmd_set`.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/flake-enforce.sh` — `do_apply_blocked_ci` captures `edit_status`/`comment_status` from the `gh pr edit`/`gh pr comment` calls instead of swallowing both with `|| true`, and only logs `apply-blocked-ci labeled` when both are zero; otherwise logs `apply-blocked-ci partial-failure ... (label=<rc> comment=<rc>)`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/flake-enforce.test.sh` — new regression section covering a `gh pr edit` failure and a `gh pr comment` failure independently, asserting the log never claims `labeled` on either failure and always emits the distinguishable `partial-failure` line with both exit statuses.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.13` → `4.1.14`.

### 4.1.13 — 2026-07-24

Fixes `shipyard-config.sh`'s `config set` reporting success even when the underlying write fails (closes #871). `cmd_set` piped the updated document into `atomic_write` and then unconditionally printed `wrote <path> = <value> to <target>` — the last statement in the function, so it also became the whole script invocation's exit code (0) — without ever inspecting `atomic_write`'s own exit status. `atomic_write` does have real failure paths (66 on a tempfile-write failure, 67 on a rename failure, each with its own stderr diagnostic), but a caller relying on `cmd_set`'s exit code or its confident "wrote ..." line (`/shipyard:config set`, the orchestrator, a human) had no reliable signal that a permission-denied or disk-full write silently left the policy field unchanged. `cmd_set` now reads `atomic_write`'s exit status via `PIPESTATUS[1]`, and on a non-zero status prints a `set: failed to write ...` diagnostic naming the failed status instead of the false-success line, and exits with that same status.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` — `cmd_set` checks `${PIPESTATUS[1]}` after the `atomic_write` pipeline; on failure it prints a `set: failed to write ...` diagnostic and exits with `atomic_write`'s own status instead of falling through to the unconditional `wrote ...` success line.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-config.test.sh` — new regression section simulating a write failure (chmod'd-unwritable `--repo` target directory) and asserting `cmd_set` exits 66, prints the failure diagnostic, never prints the false-success `wrote ...` line, and leaves no config file behind.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.12` → `4.1.13`.

### 4.1.12 — 2026-07-24

Fixes `issue-work.md` §5.85's (Post-PR-create non-close parent/epic leak verification) remediation being unreliable in a live dispatch (closes #893). A worker following §5.85 as previously written — rewrite the PR body to bare-URL form once, re-verify once — could be fooled: a live repro (`mattsears18/lightwork` issue #3024, PR #3055) showed a leaked `closingIssuesReferences` entry surviving a full body rewrite, a commit-message rewrite + force-push, and even a PR close+reopen, and only clearing once the PR was abandoned entirely for a fresh, neutrally-named branch — evidence pointing at the PR's `do-work/issue-<N>`-shaped branch name itself as a persistent auto-link vector independent of body/commit text. §5.85 now documents a three-tier escalation (body rewrite → commit-message rewrite → abandon-and-reopen-from-a-neutral-branch) that a worker must run in full before falling back to `needs-human-review`, plus a prevention callout: never name a deliberately-non-closing PR's branch `do-work/issue-<E>`-shaped in the first place. The step's scope also broadens from "parent/epic only" to cover a secondary/auxiliary PR that must not close the dispatched issue itself (the shape of the #893 repro).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — §5.85 rewritten: broadened "when this step applies" to cover the dispatched-issue-itself case, added a branch-naming prevention callout, and replaced the single rewrite-and-reverify remediation with a three-tier escalation (body → commit message → abandon/reopen-on-neutral-branch) plus matching "why tier 3 succeeds" rationale; the related "Don't" bullet section gains a companion bullet on the escalation + prevention.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/parent-epic-leak-guard.test.sh` — new regression assertions pinning the three-tier escalation language, the neutral-branch escape hatch, the branch-naming prevention callout, and that the old single-rewrite-only phrasing is gone.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.11` → `4.1.12`.

### 4.1.11 — 2026-07-24

Fixes `cost-history.sh`'s `flush --reconcile` rewrite being able to silently truncate the entire persistent cost-history ledger to an empty file (closes #869). The rewrite that drops a session's old ledger line(s) before re-appending the merged record piped `jq`'s filtered output straight into a tempfile via `jq ... > "$tmp" 2>/dev/null || true`, then unconditionally `mv`'d that tempfile over the ledger — so any `jq` failure (a compile error, or hitting a partial/malformed trailing line left mid-flight by a concurrent writer's `>>` append, the exact race window the surrounding comment already defended against for a *different* hazard) silently produced a 0-byte tempfile that the unchecked `mv` rendered permanent, wiping every session ever recorded with no stderr line, no exit code signal, and no audit trail. The fix mirrors the empty-tempfile guard `session-state.sh`'s `atomic_write` already carries for the same failure class (issue #357): `jq`'s real exit status is now captured instead of discarded, and only a genuine `jq` failure aborts the rewrite (leaving the ledger byte-for-byte untouched and printing a stderr diagnostic naming the session id + `jq`'s own error, with a non-zero exit so the caller sees the failure) — a `jq` exit-0 run that legitimately produces empty output (this session's record was the ledger's only content) still proceeds normally, so the common single-session case isn't regressed into a false failure.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/cost-history.sh` — `cmd_flush`'s reconcile-rewrite branch captures `jq`'s exit status via a proper `if ! jq ... ; then` guard (instead of `2>/dev/null || true`), aborting the rewrite with a stderr diagnostic + exit 68 on failure and cleaning up its tempfiles either way.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/cost-history.test.sh` — new regression test simulating the concurrent-writer race (a hand-appended malformed trailing JSON fragment) and asserting the reconcile call exits non-zero, surfaces a diagnostic, and leaves the ledger byte-for-byte unchanged with no leaked tempfiles.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.10` → `4.1.11`.

### 4.1.10 — 2026-07-24

Fixes a doc-honesty gap in the CODEOWNERS callout in `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md`: it held this repo up as "the reference example" for protecting `.shipyard/trusted-authors.txt` with CODEOWNERS, but the platform-level enforcement toggle (`require_code_owner_review` on the `main` branch ruleset, id `16669759`) was never actually turned on — a regression of the original ask in #139, whose acceptance criteria claimed this was verified (refs #867). The callout now says plainly that the CODEOWNERS file names the path but the ruleset toggle is off, gives the `gh api` one-liner to verify live, and links #867/#139 instead of implying the enforcement is active. This PR ships the doc-honesty slice only — flipping the ruleset itself is a repo-admin security/access-control change, out of scope for an autonomous `/do-work` worker; #867 stays open with `needs-human-review` pending that change.

### 4.1.9 — 2026-07-24

Fixes the `cost_tracking.enabled` / `cost_tracking.comment_on_pr` opt-out being documented across `CLAUDE.md`, `commands/cost.md`, and `commands/init.md` as a working control over the local `~/.shipyard/cost-history*.jsonl` ledger and the per-PR cost-tracking comment, while nothing downstream ever actually read either key (closes #855). The keys validated cleanly and landed at the correct effective-config path (the alias-remapping half of #403), but `session-state.sh`'s `bump-tokens`, `cost-history.sh`'s `flush`, and `steady-state.md`'s unconditional PR-comment post never consulted them — so `/shipyard:init --cost-tracking off` (or a direct `shipyard-config.sh set cost_tracking.enabled false --repo`) silently did nothing: every session still wrote a fresh line to the persistent ledger and still posted a `<!-- do-work-cost-tracking -->` token/cost comment to GitHub. The fix gates both write paths on the effective config, mirroring the pattern `flake_registry.enabled` already uses correctly: `cost-history.sh flush` now self-skips its ledger write (script-enforced, not just documented) when `cost_tracking.enabled: false`, failing open on a config-read error so the schema's `enabled: true` default is never silently defeated by a read failure; and `steady-state.md`'s shipped-return handling now checks `cost_tracking.comment_on_pr` before the `gh pr comment` call, independently of the ledger gate, so a user can keep the local ledger while suppressing the public PR comment (or vice versa). `cost_tracking.comment_on_issue` had no call site anywhere in the repo and no docs referencing it, so per the issue's acceptance criteria it's removed from the schema and the built-in defaults rather than wired to an invented one. Also fixes a related doc/implementation drift: `/shipyard:init --cost-tracking off` was documented to set both `cost_tracking.enabled` and `cost_tracking.comment_on_pr` but only ever wrote `enabled` — now that `comment_on_pr` is a functioning gate, the implementation matches the documented contract.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/cost-history.sh` — `cmd_flush` gates the ledger write on `cost_tracking.enabled` (fail-open on a config-read error), checked before touching the session file.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — the `shipped`-return PR-comment block reads `cost_tracking.comment_on_pr` and skips the `gh pr comment` post when false.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — the `bump-tokens`/cost-comment mandatory-attribution rule gains a carve-out documenting that `bump-tokens` itself still always runs, while the two downstream sinks (ledger flush, PR comment) are each independently config-gated.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json`, `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` — `cost_tracking.comment_on_issue` removed (no call site, no docs reference).
- `shipyard.config.json` (this repo's own committed config) — drops the now-removed `comment_on_issue` key so it still validates against the schema.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/init.md` — `--cost-tracking <on|off>` now writes both `cost_tracking.enabled` and `cost_tracking.comment_on_pr`, matching its documented contract.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/cost.md`, `CLAUDE.md` — Privacy / cost-tracking-ledger sections note the two independent, now-enforced opt-outs.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/cost-history.test.sh` — new assertions: `cost_tracking.enabled: false` suppresses both ledger writes; an explicit `true` still flushes normally (regression guard).

### 4.1.8 — 2026-07-24

Adds an explicit end-of-session `Operator queue — needs you (N):` block so a `/do-work` session that couldn't or didn't fully drive the browser hands the human an actionable to-do list instead of a routine-looking line (closes #849). Before this, when the operator layer never engaged (no reachable browser backend, `--dry-run`) or handed items back mid-session (a security/access-control carve-out, a logged-out console, a `paste-secret` tee-up, a second classifier denial, a `reply-comment` judgment call), those items were only visible as ordinary `needs-operator`/`needs-human-review` label state and a generic dispositioned mention — indistinguishable from work the agent chose to skip. Repro cited in the issue: an overnight/non-interactive `/do-work` run on `mattsears18/lightwork` left the maintainer unable to tell "stopped with work left" from "here are the N things that need you and why." The fix introduces a new session-local `operator_handbacks` ledger recording every operator-phase hand-back exit with its reason, unions it at end-of-session with anything still left in `operator_queue` (the no-backend / dry-run case, where nothing was ever popped), and renders the union as a visually-distinct block — printed right after the bucket breakdown, ahead of every routine flat line — with one entry per item (`<kind> <target> — <reason phrase> (<origin_ref>)`) plus the single command that drains it interactively (`/shipyard:my-turn` for the now-`needs-human-review` security/judgment items per #848, `/shipyard:do-work` foreground for anything still `needs-operator`). Omitted entirely when nothing needs the human, matching every other summary block's silence-by-default posture.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/orchestrator-state-reference.md` — new `operator_handbacks` cold orchestrator-state struct (reason enum, writers, and the cleanup-summary reader), struct count bumped 9 → 10.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/02-execution-and-playbooks.md` — every operator-playbook hand-back exit (`toggle-setting`/`console-action` logged-out and security-carve-out branches, `paste-secret`, `reply-comment` judgment call, `verify`'s logged-out/discrepancy branches) now appends to `operator_handbacks`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/01-queue-and-authorization.md` — the second-classifier-denial hand-back path also appends to `operator_handbacks` (`reason: "denied-by-classifier"`), alongside its existing `operator_denials` entry.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate.md` — Degradation (no-backend) section cross-references the new block and documents the synthetic `no-browser-backend` / `dry-run` reasons for items never popped off `operator_queue`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new `Operator queue — needs you (N):` block (source data, reason-phrase table, drain-command table, omit-when-empty rule, ordering relative to the bucket breakdown) and a corresponding sub-list in the on-disk HTML report's "User-action follow-ups" section.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — hot/cold struct-count references updated (nine → ten cold structures, twenty-one → twenty-two total) to include `operator_handbacks`.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.7` → `4.1.8`.

### 4.1.7 — 2026-07-24

Fixes a gap where a security/access-control-class `needs-operator` hand-back was invisible to both `/do-work` and `/my-turn` (closes #848). The operator phase's `toggle-setting`/`console-action` playbook correctly refuses to auto-drive a security mutation (password policy, MFA/2FA, OAuth redirect URIs, IAM roles/bindings, sharing/member permissions, etc.) and tees it up as a hand-back — but until now it left the issue on `needs-operator`, and `/my-turn` deliberately filters `needs-operator` out of its walked human-only queue (pointer-only there, since most `needs-operator` items genuinely are `/do-work`-drivable in a future session). The result: a security-class item was hand-back-from-`/do-work` *and* filtered-out-of-`/my-turn`, landing on neither loop's actionable queue — confirmed by an overnight non-interactive `/do-work` run on `mattsears18/lightwork` where five security-adjacent `needs-operator` issues (GCP Maps key referrer restriction, Vercel env removal, Vercel/EAS secret values, two ASC/Play privacy console forms) sat untouched despite `/my-turn` having reported nothing outstanding beforehand. The fix: the security/access-control hand-back branch now relabels the issue `needs-operator` → `needs-human-review` (ensuring the label exists first) instead of leaving it on `needs-operator` — `needs-human-review` is the label `/my-turn` walks individually, so the item gets exactly one human-facing destination. No changes were needed to `/my-turn` itself; it already walks `needs-human-review` unconditionally.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/02-execution-and-playbooks.md` — the `toggle-setting`/`console-action` step-4 security hand-back branch now relabels `needs-operator` → `needs-human-review` (with the idempotent `gh label create` + `gh issue edit --add-label --remove-label` pattern) instead of leaving the item on `needs-operator`; posts the hand-back detail as an issue comment.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/03-error-handling-and-safety.md` — Safety trust-boundary bullet notes the relabel and why `needs-operator` alone strands the item.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate/01-queue-and-authorization.md` — Degradation note clarifies that an entirely-security operator queue now ends up on `needs-human-review`, individually walkable via `/my-turn`, not silently parked on `needs-operator`.
- `CLAUDE.md` — `needs-operator` label-conventions entry documents the relabel-on-security-hand-back behavior.

### 4.1.6 — 2026-07-24

Adds a `write-probe.md` fragment to `shipyard:worker-preamble` documenting a step-0 write-capability probe, and updates `dispatch-rules.md`'s Workflow-substrate section to cite the finding (closes #895). Issue #895 found the harness's `Edit`/`Write` bg-isolation write guard rejects every write from a `Workflow`-substrate-dispatched worker in **both** possible orchestrator isolation states, not just the one #825 tested: with the parent orchestrator unisolated every write is refused outright, and with the parent isolated via `EnterWorktree` (the current step-0.5 default) the guard doesn't refuse cleanly — it silently redirects the worker's `Edit`/`Write` calls to the *orchestrator's own* worktree instead of the worker's `worktreePath`. Neither state is fixable from inside a worker dispatch, and a `Bash`-write workaround (writing files via `Bash` instead of `Edit`/`Write`, since the guard only covers the latter) is not sanctioned either — the dispatch instructing it was independently refused by the harness's own permission classifier as an isolation-guard bypass. Since no in-repo fix exists for the underlying harness behavior, this closes the worker-side pre-flight probe PR #830 explicitly deferred out of scope: a worker now runs a real `Write` call against a scratch file in its own worktree immediately after the step-0 cwd fail-fast, before any other work, and returns `blocked:` immediately on rejection instead of implementing the whole fix and only discovering the write-block at the final commit (issue #895's repro: ~150k tokens on one such wasted dispatch). The probe is a no-op under the default `Agent`-tool dispatch shape (`isolation: "worktree"`, per #830) — it is load-bearing only for the documented `Workflow`-substrate alternate shape.

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/write-probe.md` — new fragment: the two harness error signatures, the probe procedure, the `blocked:` return string, and scope relative to the #486 cwd fail-fast.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — new pointer sentence in the step-0 cwd fail-fast section plus a new on-demand-fragments index row, both linking to `write-probe.md`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — Workflow-substrate section documents the confirmed catch-22 across both orchestrator isolation states and points workers at the new probe.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — regression coverage: fragment exists, SKILL.md's index links it, the step-0 pointer sentence is present, and the fragment carries both harness error signatures plus the `blocked:` return string.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.5` → `4.1.6`.

### 4.1.5 — 2026-07-24

Restores CHANGELOG/git version-sequence integrity by investigating and documenting the 4.0.8 gap (closes #862) and the suspected 4.0.7/4.0.9 out-of-order pair (closes #864). Both were filed by the same `release-readiness` audit run and share a root cause — the version-coordination scheme used while multiple sibling PRs land concurrently — so they're resolved together. Findings, backed by a full `plugin.json` git-blame walk from 1.0.0 through the current release cross-checked against every `### <version>` heading in this file: (1) 4.0.8 was never shipped under any PR — it was a provisional reservation abandoned when its claimant (the #817 PR) found the slot already taken by the time it actually merged, so it recomputed to 4.0.10; no content is missing, only the number. (2) The 4.0.9-before-4.0.7 claim in #864 does not hold — `git merge-base --is-ancestor` confirms PR #820's merge (cutting 4.0.7) is a direct ancestor of PR #819's merge (cutting 4.0.9), so the actual merge order was 4.0.7 then 4.0.9, monotonically increasing; the audit conflated PR-*creation*-number order (#819 opened before #820) with PR-*merge* order (#820 landed first). Both findings are recorded as a note in this file directly below the 4.0.9 entry, next to the gap they explain. No CI enforcement is added here — issue #839 (shipped in 4.1.0) already added `release-bump-required.test.sh`, which independently guards future version-sequence regressions.

- `CHANGELOG.md` — new note documenting the 4.0.8 reservation-not-loss finding and the 4.0.7/4.0.9 merge-order verification, inserted directly after the 4.0.9 entry.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.4` → `4.1.5`.

### 4.1.4 — 2026-07-24

Adds `.github/dependabot.yml` configured for the `github-actions` package ecosystem with a weekly update schedule (closes #853). The repo has no runtime package manifest (no `package.json`/`pyproject.toml`/`go.mod` — this is a bash + markdown Claude Code plugin), but six workflows (`conflict-markers.yml`, `secret-scan.yml`, `label-event-audit.yml`, `tests.yml`, `shellcheck.yml`) pin `actions/checkout` and `gitleaks/gitleaks-action` by tag or SHA with no prior automated mechanism to bump those pins, so security patches and bug fixes would otherwise age indefinitely. Found by the `dx` audit on 2026-07-24.

- `.github/dependabot.yml` — new. `github-actions` ecosystem, `/` directory, weekly cadence, `shipyard` label, `chore(deps)` commit-message prefix.
