### 4.1.3 — 2026-07-23

Adds a "Never `git stash`" section to `skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — worker worktrees isolate *checkouts*, but `git stash` is not per-worktree: every stash entry lives under `refs/stash` in the shared `.git-common-dir`, so concurrent workers running in separate worktrees see and can pop the same shared stack (closes #845). A bare `git stash pop` always takes `stash@{0}` regardless of who pushed it, so on a `--concurrency > 1` session a worker can silently commit + push a different, still-running worker's in-progress changes into its own PR (contamination), or `git restore` them away as "unrelated junk" (data loss) — both worse than the worktree-reap hazard in #838/#841 because there is no completion notification to inspect and no leftover directory to recover from. The new section forbids `git stash` by default and states the `refs/stash`-is-repo-global mechanism so the prohibition is understood rather than merely obeyed; documents the isolating fallback shape (`git stash push -m "<agent-id>: <reason>"`, then pop only via `git stash list` → match-on-message → `git stash apply <ref>` → `git stash drop <ref>`, never a bare `pop`); and gives dirty-worktree guidance for step 0 (surface unexpected pre-existing changes rather than silently stashing them onto the shared stack, and `git restore` only the specific paths the worker itself is about to touch). A repo-wide grep for `git stash` turned up no other instructional occurrence in any agent spec, dispatch template, or skill — the one other hit (`hooks/tests/guard-primary-checkout.test.sh`) is a regression-test fixture asserting the primary-checkout write guard blocks `git stash`/`git stash pop` against the primary checkout, unrelated to this worker-facing hazard, so no change was needed there. `scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` gained regression assertions covering the section heading, the `refs/stash`/`git-common-dir` mechanism, the isolating push/apply/drop shape, the bare-pop prohibition, and the dirty-worktree guidance.

### 4.1.2 — 2026-07-23

Rewrites `setup/00-config-worktree.md` step 0.5 so the orchestrator relocates into its own worktree by calling the `EnterWorktree` tool directly, instead of a raw `git worktree add "$ORCH_WT" "origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH"` plus a `cd`/`-C` convention (closes #844). The raw-git form satisfies `Bash` — the shell genuinely operates in the new directory — but never registers the session as isolated with the harness; on a background-job session the harness's write guard then refuses every subsequent `Edit`/`Write`, including the orchestrator's own release-process bump and CHANGELOG edit, and the failure surfaces late, only at the first `Edit` call after the rest of setup has already run. This is the orchestrator-side instance of the harness-isolation gap #825 documented on the worker side (fixed there via `Agent`-tool `isolation: "worktree"` dispatch, restored as the default in #830) — the orchestrator has no dispatcher to hand isolation to, so it now calls the isolating tool itself. `EnterWorktree` creates the new worktree on a branch (`worktree-orchestrator-<session-id>`) rather than the raw form's detached HEAD; the rewrite documents this as a deliberate choice rather than an accident of mechanism. The raw-git form stays documented as a fallback for environments where `EnterWorktree` is unavailable, with a note that writes may be refused there. The reuse path (a prior session's `orchestrator-<session-id>` directory already on disk) is preserved via `EnterWorktree`'s `path` argument, followed by the same fetch + reset-to-origin-tip refresh the fallback form already does. The worktree path itself is unchanged (`.claude/worktrees/orchestrator-<session-id>`), so downstream consumers — `worktree-reap.sh derive-session-id`'s newest-by-mtime glob (#513), the step-1.6.5 orphan-orchestrator sweep (#280), the `.shipyard-session-id` stash convention (#365), and cleanup-summary's step-6 reap of the orchestrator's own worktree — are unaffected.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — step 0.5 rewritten: `EnterWorktree` primary path (new-worktree and reuse-via-`path` forms), raw-git fallback retained with an isolation caveat, explicit branch-vs-detached-HEAD callout.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.1` → `4.1.2`.

### 4.1.1 — 2026-07-23

Adds a `shipyard:worker-preamble` subsection covering the case where the `Bash` tool's own 600s foreground cap silently moves an overrunning command to the background and hands control back — the exact state the #529 "never arm a background process and return" rule forbids, reached through no choice of the worker's, since #529 itself mandates blocking on a foreground call for a long wait in the first place (closes #829). Without guidance a worker reads its own situation as a #529 breach and improvises: a narrative return, a blind re-run, or reaching for `Monitor`/`run_in_background` because the harness's own Auto Mode guard (which blocks a naive `sleep N; tail …` retry) reads that way — the concrete repro was two emulator-backed Jest runs on `mattsears18/lightwork` auto-backgrounded past the cap under host contention, with the worker having to invent a workaround under pressure. The new section names the auto-backgrounded state as sanctioned rather than a violation, prescribes the re-block pattern verbatim (`until [ -s "<output-file>" ]; do sleep 15; done; cat "<output-file>"` — itself a foreground, self-heartbeating, turn-blocking call that satisfies #529/#707 and is the "wait for a condition" shape the harness guard actually steers toward), states the three anti-patterns explicitly (no narrative status, no blind re-run, no reflexive `Monitor` per #753), and notes pre-splitting a known-long suite as optional insurance without treating runtime estimation as reliable under host contention. It's the worker-side half of the #829/#838 pair — #841's orchestrator-side "never reap a stalled worker before inspecting it" contract is the downstream consequence if a worker stops anyway; this section is what keeps a worker from stopping in the first place, and the two now cross-reference each other from both directions.

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — new `## A foreground call the harness auto-backgrounds past 600s` section after the Return-contract discipline bullet list (269 lines total, under the 300-line thin-core cap); the pre-existing #529 bullet's #829 parenthetical now points forward to the new section.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — new assertion block (8 checks) covering the section heading, the sanctioned-not-a-breach framing, the verbatim re-block pattern, all three anti-patterns, the host-contention framing, and the worker-side/#838 pairing language.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.1.0` → `4.1.1`.

### 4.1.0 — 2026-07-23

Adds `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/release-bump-required.test.sh`, a CI gate that fails a PR when its diff touches `plugins/**` but `plugin.json`'s `.version` was not bumped strictly higher than the merge-base's value (closes #839). The rule — "always cut a release when a PR merges" — was previously prose only in `CLAUDE.md`'s Release process section, honored by convention with nothing to enforce it; three consecutive commits on `main` (`ce7a577`, `cf8771b`, `bb31052`) all carried `plugin.json` version `4.0.3` even though `bb31052` fixed a total dispatch outage, making the fix undeliverable to any installation already on `4.0.3` until a later, unrelated PR happened to bump the version (the #835 user report). The guard is a single test file — no CI workflow edit, since the repo's existing `find plugins -type f -name '*.test.sh'` discovery in `tests.yml` already picks it up, and workers are hard-blocked from editing workflow definitions. It fetches its own base ref and progressively deepens (bounded escalating `--deepen` + `--depth` fetches, then a last-resort `--unshallow`) to cope with `actions/checkout`'s default shallow, single-commit clone, and SKIPS (never hard-fails) when the base still cannot be resolved after that — a guard that blocks every PR the moment that assumption breaks would be worse than no guard. Version comparison is version-aware (`sort -V`) and requires *strictly greater*, so an accidental downgrade is caught alongside a missing bump. It also asserts the bumped version has a matching `### <version> — <date>` CHANGELOG heading, composing with (not duplicating) the existing `changelog-monotonicity-scan.sh` (#555) so a release that deletes a prior heading is caught too. An escape hatch (`[no-release]` in the commit range's messages, or the `RELEASE_BUMP_SKIP_LABEL` env override for a future PR-label wiring) covers the legitimate no-release case. Proven against the actual motivating violation: run live against the real `bb31052` / `bb31052~1` range via a scratch worktree, the guard correctly reports `FAIL: ... .version was not bumped (base=4.0.3, head=4.0.3)`. Also proven against a genuinely shallow `git clone --depth=1` over a local `file://` remote (no network required) to exercise the fetch+deepen recovery path end-to-end, not just the base-resolved happy path. Written for bash 3.2 (macOS's shipped bash, not just CI's modern bash) — no associative arrays, no `mapfile`, no `${var,,}` — per the portability floor an earlier PR in this session tripped over.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/release-bump-required.test.sh` — new. The guard itself (`rbr_check` / `rbr_resolve_merge_base` / `rbr_version_gt`) plus its own regression suite: a live self-check against this PR's actual diff, a real-history regression pin against `bb31052`, and 9 synthetic fixture scenarios (clean bump, no-bump violation, downgrade, missing CHANGELOG heading, not-applicable, both escape hatches, unresolvable-base skip, and a real fetch-and-deepen shallow-clone reproduction over a local bare repo).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.20` → `4.1.0`.

### 4.0.20 — 2026-07-23

Specifies a single shared orchestrator contract for a worker that stops without a terminal return — never reap its worktree before inspecting it for unpushed work, regardless of whether the stop is a `status: completed` dispatch with pending-intent narrative text ("Waiting for the background test run to complete") or a `status: failed` stall-watchdog kill ("no progress for 600s") (closes #838, closes #833). Both triggers funnel through `steady-state.md` step A.0.5's existing inspect-before-reap flow, which is now explicit about three things it previously left implicit or under-specified: (1) a harness-reported `status: failed` routes through the flow **unconditionally**, independent of what the notification's own return text says, closing the gap the #813-era terminal-prefix check left for a watchdog kill whose accompanying text happens to look terminal; (2) the resume mechanism now prefers `SendMessage` against the stalled agent's own `agent_id` (preserving its transcript and worktree in one step, validated live this session against worker #826 → PR #834) for a live `Agent`-tool subagent, falling back to a fresh `agent()` call into the same worktree/branch only when no addressable agent exists (the default `Workflow`-substrate dispatch shape, which has no resume primitive) — and either way the resume message now carries the orchestrator's own freshly-read git state (commits, dirty paths, manifest version, existing PR) rather than trusting the stalled worker's last self-report; (3) the resume retry cap drops from 2 to 1 per target per session, with a second stall handing back to the existing crash-recovery salvage path (which still auto-commits and opens a PR for any recoverable work) rather than looping. Every occurrence — resumed, handed-back, or dropped-clean (nothing recoverable, same as today's dispatch-refused shape) — is now recorded in a new session-local `stalled_dispatches` ledger, mirroring `dispatch_denials` (#718) and `operator_denials` (#746), and surfaced in the end-of-session summary. None of these paths is `blocked` and none applies a `blocked:*` label — that classification stays reserved for a worker's own deliberate, terminal `blocked: <reason>` return.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — step A.0.5: new consolidation intro naming the three triggers (#813/#838 pending-intent, #358 genuine crash, #833 harness-failed) and their shared detect → inspect → (resume | recover-and-reap) flow; the terminal-prefix check now short-circuits to non-terminal on `harness_status == "failed"` regardless of return text; the resume path is rewritten to bound at 1 attempt, require the orchestrator's own pre-resume `git log` / `git status` / PR-existence read, and branch on `SendMessage`-to-`agent_id` vs. a fresh same-worktree `agent()` call depending on dispatch shape; new `stalled_dispatches` ledger appends at the resume-dispatched point (`outcome: "resumed"`) and at the crash-recovery fallback's existing wasted-dispatch-accounting site (`outcome: "handed-back"` or `"dropped-clean"`, discriminated by whether pre-reap salvage recovered a PR).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/orchestrator-state-reference.md` — new `stalled_dispatches` cold-state entry (ninth in the cold list); header count updated eight → nine.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — hot/cold struct-list pointer prose updated to name `stalled_dispatches` and the new eight → nine / twenty → twenty-one counts.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new `Stalled dispatches (#838/#833):` end-of-session summary line (omitted when the ledger is empty) plus its per-line rendering rule.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — new bullet: don't reap a worker's worktree on a non-terminal or `failed` stop without first inspecting it for unpushed work.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — one-line cross-reference from the #529 backgrounding rule to #829 (the upstream harness force-backgrounding cause, worked separately) and to this PR's orchestrator-side recovery contract.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.19` → `4.0.20`.

### 4.0.19 — 2026-07-23

Replaces the step-3b orphan-agent-worktree sweep's per-worktree `classify-lock` subprocess loop with a single bulk classification pass and bounds the sweep to a configurable per-session cap, closing the O(n)-subprocess timeout a 60-worktree/90GB backlog produced before classifying a single candidate (closes #836). Two new `worktree-reap.sh` subcommands are the single executable source of truth: `classify-all` reads every `agent-*` worktree's lock file and resolves liveness for the WHOLE batch with exactly one `ps` snapshot, one self-ancestor walk, and one batched `stat` — instead of forking `classify-lock` (and its own internal `ps`/`stat` calls) once per worktree — and emits its output pre-sorted oldest-first by worktree-directory mtime; `reap-stale` layers the bounded, checkpointed removal on top, reaping at most `worktree_reap.max_per_session` (new config key, default 10) reap-eligible worktrees per session, deferring peer-alive ones without counting them against the cap, and leaving anything beyond the cap untouched on disk — the on-disk backlog itself is the checkpoint, so a session that can't clear the whole backlog still makes forward, monotonic progress and a later session's sweep continues from where this one stopped, with no separate state file to maintain. `commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md`'s step 3b now calls `reap-stale` directly (keeping the issue-#832 in-flight guard, which still runs first via `--exclude-agent-id`) and prints a `[setup-3b] summary: reaped=<R> deferred=<D> unreaped=<U> remaining=<REMAIN>` line so the backlog is visible instead of silent, plus a new session-start advisory (`worktree_reap.warn_threshold`, default 20) naming the `agent-*` count and reclaimable disk size when the backlog is large enough to matter. Investigated whether the issue's third suggested fix applied — confirmed it does NOT: step B's per-completion reap and A.1's shipped-immediate reap both already derive the harness's exact `agent-<agentId>` worktree path from the dispatched slot's own `agent_id` (the same task-id the harness names the `isolation: "worktree"` directory after), so same-session completions are already reaped correctly; the accumulated backlog this issue reports is residue from crashed/killed sessions and worktrees predating those per-completion-reap fixes, which only the cross-session step-3b sweep ever revisits — exactly the sweep this PR fixes. Also makes explicit in `dont.md` that a harness-created worktree's branch name is never a liveness signal: it sits on `worktree-agent-<agentId>` until the dispatched worker cuts its own `do-work/issue-<N>` branch, so a name-based reap filter has a race window that can delete an in-flight agent — the concrete hazard the issue's manual workaround hit.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/worktree-reap.sh` — new `classify-all` subcommand (bulk classification, O(1) subprocess calls) and `reap-stale` subcommand (bounded, checkpointed sweep built on `classify-all`, reusing the existing `reap` action's fast-remove + audit-log transaction). Both are bash-3.2-compatible throughout (no associative arrays — parallel indexed arrays and sentinel-delimited-string membership checks instead), matching the rest of this file's portability floor.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — step 3b's canonical reap loop replaced with a `reap-stale` call (in-flight guard preserved via `--exclude-agent-id`), a one-line reaped/deferred/remaining summary, and the new threshold-warning banner.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — new bullet: branch name is never a liveness signal, with the #836 two-live-workers repro.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` / `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` — new `worktree_reap.max_per_session` (default 10) and `worktree_reap.warn_threshold` (default 20) config keys.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-reap.test.sh` — 30 new assertions (tests 90–104) covering `classify-all`'s bulk classification (mixed batch, oldest-first ordering, staleness, bad usage, empty layouts) and `reap-stale`'s bounded/checkpointed behavior (dry-run, cap enforcement + oldest-first removal, the in-flight exclude guard, peer-alive not counting against the cap, forward-progress across two successive capped runs, bad usage).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-config.test.sh` — new assertions for the two `worktree_reap.*` config keys' defaults and schema validation.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.18` → `4.0.19`.

### 4.0.18 — 2026-07-23

Makes `.in_flight` membership the authoritative liveness check ahead of `classify-lock`'s lock-PID heuristic at every sweep-style agent-worktree reap site, closing the mid-dispatch worktree-destruction hazard `classify-lock`'s `dead` classification exposes for a just-dispatched worker (closes #832). The harness can write a lock file whose PID is already gone — an intermediate spawn-time process, not the long-lived agent — for a worker dispatched only seconds earlier and actively running; `classify-lock` has no way to tell that apart from a genuinely-abandoned lock and reports `dead` (reap-eligible) either way. The session that filed this issue reproduced it live: a setup-3b-style sweep classified a worker's worktree as `dead` immediately after dispatch, and the orchestrator avoided destroying it only because it happened to have the live `agent_id` in working memory — nothing in the spec required that exclusion. Every reap loop that walks `.git/worktrees/agent-*` (setup 3b's canonical implementation, dispatch-rules §2d, drain's pre-dispatch reap, and A.1's shipped-immediate reap) now snapshots this session's `.in_flight` agent-ids and skips a matching worktree outright, before `classify-lock` is ever consulted — in-flight membership is authoritative; the lock file is only a fallback for worktrees this session doesn't currently own. The three single-target reaps that operate on a slot's own just-returned worktree (A.0.5's crash-recovery reap, step B's per-completion release) are deliberately exempted with inline rationale, since at the exact point those run the reaping slot's own entry is still nominally present in `.in_flight` — the reap *is* the release, so an in-flight-membership skip there would be the opposite bug. The optional helper-side fix (treating a dead-PID lock with a freshly-created worktree directory as `unknown` rather than `dead`) was evaluated and not implemented — it would flip the expected classification in several existing `classify-lock` tests that construct their fixtures inside a just-created `mktemp -d` directory, which is exactly the "destabilizes existing callers/tests" case the issue said to avoid it for.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — step 3b's canonical reap loop (also referenced by `setup/01-repo-recovery.md`'s pointer) snapshots `.in_flight` agent-ids and skips a match before calling `classify-lock`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — §2d's pre-dispatch head-branch reap gets the same in-flight guard, belt-and-braces alongside its existing `$head_ref` branch-match narrowing.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — the drain-phase pre-dispatch head-branch reap gets the same in-flight guard.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — A.1's shipped-immediate reap gets the same in-flight guard; A.0.5's crash-recovery reap and step B's per-completion release each gain an inline comment explaining why an in-flight-membership skip does NOT apply there (they target their own just-returned slot's worktree, whose `.in_flight` entry is still present precisely because the reap is the release).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — strengthens the "Don't reap a live-PID worktree" bullet to name in-flight membership as the primary, authoritative check, with `classify-lock`'s lock-PID check demoted to a documented fallback for worktrees the session doesn't own.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — the matching rationale section gets the full #832 repro and the in-flight-first ordering explanation, cross-referenced from the `dont.md` bullet.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-reap.test.sh` — new regression-pin test (2a): a dead-PID lock inside a freshly-created worktree-metadata directory still classifies `dead`, documenting the known limitation the orchestrator-side in-flight guard (not `classify-lock` itself) is what actually prevents from being acted on destructively.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.17` → `4.0.18`.

### 4.0.17 — 2026-07-23

Extracts the worktree-vs-primary-checkout cwd predicate (`git rev-parse --git-dir` == `--git-common-dir`) out of duplicated prose/generated-text into one script, `scripts/assert-worktree-cwd.sh`, and points every call site at it (closes #826). The bug half — the compound `if` + command-substitution snippet the harness's Auto Mode classifier refuses to run as a single `Bash` call — was already fixed on `main` by decomposing it into three separate plain `git rev-parse` calls (#802); this closes the enhancement #826 rescoped to, which is that decomposition left the comparison logic duplicated wherever the guard is invoked, exactly the "prose duplicated in two places drifts" problem #716 hit with the ungated-merge rule before extracting it into `detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh`. The script's semantics are unchanged from the pre-existing inline predicate (`blocked:`, not `reaped:`; git-dir == git-common-dir ⇒ primary checkout) and it re-resolves `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` via the established idempotent preamble (#354) before every invocation, since Bash-tool subprocess env vars don't persist across calls. Sweeping the repo for other copies of the same compound snippet turned up two more in `workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — the `Workflow`-substrate dispatch prompt's own worktree-anchor instruction, generated independently by `worktreeAnchorLines` (six modes) and `buildIssueWorkPrompt` (issue-work) — now unified behind one new `worktreeAnchorCheckLines` helper that both call, closing the drift risk there too rather than merely reducing it.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/assert-worktree-cwd.sh` — new script, the single executable source of truth for the predicate. Accepts an optional `DIR` argument (defaults to cwd) so both the step-0 and mid-session call sites can use it; prints a terse `worktree`/`primary`/`error` verdict on stdout (exit 0/1/2) with full diagnostics on stderr, mirroring `detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh`'s stdout-terse/stderr-diagnostic convention. The exact `blocked:` wording stays the caller's job — the two call sites use different phrasing for the same underlying signal, so the script is a pure predicate, not a message generator.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/assert-worktree-cwd.test.sh` — new test suite covering the script's pass/fail/error paths, path-independence (worktree/primary subdirectories), the default-to-cwd behavior, `--help`, and a shellcheck-clean check.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — § "Step-0 cwd fail-fast" and § "Mid-session cwd anchoring" both now invoke the script (with the `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` preamble as its own separate `Bash` call) as the primary path, keeping the pre-#826 three-plain-calls form documented as the fallback for an installed plugin predating this change.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — new shared `worktreeAnchorCheckLines(worktreePath)` helper generating the same script-invocation text (as three separate fenced code blocks, not one compound block) for both `worktreeAnchorLines` and `buildIssueWorkPrompt`, which previously each hand-maintained their own copy of the compound snippet.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.16` → `4.0.17`.

### 4.0.16 — 2026-07-23

Rewords the `do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` zero-work-unit throw's closing remediation sentence, which told the caller to "pass actual JSON values, not a JSON-encoded string" — advice that does nothing, because `args` was observed arriving JSON-stringified in both a pre-#817-fix and a post-#817-fix live dispatch even when the caller passed an actual JSON object in the `Workflow` tool call (closes #823). The remediation now points at the one thing actually worth checking — that `args.issues` is a non-empty array of work units, each carrying at least `mode` and `worktreePath` — and explicitly says re-encoding the call as a literal object will not by itself fix a genuinely empty or malformed `issues` array. The script header and `dispatch-rules.md`'s substrate section are updated to document the observed contract plainly: `args` may arrive JSON-stringified regardless of how it was passed, so the guarded `JSON.parse` step is required handling for the normal delivery shape, not defensive politeness for a hypothetical caller typo — a future refactor must not remove it. The received-shape diagnostic itself (`typeof args`, array-ness, length, top-level keys, parse-failure detail) is unchanged, per the issue's explicit ask to keep it — it is what made the bug diagnosable in the first place. #821's throwing behavior, the zero-unit guard logic, and the test suite's assertions are unchanged; this is a wording + documentation correction only.

- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — the thrown zero-unit `Error`'s closing sentence reworded; the file-header "`args` HANDLING MUST FAIL LOUDLY" section and its "Three properties" list annotated with the #823 observed-contract note.

### 4.0.15 — 2026-07-23

Restores the `Agent`-tool dispatch shape (`subagent_type: shipyard:<mode>-worker` + `isolation: "worktree"`) as the documented default for the seven code-writing `mode:` values, demoting the `Workflow`-tool substrate from "the one and only dispatch mechanism" (per #791) to a documented alternate shape (closes #825). A controlled retest on the `Workflow` substrate found every `Edit`/`Write` call refused with a harness-level "parent bg session hasn't isolated yet" error — reproduced identically whether the parent orchestrator session was itself isolated or not — showing the `worktreePath` pre-provisioning contract #791 introduced cannot convey worktree ownership to the harness's write-guard for a `Workflow`-dispatched worker. Since `Workflow`-substrate dispatch could not perform a single file write, shipyard's autonomous code-writing loop was non-functional through its documented path; restoring the `Agent`-tool shape as the default requires no upstream harness change and the path is demonstrably working (every PR in the session that filed this issue landed via a hand `Agent` dispatch against a retained mode shim). Per #791's own `additionalProperties: false` config schema, the `dispatch.substrate` knob is **not** reintroduced — the choice between the two shapes is spec-level, not configurable. ### 4.0.12 — 2026-07-23

Adds a proactive, one-time session-start warning when the `gh` token is missing the `workflow` OAuth scope and the session shape suggests workflow-touching work is likely (closes #818). This is the proactive half of the two-part fix for GitHub blocking `enablePullRequestAutoMerge` on a PR touching `.github/workflows/*` without that scope — #812 landed the *reactive* half a few entries below (4.0.7), where a worker discovers the gap only at the first failed `gh pr merge --auto` call. This PR's new setup-phase preflight step surfaces the same finding *before* the first workflow-touching PR is even opened: it reads `gh auth status`'s scopes once (cached alongside the existing repo-config preflight reads), and — only when the scope is genuinely missing — probes two cheap signals (an open issue/PR referencing `.github/workflows/`, or the default branch's most recent CI run having failed) to decide whether to print the warning. Silent by default on the common case (scope present, or nothing suggests workflow-touching work this session). The warning reuses `auto-merge: unavailable — gh token lacks workflow scope` — #812's own landed reactive-path token, read from `origin/main` after it merged mid-session — verbatim, so the proactive and reactive halves share one vocabulary.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/detect-missing-workflow-scope.sh` (new) — the single-source-of-truth decision script. A pure `--decide <has_workflow_scope> <workflow_signal>` mode is unit-testable with no network I/O; the live mode reads `gh auth status` plus the two cheap signals and fails toward `silent` on any read error.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — new step 1.35 ("Preflight-warn on missing `gh` `workflow` OAuth scope"), placed alongside the existing silent-direct-merge repo-shape preflight (step 1.3), in the same loud one-time-warning style as step 0.4's `shipyard.config.json` schema-validation-failure warning. Names the exact remediation (`gh auth refresh -h github.com -s workflow`) and explicitly forbids the worker from ever attempting to refresh/escalate/modify the token's scopes itself.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md` — router table description updated to name the new preflight step.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/workflow-scope-preflight-818.test.sh` (new) — the decision-logic truth table (silent-by-default on both "already has scope" and "no signal" paths, warn only on missing-scope-plus-signal), the live-mode no-repo fail-safe, and a doc-contract pass asserting the setup step invokes the script, names the exact remediation command, reuses #812's token verbatim, and never re-derives the two-signal condition as inline prose.

### 4.0.10 — 2026-07-23

Makes `workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` fail loudly instead of failing open when `args` is not the expected object shape (closes #817). The script guarded its input with two fall-open defaults and a pure `.map()` — `typeof args === 'object' ? args : {}`, then `Array.isArray(input.issues) ? input.issues : []` — so when `args` arrived as a JSON *string* rather than a parsed object (a mistake the `Workflow` tool's own docs warn is easy to make, since `args` is untyped at the tool boundary), all three collapsed to empty, `parallel([])` resolved instantly, and the run was reported as a **success with `agent_count: 0` in 37ms**. No error, no warning, and — because the script emitted no `log()`/`phase()` at all — nothing in `/workflows` or the transcript either. Since #791 made this script the only dispatch substrate, that meant a `/do-work` session could report a completed dispatch that never spawned a worker and strand its `.in_flight` pool slot waiting on a completion that had already happened. Three changes: a JSON-string `args` is now tolerated via a guarded `JSON.parse`; an `args` that is present and non-empty yet resolves to zero work units now `throw`s, naming the received shape; and dispatch emits a `log()` naming the unit count and each unit's mode/target. The bare no-args dry read stays harmless — the distinction is "no args at all" (fine) versus "args given but unusable" (throw). The verification blind spot that let this ship past the post-#809 check is called out explicitly in both the script header and the new suite: an `issues: []` probe returned `[]` on *both* the healthy and the broken path, so a zero-unit probe could never distinguish them; under this change that probe throws, and the new suite always drives a non-empty unit list instead.

- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — input handling rewritten: a string-shaped `args` is `JSON.parse`d under try/catch (the parse error is remembered for the diagnostic, not swallowed); `argsWereSupplied`/`argsAreNonEmpty` separate the harmless dry read from a real dispatch; a zero-unit outcome on a non-empty `args` throws an error naming `typeof args`, whether `issues` was an array, its length, the top-level keys, and any parse failure; a `log()` line names the unit count, repo, concurrency, and per-unit mode/target at dispatch (and says so on the dry-read path, so 0 agents is never silent). The label expression is factored into a `unitLabel()` helper shared by the `agent()` label and the log line so the two can't drift. A new header section documents the fail-loud contract, the three properties a future edit must preserve, and the zero-unit-probe blind spot. `meta` is untouched and still a pure literal (#809).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/workflow-args-fail-loud-817.test.sh` — new suite, 36 assertions, picked up automatically by `tests.yml`'s `find plugins -name '*.test.sh'` discovery. Unlike every sibling substrate suite (which inspects the file as text and so could not have seen a runtime fall-open), it **executes** the script under a harness that mirrors the Dynamic Workflows runtime — `agent`/`parallel`/`pipeline`/`log`/`args` injected as globals, top-level `await`, top-level `return`. Covers all four acceptance cases including the string-`args` case: a JSON-string `args` dispatches exactly one agent with the right mode/target label; the object path and multi-unit fan-out are unchanged; five distinct unusable shapes throw with a shape-naming diagnostic; a bare no-args invocation stays a harmless dry read; the dispatch `log()` names unit count and per-unit mode/target. Pins the former blind-spot probe (`issues: []`) as now-throwing, and cross-checks that the #809 pure-literal `meta` rule is not regressed. Proven by running it against the pre-fix file first, where 23 assertions fail.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the Workflow-substrate dispatch section's step 3 gains the caller-side contract: pass `args` as actual JSON values rather than a JSON-encoded string, expect a throw (handled by step 4's existing tool-call-failed clause) rather than an empty success on an unusable payload, and never "verify the substrate works" with a zero-unit probe.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.9` → `4.0.10`.

> **Note — 4.0.8 is intentionally absent, and 4.0.7/4.0.9 are in the correct chronological order (investigated for #862 / #864).** No commit, PR, or CHANGELOG entry was ever cut under the literal version `4.0.8` — the note above ("4.0.8 claimed by an in-flight sibling PR") recorded a *provisional reservation* made by the version-coordination scheme while multiple `/shipyard:do-work` sibling PRs were landing concurrently, not a completed release. The PR that provisionally held 4.0.8 (issue #817, merged as the 4.0.10 entry above) itself found both 4.0.8 and 4.0.9 already taken by other sibling PRs by the time *it* actually merged, so it recomputed to the next free slot (4.0.10) rather than colliding. The reservation was abandoned, not lost — no feature, fix, or file is missing from the historical record. Separately, #864 raised a suspected out-of-order release (4.0.9 appearing to land before 4.0.7, inferred from PR numbers #819 < #820). Git's actual merge-commit ancestry refutes this: PR #820's merge (`ffab91e`, 2026-07-23 01:52:29 -0400, cutting 4.0.7) is a direct ancestor of PR #819's merge (`a1e1765`, 2026-07-23 02:02:22 -0400, cutting 4.0.9) — confirmed via `git merge-base --is-ancestor ffab91e a1e1765` — so #820 merged to `main` strictly before #819 despite having a *higher* PR number, and the version sequence 4.0.6 → 4.0.7 → 4.0.9 → 4.0.10 is monotonically increasing in actual merge order. The lower PR number for #819 reflects when that PR was *opened*, not when it *merged*; #864's finding conflated the two. A full walk of every `plugin.json` version bump in git history (1.0.0 through the current release, cross-checked against every `### <version>` heading in this file) confirms this 4.0.8 gap is the only discontinuity in the entire sequence and no other entry is out of order.

### 4.0.7 — 2026-07-23

Distinguishes a `gh pr merge --auto` failure caused by a missing `workflow` OAuth scope from the generic `auto-merge: unavailable — needs manual merge` bucket, and hoists it into the end-of-session summary once per session instead of once per PR (closes #812). GitHub blocks `enablePullRequestAutoMerge` for an OAuth-app token that lacks the `workflow` scope whenever the PR's diff touches `.github/workflows/` — the `repo` scope alone (which is otherwise sufficient for `git push` and `gh pr create`) doesn't cover it. The prior spec lumped this into the same lossy `unavailable` suffix as a genuinely repo-disabled auto-merge or a transient `gh` failure, even though the three causes want three different responses and this one is uniquely a **deterministic, session-wide** precondition: every subsequent workflow-touching PR in the session (which `fix-main-ci` mode exists specifically to produce) hits the identical error until a human runs `gh auth refresh -h github.com -s workflow` once. `worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` step 1.1 now matches the GraphQL error's stable signature (`without \`workflow\` scope`) and reports a distinct `auto-merge: unavailable — gh token lacks workflow scope` token; the new `workflow_scope_blocked_prs` orchestrator-state list collects every PR hit by it across the session so the end-of-session summary prints one banner naming the fix and every affected PR, rather than repeating an unexplained-looking failure per occurrence. The optional `setup.md` preflight scope-check the issue's suggested fix also proposed (warning up front, before the first workflow-touching PR, by parsing `gh auth status`) is intentionally out of scope for this PR — it's a genuine enhancement (an earlier warning) rather than part of closing the mis-detection #812 reports, so it's filed as its own follow-up (#818).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` — new step 1.1 detects the `workflow`-scope GraphQL error signature and short-circuits the generic step-1.5 categorization; the `unavailable` bullet in step 1.5 now cross-references the split.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — step 6.b explains the split before the categorization; step 8 documents the new `auto-merge: unavailable — gh token lacks workflow scope` return line and its remediation; a new "Don't" bullet forbids collapsing it back into the generic suffix or attempting to self-escalate the token's scope.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/investigate.md`, `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/spike.md` — return-string vocabulary tables extended with the new suffix.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/worker-return.schema.json`, `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — `auto_merge` enum gains `unavailable-workflow-scope` in both the canonical JSON schema and the workflow script's inline runtime copy (kept in parity per `dispatch-substrate-cutover-790.test.sh`'s schema-drift guard).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — translation-table row added for the new structured `auto_merge` value.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — new session-local orchestrator-state field `workflow_scope_blocked_prs`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — step A.1's issue-work and investigate `shipped` handlers append the PR to `workflow_scope_blocked_prs` when the new suffix is present, without posting a per-PR advisory comment.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new end-of-session banner, silent when the list is empty, naming every affected PR plus the one-time `gh auth refresh -h github.com -s workflow` remediation.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — new regression coverage across all of the above: the fragment's error-signature match and distinct suffix, the per-mode spec's return-string documentation and remediation command, both schema files carrying the new enum value, and the orchestrator-state field / reconcile append / summary banner (including its silent-by-default framing).

### 4.0.6 — 2026-07-23

Adds a `stalled` (non-terminal-return) outcome to the orchestrator's reconcile loop, distinct from `blocked` (closes #813). A dispatched worker that finishes its real work but suspends itself awaiting a `Monitor` / backgrounded-process notification it can never receive (the harness only re-wakes a task with a *live foreground* call outstanding) previously fell through `steady-state.md` step A.1's return vocabulary — which had no branch for a narrative, future-tense "I'm now waiting… once it reports, I'll…" return — risking a `blocked` mislabel of near-complete, resumable work. The live repro this session (issue #808's worker, ~331k tokens, zero commits but a complete working tree one `git commit` from shipping) was recovered only by the orchestrator inspecting the worktree by hand and resuming the agent; that recovery is now the documented `stalled` path: step A.0.5 runs a stalled-worker check FIRST (before its existing crash-recovery reap), judging pending intent from the return text (not a keyword list) and grounding it against the worktree's actual state (`git rev-list --count` / `git status --porcelain`) to distinguish resume-worthy work from nothing-to-recover. When resume-worthy, it re-dispatches into the SAME worktree/branch (never a fresh one) with instructions to stop waiting and re-run the blocking command in the foreground, bounded by a retry cap of 2 before falling through to the existing crash-recovery/`blocked` handling. `shipyard:worker-preamble`'s synchronous-return rule is tightened with an explicit, quotable prohibition on self-suspending awaiting a Monitor/background notification for a result the worker needs to finish.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — A.0.5 gains a "Stalled-worker detection and resume" check ahead of its existing crash-detection/reap fallback: the pending-intent judgment call, the worktree-state check (`ahead_count` / `dirty`) that grounds it, the bounded (`stalled_resume_counts`, cap 2) same-worktree resume dispatch, and the fall-through to the existing crash-recovery path once resume-worthy work is absent or the cap is exhausted. A.1's return-vocabulary intro now cross-references the `stalled` outcome and states explicitly that it must never fall through to the `blocked #<N>` branch.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — the `#529` synchronous-return rule leads with an explicit forbiddance of self-suspending on a Monitor/background notification for a result the worker needs to finish, and cites the #813 repro alongside the existing #529 one.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/stalled-worker-resume-813.test.sh` — new regression coverage: the `stalled` branch, its distinctness from `blocked`, the worktree-state check, the resume mechanics and retry cap, and the worker-preamble prohibition are all documented; a runtime classifier check asserts the exact #813 repro narrative resolves to `stalled` (never `blocked`), alongside `blocked`/`shipped`/crash sanity cases.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.5` → `4.0.6`.

Every relocated cross-reference (both self-references within the operator phase and every inbound anchor from `do-work.md`, `steady-state.md`, `cleanup-summary.md`, `drain.md`, `setup/06-scope-preflight.md`, and `do-work-RATIONALE.md`) was re-pointed at its new destination and verified to resolve; instruction text was relocated verbatim, never paraphrased or condensed.

### 4.0.4 — 2026-07-23

Fixes `workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js`'s `export const meta` not being a pure literal, which made **every** `/shipyard:do-work` dispatch fail at the `Workflow` tool-call boundary on 4.0.0 through 4.0.3 (closes #809). `meta.description` was assembled by string concatenation; each `+` parses as a `BinaryExpression`, and the tool validates `meta` before the script ever runs, rejecting it with `Invalid workflow script: meta must be a pure literal: non-literal node type in meta: BinaryExpression`. Because #791 made that script the only dispatch substrate and deleted the `dispatch.substrate: "agent"` escape hatch in the same release, the product's core loop was non-functional across the whole 4.0.x line with no config-level workaround — P0. `description` is now one single-quoted string literal and the long-form prose it carried moved into the file's header comment, which is unconstrained. The root cause of it *shipping*, though, is that nothing in CI could see it: `node --check` passes on the broken file (concatenation is valid JavaScript), and the four sibling substrate suites load the module under a plain async wrapper without ever invoking the `Workflow` tool, so the tool-enforced pure-literal rule had no guard at all. The new guard therefore actually parses `meta` rather than grepping it, and was proven by running it against the pre-fix file — where it reproduces the tool's exact `BinaryExpression` verdict at the offending line — before being run against the fix.

- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — `meta.description` collapsed from eleven `+`-joined fragments to one string literal; the prose it carried (per-mode prompt-builder augmentations, structured-return validation, caller-provisioned `worktreePath`) relocated into a new header section that also documents the pure-literal constraint, why `node --check` cannot catch a violation, and the adjacent tool-boundary constraints to keep avoiding (`Date.now()` / `Math.random()` / argless `new Date()`, TypeScript annotations — none present).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/check-workflow-meta-literal.mjs` — new, dependency-free checker: locates the top-level `export const meta` (anchored to a line start so the header comment's own `export const meta = { name, description }` example can't fool it) and parses the value with a recursive-descent parser admitting only string/number/`true`/`false`/`null`/object/array literals. Reports violations using the tool's own "non-literal node type in meta: `<Type>`" vocabulary plus a line number, and additionally requires `name` and `description` to be non-empty string literals.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/workflow-meta-pure-literal-809.test.sh` — new suite, 30 assertions, picked up automatically by `tests.yml`'s `find plugins -name '*.test.sh'` discovery. Asserts every `plugins/shipyard/workflows/*.workflow.js` has a pure-literal `meta`; rejects a byte-for-byte copy of the #809 regression (and separately asserts that same fixture passes `node --check`, pinning why `node --check` was never going to catch it); rejects template literals, interpolation, bare identifiers, calls, spreads, computed keys, shorthand and method properties, ternaries, concatenation nested inside object and array values, and missing/blank `name`/`description`; and accepts the valid-but-tricky cases a grep-based guard would false-positive on — a description containing literal `+` and backtick *characters*, escaped apostrophes, double-quoted strings, nested literal objects/arrays, and comments inside the object.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.3` → `4.0.4`.

### 4.0.2 — 2026-07-22

Fixes `hooks/refuse-broad-process-kill.sh` rule 4 blocking `kill -0` (and `kill -s 0` / `kill -n 0`) liveness probes when combined with a process-pattern lookup (`pgrep`, or `ps | grep`) elsewhere in the same command (closes #804). Signal 0 sends no signal at all — POSIX defines it as a permission-and-existence check — so a `kill -0` invocation cannot terminate anything, and rule 4's classic "look up PIDs by pattern, then kill them" hazard doesn't apply to it. `kill -0` is shipyard's own documented liveness primitive (`scripts/session-state.sh`'s `is-active`), so the false positive fired on a legitimate, recurring orchestrator/worker diagnostic (checking whether a possibly-stalled worker is still alive alongside a process listing). Rules 2 and 3 (`pkill` / `killall`, which always target by pattern) are untouched, as is the existing literal-`kill <pid>` allowance and the fail-open-on-malformed-input posture; a command with a *non*-zero-signal `kill` (e.g. `kill -9 $(pgrep ...)`) still blocks even if a signal-zero `kill` also appears in the same command.

- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/refuse-broad-process-kill.sh` — rule 4's kill-detection logic in the embedded `python3` decision script now excludes `kill` matches whose signal argument is explicitly `-0` / `-s 0` / `-n 0` before checking for a combined pattern lookup; header comment block updated to document the exception.
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/tests/refuse-broad-process-kill.test.sh` — new regression coverage: `kill -0`/`kill -s 0`/`kill -n 0` combined with `ps | grep` or `pgrep` is allowed (including the issue's exact repro), a non-zero-signal `kill` combined with a pattern lookup still blocks even alongside a signal-zero `kill` in the same command, and `pkill` / `killall` remain unconditionally blocked (including `killall -0`, which has no signal-zero exemption).
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.1` → `4.0.2`.

### 4.0.1 — 2026-07-22

Fixes `shipyard:worker-preamble`'s step-0 cwd fail-fast and mid-session cwd-anchoring snippets being written as single multi-line compound bash blocks (command substitutions, `cd` subshells, an inline `if`) that the worktree-isolation `Bash` guard refuses to run, forcing every worker dispatch in every mode to eat a failed call plus a retry on its mandatory first action (closes #802). The guard's "Refusing to run it" text was also plausibly misreadable as the isolation violation the check exists to detect, inviting a spurious `blocked:` return on a healthy dispatch. Both snippets are rewritten as sequences of plain, single-purpose `git rev-parse` calls with the git-dir/git-common-dir comparison performed by the worker's own inspection of the outputs rather than scripted in bash — verified runnable as separate `Bash` calls against this dispatch's own worktree before landing.

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — "Step-0 cwd fail-fast" and "Mid-session cwd anchoring" sections rewritten: the compound derive-and-compare-and-branch blocks become three (step-0) and three-plus-anchor (mid-session) separate plain `git rev-parse` / `git -C "$WORKTREE_PATH" rev-parse` calls, each in its own fenced block, with the equality check and `blocked:`/pass decision left to the worker's own reasoning; the `blocked: dispatch-isolation cwd override is wrong` and `blocked: cwd anchor drifted mid-session` bail-message text is unchanged.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `4.0.0` → `4.0.1`.

### 4.0.0 — 2026-07-22

**BREAKING: the `dispatch.substrate` config knob is removed.** Delete any `dispatch` block from `shipyard.config.json` / `.shipyard/config.local.json` before upgrading — the repo config schema is `additionalProperties: false`, so a layer that still carries one now fails validation (`/shipyard:config validate` reports it; `shipyard-config.sh load` exits 70) instead of being silently ignored. There is nothing to replace it with.

Retires the legacy `Agent`-tool dispatch path, completing the #782 Dynamic Workflows epic (closes #791, phase 5 of 5). #787 committed `workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` as inert scaffolding, #788 wired `issue-work`, #789 wired the remaining six modes, and #790 flipped the built-in `dispatch.substrate` default to `"workflow"` while keeping the hand-rolled `Agent`-tool orchestrator for one release as an instant-revert override. That override is now gone: the `Workflow` substrate is the only way a `mode:`-driven worker is dispatched, so there is no flag to read, no branch to fork on, and no fallback — which is exactly why the surviving instructions had to become self-sufficient rather than merely lose a branch. The `Agent` tool is **not** globally retired: `shipyard:verify-worker` (with `isolation: "worktree"`), `shipyard:decompose-worker`, and the read-only scope-preflight / refinement workers still use it, and none of them take a `mode:` value.

Because the `Workflow` runtime's `agent()` primitive has no isolation option, the worktree-isolation guarantee the retired `isolation: "worktree"` parameter used to provide is now the orchestrator's job — it runs `git worktree add` itself and passes the path as each work unit's `worktreePath`. `hooks/enforce-worktree-isolation.sh` grew a second guarded shape to keep that guarantee mechanical rather than merely documented. The one genuinely new failure mode this creates — the orchestrator now creates the worktree *before* the dispatch call, so a classifier-denied dispatch strands one with no `.in_flight` slot for any reap path to find — is closed with an explicit reap at all three denial sites.

- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/enforce-worktree-isolation.sh` — now guards **two** dispatch shapes: a `Workflow` call against `do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` carrying a work unit with no (or empty) `worktreePath` is blocked (exit 2) — the direct successor to the `isolation: "worktree"` guard — and the `Agent` shape is still guarded for `shipyard:verify-worker` and hand dispatches of the retained mode shims. Recursive-descent `jq` extraction so it doesn't pin an unstable `Workflow` input key path; fails open on malformed input and on any other workflow.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the substrate section is now unconditional (flag read and both `dispatch_substrate ==` branches deleted) and retitled; the per-mode routing table's `subagent_type` column is replaced by the per-mode spec each worker loads; the `isolation: "worktree"` mandate is replaced by the `worktreePath` pre-provisioning mandate; model resolution now targets the work unit's `model` field; the denial branch reaps the orphaned worktree; new migration note for the retired knob.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`, `setup/07-pool-fill.md`, `dont.md`, `inline-trivial.md`, `do-work.md` — both dispatch sites (step C, initial pool fill) now issue `Workflow` calls with a pre-provisioned worktree; the A.1 translation note is unconditional; the denial branches reap the orphaned worktree; the `in_flight` write-through records `worktree_path`.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — worktree discipline gains **Rule 0** (anchor to the dispatch's `worktreePath` first, *then* never `cd` out), the step-0 fail-fast covers both dispatch shapes, the return contract names the structured `worker-return.schema.json` shape alongside the free-text one, and the native-auto-PR section is re-scoped to the remaining `Agent`+`isolation` dispatches.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh`, `schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — the `dispatch` block is removed from the built-in defaults and the schema.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker.md` + the six mode shims — the mode-routing table's shim column becomes a model column; each shim documents that `/do-work` no longer dispatches it by name and that it is retained as a hand-dispatch target.
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/README.md`, `do-work-dispatch.workflow.js`, `schemas/worker-return.schema.json`, `commands/status.md`, `commands/cost.md` — phase-5 status, upgrade/migration callout replacing the instant-revert box, and substrate-flag references removed.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/legacy-agent-dispatch-retired-791.test.sh` — new 75-assertion suite: the knob is gone and a stale config is *rejected*; the substrate section has no flag read or fork; **self-sufficiency** (every one of the seven modes has a worktree shape, an `args.issues[]` payload, and a real prompt builder; both dispatch sites name the `Workflow` tool; model resolution and return translation survive); the hook blocks missing/empty/mixed-batch `worktreePath` for all seven modes and still blocks an unisolated `shipyard:verify-worker`; the orphaned-worktree reap is documented at all three denial sites.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/{dispatch-workflow-substrate-788,dispatch-workflow-substrate-789,dispatch-substrate-cutover-790,shipyard-config,resolve-dispatch-model,dispatch-integration-774,spike-worker}.test.sh` — assertions that pinned the retired path (its config knob, its `subagent_type` routing, the instant-revert callout) re-pointed at the surviving contract; each prior phase's own guarantees are preserved.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `3.1.2` → `4.0.0` (major: the removed config knob is a breaking change for any repo that set it).

Fixes `/shipyard:status` rendering a raw target string and a 0-token count for `investigate` and `spike` worker kinds, instead of the `#N` target and joined per-issue token totals every other issue-numbered kind gets (closes #797). ### 3.1.1 — 2026-07-22

Fixes a dispatch-prep flaw where the orchestrator could compose a false "not blocked" / "genuinely untested" confidence claim into an `issue-work` dispatch prompt from the issue's body/title alone, contradicted by its own comment thread (closes #781). The repro: a dispatch prompt asserted four checklist items were "genuinely untested (not blocked by anything cited)" when the target issue's first two comments already documented two prior QA passes reproducing all four as blocked on a still-open infra issue — a signal a plain `comments` read would have surfaced before the worker ever ran. The worker's own verification-first step 2 caught the false premise this time, but the mitigation now stops the false claim at composition time instead of relying on the worker to re-derive it.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — new rule: any confidence/status framing added to the dispatch prompt beyond the fixed templates (including a scope agent's `phase_1_scope` string, which flows through verbatim) must be derived from or cross-checked against the candidate's `comments` field, with a cheap downgrade path ("no blocking label present — verify against the comment thread") when a full comment-thread read isn't practical at composition time.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — cross-references the same grounding requirement at the `phase_1_scope` ready-shape definition, since that field is passed to the dispatch prompt unmodified.

### 3.1.0 — 2026-07-22

Reconciles `/shipyard:do-work`'s dispatch + status layer with Claude Code's native background-by-default subagents and Agent View (closes #785). Confirmed against current platform docs: as of v2.1.198, `Agent`-tool subagents run in the background by default, and a background subagent that isolated its edits in a worktree (`isolation: "worktree"`) auto-commits, pushes its own branch, and opens a draft pull request on its own the moment it finishes. **Decision: the worker remains the sole, explicit owner of PR creation** — a compliant worker always reaches its own `gh pr create --label shipyard ...` step before its turn ends, so the native behavior finds nothing left to act on and is a no-op in the happy path; the one residual gap (a worker that pushes then bails before `gh pr create`, leaving room for a native, unlabeled, non-closing draft PR) is now caught by a new pre-flight cross-check rather than silently racing a second PR onto the same branch. This reconciliation applies specifically to the legacy `dispatch.substrate: "agent"` path — the default `"workflow"` substrate (as of #790) dispatches via the `Workflow` tool's `agent()` calls, a distinct harness mechanism not subject to the same subagent `background`/`isolation` semantics. Also reconciles `/shipyard:status` with the native Agent View dashboard (complement, not defer or re-implement — different scope, different granularity) and re-validates shipyard's worktree-reap escape hatch against the harness's own background auto-resume durability (complementary layers guarding different failure modes; no code change follows from the re-validation itself).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — new "Native background-subagent auto-PR reconciliation" section documenting the confirmed platform behavior, the PR-creation ownership decision, and the explicit "don't let the native auto-PR fire" instruction every dispatch prompt now carries via this skill.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — step 0 pre-flight now also cross-checks for an already-open PR on the canonical `do-work/issue-<N>` head branch (independent of `closingIssuesReferences`), so a native-opened draft PR left over from an interrupted prior dispatch is detected and bailed on rather than raced with a second PR.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/status.md` — new "Relationship to Agent View" section: a comparison table plus the complement-not-defer decision, since Agent View has no visibility into shipyard's own mode/target/blocked-by/token-budget semantics and `/shipyard:status` has no transcript/attach access.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/reaped-escape-hatch.md` — new paragraph re-validating the worktree-reaped escape hatch against native background auto-resume: auto-resume protects against the harness process crashing/restarting, this fragment protects against the worktree directory itself being removed — orthogonal failure modes, no regression in either.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version `3.0.0` → `3.1.0`.

### 3.0.0 — 2026-07-22

**The Dynamic Workflows substrate cutover** — phase 4 of the #782 epic (closes #782), carrying the `2.x` → `3.0.0` major bump. Flips the built-in default of `dispatch.substrate` from `"agent"` to `"workflow"`, so `/shipyard:do-work` now runs its dispatch loop on the native `Workflow` tool by default, for every one of the seven `mode:`-driven workers (`issue-work`, `fix-checks-only`, `fix-rebase`, `fix-main-ci`, `fix-failing-prs-batch`, `investigate`, `spike`) — all wired and validated across phases 1–3 (#787/#788/#789). Workers now return schema-validated structured results (`worker-return.schema.json`) that the orchestrator translates back into the existing free-text vocabulary before reconcile, so a malformed return fails loudly at the stage boundary instead of being re-parsed from prose. **This is a high-blast-radius change** — it changes shipyard's default runtime behavior for every installation on next update — so the legacy `Agent`-tool dispatch path is **retained, fully working, for one release** as an instant-revert safety valve; removing it is the final #782 phase, gated on the workflow substrate proving itself under the flipped default. **Regression? `/shipyard:config set dispatch.substrate agent` reverts the dispatch loop to the legacy `Agent`-tool path instantly, for every mode — a one-line config change, no reinstall.** `/shipyard:status` (text / `--json` / `--stale`) and `/shipyard:cost` are unaffected by the cutover **by construction**: the orchestrator writes the same per-session `.in_flight` slot and bumps the same token counters regardless of substrate (the structured→free-text translation happens *before* reconcile), so both dashboards read the same session-state file either way — no silent gap.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` — built-in `dispatch.substrate` default flipped `"agent"` → `"workflow"`.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — `dispatch.substrate` `default` flipped to `"workflow"`; both the group and field descriptions rewritten for the cutover, including the instant-revert instruction.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the workflow-substrate section reframed so `"workflow"` is the default branch and `"agent"` is the retained instant-revert override, no longer "purely additive / still-blocked #790".
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` — header comment updated to phase-4 status (default now `"workflow"`, `Agent`-tool path retained as instant-revert).
- `plugins/shipyard/workflows/README.md` — phase-4 banner, a prominent **⚠️ Regression? Instant revert** callout with the one-line revert command, and the substrate-flag table / why-scaffold / out-of-scope sections updated for the flipped default.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/status.md` — new "Substrate-agnostic" section explaining why `/shipyard:status` keeps reporting live rows under the `workflow` default (the orchestrator writes the same `.in_flight` slot regardless of substrate).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/cost.md` — new "Substrate-agnostic" section explaining why `/shipyard:cost` has no cost-tracking gap under the `workflow` default (token attribution runs after the structured→free-text translation, unchanged by substrate).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/dispatch-substrate-cutover-790.test.sh` (new) — the cutover validation suite: asserts the flipped default + that the substrate selector reads it + the instant-revert override round-trips; a node harness that executes `do-work-dispatch.workflow.js` under the same async wrapper the Dynamic Workflows runtime uses, routing all seven modes to their builders and checking each carries the structured-return schema, that the schema the script hands `agent()` matches `worker-return.schema.json` enum-for-enum, and that every documented structured-return example is schema-valid (the structured→free-text round-trip); a `status.sh` render check against a synthetic `Workflow`-dispatched session file across text/`--json`/`--stale`; and the release-bump + prominent-revert-doc assertions.

### 2.17.0 — 2026-07-22

Adds an **opt-in independent adversarial-verify gate** between implement and auto-merge — the "verify" in find → implement → verify → merge (closes #783). When `verify_gate.enabled` is `true` (default `false`), an `issue-work` worker on a `trusted`-author PR runs a new step 5.9 before arming auto-merge: it dispatches a fresh, independent `shipyard:verify-worker` (pinned to Opus) that adversarially judges whether the open PR actually and completely resolves its issue — checking on-target-ness, acceptance-criteria coverage, and reward-hacking signals (skipped/weakened tests, error-suppression, scope creep, unauthorized CI/secrets edits) — and returns a single `verified:` / `not-verified:` verdict. On `verified:` the worker arms auto-merge as normal; on `not-verified:` it labels the PR `needs-human-review`, posts the verifier's reasoning verbatim, and returns `blocked #<N> at verify: <reason>` (routed to `needs-human-review` by the existing #521 reconcile — no new orchestrator branch). The verifier only judges — it never merges, labels, or commits. The gate is the one sanctioned nested subagent dispatch in the loop, so it requires `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH=1`; step 5.9 **fails open** to a `needs-human-review` handoff (never an unverified auto-merge, never a wedged loop) if the nested dispatch is refused. With the flag off — the default — the loop is byte-for-byte unchanged.

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/verify-worker.md` — **new** thin shim (model-pinned to `opus`) mirroring the other per-mode shims; loads `shipyard:worker-preamble` + the per-mode spec, documents the `isolation: "worktree"` and `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH=1` contracts.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/verify.md` — **new** canonical verify-mode spec: read-only evidence gathering (`gh pr diff` / `gh issue view` / check rollup), the adversarial check list, the `verified:` / `not-verified:` return contract, and the judge-not-implementer `## Don't` rules.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — new `verify_gate` input; new **step 5.9** (opt-in) between §5.85 and §6 that dispatches the verifier and branches on its verdict, with the fail-open rule; §6 intro notes the 5.9 hand-off.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — verify-gate augmentation at the `mode: issue-work` dispatch site: reads `verify_gate.enabled`, appends the `verify_gate: on` Context paragraph only when enabled AND the author is `trusted`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` — `verify_gate: { enabled: false }` added to the built-in defaults.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — `verify_gate` object registered (`additionalProperties: false` requires it for config validation to accept the key).

### 2.15.0 — 2026-07-19

Adds a new `shipyard:spike-worker` agent mode — the second out-of-scope follow-up #767 named alongside `decompose-worker` (closes #773). Unlike `decompose-worker` (a no-worktree, read-mostly GitHub-API job), a spike writes code and commits a design doc, so it joins the six worktree-isolated `issue-worker` modes' family: a dedicated per-mode spec (`agents/issue-worker/spike.md`) reusing `shipyard:worker-preamble` for the shared worktree/PR/return-contract rules, with a thin shim (`agents/spike-worker.md`) pointing at it. The mode runs a spike (a feasibility/research issue with no fixed acceptance criteria) to completion: investigate → commit a design doc in-repo recording the conclusion (viable / viable-with-caveats / not-viable — all three are valid, shipped dispositions) → decompose the follow-on plan into an ordered, capped set of dispatch-ready sub-issues (referenced by bare URL to avoid the `#624` closing-reference-leak hazard, since the parent spike issue is closed by this same PR) → optionally implement whatever slice is directly committable now. No orchestrator dispatch-integration is included — detecting a spike-shaped issue and wiring the dispatch-site routing is tracked separately in #774, same split as `decompose-worker`/#772.

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/spike.md` — new per-mode spec: the investigate → design-doc → decompose → optional-implement lifecycle, an explicit override of `issue-work`'s phantom-merge guard for a design-doc-only diff, a `#624`-style leak-verification guard for freshly-created follow-on sub-issues, the `decompose.max_subissues` fan-out cap with `needs-human-review` escalation on over-cap plans, and the `spiked+shipped` / `spiked+needs-human-review` return-string vocabulary.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/spike-worker.md` — new thin shim agent pointing at the per-mode spec, carrying no `model:` pin (spike design-doc authorship needs the same reasoning tier as `issue-work`, not the cheaper five cost-optimized modes).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/spike-worker.test.sh` — new regression suite pinning the shim's frontmatter and cross-reference, the spec's distinguishing lifecycle steps, the design-doc-only-diff and sub-issue-leak guards, the return-string vocabulary, and a scope guard asserting the orchestrator's dispatch-routing files (`dispatch-rules.md`, `steady-state.md`, `enforce-worktree-isolation.sh`) do NOT yet reference this mode.

### 2.14.1 — 2026-07-19

Shipyard tracks per-worker cost post-hoc (session-state pricing table + PR cost comments); the Claude API's beta "Task Budgets" feature (`output_config.task_budget`, beta flag `task-budgets-2026-03-13`) is pre-emptive instead — a token ceiling the model sees mid-generation and paces against, distinct from `max_tokens`'s silent cutoff. This is a spike/feasibility slice: it establishes whether shipyard's `Agent`-tool dispatch surface exposes Task Budgets and, since the answer is a confirmed no, documents the finding rather than adding dead config (closes #765).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — new "Task Budgets — not exposed on the Agent dispatch surface" section: dual-sourced negative finding (the Claude Platform docs state Task Budgets is unsupported on Claude Code/Cowork surfaces; the `Agent` tool's own `AgentDefinition` parameter surface has no budget/token-ceiling field), why the `Workflow` tool's `budget` primitive cited in the issue doesn't bridge the gap (harness-level userspace accounting, not plumbed to the Messages API's `task_budget`), and why no `budgets.<mode>` config surface follows — it would be dead config with nothing to enforce it, the same anti-pattern #727 already named for `models.*`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — cross-reference from the Cost-tracking write-through section to the new RATIONALE finding, framing the existing post-hoc accounting as staying post-hoc by design (not a gap shipyard can currently close).

### 2.13.0 — 2026-07-19

Adds a new `shipyard:decompose-worker` agent — a first-class, model-pinned dispatch identity for the epic-decomposition logic that previously only ran as an anonymous `general-purpose` sub-agent inlining a copy of `/decompose-epic`'s Worker prompt template. This is the out-of-scope follow-up #767 explicitly named (closes #772): the agent points at `commands/decompose-epic.md`'s Worker prompt template as the single source of truth rather than forking the sharding logic, and — unlike the six worktree-isolated `issue-worker` modes — is deliberately dispatched **without** `isolation: "worktree"`, since decomposition only reads the codebase read-only and calls the GitHub API. No orchestrator runtime dispatch-integration is included; that's tracked separately in #774.

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/decompose-worker.md` — new standalone agent (model: sonnet) documenting the no-worktree-isolation contract, the pointer to `decompose-epic.md`'s Worker prompt template, the untrusted-input discipline, and the `decomposed:` / `escalated:` / `blocked:` return contract.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/decompose-worker-agent.test.sh` — new regression suite pinning the agent's frontmatter, its no-fork pointer to the canonical spec, the no-worktree-isolation contract (including that `enforce-worktree-isolation.sh` must NOT guard this agent), and the return-string vocabulary.

### 2.12.1 — 2026-07-19

`/do-work`'s pre-dispatch head-branch reap didn't reliably clear a completed worker's `.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` worktree, so a re-dispatched fix-checks-only or fix-rebase worker against the same PR would bail with `blocked: head branch <head> locked in another worktree` — costing a wasted dispatch plus a manual `git worktree remove --force` each time, twice in one repro session (closes #771).

Issue #576 had already taught two reap call sites — the A.1 issue-work `shipped`-only immediate-reap and the drain-phase pre-dispatch reap (restricted to `do-work/issue-*` branches) — to force-reap a worktree lock classified `peer-alive` rather than deferring on it, since by the time either site runs the originating worker has already returned its terminal string and the lock PID is just a transient harness artifact, not a genuinely-live conflict. That fix left two other call sites still deferring unconditionally with no override: `steady-state.md`'s step B (the general per-completion reap that actually covers fix-checks `green`/`blocked`, fix-rebase `rebased`/`blocked`, and the synthetic-divert `shipped` variants — everything *except* the A.1 issue-work `shipped` special case), and `dispatch-rules.md`'s 2d pre-dispatch reap (the steady-state, non-drain fix-checks dispatch site, which — unlike drain's #370 reap — had no force-reap fallback at all). Both now apply the same "agent is done by definition, so `peer-alive` is a transient harness artifact" reasoning already established for the two #576 call sites, closing the residual gap.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — step B's per-completion reap now force-reaps `peer-alive` locks (classification `peer-alive-force`, existing `phase: "steady-state-B-completion"`) instead of deferring; updated four other prose passages (A.0.5's crash-detection rationale, the drain-precedent paragraph, and the interaction-with-step-B note) that had described step B as still conservatively deferring, so the spec stays internally consistent.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the 2d pre-dispatch head-branch reap (steady-state fix-checks-only dispatch site) now force-reaps `peer-alive` locks the same way, instead of the prior unconditional defer with no override.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/completion-reap-force-771.test.sh` — new regression guard asserting both call sites classify-override to `peer-alive-force`, no longer emit an `--action deferred` call for `peer-alive`, and that the `worktree-reap.sh reap` helper accepts the classification/phase pairs both sites now use.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/crash-recovery-A05.test.sh` — updated an ordering-anchor string that referenced the now-stale "unlike step B" prose in `steady-state.md`'s A.0.5 crash-aware-reap comment.

### 2.12.0 — 2026-07-19

`/do-work`'s scope pre-flight previously let "has open design decisions" pass as a legitimate `human-decision-required` defer reason, so epics, spikes, and design-needed issues were routinely parked as `needs-triage` / `needs-human-review` instead of worked — the repro was a `mattsears18/lightwork` session where three freshly-filed design/spike issues sat deferred until the maintainer manually overrode the orchestrator ("do the spikes, decompose the epics, do the designs... whatever needs to be done — YOU DO IT"). This is a phase-1 taxonomy-tightening slice: it extends the existing #661 "autonomous design/architecture decisions" default from in-worker implementation choices to issue *scoping* itself — a plain open design or architecture decision is no longer, by itself, a valid defer reason at any layer of the spec (closes #767).
