- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — new "Design/architecture/epic/spike decisions are in-scope by default, not a defer reason" callout; tightened the `human-decision-required` class definition, per-class evidence-shape table row, rejected-shapes list, scoping-agent prompt instruction, and validator prose to explicitly reject a design/architecture decision (specific or generic) as `human-decision-required` evidence, while keeping product/business/legal/policy decisions and the `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` epic-decomposition path unchanged.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — extended the #661 overview bullet to state the default now covers issue scoping, cross-referencing the scope-preflight callout.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — new rule forbidding a defer on "has open design decisions" alone, naming the correct routing (ready + `phase_1_scope`, or `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` for a genuine non-mechanical epic).
- `CLAUDE.md` — updated the `needs-human-review` "Naming note (#608)" to drop "design" from the genuine-human-decision category list and cross-reference the new scope-preflight callout.
- Out of scope for this PR (filed as follow-ups): a first-class `shipyard:decompose-worker` agent mode, a first-class `shipyard:spike-worker` agent mode, and the orchestrator runtime dispatch-integration wiring for either.

### 2.11.0 — 2026-07-19

During a long `/do-work` burndown a model tier can be briefly overloaded, failing a dispatch that would otherwise succeed on retry or on an adjacent tier. Claude Code (v2.1.166+) exposes `fallbackModel` in `settings.json` for exactly this — up to three fallback models tried in order — but shipyard's docs never mentioned it, so users had no guidance to adopt it and no way to compute a sensible per-family chain that complements the existing `models.<mode>` dispatch-tier resolution (closes #766).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — new "`fallbackModel` — degrade gracefully under model-tier overload" section: what the setting is, why it matters most on an unattended overnight burndown (doubly so on a self-hosted-runner host), a worked `settings.json` example, and an explicit distinction between `fallbackModel` (session-level overload resilience) and `models.<mode>` (dispatch-time tier assignment) so the two aren't conflated.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — cross-reference to the new RATIONALE section added alongside the existing "Recommended permission posture" and "Runaway-fanout guards" callouts.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/resolve-dispatch-model.sh` — new advisory `--fallback-chain <family-or-model-id>` subcommand computing the recommended per-family degrade chain (opus → sonnet,haiku; sonnet → haiku; haiku → empty, already the floor tier; fable → opus,sonnet,haiku, capped at the documented 3-model limit). Explicitly documented as NOT wired into dispatch (the `Agent` tool has no fallback parameter) — a pure helper for computing a `settings.json` value, not a repeat of #727's dead-config-surface mistake.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/resolve-dispatch-model.test.sh` — new test group (H) covering the fallback-chain table, case-insensitivity, full-model-id resolution, and the fail-open/usage-error paths.

### 2.10.8 — 2026-07-19

A `/do-work` burndown fans out deeply — a rolling worker pool, subagents nestable 5 levels deep, and any worker doing research can issue unbounded web searches — with nothing in the spec putting an explicit ceiling on either dimension. Claude Code exposes two harness-level env vars built exactly for this (`CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENTS_PER_SESSION` / `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION`, v2.1.212+, default 200 each), but nothing in the docs mentioned them — a gap that matters most on a host that also runs the target repo's CI as a self-hosted runner, where an unbounded fanout compounds into resource contention with in-flight CI (closes #764).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md` — new "Runaway guards — subagent + web-search session caps" section documenting both env vars, why the self-hosted-runner case makes them matter more, and recommended values (the 200 defaults are sane for a normal burndown; consider tightening for an unattended overnight run on a shared runner host).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — cross-reference to the new RATIONALE section added alongside the existing "Recommended permission posture" callout.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/status.md` — new `Don't` bullet documenting that `/shipyard:status` does not (and currently cannot) render a live count against either cap, since the session state file has no visibility into the harness's internal counters — an honest gap rather than a fabricated number.

### 2.10.7 — 2026-07-19

Claude Code's **Auto mode** (research preview week of 2026-03-27, since rolled to Pro and Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry) is a classifier-based middle ground between default-deny and full permission bypass — it still runs unattended, auto-approving routine tool calls, but hard-blocks destructive git (`reset --hard`, `clean -fd`, `stash drop`), infra-destroy commands (`terraform`/`pulumi`/`cdk destroy`), transcript tampering, and `rm -rf` on an unresolved variable. Shipyard's docs already assumed a live Auto-mode classifier throughout the dispatch/operator machinery (`dispatch_denials`, `operator_denials`, the worker-preamble "After a classifier denial" contract) but never stated the recommendation itself, leaving a gap between what the spec's failure-handling assumes and what a user is actually told to run `/do-work` under (closes #763).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — new "Recommended permission posture" section (right after the intro, before `## Args`) recommending Auto mode over `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, explaining why the existing classifier-denial machinery throughout the spec already assumes it, and verifying (with citations, not assumption) that it composes cleanly with shipyard's own `PreToolUse` hooks (`enforce-worktree-isolation.sh`, `enforce-edit-scope.sh`, `refuse-broad-process-kill.sh`, `refuse-escape-symlink-commit.sh`, `guard-primary-checkout.sh`) — the hooks are a separate deterministic layer that fires regardless of permission mode, so switching from full bypass to Auto mode neither weakens nor duplicates them.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — "After a classifier denial" now leads with a cross-reference to the new recommendation (a classifier denial is the recommended posture working as intended, not a misconfiguration) and a paragraph distinguishing the classifier layer from shipyard's own `PreToolUse` hooks, so a worker hitting either doesn't conflate the two.

Deliberately left the full bypass posture available as a user choice rather than deprecating it — nothing in the spec's dispatch, worker-return, or hook behavior depends on which posture is active; only the classifier-denial branches go permanently dormant under full bypass.

### 2.10.6 — 2026-07-19

The 10 auditor agents that carried no `model:` frontmatter (`api`, `docs`, `dx`, `functional-qa`, `mobile-ux`, `observability`, `security`, `tech-debt`, `testing`, `web-ux`) inherited the session's top model (Opus) on every `/shipyard:audit` run. They are now pinned to the `sonnet` family alias (current Sonnet = Sonnet 5 — near-Opus quality at a fraction of the cost), a global plugin default that trades some audit depth on the judgment-heavy dimensions (`security`, `functional-qa`, `testing`, `tech-debt`) for cost. Deliberately-pinned auditors (`haiku` / `sonnet`) are untouched (closes #761).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/{api,docs,dx,functional-qa,mobile-ux,observability,security,tech-debt,testing,web-ux}-auditor.md` — add `model: sonnet` frontmatter to each of the 10 previously-unpinned auditors.

### 2.10.5 — 2026-07-16

On a large React-Native monorepo, individual worker steps legitimately exceeded the harness's ~600s stream-watchdog window (and, more immediately, the `Bash` tool's own 120s default per-call timeout) with no intervening output: `npm ci` on the app workspace stalled a worker into opening a `Monitor` and ending its turn to "wait"; a slow pre-commit hook (typecheck + lint + prettier + secret-scan) stalled another worker mid-`git commit`, which narrated "I'll continue when it resolves" and stopped without pushing — the orchestrator had to finish the commit + push by hand; and three separate workers were killed outright running a full emulator-backed `npm run test:unit`. Distinct from #753 (CI-*watching* monitor loops) — this is the worker's own *local* verification chain stranding it before it ever gets to CI (closes #757).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/ci-pitfalls.md` — the "Heartbeat emission around long-running commands" section now names `npm ci` on a large monorepo and a hook-wrapped `git commit` as watchdog-risk commands alongside the existing CI-babysitting examples; documents the `Bash` tool's own 120s default timeout as a distinct, earlier trap and prescribes passing an explicit bounded `timeout` (up to the tool's 600000ms cap); broadens the fragment's scope to explicitly cover issue-work's local install/hook/test steps rather than reading as fix-checks-only/fix-main-ci-specific; adds a "never open a Monitor / background-wait for a local install, hook, or test run and end your turn" callout naming this as the local-step instance of the existing #529 return-contract rule; and adds a "verify actual state before re-running anything" recipe for a step that was interrupted mid-flight.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — cross-references the strengthened `ci-pitfalls.md` guidance from the §4 dependency-bootstrap bullet (large-repo `npm ci`), from §4.6's local unit-test-gate step (emulator-backed suites), and from a new note ahead of §5's commit step (the slow-pre-commit-hook repro).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — unaffected; the existing 124-assertion suite still passes against the strengthened fragments, confirming no prior guidance was displaced.

Deliberately did **not** implement two of the issue's suggested fixes: committing with `--no-verify` to skip a duplicative pre-commit hook (this repo's `worker-preamble` unconditionally forbids `--no-verify` — see "Never `--no-verify`" — a local hook duplicating a CI gate is not an exception), and skipping the full local unit-test suite in favor of "let CI catch it" (that would reopen the exact CI-round-trip-waste gap issue #658's pre-push unit gate was added to close). Both are addressed instead by making the *existing* long-running-command machinery — bounded timeouts and the heartbeat loop — apply to these local steps, rather than working around the steps themselves.

### 2.10.4 — 2026-07-16

A prior crashed `/do-work` session left ~25 stale `agent-*` worktrees behind; setup's step-3b orphan sweep reaps every one whose lock-holding PID is dead, but several stayed classified `peer-alive` indefinitely — PID liveness alone can't distinguish a genuine live peer from a dead prior-session PID the OS has since recycled onto an unrelated running process. Because the mid-session pre-dispatch reaps (`steady-state.md` §2d for fix-checks, `drain.md`'s #370 for fix-rebase) call the exact same classifier, they hit the identical false-peer verdict on every retry, so re-dispatched workers repeatedly bailed `head branch ... locked in another worktree` and the branch stayed locked until a human manually ran `git worktree unlock` + move-aside + `prune` (closes #755).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/worktree-reap.sh` — `classify-lock` now applies a second, mtime-based gate before committing to `peer-alive` (mirroring the existing PID-liveness + 30-min-mtime "two gates" pattern already used for orphan session files, issue #253): a lock whose file is older than a staleness floor (default 60 min; `SHIPYARD_PEER_LOCK_STALE_MIN` env var or `--peer-stale-min <N>` flag) now classifies as the new `peer-alive-stale` value instead, which every existing exact-string `= "peer-alive"` check across the codebase already treats as safe-to-reap — no per-call-site changes needed, so setup 3b and both mid-session pre-dispatch reaps pick up the fix automatically.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — step 3b's prose updated to document the staleness corroboration and the `peer-alive-stale` reap path.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-reap.test.sh` — new regression coverage for `peer-alive-stale`: fresh-lock override activation, the 90-minute #755 repro, floor precedence (flag over env var over default), and bad-usage/malformed-input cases.

### 2.10.3 — 2026-07-16

Two `fix-checks-only` dispatches against the same PR returned success while the PR's required check rollup was still red: one fixed only `Lint & Typecheck`, saw `Unit Tests` (required) still `FAILURE`, and reasoned the other checks were "pre-existing/unrelated" before returning `green`; another ran the unit suite locally three times, all green, and returned `noop: already green` while CI's `Unit Tests` check stayed `FAILURE`. The orchestrator's trust-but-verify spot-check caught both, but the worker's own return contract shouldn't have allowed either claim. The pre-existing #416 gate already required "the overall rollup is all-green," but its parenthetical — "no *other* check regressed" — was ambiguous enough to read as "a check that was already broken before my dispatch doesn't count against me," which is exactly the reasoning both workers used (closes #754).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — up-front definition at the top of the return contract: `green` / `noop: already green` mean the PR's FULL check rollup is green on the pushed head SHA, never "my assigned check passed" and never "it passes locally." Rewrites the ambiguous "no *other* check regressed" bullet in the named-failing-check re-verification gate to explicitly reject the pre-existing/unrelated-check exemption reading, and to state the entire rollup (not just the named check) must be green before a `green` return. Strengthens the `noop:` bullet to forbid deriving "already green" from any number of local test runs. Adds a matching `## Don't` bullet.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-checks-full-rollup-required.test.sh` — new regression coverage pinning the full-rollup requirement, the disclaimed ambiguous reading, the pre-existing-check coverage, the local-run prohibition, and the corresponding `Don't` bullet, citing the #754 repro PR verbatim.

### 2.10.2 — 2026-07-16

Two dispatched workers, instead of returning their terminal contract string, started their own `Monitor`/poll loop to watch CI and sat there — a `fix-main-ci` worker re-ran main's failed E2E jobs then spun up "a clean Monitor polling the CI rerun every 60s" and burned ~300k tokens across two re-fires before being killed, and a separate issue-work worker rebased + pushed then returned "the background waiter will notify me… Waiting" instead of a terminal string. Both duplicated the orchestrator's own cheaper CI watch at per-worker model cost and stranded their dispatch slot. The existing `#529`/`#707` rules already forbade this in prose, but the anti-pattern wasn't called out as its own explicit DON'T bullet anywhere a worker would scan for it (closes #753).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — new explicit bullet in "Return-contract discipline" naming the exact anti-pattern (opening a `Monitor`/poll loop to watch CI to completion instead of returning) and citing the #753 repro verbatim, alongside the existing #529/#707 rules.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/{issue-work,fix-main-ci,fix-rebase,fix-failing-prs-batch,investigate,fix-checks-only}.md` — matching `## Don't` bullets in every per-mode spec, each pointing back to the shared preamble rule and naming the mode-specific correct return path.

### 2.10.1 — 2026-07-16

A `/shipyard:do-work` worker ran `pkill -9 -f "playwright test"` to clean up what it believed were its own leftover local processes, but the target repo runs GitHub Actions on self-hosted runners installed on the same physical host the worker was operating on. Nothing in the worker's context distinguishes "processes I spawned" from "processes the self-hosted runner spawned" — both run as the same user, on the same host, under identical process names — so the pattern kill matched the runner's in-flight CI and killed two E2E shards of the very PR the worker was trying to get green, a silent violation of the never-cancel-CI rule the worker had no way to know it broke (closes #751).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — new "Never run a broad process kill" hot-core section (same tier as the worktree-isolation rules): forbids `pkill`/`killall`/pattern-fed `kill`, explains the self-hosted-runner hazard, tells workers to track and kill only PIDs they themselves spawned, and gives a cheap self-hosted-runner-detection snippet (`gh api .../actions/runners`, an `actions-runner*` home-directory check).
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/refuse-broad-process-kill.sh` — new `PreToolUse` `Bash`-matching hook mechanically blocking `pkill`, `killall`, and `kill` invocations fed PIDs from a `pgrep`/`ps | grep` pattern lookup, mirroring the "stick" pairing `enforce-worktree-isolation.sh` and `enforce-edit-scope.sh` already use for their prose rules. `kill <literal-pid>` remains allowed — that's the safe form the new preamble section directs workers toward.
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/hooks.json` — wires the new hook into the `Bash` matcher alongside the existing `refuse-escape-symlink-commit.sh` / `guard-primary-checkout.sh` entries.
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/tests/refuse-broad-process-kill.test.sh` — new regression coverage for the hook's decision rules, including the literal-PID allow-path and the #751 repro command verbatim.

### 2.10.0 — 2026-07-13

The operator layer's docs claimed a blanket "running `/do-work` is itself standing authorization" for every browser-completable action, but a live session found that claim doesn't hold for `merge-pr` / `close-pr` items against a PR the session never opened — the Claude Code auto-mode permission classifier evaluated `gh pr close` on an inherited Dependabot PR independently and denied it *by name* ("an item the agent did not create this session"), with no defined fallback once denied. The classifier's denial is correct (closing someone else's PR is outward-facing and irreversible); the defect was a spec that over-promised an authorization the harness rightly withholds and left the refusal with no next step (closes #746).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — the Args section's standing-authorization sentence now scopes explicitly to **session-owned artifacts** (`session_prs`, issues the session dispatched against); adds the nineteenth orchestrator-state struct, `operator_denials` (mirroring `dispatch_denials` from #718), recording every operator action the classifier refused; the opening struct-count sentence updates from "eighteen" to "nineteen."
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate.md` — new "Scope of standing authorization" section defines session-owned vs. inherited third-party PRs and specs a single, session-scoped `AskUserQuestion` batching every pending inherited-PR `merge-pr`/`close-pr` item into one confirmation (never one round-trip per item); new "Operator action denied by the harness permission classifier" section mirrors #718's dispatch-denial discipline for operator actions — record the denial, at most one re-attempt (only to cite an explicit confirmation already on record, never to reword against the classifier), and a second denial degrades the item to a `needs-operator` hand-back rather than dropping it from `operator_queue`; the `close-pr`/`merge-pr` playbook and the Don't list cross-reference the new gate.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new `Operator denied (#746)` block in the end-of-session summary (mirrors `Dispatch denied (#718)`), read from `operator_denials`; silent by default, surfaces per-item outcome and the verbatim (local-only) denial text when non-empty.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — new regression coverage pinning the struct addition, the scope correction, the batched-confirmation mechanism, the denial-record/one-reattempt/hand-back branch, and the content-integrity boundary (classifier reasoning never reaches a public GitHub artifact).

### 2.9.9 — 2026-07-13

The `shipyard:worker-preamble` skill's step-0 cwd fail-fast (#486) only asserts a worker's cwd is anchored to its isolated worktree once, at dispatch start — but a session reproduced plain relative-path `Bash` calls resolving against the PRIMARY checkout *later* in the same dispatch, with no `cd` ever issued and step-0 having passed cleanly. Read-only misroutes only waste diagnostic budget (the repro burned real time chasing a test-count flip as flakiness before finding the cause), but a mutating command landing in the same misrouted slot would silently corrupt the user's primary checkout with nothing to catch it. The underlying cwd-persistence drift is a harness-level gap shipyard cannot fix at the source — the same class as #486's AC items 1/2 — so the fix is defensive anchoring in the worker's own commands rather than a root-cause patch (closes #748).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — new "Mid-session cwd anchoring" section: cache `WORKTREE_PATH` once confirmed valid, then re-derive **and** re-verify it (the same git-dir/git-common-dir inequality check from the step-0 fail-fast) immediately before every mutating command — `git add`/`commit`/`push`, `gh` mutations, file-destructive ops — anchoring explicitly via `git -C "$WORKTREE_PATH"` rather than trusting ambient cwd. The same anchoring is recommended (not mandatory) for read-only commands, since a misrouted read is recoverable but wastes time. Documents the underlying harness limitation with a pointer to #748's repro rather than attempting to fix it.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — new regression coverage asserting the section, its bail message, the mandatory-vs-recommended framing, and the `git -C "$WORKTREE_PATH"` anchoring pattern are all present.

### 2.9.8 — 2026-07-13

#743's `flush --reconcile` (PR #744) let a re-entrant dispatch's tokens land in the cost ledger by REPLACING an existing session record with a freshly-projected one — but it replaced unconditionally, with no check that the incoming record was actually an improvement. A `--reconcile` flush against a degraded or freshly re-`init`'d session file (empty `tokens.totals`, empty `issues_worked`/`prs_created`) silently overwrote a complete, correct ledger record with zeros, exiting 0 both times with no warning. Reproduced live in session `do-work-20260713T155328Z-32780`: a real $2.67 / 889k-token record was destroyed and only recoverable because the numbers were still in working memory (closes #745).

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/cost-history.sh` — `flush --reconcile` now MERGES the existing ledger record with the fresh projection (element-wise max on `tokens.*`/`estimated_usd`/`by_model`/`by_mode`, set union on `issues_worked`/`prs_created`/`unpriced_models`) instead of replacing it — max/union is monotonic-or-refuse by construction, so a degraded incoming projection can never shrink the existing record, while the ordinary re-entrant-dispatch case (fresh ⊇ existing) is indistinguishable from the old replace behavior. A genuine destructive replace is still available via the new `--force-replace` flag (requires `--reconcile`), which backs up `cost-history.jsonl` to `.bak.<ts>` first, matching the `reset` subcommand's posture. A loud stderr advisory fires when the merge detects a degraded-vs-populated conflict, even though the write itself succeeds safely.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/cost-history.test.sh` — new regression coverage: `flush --reconcile` against an empty/degraded session file over a populated existing record must not zero it (issues/PRs/tokens all preserved), the stderr advisory fires on that path, and `--force-replace` (a) requires `--reconcile`, (b) genuinely discards the existing record, and (c) backs up the ledger first.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — updates the re-entrancy safety-net paragraph to describe the new merge semantics instead of the old unconditional replace.

### 2.9.7 — 2026-07-13

End-of-session friction-issue filing (the maintainer convention of filing follow-up issues for spec gaps a session hit, before declaring the session done) ran *after* [`cleanup-summary.md`](plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md)'s cost-ledger flush and session-state teardown — but a freshly-filed issue is, by construction, workable, so [`dont.md`](plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md)'s "attempt-then-escalate" rule obligated the orchestrator to dispatch it anyway. That dispatch then ran with no durable session record to attribute its tokens to, so `~/.shipyard/cost-history.jsonl` silently under-reported the session by the cost of that last dispatch (closes #743).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — the termination assertion now states explicitly that any end-of-session friction/follow-up filing must happen *before* the four-step assertion runs, never during or after cleanup; the assertion's own step-4 fresh-fetch then picks up the newly-filed issue as a net-new candidate and loops back through dispatch instead of terminating around it.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — new rule codifying the filing-side corollary of "don't hand a workable issue to the human": don't file friction issues after the assertion has run.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — documents the re-entrancy safety net for any other path that still dispatches post-cleanup: `bump-tokens --allow-degraded-init` already self-heals a missing session file under the same session id (no change needed there), but a second `cost-history.sh flush` for that id now needs `--reconcile` to actually land the update — the plain call hits the ledger's own idempotency dedupe gate and silently skips.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/cost-history.sh` — `flush` gains a `--reconcile` flag: when the session id already has a ledger record, replace it with the freshly-projected cumulative one (via a temp-file + rename) instead of silently skipping. The routine, first-and-only flush call is unaffected — `--reconcile` is opt-in and reserved for a session's second flush.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/cost-history.test.sh` — new coverage: a plain re-flush still skips (unchanged behavior), and `--reconcile` replaces the record in place and picks up tokens bumped into the session between the two flushes.

### 2.9.6 — 2026-07-13

`hooks/guard-primary-checkout.sh` — the primary-checkout guard hook — existed fully implemented but was never wired into `hooks.json`, making it dead code, and even once wired its Bash-scope gate only recognized `git commit`, letting `git checkout` / `switch` / `reset` / `branch -D` slip past uncaught. A dispatched issue-work worker's `git checkout -B` against the PRIMARY checkout during session `do-work-20260713T155328Z` (working #734) went completely unenforced as a result — the worker happened to self-correct, but the backstop that should have caught it wasn't running (closes #741).

- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/hooks.json` — registers `guard-primary-checkout.sh` under both the `Edit|Write|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit` matcher and the `Bash` matcher, appended alongside the existing hooks on each (hooks compose).
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/guard-primary-checkout.sh` — widened the Bash-scope gate from `git commit` only to the full write-class git surface: `commit`, `checkout`, `switch`, `reset`, `branch -d`/`-D`/`-f` (deletion only), `merge`, `rebase`, `cherry-pick`, `stash`, `clean`. Read-class git (`status`, `log`, `show`, `diff`, `ls-remote`, `rev-parse`, `worktree list`, `fetch`) keeps falling through to exit 0, and the guard still keys off the calling `cwd` (not the command string alone) — the sanctioned `git -C <primary> checkout <default>` corrective write from [#387](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/387)'s branch-leak guard runs from the orchestrator's own worktree cwd and stays unaffected. The header/scope comments and the now-inaccurate "not registered in hooks.json" claims are updated to match.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/init.md` — the `/shipyard:init` opt-in flow (issue #482) now documents itself as a **user-level** wiring layered on top of the plugin-level default, not the exclusive registration path it used to be.
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/tests/hooks-json.test.sh` — new assertions that `guard-primary-checkout.sh` is registered under both matchers, and that the `Bash` matcher composes it alongside the pre-existing `refuse-escape-symlink-commit.sh` rather than replacing it.
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/tests/guard-primary-checkout.test.sh` — new deny-list / allow-list coverage for the widened write-class git surface, plus a regression case pinning the #387 sanctioned corrective write as unaffected.

### 2.9.5 — 2026-07-13

#736's fix (PR #738) made issue-work.md's branch step collision-aware: on a worktree-name collision it falls back to a differently-named LOCAL branch (`do-work/issue-<N>-<timestamp>`) while still pushing to and opening the PR against the canonical REMOTE branch `do-work/issue-<N>`. That divergence was invisible to the orphan-triage sweep in `00-config-worktree.md`'s step 3c, which did an exact-match extraction and lookup on the local worktree's branch name — a suffixed branch would extract a garbage issue number, miss the real remote push, push a second spurious remote branch, and then open a duplicate PR (closes #739).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — step 3c now extracts the issue number with a permissive regex (`sed -E 's|^do-work/issue-([0-9]+).*|\1|'`) and resolves a `canonical_branch` independent of the local worktree's branch name; the remote-branch check, push, PR-head lookup, and `gh pr create --head` all key off the canonical name. The row-5 stale-assign check's "does a worktree already handle this issue" test uses the same permissive extraction so a suffixed worktree is still recognized as already-handled.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — new regression section pinning the permissive extraction, the canonical-branch resolution, and the row-5 recognition fix.

### 2.9.4 — 2026-07-13

Git enforces one-worktree-per-branch, so a re-dispatched issue-work worker's `git checkout -B do-work/issue-<N>` hard-failed whenever a prior (usually reaped) dispatch for the same issue had left its worktree on disk still holding that branch name — a `locked` worktree looks identical whether it's a dead scaffold or a live concurrent worker's, so the spec gave the worker no safe way to tell and no defined recovery, leaving it to improvise exactly where an improvisation (removing another worktree, force-deleting its branch) could destroy unpushed work (closes #736).

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — §3's branch step is now collision-aware: it attempts the normal `git checkout -B` first, and on an `already used by worktree` failure falls back to a collision-free LOCAL branch name while never touching the other worktree. §5's push and `gh pr create` calls now target the canonical `REMOTE_BRANCH` (`do-work/issue-<N>`) explicitly via `HEAD:refs/heads/<name>` and `--head`, so the PR — and the orchestrator's orphan triage — still resolve correctly even when the local checkout diverges. A new `Don't` bullet documents the guardrail.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — new regression section pinning the collision detection, the local/remote branch split, the never-touch-the-other-worktree rule, and the explicit `--head` on PR creation.

### 2.9.3 — 2026-07-13

On an ungated repo shape, issue-work §6.a correctly makes a worker block on `gh pr checks --watch` and merge by hand only once green — but the return string that outcome reported was `auto-merge: merged-direct`, the exact same token `gh pr merge --auto` silently falling through to an immediate merge would report. Both events leave a PR at `state: MERGED` with `autoMergeRequest: null`, so a worker deriving the outcome from `gh pr view`'s post-merge snapshot alone can't tell "I watched checks and merged correctly" from "the detector mispredicted and `--auto` merged unverified" — the exact regression shape #716 exists to catch. In one session, 2 of 4 observed ungated-branch merges reported `merged-direct` for what was, on inspection, a correctly-gated manual merge, erasing the one signal that would reveal a real #716-class regression (closes #734).

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` — the canonical fragment now returns a distinct `auto-merge: gated-manual` token for the step-0.5 checks-watch-then-merge branch, with an explicit callout that the step-1.5 `merged-direct`/`merged-direct-ungated` categorization applies only to the step-1 `--auto` call and must never be reused for the manual-merge outcome.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — §6.a's green-checks merge now returns `gated-manual` directly and skips §7's categorization; §7 opens with a short-circuit note; §8 gains the `gated-manual` return line and a `Don't` bullet against relabeling it `merged-direct`.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-main-ci.md` and `fix-failing-prs-batch.md` — mirror the same `gated-manual` return line and disambiguation on their 7.a/8 steps.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/investigate.md` — the fixable-path return vocabulary table adds `gated-manual` alongside the existing outcomes.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — the auto-merge fragment summary names `gated-manual` and warns against collapsing it into `merged-direct`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/ungated-merge-gate-reachability.test.sh` — new section (I) pins the `gated-manual` token, its issue-#734 citation, and the explicit merged-direct exclusion across every site above.

### 2.9.2 — 2026-07-13

This repo's own committed `shipyard.config.json` asked for `concurrency.default: 2` while its own merge shape is `ungated` (admin + zero required status checks) — exactly the shape step 1.3's detector warns produces a steady-state manifest/CHANGELOG leapfrog at C≥2, since the first PR to direct-merge advances `main` and re-DIRTYs every sibling regardless of version coordination. The warning printed the recommendation but nothing enforced it, so avoiding the collision depended on the orchestrator LLM noticing the contradiction and overriding the committed config by judgment on every single run (closes #733).

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — step 1.3 now resolves an `EFFECTIVE_CONCURRENCY` value (explicit `--concurrency` CLI flag, else the repo's committed `concurrency.default`, else the built-in default of `1`) and clamps it to `1` whenever the detector's verdict is `ungated` AND no explicit CLI flag was passed — an operator-supplied `--concurrency N` always overrides the clamp. Step 1.5's `session-state.sh init` now consumes `$EFFECTIVE_CONCURRENCY` instead of independently re-reading the CLI arg.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — the `--concurrency` flag doc now documents the clamp behavior.
- `shipyard.config.json` — this repo's own `concurrency.default` corrected from `2` to `1`, matching its actual `ungated` merge shape (necessary on its own, but the step 1.3 clamp is what keeps the trap from re-arming on any repo with the same shape).

### 2.9.1 — 2026-07-13

The orchestrator's own worktree reap fell through to the destructive-looking `--force` path on literally every session, defeating #712's non-force-first safety property for the one worktree guaranteed to need reaping every run (closes #729). The cause: step 0.55 stashes `.shipyard-session-id` — an untracked bookkeeping file — into the orchestrator worktree for the session's lifetime, so the tree is never actually clean by the time end-of-session cleanup tries the plain, non-force `git worktree remove`; git refuses `remove` on any dirty tree by design, so the `--force` fallback fired unconditionally. #712's mitigation (try non-force first, since a bare `--force` reads as `[Irreversible Local Destruction]` to Claude Code's auto-mode classifier) was dead code for this specific worktree from the day it shipped.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — step 6 now deletes the session-id stash immediately before attempting the remove (nothing downstream reads it — steps 7/7.5/8 all use the already-known `<session-id>` template value, never a file re-read), and routes the actual remove through `worktree-reap.sh`'s evidence-gated `reap` action instead of a raw `git worktree remove --force` fallback — matching every other reap call site in the file and giving this reap an audit-log line (`~/.shipyard/reap-audit.jsonl`) for the first time.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — new regression assertions pin the stash-delete, the evidence-gated routing, the absence of the old raw `--force` call, and the ordering (stash deleted strictly before the reap call runs).

### 2.9.0 — 2026-07-13

**`models.*` is a real config surface now — it was documented, schema-validated, settable, tested, and read by absolutely nothing** (closes #727). A user could run `/shipyard:config set models.issue_work claude-sonnet-4-6`, watch it validate, see it in `/shipyard:config show`, and still have every issue-work dispatch run on Opus — because model selection lived exclusively in each agent shim's `model:` frontmatter, which no consumer repo can override, and no dispatch path ever read the key. This repo's own committed config asked for Sonnet; all five issue-work dispatches of one session ran on `claude-opus-4-8` anyway, ~5x the cost, with no warning anywhere. A key that silently no-ops is worse than no key: the config surface exists precisely to make the #157 per-mode model pins overridable, and it didn't.

The fix honors the config rather than deleting the surface (option (a) on #727 — per-repo model policy is exactly what a config layer is for). Every `Agent` dispatch now resolves `models.<mode>` from the merged config and passes it as the tool's `model` parameter, which takes precedence over frontmatter; frontmatter stays the **default**. The resolution is one script, not prose restated at six dispatch sites — the same anti-drift shape as `detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh` (#716). It also bridges a type mismatch the config surface quietly implied but never had to honor: the `Agent` tool's `model` parameter is an **enum** (`opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku`/`fable`) while the config (and the pricing table) speak in concrete ids like `claude-sonnet-4-6`, so the raw value can't be passed through — it's mapped onto its model family. Posture is fail-open: an unset key, an unreadable layer, or an unrecognized id resolves to empty, the `model` parameter is omitted, and the shim's frontmatter default applies — a typo must never fail a dispatch or silently substitute a wrong-tier model.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/resolve-dispatch-model.sh` — **new script**, the single executable source of truth for "which model does this mode dispatch on". Reads `models.<mode>` from the merged config, maps the id onto its Agent-tool family alias (concrete ids, dated ids like `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, and bare aliases all resolve), accepts hyphenated *or* underscored mode names, and prints empty (exit 0, with a stderr warning) on anything it can't resolve.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — new per-dispatch model-resolution rule under the `subagent_type` routing table: every dispatch site calls the resolver and sets `model` on the `Agent` call, omitting it when resolution is empty. The frontmatter column of the routing table is relabeled as the **default** it always was.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/07-pool-fill.md` — the initial-pool-fill dispatch site resolves the model too, so the very first wave of workers honors the config rather than only step C's replacements.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/resolve-dispatch-model.test.sh` — **new suite** (41 assertions). The regression guard the issue asked for: a configured `models.<mode>` must be the *effective* dispatch model, across the repo / local / user-global (`default_models.*` alias) layers and their precedence. Also pins the fail-open contract, and asserts the built-in defaults still mirror each shim's frontmatter pin family-for-family — so honoring the config is a behavioral no-op on a repo that sets no override, and can't silently re-tier anyone.
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.user-config.schema.json` — adds the missing `default_models.investigate` key, so the user-global layer covers all six worker modes rather than five.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/config.md` — documents that `models.<mode>` is honored at dispatch time, what the family matching accepts, and that an unrecognized id is a warned no-op rather than a failure.

### 2.8.1 — 2026-07-13

**An unknown model no longer costs a confident $0.00 — the cost ledger now distinguishes "nothing to charge" from "I don't know what to charge"** (closes #728). The USD estimate rides a hand-maintained pricing table (`PRICING_JQ`), and the documented fallback for a model it had never heard of was *zero* — not an error, not a warning, not even a null. So the table going stale (which it does on every model release) degraded **invisibly**: `claude-opus-4-8`, the current default session model, was simply absent, and six of seven dispatches in one session were each booked at $0.00. A multi-dollar session reported **$0.72**, and — because end-of-session cleanup flushes to the persistent ledger — `/shipyard:cost report` silently under-reported by ~6x, permanently, for that record. #226 had fixed the *lookup* (dated suffixes and bare aliases used to miss); it left the silent-zero *fallback* in place.

The fix treats `$0.00` as a legitimate value that must never double as the error sentinel. An unpriced model is now **loud and structured**: token counts are still recorded (no data loss, and the call stays fire-and-forget so it can't fail a dispatch), but `bump-tokens` warns on stderr, adds the id to a session-level `.tokens.unpriced_models` set, and stamps `unpriced: true` on the `per_invocation` entry — and every downstream reader (the cost comment, the end-of-session summary, the persistent ledger, `/shipyard:cost report`) labels the USD figure a **LOWER BOUND** and names the offending model. The audit also caught a second instance of the same "confidently wrong number" class: the `claude-opus-4-7` / `4-6` rows still carried the pre-Opus-4.5 rate (`$15`/`$75`), **over**-reporting every Opus dispatch by 3x in the opposite direction. Opus-tier is `$5`/`$25`; both rows are corrected here.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/session-state.sh` — adds `claude-opus-4-8` (plus `claude-sonnet-5` / `claude-fable-5`) to `PRICING_JQ`; corrects the stale Opus rows to `$5`/`$25`; extracts the alias + longest-prefix lookup into a single `resolve_pricing_row` helper so the warning and the USD arithmetic can never disagree about whether a model was priced; records `.tokens.unpriced_models` + the per-invocation `unpriced` flag; surfaces the set through both `read-tokens` formats.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/cost-history.sh` — persists `unpriced_models` into each session's ledger record (without it, a flushed row is indistinguishable from a genuinely cheap session once the session file is reaped) and unions them across the report window; `report` tags the `Spend:` line `[LOWER BOUND]` and prints an advisory naming the models.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/pricing-coverage.test.sh` — **new suite** (24 assertions), the guard that stops this recurring: every model id shipped in the repo (the built-in `models.*` defaults **and** the effective config **and** every agent shim's `model:` frontmatter) must resolve to a real pricing row, so a new model can't ship into a $0 hole without turning CI red first. Also pins the loudness contract (warn / record / stamp / dedupe) and the non-regression side (a *known* model must not be flagged — the guard must not cry wolf).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/session-state.test.sh` — updates the USD assertions to Opus-tier pricing and asserts the unknown-model path now flags rather than silently zeroes.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new `Unpriced model(s)` end-of-session banner, deliberately distinct from the existing `Cost attribution` one (that means *price known, token breakdown unknown*; this means *tokens known, price unknown*).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/cost.md`, `CLAUDE.md` — document the lower-bound semantics and the "update `PRICING_JQ` when a model ships" obligation.

### 2.8.0 — 2026-07-11

**A dispatch the harness refuses is now a documented branch with a guardrail, instead of an undefined state the orchestrator improvises around** (closes #718). Claude Code's auto-mode permission classifier evaluates the orchestrator's own `Agent` dispatch call and can deny it outright — observed live when the dispatch for #714 (an issue *about* offering to add an allow rule to `.claude/settings.json`) was refused, because the prompt described the work in the vocabulary of the live settings file rather than the vocabulary of the plugin spec file the worker would actually edit. The dispatch tool call never happens: no worker, no worktree, no `agent_id`, and no completion notification — so the spec's enumeration of a dispatch's outcomes (`shipped` / `blocked` / `noop` / errored) had no case for it at all. The slot silently went unfilled and the target quietly stopped being worked. This is adjacent to but distinct from #712 (a *worker's* Bash call denied by the same classifier, shipped in #713); this is the orchestrator's dispatch call itself being refused.

The load-bearing part of the fix is the **guardrail, not the plumbing**. The tempting default under a denial is to keep rewording the prompt until it gets through — and that is precisely the bypass the classifier exists to prevent; an orchestrator that learns *"launder the prompt until it's allowed"* has defeated the safety boundary on every future dispatch, not just this one. So the branch makes the distinction explicit and testable: **exactly one** re-dispatch is permitted, and only to *correct* a prompt that **overstated** the work's blast radius (it implied writing a live permission file when the deliverable is plugin source in an isolated worktree) — the reframe must make the prompt **more accurate**, never merely more permissive-sounding. Iterating wording, softening language, retrying when the work genuinely *does* require the denied capability, and routing around the deny (a different `subagent_type`, a split dispatch, inline execution on the orchestrator thread — classifier policy targets *effects*, not tool names) are all forbidden. A **second** denial stops and hands back to the human; there is never a third attempt.

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — **new section** "Dispatch denied by the harness permission classifier", the single home for the branch (both dispatch sites already consult this file). Carries the four steps: record the denial; the one permitted accuracy-correcting re-dispatch, gated on the operator being able to name a *specific factual inaccuracy* in the original prompt ("the classifier was probably being over-cautious" is not one); the explicit prohibition list; and the two-denials hand-back. Also adds the ordering rule that makes the branch well-defined — the `.in_flight` write-through runs only **after** the `Agent` call is accepted, so a refused dispatch leaves no phantom slot to reap.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — new `dispatch_denials` orchestrator-state struct (18th), recording `{ target, mode, denied_at, denial_text, attempt, outcome }` per refusal.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new `Dispatch denied (#718):` block in the end-of-session summary (omitted when no denial fired). Without it a refusal is invisible; a `handed back (needs-human-review)` outcome is the one that wants operator attention. The verbatim denial text is printed **here** — the summary and the local HTML report are the only surfaces it belongs on.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` (step C) + `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/07-pool-fill.md` (step 7) — both dispatch sites now route a refused `Agent` call to the branch, and a denial consumes a *candidate*, not the *slot*: the turn continues to the next candidate, and a parked slot must name the denial in its `idle_reason`.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — three prohibitions: don't iterate prompt wording against the classifier; don't let a denied dispatch silently cost a slot; and don't post the classifier's reasoning to a public GitHub artifact. The last preserves the content-integrity boundary the worker already operates under (#341) — the hand-back issue comment states the fact and the outcome only, never quoting, paraphrasing, explaining, or disputing *why* the harness denied the dispatch.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — 16 assertions covering the branch's existence and reachability from both dispatch sites, each individual prohibition (the guardrail is what regresses first if someone "simplifies" this later), the record-and-surface path, and the no-phantom-slot ordering.

### 2.7.3 — 2026-07-11

**A CI verification that observed nothing no longer reports green — an empty run list is now `unknown`, and the full-SHA requirement is enforced by the code instead of being remembered by the worker** (closes #717). `gh run list --commit <sha>` matches only on a **full 40-char SHA**; an abbreviated SHA silently matches zero runs and gh exits 0 with an empty list. Any verification shaped as *"count the non-success runs, assert the count is 0"* therefore **passes on the empty set** — reporting green while having observed nothing at all. That is the dangerous direction of failure: it converts "I could not verify" into "verified green", and every downstream `checks: green` claim in the return contract inherits a verdict that was never actually taken. Self-reported by a worker that hit it while verifying `main` after a merge, and independently hit by the same session's orchestrator.

The fix generalizes rather than patching each call site, because the theme is broader than one gh flag: **an absence-assertion that cannot distinguish "nothing bad found" from "nothing looked at" is not a check.** The same session produced two more instances of the family — GNU grep printing its binary-file notice instead of matching lines (so a scanner silently extracted nothing and read it as clean), and `grep -P` erroring out unsupported on macOS with the error swallowed, false-negativing the check.

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/assert-ci-green.sh` — **new.** The single executable answer to "did CI actually pass for this commit/branch?" It resolves `--commit <ref>` to the full SHA *itself* (via `git rev-parse`, so a caller passing `HEAD` or a short hash cannot reintroduce the trap), refuses to answer green on an empty result set, and aggregates at per-workflow / latest-completed-run granularity (skipping supersession-`cancelled` runs, per #261/#333). Four verdicts, exit-code-encoded: `green` (0), `red` (1), `unknown` (2), `pending` (3). **`unknown` is deliberately its own outcome** — folding it into green is the bug, and folding it into red would make every transient read failure look like a broken build. Any signal that can't be read resolves toward `unknown`, never green.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/ci-pitfalls.md` — new hot section "An absence-assertion that observed nothing is not a pass", carrying the three rules (full SHA always; `total == 0` is `unknown`; `unknown` is not a verdict to act on), the bad-vs-good code pair, and the grep/`grep -P` cousins. Indexed from `SKILL.md`'s fragment table, whose load-trigger now names *any* mode that verifies a CI result.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-main-ci.md` — the step-1 green-main pre-flight was itself a hand-rolled absence-assertion (a single `--status completed --limit 1` read across all workflows). It now calls the script, and **only exit 0 permits `noop: main already green`** — a `pending` or `unknown` read proceeds with the fix on the dispatch's premise rather than declaring the red branch healthy.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — the named-failing-check re-verification gate (#416) had a latent vacuous pass: "every named check came back SUCCESS" is *vacuously true* over an empty match set, so a typo'd or renamed check name would satisfy the gate while observing nothing. The gate now also requires `named_checks | length` to equal the number of checks recorded as failing at dispatch.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — the release-train deploy watch is the one live `--commit` call site; it now asserts the merge SHA is a full 40 hex chars before the lookup, so an empty result there means "no deploy workflow" rather than "we looked at nothing."
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/assert-ci-green.test.sh` — **new**, 22 assertions. Its load-bearing case is a **negative control**, per the issue's own guidance: it deletes the marker-delimited empty-set guard from a copy of the script and asserts the mutant then *does* vacuously report green on an empty run list. A guard whose removal changes nothing isn't being tested — a test that passes both with and without the fix would itself be an instance of this very bug.

### 2.7.2 — 2026-07-11

**The remaining six `gh pr merge --auto` call sites now run the ungated-merge detector — #716 had fixed only issue-work, and the other six were still landing unverified code on `main`** (closes #720). #716 extracted the two-shape ungated-admin-direct-merge condition into one executable script and restructured `issue-work.md` §6 into §6.a (guard) / §6.b (arm) so the guard structurally precedes the merge — but issue-work was one of *seven* call sites. On this repo's live shape (`viewer=ADMIN, allow_auto_merge=true, required_checks=0` ⇒ `ungated`, re-verified before fixing) the other six fired `--auto` with no guard, which is not a queue but an **immediate merge**. The bug bit twice more in the same session that filed this issue: PR #719 (the #716 fix itself) merged `merged-direct` and landed a red regression on `main` — a raw control byte in `CHANGELOG.md` (such a byte must always be written as visible escape text — the four characters backslash-u-0-0 followed by the hex digits — never emitted raw) made GNU grep treat the file as binary and silently broke the monotonicity gate on CI while passing locally on macOS — and the `fix-main-ci` PR #723 that cleaned *that* up **also** merged ungated. `fix-main-ci` was the sharpest site: its entire job is restoring a red default branch, so it could land its own unverified fix with no gate, which is how a red-main window compounds instead of closing.

The fix routes every site through the same script (`scripts/detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh`) — no new prose copy of the condition anywhere, since prose drift across two copies is precisely what caused #716. A blanket "always wait when the viewer is admin" was **rejected**: on a ruleset-protected repo (admin + required checks) `--auto` genuinely queues, so an unconditional `--watch` would burn a worker slot for a full CI duration for nothing. Instead each site is decided by **whether the caller can afford to block**:

- **Worker sites re-create the gate by blocking** — they own a dispatch slot, so a `--watch` stalls nobody. `agents/issue-worker/fix-main-ci.md` and `agents/issue-worker/fix-failing-prs-batch.md` gain a §7.a (guard) / §7.b (arm) split mirroring issue-work's, blocking on `gh pr checks --watch` and merging only if green; each mode's "Don't `--watch`" rule is reconciled rather than left to contradict the new guard. `agents/issue-worker/investigate.md`'s auto-merge shorthand is tightened so `trusted` no longer reads as "type `gh pr merge --auto`" — the trust gate and the ungated-merge gate are orthogonal.
- **Orchestrator-turn sites defer instead of blocking** — `commands/do-work/inline-trivial.md` §E, the `commands/do-work/steady-state.md` A.0.5 crash-recovery re-arm, the `commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` setup-3c orphan-recovery re-arm, and the `commands/do-work/drain.md` release-train sweep all run on the orchestrator's own turn, where a multi-minute block would stall the dispatch loop, every in-flight reconcile, and every other PR. On `ungated` they leave the PR **open and unarmed**.
- **New: the deferred-merge lander** (`commands/do-work/drain.md`) is where those unarmed PRs actually land. Without it, "leave it unarmed" would be a leak — nothing would ever merge the PR and drain would spin to its ceiling, a hang strictly worse than the ungated merge it replaced. The insight is that drain is *already* a poll loop, and on an ungated repo "arm auto-merge" and "merge it when the checks go green" are the same statement — so the queue is drain's own poll. It merges only **green** PRs (merging a green PR directly is the already-sanctioned `merged-direct` + `checks: green` case), and its candidate set is deliberately narrow: `shipyard`-labelled (admits session PRs *and* the orphan-recovery PR, excludes the user's hand-authored PRs — never surprise-merge a human's WIP), unarmed, not `blocked:ci`, and **not `needs-human-review`**. That last exclusion is load-bearing, not boilerplate: *unarmed* is two distinct states, and the external-author trust gate leaves PRs unarmed too — without the exclusion the lander would merge them on green and silently defeat it.
- **A third copy of the condition is deleted.** `commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` §1.3 carried its own ~55-line bash reimplementation of the two-shape rule (with its own `#479` normalize and `#645` ruleset-fallback history) — a third copy alongside the fragment and `issue-work.md`, and exactly the drift hazard #716 was filed for. It now calls the script and keeps only the operator warning, plus a call-site table showing where the behavioral gates live.
- `scripts/tests/ungated-merge-gate-reachability.test.sh` grows sections (E)–(H): every call site invokes the detector; the worker modes invoke it *before* their `--auto` template (the same ordering trap as #716); the orchestrator-turn sites defer to the lander, the lander exists, and it preserves the trust gate; and setup no longer re-derives the condition from raw signals. Each new assertion was negative-controlled — removing the guard or the trust exclusion makes it fail.

### 2.7.1 — 2026-07-11

**The #598/#602 pre-merge check-wait is now reliably reached — its condition lives in one executable script instead of two prose copies that had drifted into contradicting each other** (closes #716). The guard was correct and tested, yet PR #715 still admin-direct-merged while its CI was `IN_PROGRESS`, while its sibling PR #713 — same session, same repo shape — correctly held for checks. The condition was never wrong; it was **unreachable** on one of the two paths. The rule existed as prose in *both* `skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` §0.5 (an **on-demand** fragment — correct: fires on admin AND (`allow_auto_merge: false` **OR** zero required checks), and says explicitly "Do NOT skip on `ALLOW_AUTO_MERGE == true` alone") and `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` §6 (loaded by **every** issue-work worker — wrong in three compounding ways). Whichever copy a worker happened to read decided whether the gate fired at all: the #713 worker loaded the fragment; the #715 worker followed issue-work.md, concluded *"auto-merge isn't enabled on the repo"* (it is — `allow_auto_merge: true`), and merged ungated. Verified before fixing: running the detector live against this repo yields `viewer=ADMIN, allow_auto_merge=true, required_checks=0` ⇒ `ungated`, so the guard *should* have fired on #715. The three defects in issue-work.md §6, all now closed:

- **Ordering trap.** §6 opened with "arm auto-merge as usual:" and a fenced `gh pr merge --auto --merge` block, and only *then* said "**First** run the pre-check." A worker executing commands as it read had already merged before reaching the guard. §6 is now split into **§6.a (run the detector)** and **§6.b (arm auto-merge — only when §6.a returned `gated`)**, so the guard structurally precedes the merge and the `--auto` command appears only inside the gated branch.
- **Shape-2 omission.** §6's prose stated the trigger as an AND of all three signals (shape 1 / #438 only), omitted shape 2 (#465) entirely, and named *"repo allows auto-merge"* as a **skip** condition — the exact inverse of the fragment's rule, and on this repo (`allow_auto_merge: true`) an affirmative instruction to skip the wait.
- **Explanation drift.** §6/§7/§8 hard-coded *"because `allow_auto_merge: false`"* as the cause of a `merged-direct` outcome — verbatim what the #715 worker echoed back as its reasoning, proving it was reciting the doc rather than reading the API. The parentheticals now name both shapes, and §7 explicitly flags that false attribution as the #716 tell.

Rejected the issue's suggested candidate 3 ("make the check-wait unconditional whenever the viewer is admin") with evidence: it would over-gate ruleset-protected repos (admin + required checks configured, e.g. `lightwork`'s `main`), where `--auto` genuinely queues — forcing a `--watch` there would block the worker for the full CI duration and tie up its concurrency slot for no gain. That row is pinned in the new truth table. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/detect-ungated-admin-direct-merge.sh` — **new.** The single executable source of truth for the two-shape decision. `<owner/repo>` mode reads the three signals live (including the #645 ruleset-aware fallback, which checks *only* `required_status_checks` and not `pull_request` — gating on the latter would over-gate this repo's shape and falsely skip the wait) and prints `ungated`/`gated`; `--decide <perm> <allow_auto_merge> <required_checks>` exposes the pure logic hermetically. Unreadable signals fail **safe** (toward waiting): waiting needlessly costs one worker's time, not waiting lands a red commit on `main` with no gate at all.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — §6 split into §6.a/§6.b (guard before merge); the guard is now a script call, not a condition the worker re-derives; shape-1-only skip language removed; `merged-direct` explanations in §6/§7/§8 corrected to name both shapes.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` — §0.5 now invokes the same script as its normative mechanism and names it the single source of truth; the existing two-shape prose is retained as the reference *explanation*, explicitly not a second implementation to hand-execute.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/ungated-merge-gate-reachability.test.sh` — **new**, 25 assertions: the detector's full decision truth table (the #465/#715 shape first — if that row ever flips to `gated`, #716 is back), the ordering guard (detector must precede any executable `gh pr merge --auto` in §6), the absence of the shape-1-only skip prose, and cross-file confirmation that both call sites invoke the one script.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version bump to 2.7.1.

### 2.7.0 — 2026-07-11

**`/shipyard:init` can now pre-authorize the worktree-reap commands, removing a classifier denial as a possibility rather than merely surfacing it** (closes #714). This is the fourth remedy from #712, deliberately carved out of #713 because it writes to the user's **harness config** (`.claude/settings.json`) rather than shipyard's own reap machinery — a distinct blast radius that deserved its own review. #713 made the reap *more likely to be permitted* (plain non-force remove first, `--force` only behind an evidence gate) and made a denial *visible* (the `reaped-failed` audit action + the `report-unreaped` probe); neither *guarantees* Claude Code's auto-mode classifier permits the reap. An opt-in `permissions.allow` rule does. The premise was **verified against the Claude Code docs before building on it** — the issue rightly warned it is not obvious that an `allow` entry and the auto-mode classifier are the same gate. They are, subject to one carve-out that turns out not to bite: allow rules "resolve immediately, except writes to protected paths, which route to the classifier even when an allow rule matches" — and while `.claude/**` *is* a protected path (which would have sunk this, since shipyard's worktrees live at `.claude/worktrees/<name>`), **`.claude/worktrees` is explicitly carved out of the protected list** because it's where Claude stores its own git worktrees. Shipyard's worktrees therefore sit in the one `.claude/` subtree the exception doesn't cover, so the rule short-circuits the classifier as intended. That carve-out is load-bearing and is documented as the thing to re-check if this ever stops working. The offer is **opt-in, default off** with explicit consent required — an `allow` entry widens the permission surface, and silently widening it is precisely what the permission system exists to prevent. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/init.md` — new step 9.6 (worktree-reap allowlist): the consent prompt naming the exact rules and the permission-surface tradeoff, the idempotent `jq` merge that appends only missing entries to `permissions.allow` (preserving the user's existing entries and ordering, never touching `permissions.deny`), `--dry-run` support, and the verified-mechanics rationale with its two honest caveats — the rules gate *agent-typed* Bash calls and cannot introspect commands run inside `worktree-reap.sh`, and a compound command still routes to the classifier if any subcommand is unmatched, so this reduces the denial surface rather than eliminating it. New `--reap-allowlist <on|off>` and `--reap-allowlist-scope <global|repo>` flags; new interactive-prompt bullet; new `Don't` rule barring a permission-surface widening without consent; Related section updated.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-init-config.test.sh` — nine new spec-contract assertions pinning the flags, the three exact allow rules (`Bash(git worktree remove:*)` / `prune` / `unlock` — a typo'd rule is a silent no-op), the explicit-consent requirement, the `.claude/worktrees` protected-path carve-out, and the prohibition on touching `permissions.deny`.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version bump to 2.7.0.

### 2.6.3 — 2026-07-11

**Worktree reaps no longer reach for `--force` first, and a reap that doesn't happen is no longer silent** (closes #712). Every `/do-work` cleanup sweep bottomed out in `git worktree remove --force`, which Claude Code's **auto permission mode** classifier denies outright as `[Irreversible Local Destruction]` — and because each reap was fire-and-forget (`2>/dev/null || true`) inside a background subshell, the denial was indistinguishable from success. The whole worktree-cleanup subsystem (#280 / #284 / #513) silently no-ops for every user running unattended in auto mode; one repo accumulated five stale worktrees, every one the residue of an already-merged PR, and the count only grew because nobody could see it happening. Two fixes, per the issue's stated minimum: (1) **make the command look as safe as it is** — try the plain, non-destructive `git worktree remove` first (git itself refuses it on a dirty tree, which is the exact safety property the classifier protects) and escalate to `--force` only behind an evidence gate that establishes the force destroys nothing existing solely in that worktree; (2) **stop swallowing the failure** — a removal that doesn't happen now emits a `reaped-failed` audit action with a reason instead of a silent `reaped`, and a new `report-unreaped` post-sweep filesystem probe catches the case nothing else can (a classifier denial kills the whole Bash tool call, so no audit line is ever written — only an after-the-fact end-state assertion can see it). The end-of-session summary now pairs the leftover count with the one command that fixes it: `run /clean_gone`. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/worktree-reap.sh` — new `worktree_force_is_safe()` evidence gate (caller-supplied evidence / all commits on a remote-tracking ref / no commits beyond the default remote branch); `fast_worktree_remove()` slow-path ladder now runs plain `git worktree remove` before an evidence-gated `--force`, and takes an optional force-evidence argument; the `reaped` action emits the new `reaped-failed` variant (with `reason`: `unsafe-to-force-unpushed-work` | `worktree-remove-failed`) when the removal did not happen; `reap-session-worktrees` reports the verified end state (`unreaped: agent-<id>`) rather than printing `reaped:` before the removal ran; new `report-unreaped` subcommand; new `--force-evidence` flag; usage/docstrings updated.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` — new step 5.5 post-sweep verification (`report-unreaped` → `unreaped_worktrees`) with the classifier-denial rationale and the "don't swallow a denial" instruction; step 3.0 counts the new `unreaped:` status line; step 6's orchestrator-worktree reap uses the non-force-first ladder; the chat summary and HTML report gained a `Cleanup: <N> worktree(s) could not be reaped — run /clean_gone` line (omitted at 0).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — step 3c's orphan-abandon path uses the non-force-first ladder, with the `ahead -eq 0` check named as the preceding evidence that makes the escalation safe.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — step 3b's stale-worktree reap routes through `worktree-reap.sh reap` (evidence-gated + audited) instead of a raw `--force`, and counts `reaped_stale` from the verified end state with a new `unreaped_stale` counter.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md` — new rules: don't reach for `--force` as the first move; don't swallow a failed or denied reap.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-reap.test.sh` — seven new cases (78–84) covering `report-unreaped` (clean layout / leftovers with own-orchestrator + `.reap-dead-*` exclusions / bad usage), the `reaped-failed` audit line on a removal that can't happen, the `unreaped:` status line, `--force-evidence` acceptance, and a structural regression guard that the helper keeps at most one evidence-gated `--force` call site.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version bump to 2.6.3.

### 2.6.2 — 2026-07-11

**node-bootstrap guidance now installs nested, non-hoisted packages before running their gates** (closes #708). The `node-bootstrap` fragment told a worker to `npm ci` at the repo root and in the app workspace, but that is insufficient for a nested, non-hoisted package whose test/build suite resolves its own deps from its own `node_modules` — the canonical case being a Firebase `functions/` dir (its deps are deliberately not hoisted into the app's `node_modules`, so `npm run test:unit:functions` can't resolve `firebase-functions` until you `npm ci` inside `functions/` too). A functions worker on `mattsears18/lightwork` (session `session_01Hs4CqGT53F6kwVasHiyLnH`, #2444) burned tool calls diagnosing "cannot resolve firebase-functions" before finding the fix. The new guidance adds a focused, generic rule with the cheapest heuristic — if your change modifies files under a dir that owns its own `package.json`, `npm ci` there before running that package's gates — bounded to the nested packages your diff actually touches (not a full-tree sweep), and marked distinct from the root-husky/commit-hook install gap (#680). Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/node-bootstrap.md` — added a "Nested non-hoisted packages need their own install before their gates" section (cheapest heuristic, bounded-search scope, prefer-`npm ci`-in-the-nested-dir remediation, generic non-hardcoded framing, distinct-from-#680 note) plus a parallel exception pointer in the Dependency-bootstrap section's "When NOT to run" area.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — added regression assertions that the nested-non-hoisted-install rule (header, #708 citation, cheapest heuristic, bounded search, distinct-from-#680, generic framing) is present.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version bump to 2.6.2.

### 2.6.1 — 2026-07-11

**Worker return-contract clarification: commit locally-verified work; never gate the commit/push/PR on a CI result, and never *wait* on a background CI-watch** (closes #707). A worker on a CI-congested host (self-hosted runners draining a deep queue) finished its full implementation, verified it locally, spawned a background command to watch CI, and then stalled — returning a non-terminal narrative (`"I'll wait for the background waiter … before proceeding to commit"`) with the work left **uncommitted** on disk: no PR, no `shipped`/`blocked` return. The worker treated "wait for CI to confirm" as a *precondition for committing*, and the background watch could not report within the turn. This is distinct from #529 (which forbids using a backgrounded process *as the wait mechanism*); #707 forbids treating CI confirmation *as a commit precondition at all*. The fix adds a fourth universal rule to the worker return-contract making the terminal state explicit — **LOCAL gates (typecheck / lint / unit) + PR-opened + auto-merge-armed** — with CI confirmation reframed as the orchestrator's job, and strengthens the `§7 do-not-watch` guidance to `do-not-wait` (fire-and-forget or skip). The optional orchestrator-side auto-resume detection the issue mentions as a nice-to-have is **not** implemented here (see the PR body) — this is a focused worker-contract clarification. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — added a fourth bullet to the "Return-contract discipline" section: the terminal state is reached from LOCAL gates + PR-opened + auto-merge-armed; never gate the commit / push / PR-open on a CI result; CI confirmation is the orchestrator's job; the post-PR check-rollup read is a one-shot snapshot, never a wait; if a background CI-watch is spawned at all, run it fire-and-forget or skip it. Bumped the section's rule count from three to four.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` — reframed the step-2 check-rollup snapshot as a "one-shot read for the return string, never a wait", adding the do-not-*wait*-on-a-background-CI-watch prohibition (cross-referencing #707) alongside the existing don't-`--watch` rule.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — strengthened §7's "Do not `--watch`" to also forbid *waiting* on a background CI-watch (fire-and-forget or skip), and added a §8 return-section clause stating CI confirmation is not a precondition for returning — both citing #707.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` — added regression assertions that the #707 terminal-state rule is present in SKILL.md and auto-merge.md, and that issue-work.md's §7/§8 carry the do-not-wait / not-a-return-precondition guidance.
- `plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — version bump to 2.6.1.

### 2.6.0 — 2026-07-11

**Drop the per-entry PR number from the CHANGELOG convention and retire the entire `PR #TBD` backfill machinery** (closes #691). Per-entry PR numbers were never load-bearing — GitHub already resolves each `closes #N` to the PR that closed it — yet they were the root cause of a whole bug family: worker PR-number prediction (#690), the orchestrator's CHANGELOG-backfill machinery (#581 / #583) and its two mis-target hardenings (#700 / #704), plus one `do-work/changelog-backfill-<M>` PR per shipped PR (PR-volume churn + PR-number consumption that shifted worker predictions off-by-one). Per the maintainer's Option-3 decision, the number is dropped at the source: entries now cite `(closes #N)` — the issue only, no PR number, no placeholder — so there is nothing to backfill and the orchestrator runs no backfill at `shipped` reconcile. This very entry is authored under the new convention (no PR number, no `PR #TBD`) — the transition working as intended. Files touched:

- `CLAUDE.md` — the Release-process CHANGELOG rule changed from "reference the closed issue numbers + PR number inline" to cite `closes #N` (the issue) only, with no PR number and no `PR #TBD` placeholder.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — removed the step-4 `PR #TBD` placeholder mandate (added by #690); workers now write `(closes #N)` with no PR reference and no placeholder.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — deleted the entire A.1 `shipped`-reconcile CHANGELOG backfill block: the `PR #TBD` grep/sed, both write paths (direct Contents-API commit + the auto-merged `do-work/changelog-backfill-<M>` PR fallback), and the Scope-and-conditions docs.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/` — deleted `changelog-backfill-entry-anchor.test.sh`, `changelog-backfill-protected-branch.test.sh`, and `changelog-pr-ref-placeholder.test.sh`; replaced the backfill spec-coupling assertions in `do-work-split.test.sh` with a guard asserting no `PR #TBD` / `changelog-backfill` machinery remains in the do-work spec.
