### 2.5.2 — 2026-07-11

**Harden the A.1 CHANGELOG backfill to an exact-substring anchor — the line anchor still mis-targeted a meta-entry whose prose precedes its own placeholder** (closes #704; PR #705). The #700 fix changed the steady-state A.1 backfill from a file-global `sed "s/PR #TBD/PR #<M>/"` to a line-anchored `sed "/closes #<N>[;) ]/ s/PR #TBD/PR #<M>/"`, correctly targeting the reconciled entry's line. But `sed` still replaces the *first* placeholder token on that line — wrong when the entry's own summary prose mentions the token before its `(closes #<N>; PR #TBD)` anchor, which is exactly the shape of a CHANGELOG entry that is itself *about* the backfill machinery. The #704 repro (session `do-work-20260711T170603Z`): release 2.5.1's entry describes the #700 fix, so its title prose carried the token and the line-anchored sed resolved that mention instead of the real placeholder, leaving #700's own entry unresolved until a second corrective PR fixed it. The fix anchors on the exact placeholder substring `closes #<N>; PR #TBD` (both `<N>` and `<M>` known at `shipped` reconcile time), which matches the whole placeholder verbatim, ignores every prose token mention on the same line, and stays idempotent via the existing `updated != changelog_on_main` guard. Both write paths (direct Contents-API commit + auto-merged-PR fallback) share the single substitution, so one change covers both. Composes with #700 (line anchor), #691 (backfill volume/churn), and #690 (the placeholder contract this anchors on). Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — the A.1 shipped-block backfill sed changed from `sed "/closes #<N>[;) ]/ s/PR #TBD/PR #<M>/"` to `sed "s/closes #<N>; PR #TBD/closes #<N>; PR #<M>/"` (both the direct Contents-API commit path and the auto-merged-PR fallback path share this single substitution); the inline comment and the "Scope and conditions" bullet rewritten from "entry-anchored replacement" to "exact-substring-anchored replacement" with the #704 meta-entry rationale.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/changelog-backfill-entry-anchor.test.sh` — extended with a Layer-3 meta-entry regression: a fixture whose summary prose mentions the placeholder token before its own `(closes #<N>; PR #TBD)` anchor, asserting the exact-substring sed resolves only the placeholder and leaves the prose untouched, plus a control proving the old line-anchored sed mis-targets the prose and an idempotency re-run. Spec-coupling greps updated to the exact-substring form (and a negative assertion that the superseded line-anchored sed no longer runs).
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh` — the backfill spec-coupling assertion + commentary updated to require the exact-substring sed form.

### 2.5.1 — 2026-07-11

**Anchor the A.1 CHANGELOG `PR #701` backfill on the reconciled issue's own entry, not the first file-global occurrence** (closes #700; PR #701). The steady-state A.1 backfill (#581 / #583) resolved the `PR #TBD` placeholder with an un-anchored `sed "s/PR #TBD/PR #<M>/"`. `sed` without `g` replaces the first match *per line*, and since each release entry's placeholder sits on its own line, when two or more release PRs merged in the same window — leaving `main` carrying multiple pending `PR #TBD` placeholders at once — the backfill for one PR stamped its number onto a *sibling* entry belonging to a different PR, stranding the intended entry at `PR #TBD`. The #700 repro: PR #692's `2.3.10` entry stayed `PR #TBD` on `main` across sessions because a later backfill sed resolved a different topmost placeholder. The fix anchors the substitution on the entry whose summary line carries `(closes #<N>; PR #TBD)` — `<N>` is known at `shipped` reconcile time — so exactly the reconciled entry is resolved regardless of how many sibling placeholders pend, and the existing `updated != changelog_on_main` guard keeps the step idempotent. ### 2.5.0 — 2026-07-11

**Add the `marketing-auditor` — an evidence-anchored marketing / conversion auditor** (closes #695; PR #698). None of the existing auditors reviewed an app's marketing/growth surfaces: `seo-auditor` checks discoverability *plumbing* and explicitly refuses copy/positioning critique, and `web-ux-auditor` judges visual design — so "is the value proposition clear," "does the landing page have a conversion path," "is the store listing persuasive," and "is positioning consistent across surfaces" went un-audited. The new auditor (dimension `marketing`, label `audit:marketing`, `model: sonnet`) tours the landing/marketing site, App Store & Play listings, and in-app conversion touchpoints, filing GitHub issues for structural gaps only. The design center — mirrored from `seo-auditor`'s copy-critique refusal — is that every finding stays *evidence-anchored* (missing/broken CTA, value-prop absence, measurable cross-surface positioning divergence, funnel dead-ends, objective store-metadata gaps) and pure prose taste ("punch up this headline") is out of scope. As a first step the auditor reads the repo's brand/voice docs (`docs/brand/**`, `BRAND.md`, `docs/voice*.md`, `CLAUDE.md` brand sections) and treats them as binding constraints, so it never proposes copy that violates an opinionated repo's stated voice/posture — keeping it safe to run across arbitrary repos. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/marketing-auditor.md` — new auditor agent, mirroring the `seo-auditor` skeleton: untrusted-input contract, brand-doc deference pass, landing-surface / store-listing / cross-surface / in-app conversion passes, evidence-anchored rubric with an explicit taste-refusal `Don't` section, and `audit:marketing` filing.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/audit.md` — `marketing` added to the audit-type enumeration, a `marketing → shipyard:marketing-auditor` dispatch-table row (needs URL: yes), and inclusion in the URL-dependent `all` bucket.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/filing-github-issues/SKILL.md` — `marketing-auditor` / `audit:marketing` (`c5def5`) row added to the label auto-create table.
- `README.md` — the auditor-dimension prose and the `/audit` command listing gain a marketing entry.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/marketing-auditor.test.sh` — new regression test asserting the agent file, frontmatter (`model: sonnet`), required passes, taste-refusal + brand-deference discipline, `audit.md` wiring, and the filing-skill label row.

### 2.4.0 — 2026-07-11

**Default to the latest stable version when a worker introduces a new dependency, with an unconditional peer/SDK carve-out** (closes #694; PR #696). An agent writing code is a frequent introducer of new dependencies, and introduction time is the cheapest place to prevent version debt — a dep first added at a training-data-remembered stale version starts behind and only drifts further. The motivating repro: a two-month-old consumer repo (`mattsears18/lightwork`) had accumulated multi-major dependency debt, several packages caught only when they surfaced as CI failures or a shipped native crash (a `react` / `react-native-renderer` version skew). The new worker-contract rule establishes latest-stable-by-default for a NEWLY-added dependency, records the resolved version in the PR body, and carries the load-bearing carve-out that peer/SDK-constrained packages (React / React Native, `@react-native-firebase/*`, `expo-*`) must use the framework-required version instead — with `npx expo install <pkg>` preferred on Expo repos. Scoped to dependency *introduction* only, never auto-upgrading existing deps. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/node-bootstrap.md` — new `## Adding a NEW dependency — default to the latest stable version` section: latest-stable default + install recipe, the unconditional peer/SDK carve-out with detection guidance, the Expo-installer preference, the record-the-version-in-the-PR-body requirement, the `dependencies.new_dep_version` policy knob, and introduction-only scope. The fragment header's section-citation list gains the new section name.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — the on-demand-fragments index row for `node-bootstrap.md` gains the new section and its "introducing a new dependency" load trigger.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — step 4 (implement) gains a bullet pointing at the new fragment section where the worker may modify `package.json`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` + `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — new `dependencies.new_dep_version` config knob (enum `latest-stable` | `conservative`, default `latest-stable`); the peer/SDK carve-out is documented as unconditional and not disabled by `conservative`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh` + `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-config.test.sh` — regression assertions covering the new fragment section, the carve-out, the Expo-installer preference, the version-recording requirement, introduction-only scope, and the config knob's `latest-stable` default.

The orchestrator's CHANGELOG backfill (#581 / #583) matches the placeholder with a literal `grep -qF "PR #TBD"` at `shipped` reconcile time, but no worker spec documented that contract — so workers improvised the PR reference and did so inconsistently: some predicted a PR number (which the interleaved backfill/sibling PRs shift off-by-one), one wrote a non-canonical `PR #<PR>` shape the literal grep never matched. In the #690 repro three of five sequential release entries shipped wrong to `main` and needed a manual cleanup PR. The fix adds the worker-side mandate so the two sides are visibly one contract. ### 2.3.9 — 2026-07-11

**Resolve the absent-rationale-sink branch in `/optimize-markdown` and warn about illustrative / label-form `#NNNN` refs** (closes #678; PR #688). Step 5 listed provenance-relocation as the archetypal SAFE transform, but it assumed a rationale sink already exists — step 3 acknowledged the sink may be absent yet step 5 gave no instruction for that case, and `## Don't` forbade "inventing conventions," leaving an unresolvable branch (relocation is unperformable in any repo without a sink, and the two obvious resolutions were ambiguously-forbidden or lossy). The fix makes the absent case unambiguous: creating a sink is SAFE because a sink is a relocation *destination*, not a *loading* convention, and nothing loads it at runtime. It also warns against naive `#NNNN` stripping, which destroys refs that are the instruction's own illustrative examples (lightwork `CLAUDE.md:30`) or label forms whose removal forces a reword. Spec/docs only; no helper-script runtime change. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/optimize-markdown.md` — step 5 SAFE list gains an explicit "create a rationale sink when none exists" branch resolving step 3's absent-sink question; step 4 bloat-class-2 gains a warning that illustrative-example and label-form `#NNNN` refs are RISKY (not strippable provenance) with the lightwork `CLAUDE.md:30` worked example; the `## Don't` "don't invent a loading convention" bullet is tightened to distinguish a forbidden *loading* convention from a safe-to-create *relocation destination*.

### 2.3.8 — 2026-07-11

**Add a de-minimis floor to `/optimize-markdown` so a sub-1% safe set reports instead of opening a negative-value PR** (closes #677; PR #686). Step 6 said to apply the SAFE set and step 7 said to open a PR, with no floor — so on a repo where the mechanically-safe transforms are real but trivially small, following the contract literally churns the most-edited, most-conflict-prone files (e.g. `CLAUDE.md`, loaded every turn) to save a fraction of a percent, costing more in review + merge-conflict risk than the context it buys. Repro: `mattsears18/lightwork` session `7dbbb811`, where the only safe transform relocated 16 provenance parentheticals for a ~148-byte / 0.33% saving on a checkout with 15 active worktrees. The fix adds a `--min-saving KB|PCT` flag (default `max(2%, 2 KB)` of the eagerly-loaded surface) and a de-minimis gate stated in step 5 (partition) so the SAFE/RISKY split accounts for it: below the floor the command reports the measured saving and folds the declined sub-threshold transforms into the RISKY issue as a "cheap wins to bundle with the real fix" section rather than opening a PR or silently dropping them. The 256KB-over-`Read`-limit correctness split is exempt — it always warrants a PR regardless of bytes saved. Spec/docs only; no helper-script runtime change. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/optimize-markdown.md` — new `--min-saving` flag (Args + step 1 parse); de-minimis floor logic in step 5 (partition) with the 256KB-over-limit exemption; step 6/7 skip-below-floor gating; step 8 mandate to list declined transforms in the RISKY issue; step 9 report of the measured saving vs floor; new `## Don't` bullet "Don't open a PR whose entire saving is below the floor."

### 2.3.7 — 2026-07-11

**Drop the phantom "never edit `.github/workflows/`" clause from `do-work.md`'s phase-routing Don't-summary, so it no longer asserts a rule absent from the `dont.md` it points at** (closes #679; PR #685). The router summary in `commands/do-work.md` § phase routing listed "never edit `.github/workflows/`" as a dispatch-hygiene prohibition, but `commands/do-work/dont.md` — the file that section names as the load-bearing source of truth — contains no such rule anywhere in its Dispatch-hygiene block (its only `.github/workflows/` mention is the unrelated `label-event-audit.yml` security-boundary reference). The disagreement gated a whole class of work: an orchestrator trusting the summary would refuse to fix a red-CI `main` at all, while one trusting `dont.md` couldn't tell whether it had violated a load-bearing rule. `dont.md` is authoritative — shipyard's own `fix-main-ci` / `fix-failing-prs-batch` modes exist to repair CI, which on every GitHub repo lives in `.github/workflows/`, so a blanket ban would make those modes structurally unable to do their job. Fix per the issue's chosen branch: drop the clause from the summary rather than add a banned-with-carve-outs rule to `dont.md`. Repro: session `session_01Hs4CqGT53F6kwVasHiyLnH` on `mattsears18/lightwork`, top P0 `lightwork#2404` required editing `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Spec/docs only; no helper-script runtime change. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — removed the `never edit `.github/workflows/`` clause from the dispatch-hygiene item of the § phase-routing Don't-summary; the summary now matches `dont.md`'s actual Dispatch-hygiene bullets.

### 2.3.6 — 2026-07-11

**Teach the `worker-preamble` node-bootstrap fragment about root-owned commit hooks in non-workspace monorepos, so a worker stops pushing an empty branch when a rejected commit-hook fails** (closes #680; PR #684). In a monorepo whose app lives in a workspace sub-dir but whose husky / commitlint / lint-staged tooling is installed from the **root** `package.json` (not a workspace member), the fragment's sub-dir-only bootstrap guidance led a worker to `npm ci` only the app workspace and leave the root `node_modules` empty. `.husky/commit-msg` then resolved `npx --no commitlint` against the absent root deps, `git commit` was rejected, and — because the worker had already run `git push` — an empty branch was left on the remote (the orphan-branch-triage / `git branch -r` debris). Repro: session `session_01Hs4CqGT53F6kwVasHiyLnH` on `mattsears18/lightwork`, `lightwork#2388`. The fix adds a dedicated fragment section covering the three remedies the issue asked for: (1) bootstrap the repo **root** `node_modules` whenever the root owns the commit hooks — detected via a `.husky/` dir or a husky/commitlint/lint-staged declaration/config/`prepare` script in the root `package.json` — even when the code change is confined to a sub-dir; (2) an ordering rule to **never `git push` before the first `git commit` succeeds** (gating the push on the commit's exit status), bounding the blast radius of *any* commit-hook failure; (3) a diagnostic that a rejected commit with `.husky/` present is a missing-root-`node_modules` tell rather than a bad diff. Spec/docs only; no helper-script runtime change. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/node-bootstrap.md` — new `## Root-owned commit hooks in a non-workspace monorepo` section (root-bootstrap detection + remediation-ladder pointer, commit-before-push ordering rule, missing-`node_modules` diagnostic); intro sentence updated to enumerate the #680 guard; a root-hooks exception cross-reference added to the dependency-bootstrap "When NOT to run the check" note.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md` — added the new section to the `node-bootstrap.md` row of the on-demand-fragments index.

### 2.3.5 — 2026-07-11

**Harden the `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` re-export preamble so it can't resolve to a stale `.bak` marketplace copy** (closes #681; PR #682). The step-0.3 idempotent fallback that every `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/*.sh` call routes through globbed `marketplaces/*/plugins/shipyard | head -1` when the harness leaves the env var empty. Because `ls -d` sorts and `.` (`0x2E`) sorts before `/` (`0x2F`), a sibling `shipyard.bak` (or `.old`/`.orig`/`.disabled`) directory won `head -1` over the real `shipyard` dir — silently running helper scripts from a backup copy (observed on `mattsears18/lightwork`, session `session_01Hs4CqGT53F6kwVasHiyLnH`). The glob also targeted `marketplaces/` while the loaded install lives under `cache/<mp>/<plugin>/<version>/`, so the two could diverge in version even with no `.bak` present. The rewritten fallback probes in a safer order: repo-local dogfood → **authoritative `installed_plugins.json` `installPath`** (the exact loaded install, immune to both failure modes) → **hardened marketplace glob** (tries the exact `marketplaces/shipyard` path first, excludes `.bak`/`.old`/`.orig`/`.disabled` siblings, requires a real `scripts/` dir) → repo-local error path. Step 0.4 now also echoes the resolved value once (`resolved CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT=… >&2`) so a wrong pick is visible in the session log instead of resolving invisibly. Spec/docs + test only; no helper-script runtime change. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/00-config-worktree.md` — rewrote the canonical step-0.3 preamble and its semantics prose (now a 4-layer probe), and added the one-shot `echo "resolved CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT=…" >&2` to the step-0.4 block.
- The canonical preamble string was swept across all 12 orchestrator-phase / worker / dispatch files that carry it (61 occurrences): `commands/do-work/{steady-state,drain,cleanup-summary,inline-trivial,dispatch-rules}.md`, `commands/do-work/setup/{00-config-worktree,01-repo-recovery,04-backlog-divert,06-scope-preflight}.md`, and `agents/issue-worker/{issue-work,fix-rebase}.md`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/claude-plugin-root-preamble.test.sh` — updated the `EXPECTED_PREAMBLE` anchor (now a quoted here-doc, since the preamble embeds a single-quoted jq filter), refreshed the probe-order doc comment, and added two #681 scenarios (installPath preferred over a shadowing `.bak`; marketplace-glob fallback excludes `.bak` and picks the real dir).

### 2.3.2 — 2026-07-06

**`fix-rebase.md` §4.6 version-leapfrog resolution is now bump-level-aware — a rebased major/minor PR no longer gets downgraded to a patch release** (#673, PR #682). §4.6's version-coordinated conflict-resolution recipe (the #466 carve-out) re-numbered a version-leapfrogged PR to *main's floor + next free **patch***, the same patch-only assumption #671 (PR #672) just fixed in the dispatch-time next-available-version computation. The trap: a PR pre-allocated a **major** (breaking) or **minor** (feature) release that then gets version-leapfrogged during a drain-phase rebase was silently collapsed to a patch — e.g. a PR pre-allocated `3.0.0` whose main advanced to `2.4.5` resolved to `2.4.6`, shipping the breaking change as a patch. §4.6 step 1 now infers the PR's intended release level from the **ground truth the PR already encodes** — the delta between its own pre-allocated manifest version and its merge-base with main (read from `origin/$HEAD_REF`, a stable ref independent of the in-progress rebase) — rather than a patch assumption or the issue title, then bumps main's floor at *that* level using the same major → `(X+1).0.0` / minor → `X.(Y+1).0` / patch → `X.Y.(Z+1)` shape #671 landed. The floor stays the hard collision constraint; only the semver level is level-aware, so the benign over-estimate edge case (a patch PR pre-allocated above an in-flight minor sibling's floor) bumps one level high but never below the floor and never collides — strictly safer than the downgrade this fixes. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md` — §4.6 Resolution step 1 replaces the patch-only "next free patch" computation with a `bump_level`-aware one (infer level from pre-allocated-version-vs-merge-base delta, bump floor at that level); §4.6's intro and step 4's version-coordinated bullet reworded from "next free patch" to "bump at the PR's release level to the next free slot".
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the two fix-rebase divert-prompt spots that summarized §4.6 as "bump to the next free patch" now say "bump at the PR's release level (major/minor/patch) to the next free slot".
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-rebase-version-coordination.test.sh` — spec assertions re-pinned to the level-aware wording (+ links to #673); the behavioral fixture is parameterized over the PR's release level, adding minor (`2.4.0` preserved, not `2.3.13`) and major (`3.0.0` preserved, not `2.4.6`) variants alongside the original patch case.

### 2.3.1 — 2026-07-06

**Version-coordination next-available-version is now bump-type-aware, no longer patch-only** (#671, PR #682). The per-dispatch next-available-version computation in `dispatch-rules.md` (and the parallel A.0.5 crash-recovery bump in `steady-state.md`, #575) only ever computed a patch bump (`max_inflight_version + 1` on the Z segment), blind to issues that explicitly require a major (breaking) or minor (feature) release. On a version-coordinated repo that cuts a release per PR, the injected "Next-available version (orchestrator-supplied)" paragraph then contradicted the issue body's stated version intent with a semver-wrong "use this exact value" directive — the latent trap the #659 epic's MAJOR/minor shards would have hit (main `1.27.1`: #661 needed `2.0.0` but the formula gave `1.27.2`; the feature shards needed minor bumps but got patch). The computation now infers the bump level from the dispatched issue's Conventional Commits title/body and computes the next free slot at that level; the `version_cursor` still advances to the exact computed value so a batch of N dispatches stays strictly monotonic (collision-avoidance guarantee preserved), and the injected paragraph is weakened from a patch-only "use this exact value" to a floor + recommended-slot form that leaves the semver level to the worker (the issue body stays the single source of truth for release intent). Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — the Next-available-version computation now infers `bump_level` (feat → minor; `!` / `BREAKING CHANGE` / `major version bump` / `(X.0.0)` → major; else patch) and bumps `max_inflight_version` at that level; the injected paragraph presents `max_inflight_version` as the hard collision floor plus the level-derived recommended slot, and permits the worker to raise the level when its read of the issue body requires it.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — the A.0.5 `a05_version_bump` crash-recovery helper (#575) gains the same Conventional-Commits bump-level inference so a recovered breaking-change / feature PR isn't stamped with a semver-wrong patch.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/version-coordination-bump-level.test.sh` — new suite pinning both the spec (bump-type-aware logic + weakened floor/level paragraph in both files) and the behavior (per-level bumps, the #659 shard sequence → `2.0.0`/`2.1.0`/`2.2.0`/`2.2.1`/`2.3.0`, and same-level batch monotonicity).

### 2.3.0 — 2026-07-06

**Inline auto-decompose confirmed epics inside `/do-work` by default — retire the manual `/decompose-epic` round-trip for the mechanical classes** (#665, PR #682). Previously `/do-work` only made a confirmed epic *visible*: when a scope agent returned `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR`, step 6 applied `needs-human-review` + the `<!-- do-work-needs-decomposition -->` trigger marker and parked it, requiring a human to run `/decompose-epic` — even for the obviously-mechanical cases (an ordered `Multi-PR sequence:` breakdown already spelled out in the body, or a `Missing dependency:` add-then-use split). This adds the inline auto path and makes it the **default** for those two mechanically-decomposable evidence classes: the scope-preflight/defer recording path (and the drain re-validation) now inline-invokes `/decompose-epic`'s existing decomposition-worker logic — the single source of truth for the sharding, not duplicated — to shard the epic into an ordered `Blocked by #<sibling>` sub-issue chain right then, so the shards re-enter the normal dispatch loop the same session without a human round-trip. The confidence gate + `--max-subissues` cap (both inside the reused worker) remain the guardrails: a low-confidence, over-cap, or non-mechanical epic (`Multi-service coordination:`, `Body cites <artifact>:`) escalates to the human handoff exactly as before — strictly no-worse than the pre-#665 park. A new `decompose.auto` config knob (default `true`) is the opt-out; `decompose.max_subissues` (default `8`) is the cap. Untrusted-input discipline is unchanged — the epic body is a claim the worker re-derives against the codebase, never instructions. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — adds Recording-path step 5: for a `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` defer with a `Multi-PR sequence:` / `Missing dependency:` `evidence_pointer` and `decompose.auto == true`, inline-dispatch `/decompose-epic`'s Worker prompt template (no worktree, `--max-subissues <decompose.max_subissues>`) after the diagnosis comment + `needs-human-review` are recorded; records the `decomposed:` / `escalated:` / `blocked:` outcome and notes the shards re-enter dispatch the same session.

### 2.2.1 — 2026-07-06

**Fast worktree-lock reap: rename + prune + backgrounded delete so a large `node_modules/` can't hang the reap** (#664, phase d of #659, PR #682). `git worktree remove --force` recursively unlinks the worktree directory *inline*, which stalls for many seconds to minutes when the worktree carries a large `node_modules/` (tens of thousands of small files) — and the branch checked out there stays locked until the remove completes, blocking `fix-checks` retries that need the head branch ("head branch locked in another worktree"). The new fast path decouples branch-freeing from bulk deletion: `git worktree unlock` → `mv <wt> <wt>.reap-dead-<pid>-<epoch>` (instant inode relink on the same filesystem) → `git worktree prune` (drops the now-missing registration, freeing the branch immediately) → backgrounded `rm -rf <dead> &` (the only expensive step, detached so it can't stall the caller). Steps up to the prune are synchronous and independent of tree size; only the recursive unlink is backgrounded, and the path falls back to the synchronous `git worktree remove --force` when the rename can't be performed (path missing, cross-device mount, permission, race). Both reap actions in the `reap` helper — `reaped` (agent worktrees) and `reaped-orphan-orchestrator` (setup 1.6.5 crash-orphan reap) — route through it, so every reap call site that funnels through the #284 single-source-of-truth helper (setup 3b, steady-state pre-dispatch reap, drain pre-dispatch reap, cleanup-summary) gets the fast path with no per-site change. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/worktree-reap.sh` — adds the `fast_worktree_remove` helper (unlock + rename-aside + prune + backgrounded delete, with fallback), routes the `reaped` and `reaped-orphan-orchestrator` reap actions through it, and updates the `reap`-subcommand docstring; the crash-orphan case now takes the fast path, so the `-raw-rm` / `-failed` audit variants are reserved for the genuine rename-then-remove last resort.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-reap.test.sh` — updates the orphan-orchestrator crash-orphan test (34/34a/34b) to the new fast-path action, and adds a fast-reap regression group (76–77) asserting the rename → prune → branch-freed sequence on a real registered worktree, audit-line continuity, and the already-removed-path no-op fallback.

### 2.2.0 — 2026-07-06

**Own-the-tail behaviors for `/do-work`: auto-heal flaky CI, update dependent PRs, auto-arm release PRs, auto-repair recurring failures** (#663, phase c of #659, PR #682). Adds the tail-completion behaviors so `/do-work` drives its own PRs all the way to merged/deployed without a human nudging a stalled tail, building on the autonomous-by-default (#661, phase a) and full-completion-termination (#662, phase b) framing. Four behaviors land as spec across the three orchestrator command files: (1) **CI auto-heal reconciliation** — the classification-gated flake rerun (already in the `fix-checks-only` worker per #654) is formally reconciled with the `ci.skip_speculative_rerun` posture: the key governs only *blind, undiagnosed* reruns the orchestrator never issues, while the infra-flake-classified rerun is the sanctioned non-speculative path and is deliberately NOT suppressed by the key. (2) **Cross-PR dependency-update sweep** — when a session PR merges and leaves siblings merely `BEHIND` (stale base, no conflict) on a non-strict-required-checks repo, the orchestrator runs `gh pr update-branch` on them directly (cheaper than a `fix-rebase` worker) so a shared-file fix that unblocks dependents doesn't strand them. (3) **Recurring-failure-signature auto-repair** — a normalized `<workflow>|<job>|<error-line>` signature recurring across ≥2 PRs is recognized as a shared root cause; one targeted fix-checks is dispatched against the canonical (lowest-numbered) PR and the fix propagates to siblings via the dependency-update sweep, with a converge-on-green thrash guard mirroring `main_ci_fix_attempts`. (4) **Release-PR auto-arming + deploy watch** — under the repo's standing authority, drain arms auto-merge on standalone release PRs (release-please / release-bump) and watches the post-merge deploy within the `max_drain_hours` window, preserving the external-author security carve-out verbatim (only trusted / listed-bot authors are armed; external authors get `needs-human-review`). Spec/docs-only; no runtime/helper change. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — rewrites §2c to reconcile `ci.skip_speculative_rerun` (speculative/undiagnosed reruns, still off) with the classification-gated flake auto-heal path (#654, always available, not gated by the key), so a reviewer reads the key as "the orchestrator never blind-reruns," not "shipyard never reruns."
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — adds a `D-tail` subsection under the periodic refresh (fires on merge-completion triggers, skipped during drain) covering the CI-auto-heal recap, the cross-PR `gh pr update-branch` dependency-update sweep (CI-minute-bounded, `BEHIND`-only), and the `recurring_failure_signatures` session-local detection → canonical-PR fix-checks dispatch.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — adds a Release-PR auto-arming and deploy-watch section (release-PR identification, the preserved trust carve-out, `--auto` arming with the admin-direct-merge pre-check, best-effort deploy-run watch bounded by `max_drain_hours`, and next-release-PR pickup).

### 2.1.0 — 2026-07-06

**Redefine the `/do-work` termination contract: complete only when every workable issue is closed/dispositioned AND every session PR is merged (or confirmed external-blocked) — not when the dispatch pool drains; drive the drain tail toward that** (#662, phase b of #659, PR #682). Previously "backlog empty + in-flight drained" read as the finish line: the dispatch loop stopped, the end-of-session drain watched the merge train until every session PR "settled," and settled included non-merged states (`blocked:ci`, rebase-blocked, rate-limit-capped, still-pending at the ceiling) — so a session could report "done" with red/DIRTY/unmerged PRs still open. Phase b splits the one condition into two: **dispatch-pool-empty** (all queues empty + live backlog no net-new) is the *drain-entry gate* that ends the dispatch loop, while **session completion** is the stronger, drain-owned assertion — every open workable issue closed or dispositioned (merged, or labeled to a genuine human/operator class: `needs-human-review` / `needs-operator` / `needs-triage`) AND every session PR merged, with the *only* legitimate residual stopping point being a confirmed external dependency the agent cannot perform (a human-supplied secret, an upstream release, a provider-console action). The drain's job is reframed as **driving the tail** toward those completing states; every other "settled" class is now an explicit **bounded-exit concession** (stops the drain from spinning forever, but leaves the session incomplete and surfaces the PR for the next run) rather than a completion. The concrete auto-heal / dependency-update / release-arming *step mechanics* remain phase c (#663); this phase defines the contract they satisfy. Spec/docs-only; no runtime/helper change. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — the **Termination assertion** section now opens with the two-conditions framing (dispatch-pool-empty gate vs session-completion assertion) and the full-completion definition; the four-step assertion is reframed as necessary-but-not-sufficient (the drain-entry gate); **End-of-session drain** reframed as drive-the-tail with a phase-c pointer; a new **Completion vs bounded-exit** paragraph precedes the progress-based Termination criterion; the on-exit reporting line splits PRs into completing vs bounded-exit/incomplete.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — the loop intro, the step E terminating idle-proof staleness-guard note, and the `idle_reason` enumeration now state that `all queues empty (terminating after in_flight drains)` marks dispatch-loop termination + drain handoff, **not** session completion (the shared idle_reason token string is unchanged, so `dont.md` / RATIONALE references stay in lockstep).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — the frontmatter `description`, the intro "ends when the backlog and in-flight set are both empty" prose, and the `session_prs` orchestrator-state struct reframed from "settled → done" to the full-completion / bounded-exit model.

### 2.0.0 — 2026-07-06

**BREAKING: make autonomous, operator-inclusive operation the `/do-work` default; add a `--no-operate` / `--hands-off` opt-out** (#661, phase a of #659, PR #682). Previously `/do-work` was code-work-only and the browser-operator layer was opt-**in** via `--operate` (or the `/my-turn-and-do` alias). This flips the default: a bare `/do-work` now runs the autonomous code loop **plus** the browser-operator layer — it drains the `operator_queue` of browser-completable actions (close a superseded PR, paste a CI secret, toggle a referenced provider setting, post an unambiguous reply) by driving the user's real, logged-in Chrome, and it makes reasonable design/architecture decisions itself rather than round-tripping to the human for every choice. The **security / access-control carve-out is preserved verbatim** — modifying an auth/security setting (password policy, MFA, OAuth redirect URIs, authorized domains, IAM, sharing permissions, any "security" toggle) is a safety boundary that outranks the default standing authorization, so those are teed up and handed back, never auto-driven — and the graceful degradation is unchanged (no browser backend reachable ⇒ code-loop-only with operator items surfaced as hand-backs). The new `--no-operate` / `--hands-off` opt-out (synonyms) restores the prior code-only, dispatch-only behavior for the rare case that wants it; `--operate` is retained as a backward-compatible no-op (it's the flag `/my-turn-and-do` still forwards). This is the breaking phase (a) of the #659 epic — it changes what `/do-work` does by default for every invocation — hence the major version bump. Spec/docs-only; no runtime/helper change. ### 1.27.1 — 2026-07-06

**Make the repo's unit-test suite a hard pre-push gate in issue-work and fix-checks-only — not just typecheck + lint** (#658, PR #682). Workers pushed after local typecheck + lint but did not run the repo's full unit-test suite, so repo-specific test failures surfaced only in CI — each costing an orchestrator diagnosis + a separate fix-checks dispatch. The wild repro (a `mattsears18/lightwork` session, 2026-07-06): three issue-worker PRs (#2275, #2284, #2290) each added `Strings.*` leaves without the paired `locales/en.json` key, passed typecheck + lint locally, then failed the required **Unit Tests** gate on a jest i18n-parity assertion — a class of failure a generic typecheck/lint pass can't see. The soft "If nothing exists, skip — CI will tell you" framing in the Implement step read as permission to skip the suite entirely. The fix elevates the unit suite to a first-class pre-push blocker with the same standing as typecheck/lint: a new `### 4.6 Pre-push local unit-test gate` in `issue-work.md` (detect the unit-test command — `test:unit` / `test` npm script, jest/vitest/pytest config, `Makefile` target — run it scoped to changed files when possible, treat a red suite as a `blocked #<N> at pre-push` blocker, skip ONLY when the repo has no suite at all), and the same run added to the `fix-checks-only.md` fix-loop before its push step. Skipping is narrowed so "slow" / "looks unrelated" are explicitly not skip reasons, and the gate is scoped to the unit suite (not the full E2E/browser matrix). Spec-only; no runtime/helper change. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — adds `§4.6 Pre-push local unit-test gate` (detection → run → pre-push blocker → narrow skip → don't-over-run), ordered after §4.5 (diff sanity) and before §5 (commit + push); rewrites the Implement-step test bullet to point at §4.6 and drop the soft "skip — CI will tell you" framing.
- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — adds fix-loop step 4.5 (run the unit suite locally before pushing a fix, ties the i18n-parity signature to the named-failing-check gate) and a `Don't` bullet forbidding a push without a local unit run.

### 1.27.0 — 2026-07-05

**Add a `functional-qa-auditor` — the auditor that drives the running app as a signed-in user and verifies features actually work** (#655, PR #682). Every existing shipyard auditor is either a quality/design lens on live surfaces (`web-ux`, `a11y`, `lighthouse`) or a static-codebase analysis (`security`, `privacy`, `data-lifecycle`, `testing`, `api`, `observability`, `docs`, `tech-debt`, `dx`); none exercises a flow end-to-end and asserts the resulting state change, so functional-correctness defects (a data-export that silently never completes, a filter chip that drops valid rows, a missing Firestore index that 400s on load) fall outside every auditor's remit. The new auditor signs in via the `auditing-authenticated-surfaces` harness, builds an explicit feature inventory (auto-derived from the router/nav, augmentable by a committed checklist), drives real flows (create/edit/submit/delete, list/detail, search/filter, messaging, settings, data export), captures `console.error`/`pageerror` + failed network requests, **asserts the expected state change**, root-causes each failure in the codebase (cites `file:line` + mechanism, deliberately stopping short of a full fix sketch), and files dispatch-ready `audit:functional-qa` issues via the shared `filing-github-issues` + `audit-rubrics` conventions. It is complementary to `web-ux-auditor` (design/interaction quality) — run both. The three open questions are resolved with their inline-suggested defaults: feature-inventory auto-derives from the router with a committed-checklist override; root-cause depth stops at file:line + mechanism; destructive-action policy and target-env selection are first-class agent config, safe/non-destructive by default (never deletes accounts or mass-messages, defaults to the test env / read-mostly on prod, and logs skipped flows instead of silently omitting them). The `audit:functional-qa` provenance label is auto-created on first filing via the filing skill's ensure-then-label pattern — matching every other `audit:*` label — rather than by `/shipyard:init` (which creates no audit labels; the issue's "created by /shipyard:init" wording is reconciled to the actual convention). Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/functional-qa-auditor.md` — new agent definition mirroring `web-ux-auditor`'s structure: required inputs, safe-by-default safety config (`target_env` / `destructive_policy`), the auth → inventory → exercise → instrument+assert → root-cause → file → log-skips process, a return-summary template, and a Don't section.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/audit.md` — adds `functional-qa` to the args enumeration and a `functional-qa → shipyard:functional-qa-auditor` (needs-URL) dispatch-table row, so `/shipyard:audit functional-qa` resolves and `all` includes it.
- `plugins/shipyard/skills/filing-github-issues/SKILL.md` — adds the `functional-qa-auditor → audit:functional-qa` row to the agent-identifying-label table so the auto-create-on-file mechanism applies.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/functional-qa-auditor.test.sh` — new regression guard asserting the agent's frontmatter, skill reuse, required phases, the three open-question resolutions, the safe-by-default posture, and the audit.md + filing-skill wiring.

### 1.26.1 — 2026-07-05

**Teach `fix-checks-worker` to detect infrastructure flakes and re-run instead of bailing "logs unavailable"** (#654, PR #682). The Haiku `fix-checks-worker` had no infra-flake path: when a PR's failing CI run was runner contention / job cancellations / dev-server-boot timeouts rather than a code defect, it reported `blocked #<PR> at fix-checks: … logs unavailable while run still in progress` and gave up — pushing no fix, mislabeling a healthy PR, and burning a full dispatch (~139k tokens in the wild repro, lightwork PR #2273 on a self-hosted-runner host starved by concurrent workers). The fix adds an **Infra-flake classification and re-run** gate to `fix-checks-only.md`: (1) "logs unavailable while run in progress" is a wait-signal, not a bail — block on the watch until the run settles, then fetch logs; (2) classify the failure — a cancelled-required-job (`The operation was canceled`) / dev-server-timeout (`config.webServer`) / setup-job-failure / runner-lost signature WITH passing local gates AND no deterministic code error returns a distinct `flake #<M>: re-ran failed jobs (<signature>)` disposition that `gh run rerun --failed`s instead of attempting a code fix, does NOT count toward the `blocked:ci` 3-attempt cap, and is bounded by the run's attempt count so a persistently-starved host escalates to `blocked:ci` rather than re-running forever; (3) fall through to the code-fixing loop only on a deterministic code error. The orchestrator's reconcile recognizes the new `flake #<M>` return (no `blocked:ci` label, no `failed_prs` enqueue, re-run watched via `session_prs`). Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md` — new `## Infra-flake classification and re-run (load-bearing)` section (Step A wait-don't-bail, Step B signature match + local-gate + no-code-error conditions, Step C bounded `gh run rerun --failed` + `flake` return); adds the fourth terminal return string to the return contract; Hard-rules cap note; fix-loop step 2.5 + never-bail-logs-unavailable note; two `Don't` entries.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — reconcile branch for `flake #<M>: re-ran failed jobs` (no `blocked:ci`, no `failed_prs`, `session_prs` + cost-comment refresh, `[fix-checks-flake]` log); adds `flake` to the unrecognized-return recognized-prefix list; extends the divert-refresh trigger 2 to include flake.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dispatch-rules.md` — advertises the `flake #<M>` return value in the fix-checks-only dispatch prompt.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-checks-infra-flake-classification.test.sh` — new regression guard asserting the classification gate, the signature set, the attempt-count bound, the cap carve-out, section ordering, and the orchestrator-side reconcile + dispatch-prompt wiring.

### 1.26.0 — 2026-07-01

**Add a 16th auditor — `data-lifecycle-auditor` — owning the data-model mutation & cascade-completeness surface no existing auditor covered** (#652, PR #682). Shipyard's 15 auditors left a structural gap: none owned *referential integrity + cascade completeness + denormalization consistency* — i.e. when a document is created, updated, or deleted, does the right thing happen to everything that references it? privacy-auditor reviews the account-deletion flow through a GDPR-erasure lens (PII retention, not correctness), security-auditor owns authz (who *may* write, not whether cascades are *complete*), api-auditor owns API-contract coherence, and testing-auditor finds missing tests — so an orphaned recurring-series generator minting rows for a deleted owner, a denormalized name that drifts on rename, or an ephemeral collection with no TTL fell through every net. A single manual Lightwork session surfaced ~11 distinct integrity gaps of exactly this class, proving it's a repeatable methodology worth codifying. The new auditor walks a 6-pass process (discover model + build reference graph → delete cascades → update/denormalization drift → create-time side-effects → GC/TTL → storage/external side-effects), short-circuits to `n/a` on repos with no persisted data model, honors a `<!-- data-lifecycle-audit:skip -->` marker, and cross-references the four adjacent auditors' dimensions rather than re-filing their findings. - `plugins/shipyard/commands/audit.md` — registers the `data-lifecycle` dimension: adds it to the audit-type arg list and a dispatch-table row pointing at `shipyard:data-lifecycle-auditor` (so `/audit all` includes it).
- `README.md` — adds the dimension to the "Finds work" summary and a `/audit data-lifecycle` bullet in the slash-command list.

### 1.25.5 — 2026-06-27

**Narrow the #645 ruleset-aware ungated-path detector to `required_status_checks` only — a `pull_request` ruleset rule must not count as gating the merge** (follow-up to #645, PR #682). The #645 fix (1.25.4) probed the rulesets endpoint and treated the branch as gated when an active ruleset required status checks **or a PR**, borrowing the CHANGELOG-backfill write-path idiom. But a `pull_request` ruleset rule only requires the change to arrive via a PR — it does NOT gate the *merge* on CI, so an admin `--auto` still direct-merges *immediately* (ungated) on a repo whose ruleset has `pull_request` but no `required_status_checks`. That is exactly the `mattsears18/shipyard` shape (ruleset `[deletion, non_fast_forward, pull_request]`), and the over-broad check regressed it: the detector falsely read it as gated, *disabling* the #598 protective `--watch` and allowing ungated merges (observed live — this dispatch's own first PR #648 admin-direct-merged with checks still in progress). The correct signal for an ungated-*merge* detector is `required_status_checks` only; lightwork's gated `main` still reads correctly because its ruleset carries `required_status_checks` alongside `pull_request`. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` — narrows the step-0.5 ruleset probe to `contains(["required_status_checks"])` and documents why `pull_request` is intentionally excluded (it gates direct writes, not CI-on-merge).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — same narrowing for the mirrored §1.3 warning detector, so the silent-direct-merge warning still fires on the pull_request-only shape.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/ruleset-aware-required-checks.test.sh` — updates the spec + behavioral assertions: a `pull_request`-only ruleset now reads ungated, the shipyard-shape ruleset still fires shape 2, the lightwork-shape ruleset reads gated, and the probe is asserted NOT to include `pull_request`.

### 1.25.4 — 2026-06-27

**Make the issue-worker auto-merge ungated-path detector ruleset-aware so it stops false-positiving on repository-ruleset branch protection** (#645, PR #682). The detector that decides whether a PR is on the "ungated admin-direct-merge" path (where `gh pr merge --auto` lands the PR before its CI completes) read the default branch's required status checks only from **classic** branch protection (`.../protection/required_status_checks/contexts`). On a repo whose `main` is protected by a GitHub **repository ruleset** (a separate gating mechanism — the now-common shape, e.g. `mattsears18/lightwork`'s `main` requires 4 ruleset checks), that endpoint reports 0 contexts, so the detector read `admin + required_checks=0` and falsely concluded the branch was ungated — sending the worker into an unnecessary `--watch`-then-merge block and surfacing a misleading orchestrator-side warning. The fix: when the classic probe returns 0, also probe `repos/{owner}/{repo}/rules/branches/{branch}` and treat the branch as gated if an active ruleset requires status checks **or** a PR — the same two-shape ruleset idiom the orchestrator's CHANGELOG-backfill write-path probe already uses. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/auto-merge.md` — adds the ruleset-aware fallback to step 0.5's signal-(c) `REQUIRED_CHECKS` read (sets a non-zero sentinel when a ruleset gates the branch so the two-shape test no longer classifies it as ungated) plus a prose note documenting the classic-vs-ruleset distinction.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup/01-repo-recovery.md` — applies the same ruleset-aware fallback to the mirrored §1.3 silent-direct-merge warning detector, keeping the two detectors consistent on Rulesets-protected repos.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/ruleset-aware-required-checks.test.sh` — new regression guard asserting both detectors carry the ruleset fallback (ordered after the classic read), plus a behavioral test of the ruleset-gating jq decision (checks/PR ruleset ⇒ gated; empty/no-gate ruleset ⇒ ungated) and the end-to-end shape-2-suppression check.

### 1.25.3 — 2026-06-27

**Add a post-rebase empty-diff guard to fix-rebase mode so a conflict resolution that silently drops the PR's substantive change is caught before the force-push, not after it auto-closes the PR** (#646, PR #682). A `fix-rebase` worker dispatched against a DIRTY PR (lightwork #2165) resolved a same-line provenance conflict by taking main's side **wholesale** (a whole-file `--theirs`, which also discarded the PR's non-conflicting substantive hunk in the same file). The net diff vs base went empty, the force-push **auto-closed the PR** (GitHub auto-closes a PR whose head force-pushes to an empty-diff state), and the worker returned `rebased #<M>` (success) — silent data loss with no `blocked` signal. The work had to be redone from scratch. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md` — adds **§5.7 Post-rebase empty-diff guard** (the root fix): before the §6 force-push, compares the PR's net contribution vs base before (`origin/$HEAD_REF`, untouched until the push) and after the rebase; bails `blocked rebase #<M>: rebase produced an empty diff (conflict resolution dropped the PR's change) — needs manual rebase` when a previously-non-empty change went empty, leaving the PR DIRTY for a human rather than force-pushing an auto-close. Also adds a step-4 **hunk-level resolution rule** forbidding whole-file `git checkout --theirs/--ours <file>` (the proximate cause), with the §4.6 coordinated-manifest path carved out as the one safe exception.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-rebase-empty-diff-guard.test.sh` — new regression guard asserting the §5.7 guard's bail string, the pre/post-rebase diff comparison, the before-force-push placement, and the whole-file-checkout prohibition + §4.6 carve-out.

### 1.25.2 — 2026-06-23

**Add first-class handling for local-only-CI merge gates — a `merge_gate.*` config block plus the per-PR gate-run + single-shared-resource pacing pattern documented in the drain phase** (#643, PR #682). On a repo where the merge-blocking commit status is posted by a **manually-run command** (e.g. lightwork's `local-ci` status, posted only after a session runs `npm run ci:report` — a ~20-min emulator-backed suite) rather than by cloud CI that auto-runs on push, `/do-work` previously armed `--auto` on every shipped PR but nothing ever posted the gate status, so the loop "succeeded" (PRs shipped) while the backlog never drained. The orchestrator had to improvise the whole gate loop off-book. This makes it first-class and opt-in: `merge_gate.command` defaults to empty (cloud-CI behavior unchanged), and only a non-empty value engages the new drain-phase loop. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh` — adds the `merge_gate` block to `DEFAULTS_JQ` (`command: ""`, `serialize: false`, `max_unmerged_ahead: 2`, `clear_state_command: ""`).
- `plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json` — adds the `merge_gate` object (`additionalProperties: false`) with per-key descriptions covering the gate command, the serialize-on-shared-resource flag, the unmerged-ahead pacing cap, and the reused-worktree stale-state cleanup command.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md` — new **Local-only-CI merge gate** section: the per-poll gate-run action (paced to `max_unmerged_ahead`), the `P_settled` correction so a shipped-but-ungated PR isn't prematurely declared settled, the **single-shared-resource pacing pattern** (wait-for-free + orphan-reap + serialized runs) for emulator/port-locked gates, and the reused-gate-runner stale-generated-state note.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — cross-reference on the `shipped #<N>` reconcile noting the gate fires at drain (not at reconcile) when `merge_gate.command` is set.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-config.test.sh` — `merge_gate` defaults, override, deep-merge, and schema-rejection coverage.

### 1.25.1 — 2026-06-21

**Reconcile the repo `CLAUDE.md` with the actual `main` branch ruleset: release bumps and other changes to `main` go through a PR with auto-merge, never a direct push** (#641, PR #682). The **Release process** and **Permissions** sections both claimed the release bump (and trivial docs/config changes) could "land directly on `main`" with "no separate PR needed" — but the repo's branch ruleset rejects direct pushes (`GH013: Changes must be made through a pull request`), so every release bump that followed the stale instruction hit a failed-push detour before recovering via a PR. The docs now describe the real mechanism (push a branch, open a PR, arm auto-merge) while preserving the intent — this is personal tooling, so you don't need a review gate or to ask first; what's PR-required is the *push to `main`*, not human sign-off. Files touched:

- `CLAUDE.md` — **Permissions** section: rewords the "update `main` directly / push commits to `main`" bullet to "land changes on `main` without asking — but `main` requires a PR", naming the `GH013` ruleset and the push-branch → PR → auto-merge mechanism; the worktree-isolation bullet's cross-reference is updated from "push-to-main permission" to "PR-via-auto-merge permission". **Release process** section: step 3 drops the "this can land directly on `main` … no separate PR needed" claim and instead routes the bump through a PR with auto-merge like any other change.

Note: the issue offered a second option — relax/exempt the branch ruleset to allow direct pushes to `main` — which was intentionally NOT taken. That weakens the PR gate and is a GitHub repo-settings change (out of code scope); option 1 (correct the docs to match the ruleset) is the right fix.

### 1.25.0 — 2026-06-20

**Add a `SessionEnd` hook so worktree cleanup no longer depends on the orchestrator completing a clean turn** (#638, PR #639; the structural fix behind #637). Every reap in shipyard previously ran as best-effort bash *inside the orchestrator's own turn* (the in-loop reaps in `steady-state.md`, the `cleanup-summary.md` end-of-session sweep), so if a session crashed, was interrupted, or the orchestrator's turn ended before cleanup ran, stale worktree state orphaned — the failure mode #280 / #509 / #529 / #326 each patched piecemeal but never eliminated (observed on one real checkout: 33 stale worktree dirs, 39 dangling `worktree-agent-*` branch refs, 23 `[gone]` `do-work/issue-*` refs). A new `SessionEnd` hook now backstops cleanup independent of the orchestrator's turn, firing on every session exit. v1 scope is deliberately the single clearest unbounded leak — `worktree-agent-*` refs (#326) — because that's the only reap provably safe with no session-liveness reasoning (`reap-orphan-branches` deletes only refs with no live worktree); reaping this-session worktrees and dead-session orchestrator dirs stays with the orchestrator's in-loop reaps + the next-session startup sweep. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/reap-on-session-end.sh` — thin shim: consumes stdin, runs the helper bounded, never propagates non-zero (a cleanup failure must not break session exit); mirrors `report-plugin-error.sh`.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/session-end-reap.sh` — helper: reaps stale `worktree-agent-*` branch refs via `worktree-reap.sh reap-orphan-branches`, then `git worktree prune`; best-effort, bounded via `timeout`, never errors.
- `plugins/shipyard/hooks/hooks.json` — registers the `SessionEnd` hook.
- `plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/session-end-reap.test.sh` (7 cases) + `plugins/shipyard/hooks/tests/reap-on-session-end.test.sh` (4 cases) — orphan ref reaped, unrelated branch untouched, reads cwd from stdin payload, non-git / missing-helper / empty-stdin no-ops, end-to-end reap through the shim.
- `CONTRIBUTING.md` (hooks list) + `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md` (a "Backstop — the SessionEnd hook" note) document it.

### 1.24.0 — 2026-06-19

**Reconcile the three commands' charters and make `/my-turn` a looping, human-only walkthrough instead of stopping after one item** (#635, PR #636). Recent workflow changes established a clean three-command division of labor that `/my-turn` didn't yet honor, and the README's command descriptions were stale and contradicted it. The division is now consistent across every surface: `/do-work` = autonomous continuous loop, **code work only**; `/do-work --operate` (alias `/my-turn-and-do`) = the `/do-work` loop **plus** driving Chrome to complete browser-completable operator actions; `/my-turn` = surface **only** genuinely human-required items (decisions, judgment calls — anything `--operate` can't complete) and **walk the human through them one at a time, advancing to the next until the human-only queue is empty**. `/my-turn` stays human-facing and non-autonomous (no agent dispatch, no sharing of `/do-work`'s worker machinery) and reuses `/shipyard:resolve-decisions`' interactive walkthrough for decision-gated items. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md` — rewrites the charter (frontmatter + intro) from "surface the single next action, read-only/advisory-only" to the looping human-only walkthrough with the three-command division table; replaces "single-action mode (default)" with **Walkthrough mode (default)** (an advancing loop: surface → walk → confirm-done → advance → terminate); adds a **Human-only queue filter** section that excludes `needs-operator` / browser-completable items (surfaced only via a one-line **operator pointer** to `/my-turn-and-do`); replaces the read-only "decision-gated handoff offer" with an inline **Decision-gated walkthrough** that reuses `resolve-decisions`' per-decision flow + record-and-unblock; adds a **Termination contract** (queue empty / user-stop / lone-release-PR); renames "list mode" → "list-snapshot mode"; reconciles the `needs-operator` Pass B bucket, the P0 tier list, the leverage-score table, chrome-prompt mode, empty state, limit-overflow, and the `Don't` rules (the "don't mutate / don't stop after one item" rules now permit exactly the human-directed decisions mutation and forbid the one-item-and-exit behavior).
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn-and-do.md` — updates the re-parenting note and the `Don't` cross-references so the description of `/my-turn` matches its new looping, human-only-walkthrough semantics (was "single, one-shot" / "read-only single-item survey").
- `README.md` — rewrites the stale `/my-turn` and `/my-turn-and-do` command-list descriptions to match the division; adds a scannable three-command **"Which do I run?"** table to the quick-start; updates the inline quick-start comments and the How-it-works human-review step.

### 1.23.4 — 2026-06-18

**Add `verify` (read-only console verification) as a first-class `--operate` operator outcome** (#627). The operator-phase playbooks were all about *completing* an action (`close-pr`, `merge-pr`, `toggle-setting`, `paste-secret`, `reply-comment`) — there was no first-class **read-only** outcome, yet reading consoles turned out to be the highest-value thing `--operate` does on a security-setting-heavy backlog that is otherwise all hand-backs (it confirms the premise, makes hand-backs concrete, and verifies a human's just-completed change took). This documents `verify` as the read-only complement to #633's security/access-control carve-out: where a security toggle must be handed back, *reading* that same setting stays inside the safety boundary (reading isn't mutating), so the operator can still add value. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate.md` — adds a `verify` playbook to **Playbooks by kind** covering both facets (premise check / hand-back enrichment for any hand-back item, and post-action pass/fail verification that feeds the reconcile closing the originating issue); reframes the Playbooks intro to distinguish the mutating outcomes from the read-only `verify`; adds a `verify`-read step to the **Draining** and **D-hook drain** descriptions (read the console before a hand-back to make it concrete; re-read after a human completes an action to report pass/fail and clear `needs-operator`); adds a **Safety — trust boundary** bullet stating read-only verification is always inside the boundary; adds a **Don't** entry against handing back blind when a `verify` read would make it concrete (and against letting `verify` mutate).

### 1.23.3 — 2026-06-18

**Add a security/access-control carve-out to the `--operate` console-action playbook so Claude-as-operator tees up auth settings instead of trying to flip them** (#626). The operator phase's `toggle-setting` / `console-action` playbook told the operator to *"execute it"* for any mechanical toggle — but when the operator is **Claude** (the `--operate` path), Claude's safety boundary forbids modifying security/access-control settings, and that boundary outranks `--operate`'s standing authorization *and* explicit user authorization. So for the most common class of provider-console operator work (auth/security config — password policy, OAuth redirect URIs, authorized domains, IAM, sharing permissions), the playbook instructed an action Claude structurally won't take, and an auth-heavy `--operate` backlog silently degraded to hand-backs with no spec acknowledgement. The fix mirrors the existing `paste-secret` tee-up-and-hand-back: classify each console action into "Claude-safe to auto-drive" vs "hand back (security/access-control)" and route accordingly. Files touched:

- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate.md` — rewrites the `toggle-setting` / `console-action` playbook's step 4 to classify before mutating; adds a **"Claude-safe to auto-drive vs hand back"** subsection with a concrete two-column table and a conservative-default rule; adds a **security/access-control bullet** to the Safety — trust boundary section (the boundary outranks standing authorization); adds a **"security/access-control-heavy operator queue"** degradation note framing an auth-heavy backlog as expected (teed up, your turn) rather than a failure.
- `plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md` — extends the `--operate` arg docs to note that security/access-control settings are teed up and handed back, so `--operate` auto-completes only the mechanical, non-security subset on an auth-heavy backlog.

Sibling issue #627 (a read-only verify-and-report operator outcome) also touches `operate.md` but is out of scope here; this change leaves that work unobstructed.

### 1.23.0 — 2026-06-18

**Route "set up `<external service>`" work that needs a not-yet-provisioned credential away from autonomously committing dead config and toward the operator handoff** (#628). A user asked shipyard to work their backlog; one ticket was "set up Sentry". Because they manage infra with Terraform, a worker wrote the Terraform autonomously and it deployed config with **no functioning values** — they hadn't created a Sentry account yet, so there was no DSN to set. The committed config was structurally dead. The category is "work with a human-shaped hole in the middle": shipyard can write the code *around* a credential, but the credential itself (a DSN, an API key, a service account) can't be written, inferred, or **fabricated** until a human provisions the external service. Shipyard already had the right destination for this (the `external-dependency` defer → `needs-operator` label → surfaced by `/my-turn`, drivable by `/do-work --operate`, #608) but nothing reliably *routed* provisioning work into it and nothing stopped a worker from committing the placeholder. This closes both gaps at three layers — and explicitly keeps the intentional boundary that shipyard never creates the account or types a real secret it derived from issue text (the `paste-secret` tee-up-and-hand-back model). Files touched:

- `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — adds **§4.4 External-provisioning guard**: before any `git add`/`commit`, if completing the issue would require fabricating a placeholder for a required credential that doesn't exist yet (no env var / CI secret / populated `*.tfvars` to reference), the worker bails `blocked: external provisioning required — <service>: <what to provision and where the value goes>` instead of committing dead config. Scoped narrowly — it does **not** fire on already-provisioned references or inert variable declarations that can't deploy dead. Placed before §4.5 because placeholder config is a *non-empty* diff the empty-diff guard waves through.
- `commands/do-work/steady-state.md` — adds an **operator** class to the bail reason→class table: `external provisioning required` routes to the `needs-operator` label (a browser/console operator action), not `needs-human-review` — the same destination as the scope-preflight `external-dependency` defer, so a provisioning-gated issue lands in the operator queue whether caught before dispatch or mid-work.
- `commands/do-work/setup/06-scope-preflight.md` — adds a **provisioning carve-out** to the scope-agent prompt: the "default to slicing, ship it" bias does NOT apply to external-service setup, because there is no inert phase-1 (shipping the scaffold with a fabricated value *is* the harm) — defer `external-dependency` with a `Sentry account + DSN provisioning pending`-style evidence pointer. The `external-dependency` diagnosis comment now renders a concrete **`What to provision` operator checklist** so the `/my-turn` handoff is actionable rather than a bare "blocked on upstream" note.
- `commands/do-work/operate.md` — the A.1 reactive-enqueue list now explicitly recognizes the `external provisioning required` bail as a browser-completable operator handback under `--operate`.
- `scripts/tests/external-provisioning-guard.test.sh` — new regression suite (19 assertions) pinning all three layers: the §4.4 guard's presence, narrow scope, bail string, and pre-§4.5 placement; the `needs-operator` routing in steady-state.md; the scope-preflight carve-out, Sentry example, and provisioning checklist; and the operate.md handback recognition.

The full `*.test.sh` suite, repo-wide shellcheck, and the changelog-monotonicity scan are green.

### 1.22.0 — 2026-06-17

**Guard the issue-work worker contract against a child PR silently auto-closing a "do NOT close" parent epic it was told only to *reference*** (#624). When an `issue-work` dispatch references a parent/tracking epic without closing it, GitHub can promote a bare `#<E>` token — in the PR body, a squashed-commit message, or a CHANGELOG entry that rides the merge — into the PR's `closingIssuesReferences` even with **no** closing keyword, so the merge silently auto-closes the epic (observed: PR #621 carried only "Part of #613" / "Does NOT close #613" phrasing yet closed epic #613 on merge). The avoidance previously only worked because the orchestrator hand-rolled the check into each dispatch prompt; the worker contract itself had no protection, leaving a normal `/do-work` run exposed. This is the symmetric inverse of #481 (a resolving PR leaving its *own* issue stuck OPEN). Files touched:

- `agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md` — adds **§5.85 Post-PR-create non-close parent/epic leak verification**: when a non-close parent/epic relationship is in scope, after `gh pr create` it asserts the epic is absent from `closingIssuesReferences`, and on a leak rewrites the PR body to reference the epic by **bare URL** (not a `#<n>` token, which GitHub does not auto-promote), re-verifies, and reopens the epic if an admin direct-merge already closed it. Ordered after §5.8 (dispatched-issue closing-link verification) and before §6 (auto-merge) so a protected epic can never ride an armed auto-merge to a silent close. The §5 closing-keyword guidance now documents the real trigger (bare `#N` tokens + commit/CHANGELOG mentions, not only `Closes`/`Fixes`/`Resolves` keywords) and the bare-URL mitigation; a matching `Don't` bullet was added.
- `scripts/tests/parent-epic-leak-guard.test.sh` — new regression suite (24 assertions) pinning the §5.85 guard's presence, its load-bearing semantics (`closingIssuesReferences` verify, bare-URL remediation, reopen-if-merged, the `blocked #<N> at parent-epic-leak-verify:` bail string, the broader-than-closing-keyword trigger language), its placement between §5.8 and §6, and the issue-work-only scope (the four other worker-mode specs must not inherit the guard).

The full `*.test.sh` suite, repo-wide shellcheck, and the changelog-monotonicity scan are green.
