### 1.16.0 — 2026-06-17

**New `/shipyard:optimize-markdown` command — a repeatable context-bloat optimizer for repos where markdown _is_ the runtime** (#613, PR #682). Plugin/agent repos load their command, agent, and skill prompts (plus `CLAUDE.md`) into the agent's context on every invocation, so those files bloating is a direct token/latency tax — and once any single file crosses the 256KB `Read` limit the agent can no longer read its own instructions in one call (the failure mode behind #611's `setup.md`). This command codifies the manual "review every markdown file for bloat + progressive-disclosure wins" pass we ran to file #613: it measures the runtime-loaded markdown surface, flags files near/over the limit, detects the three bloat classes (inline historical provenance, duplicated conventions, hot-path/reference interleaving), **applies the safe behavior-preserving optimizations and opens a PR** (provenance relocated to a rationale sink behind anchor links, duplicated conventions collapsed to pointers at their canonical source, oversized files split along the repo's _existing_ thin-router pattern), and **files a dispatch-ready issue for the riskier restructuring** rather than forcing it into one autonomous PR. Generic across any repo — it discovers the loading model and existing conventions, applying shipyard-aware heuristics only when it recognizes them. Safe/risky partitioning is conservative (when unsure, file rather than auto-apply); every moved cross-reference is re-resolved and the test suite must be green before the PR ships.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/optimize-markdown.md`** — NEW command: repo resolution + refusal gate, runtime-loaded-surface discovery/measurement, loading-model mapping (via an `Explore` dispatch), bloat-class detection, SAFE-vs-RISKY partitioning, safe-fix application with reference re-resolution + test gate, PR open, dispatch-ready issue filing for the remainder, and `--dry-run` / `--issue-only` / `--threshold KB` flags.
- **`README.md`** — added the `/shipyard:optimize-markdown` bullet to the command listing.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.15.0` → `1.16.0`.

### 1.15.0 — 2026-06-17

**`/shipyard:do-work --operate` — the autonomous loop now also drives browser-completable operator actions in the user's real Chrome, and `/my-turn-and-do` is re-parented onto `/do-work` as its alias** (PR #682). Previously `/my-turn-and-do` extended `/my-turn` (a single, one-shot, human-operator action) — the wrong lineage for the actual intent: a *continuous* loop that does the normal `/do-work` issue-burndown **as well as** any browser-completable operator action it needs. This release adds an `--operate` flag to `/do-work` that turns the work the loop otherwise *defers/hands back* into *done-work*: the orchestrator drains an `operator_queue` of browser-completable items by driving the user's real, logged-in Chrome (preferring the `claude-in-chrome` extension). The queue is fed reactively (worker hand-backs + `external-dependency` defers) and proactively (a `/my-turn`-style sweep filtered to browser-completable items, including `needs-operator`-labeled issues). Code workers still parallelize per `--concurrency`; browser actions serialize on the main orchestrator thread (the real browser is a singleton). Genuine human-judgment items still hand back. Invoking with `--operate` is standing authorization for browser-completable actions (#608); `--dry-run` previews. If no browser backend is reachable, `--operate` degrades to the normal code loop and surfaces operator items as hand-backs.

**Label taxonomy — the operator/decision split.** A new **`needs-operator`** label marks a browser/console operator action (a human *or* Claude-via-`--operate` can perform it); **`needs-human-review`** is kept verbatim and now means specifically a genuine human *decision*. The `external-dependency` scope-defer re-routes from `needs-human-review` to `needs-operator` (it's an operator action `--operate` can drive); `human-decision-required`, the epic-decomposition handoff, and the external-author gate all stay on `needs-human-review`. `/do-work` excludes `needs-operator` from code-worker dispatch; `/do-work --operate` drains it; `/my-turn` surfaces both labels. The browser-driving machinery (backend selection, self-onboarding preflight, #607 stale-tab recovery, #608 standing authorization, read-page perception) moved from `my-turn-and-do.md` into the new operator phase.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/operate.md`** — NEW operator phase (`--operate` only): browser backend selection + preflight + standing authorization + per-kind playbooks + the `operator_queue` drain loop + the proactive browser-completable sweep + trust-boundary safety.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md`** — added the `--operate` arg, the `operator_queue` orchestrator-state struct + session-state schema field, the `operate.md` routing-table entry + load-on-demand rule, and the "operator queue is not a worker-dispatch source" note.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — create the `needs-operator` label (both create blocks); route `external-dependency` defers to `needs-operator` (class-keyed `GATE_LABEL` in the step-6 recording path); add `needs-operator` to the step-4 dispatch-exclusion set.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — new "Operator layer (`--operate`)" section: the A.1 reactive-enqueue hook and the step-D proactive-sweep + main-thread drain hook.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — termination adds the `operator_queue`-empty gate under `--operate`; `needs-operator` added to the fresh-fetch client-side filter.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn-and-do.md`** — slimmed to a thin alias for `/do-work --operate` (re-parented off `/my-turn`).
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md`** — surfaces `needs-operator` as a human-actionable operator signal (distinct from the `needs-human-review` decision signal).
- **`CLAUDE.md`** — documented the `needs-operator` label and the operator/decision split in the Gate-labels section.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.14.0` → `1.15.0`.

### 1.14.0 — 2026-06-17

**`/shipyard:my-turn-and-do` now treats command invocation as standing authorization for browser-completable actions — it does the mechanical action the ranking surfaced instead of stopping for a per-action in-chat confirmation** (#608, PR #682). The command's guardrails previously hard-confirmed every mutating / outward-facing browser action (close, merge, comment, submit, delete) with a fresh "say 'close it' / 'merge it' to proceed" prompt, even mid-run and even with `--yes` — a deliberate safety design that conflicted with the command's actual user, a power-user driving their own real, logged-in browser who experiences the repeated round-trips as the agent refusing to act. Repro (#608): on `mattsears18/lightwork`, the top-ranked action was "close superseded duplicate PR #1994"; the command teed it up and waited for an in-chat "close it" twice across turns until the user said *"stop waiting for my permission. when I run the /my-turn-and-do command I am explicitly giving you permission to do anything and everything that you need to do in the browser."* The fix adopts that intent: **invoking the command is itself the authorization** for the duration of the run. Mutations classified by the plan as browser-completable are now announced, executed, and reported — no per-action yes/no. The transparency **plan step is preserved** (it's always printed before the browser is touched), and **`--dry-run` remains the "preview, do nothing" escape hatch**. The one class the standing authorization does NOT cover is **judgment calls** — actions needing the user's *evaluation* (a substantive PR review, a nuanced question reply, "should this be done at all?") are still teed up and handed back; the dividing line is *decision*, not *reversibility*. `--yes` is retired as a no-op (the invocation is now the consent it used to grant), and actions outside the browser remain out of scope.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn-and-do.md`** — added the standing-authorization model to the intro; rewrote the "Confirmation gate" execution subsection into "Execution under standing authorization" (announce → do → report, no per-action prompt); rewrote the "Hard confirmation for irreversible / outward-facing actions" guardrail into "Standing authorization satisfies the gate for browser-completable actions" (with the judgment-call / outside-browser / plan-step / `--dry-run` carve-outs); reclassified the plan step's gate line (`Mutation — will execute: …`); dropped `--yes` from the frontmatter `argument-hint` and Args (now a documented no-op) while keeping `--dry-run`; updated the third-party-console playbook step and the Single-item / Dry-run output examples (now a clean "close PR #1994" mutation that executes); and reworked the Don't list (replaced "Don't bypass the hard confirmation gate with --yes" with "Don't re-ask for per-action confirmation", tightened "Don't rubber-stamp judgment calls" around the decision-vs-reversibility line, and added "Don't act outside the browser").
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.13.2` → `1.14.0`.

### 1.13.2 — 2026-06-17

**`/shipyard:my-turn-and-do` now recovers a stale or closed MCP tab group instead of misreading the resulting `Permission denied by user` error as a user permission decline and bailing to read-only `gh`** (#607, PR #682). On the `claude-in-chrome` backend the execution step holds a `tabId` across turns; when the user closes that tab/group (or it ages out), the next `navigate` fails with the *exact same* `Permission denied by user` string a genuine site-grant decline produces. The command was reading that as authoritative, concluding the user had denied site access, and degrading to read-only `/my-turn` + `gh` — even though nothing was denied and the real fix is to recreate the tab group. The Error-handling section now treats `Permission denied by user` on the extension backend as ambiguous: it first recovers the tab group (`tabs_context_mcp`, then `tabs_context_mcp({createIfEmpty:true})` if it reports `No MCP tab groups found` / an empty context), retries the navigation once, and only treats the failure as a genuine permission decline if the *retry* still denies — reserving the read-only / `gh` fallback for a genuinely unreachable backend, never a closed tab.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn-and-do.md`** — rewrote the Error-handling section's permission-failure branch into an ordered recover-then-retry recovery (distinguishing "stale tab group → recreate + retry" from "genuine user denial → guide + fall back"); extended preflight connectivity check 3 to recreate an absent MCP tab group via `createIfEmpty:true` rather than treating it as a connectivity failure; added a Don't-list entry forbidding treating `Permission denied by user` as authoritative before the tab-group recovery.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.13.1` → `1.13.2`.

**Refreshed the root `README.md`'s `/my-turn-and-do` documentation to match the 1.12.0 / 1.13.0 behavior** (PR #682). The README's command bullet still described the command as driving Chrome "via `chrome-devtools-mcp` (requires Chrome 144+ with `--autoConnect`)" — the exact mechanism replaced in 1.12.0 — and the command was absent from the quick-start examples, so a reader couldn't tell it now prefers the Claude Chrome extension or that it self-onboards. Updated the bullet to describe the backend-agnostic selection (extension preferred → `chrome-devtools-mcp` → read-only), the self-onboarding preflight, and the full flag set (`--setup`, `--dry-run`, `--yes`, `--record`, `--all`), and added a `/my-turn-and-do --setup` + `/my-turn-and-do` pair to the "Run your first command" block so first-run users see the onboarding path.

- **`README.md`** — rewrote the `/my-turn-and-do` entry in the command list (now linked to the command doc) to reflect backend-agnostic driving + the self-onboarding preflight + all five flags; added a `--setup`-first quick-start example after `/my-turn`.

### 1.13.0 — 2026-06-17

**`/shipyard:my-turn-and-do` is now self-onboarding — a preflight diagnoses missing prerequisites and walks the user through fixing them interactively, instead of failing opaquely or dropping straight to read-only** (PR #682). The 1.12.0 command detected its browser backend but, on a gap (no Claude Chrome extension, `gh` not authed, backend present-but-disconnected, no site permission), printed a terse failure and fell to read-only. For a command whose whole value is "drive my real Chrome for me," that left first-run users stuck without knowing what to fix. The command now runs a **preflight** at the start of every invocation that verifies each prerequisite in order — (1) GitHub CLI auth, (2) browser backend present, (3) backend connectivity, (4) site permissions (extension, non-blocking), (5) Chrome recency (soft advisory) — and on any gap runs a **self-heal loop**: alert the gap in plain language → print exact fix steps → wait via `AskUserQuestion` (`["Done — re-check", "Use fallback backend", "Skip to read-only"]`) → re-detect on "Done" → advance when clear, capped at 2 retries per gap before falling back. Preflight is **silent on success** — when everything is already configured it prints a one-line summary (`Preflight: ✓ gh auth · ✓ backend: claude-in-chrome · ✓ github.com permitted`) and proceeds with zero added friction. A new **`--setup`** flag runs preflight standalone (configure + verify, then stop — no survey, no browser action) for first-run onboarding or re-verifying after a Chrome / extension / machine change. Hard blockers (gh auth, no backend at all) never get silently bypassed; non-blocking items (site permissions) front-load the grant to avoid a stall mid-action but don't gate the run.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn-and-do.md`** — added a "Preflight — detect gaps and guided setup" section (self-heal loop, the five ordered checks, the summary line); added the `--setup` flag (frontmatter `argument-hint`, Args entry, a "Setup mode" output example); reworked the "Setup" steps so step 2 runs preflight and short-circuits under `--setup` and step 3 notes auth/backend were already resolved by preflight; added "Don't skip preflight / don't fail opaquely" and "Don't loop forever on a gap" to the Don't list.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.12.0` → `1.13.0`.

### 1.12.0 — 2026-06-17

**`/shipyard:my-turn-and-do` is now backend-agnostic and prefers the Claude Chrome extension (`claude-in-chrome`) over `chrome-devtools-mcp`, and adopts the extension's modern driving idioms** (PR #682). The command was added in #585 hardcoded to `chrome-devtools-mcp` + `--autoConnect` — but that's the wrong-fit backend for its actual job ("drive my *real, logged-in* Chrome to GitHub / provider consoles"): `--remote-debugging-port` is ignored on the default profile since Chrome 136, the `chrome://inspect` toggle is fiddly setup, and the spec spent paragraphs warning about the logged-out-isolated-instance hazard that mechanism creates. Meanwhile the plugin had **zero** references to the first-party `claude-in-chrome` extension — the path that runs *inside* the user's real Chrome and natively inherits every logged-in session with no remote-debugging setup. The command now detects and selects a backend at setup (extension preferred → `chrome-devtools-mcp` fallback → read-only `/my-turn`), with execution playbooks written backend-neutral against a tool-mapping table so the logic isn't duplicated per backend. Beyond the backend swap it adopts the extension's modern idioms: **read the page** (`read_page` / `get_page_text`) as the default perception instead of reflexively screenshotting (cheaper, more reliable, fewer tokens; screenshot reserved for when visual state genuinely matters), **tab reuse** via `tabs_context_mcp` at setup (act in an already-open tab instead of cluttering the browser), **`browser_batch`** for deterministic multi-step sequences, and an opt-in **`--record`** flag that captures the action as a GIF via `gif_creator` for later review. All guardrails are preserved unchanged — the survey/ranking reuse, plan step, confirmation gates, hard-confirm for irreversible/outward-facing actions, and judgment-call tee-up-and-hand-back — with the logged-out-isolated-Chrome warning rescoped to the fallback path only (it can't occur on the extension backend).

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn-and-do.md`** — replaced the `chrome-devtools-mcp`-only "Prerequisites" section with a "Browser backend — selection and detection" section (selection order, detection checks, backend tool-mapping table, absence detection); rewrote the execution step to add tab reuse, read-the-page perception, and `browser_batch` efficiency; added the `--record` flag (arg docs + a "Recording" subsection) and a `Backend:` line in the plan/output; rescoped the logged-out hazard and `--autoConnect` caveats into a fallback-only "chrome-devtools-mcp fallback notes" section; updated the frontmatter description/`argument-hint` and the "Don't" list. The survey/ranking, guardrails, third-party deep-links, and shipyard-label conventions are unchanged.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.11.4` → `1.12.0`.

### 1.11.4 — 2026-06-17

**The issue-work step-0.5 ungated-admin-direct-merge wait now fires on the `#465` shape (admin + zero required status checks, *regardless of `allow_auto_merge`*), not only the `#438` shape (`allow_auto_merge == false`)** (#602, PR #603). PR #600 shipped the #598 pre-merge wait keyed solely on `ALLOW_AUTO_MERGE == false`, so on `mattsears18/shipyard` itself — admin dispatcher, no required checks, but `allow_auto_merge: true` — the gate was skipped and every issue-work PR still admin-direct-merged ungated (PRs #600/#601 both landed `merged-direct-ungated` with checks pending at merge). The fix extends worker-preamble §0.5's detection to mirror the orchestrator-side two-shape logic already in `commands/do-work/setup.md` §1.3: fire the proactive wait-for-green-then-merge when the dispatcher is `ADMIN`/`MAINTAIN` AND (`allow_auto_merge == false` OR zero required checks), because `gh pr merge --auto` admin-direct-merges immediately when there is no required-check queue to gate on. The skip is preserved only when required status checks ARE configured OR a real auto-merge queue forms (`autoMergeRequest != null`); `allow_auto_merge: true` alone is no longer sufficient to treat the merge as gated.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — rewrote §0.5's fire-condition from the single `ALLOW_AUTO_MERGE == false` AND-of-three test to the two-shape OR (mirroring setup.md §1.3), reworded the detect-block intro and the skip-condition paragraph so the #465 shape (shape 2) fires regardless of `allow_auto_merge`, and updated the dogfood-repo intro to note both the #438 and #465 configs.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh`** — added four assertions covering the two-shape detection, the #465-shape fire-regardless-of-`allow_auto_merge` clause, the OR fire-condition source line, and the preserved-skip-only-when-gated wording, each citing #602.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.11.3` → `1.11.4`.

### 1.11.3 — 2026-06-17

**Issue-work workers now resolve the issue author's collaborator permission live when the dispatch omits `originating_author_trust`, instead of hard-defaulting to `external`** (#599, PR #682). On solo / small repos — the common case for this marketplace's users — the issue author is almost always the repo owner, who is unconditionally trusted; the old hard-`external` fail-safe turned every dispatch with a missing trust field into a guaranteed false positive (PR gated with `needs-human-review`, then a manual label-removal + re-arm), defeating the autonomous run. Repro (#599): a single-issue C=1 run on `mattsears18/mksolutionsky.com` shipped a PR but gated it with `needs-human-review` even though the issue author was the repo owner/admin, solely because the dispatch prompt omitted the trust field. The fix has the worker query `repos/{owner}/{repo}/collaborators/{author}/permission` and treat the push-capable roles (`admin`/`maintain`/`write`) as trusted — anything else (a non-collaborator's `read`/`none`, or an API error) resolves to `external`. The security boundary is preserved (genuinely untrusted authors still gate), keyed on the permission *value* not the call's exit status (the endpoint returns `200`+`read` for non-collaborators rather than 404, so a success-status check would mis-trust every stranger). When the dispatch prompt *does* carry the field, it's used directly as the orchestrator's authoritative session-cached resolution; the live fallback fires only on the field-absent path.

- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`** — rewrote step 6's field-absent fail-safe from "treat as `external`" to the live collaborator-permission resolution (with the `case` snippet, the permission-value-not-exit-status rationale, and the #599 repro); updated the step-7 Inputs summary and the corresponding "Don't" bullet so all three references describe the same fallback rather than the old hard-`external` default.
- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — updated the "Author-trust → auto-merge gating" line in the "What this skill does NOT cover" section to document the field-absent live-fallback behavior, satisfying the AC that the behavior be documented in the dispatch contract / worker-preamble.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.11.2` → `1.11.3`.

### 1.11.2 — 2026-06-17

**Issue-work workers now wait for a PR's own checks before an ungated admin-direct merge instead of merging ungated and fixing `main` forward** (#598, PR #682). On the repos shipyard is dogfooded against (admin dispatcher + `allow_auto_merge: false` + no required status checks), `gh pr merge --auto --merge` silently direct-merges *immediately* — landing the PR before its own CI completes. The pre-existing `merged-direct-ungated` advisory (#457) only flags this *after* the merge has fired, relying on the orchestrator's `fix-main-ci` divert to fix `main` forward; on the dogfood repos that's the common path, not the edge case, converting "wait ~1 min for the PR's checks" into "redden `main` + spend an extra PR + 2 CI cycles." Repro: PR #596 admin-direct-merged ungated, dropped a `decompose-epic.test.sh`-asserted README substring, reddened `main`, and cost PR #597 + a ~9-minute red-`main` window to recover. The fix adds a *pre-merge* gate (step 0.5) that detects the ungated configuration before arming the merge and waits for the PR's own checks to settle, merging only when green (a red PR is handed back so the orchestrator dispatches a fix-checks-only worker — no inline mode-switch). The `merged-direct-ungated` advisory + `fix-main-ci` divert remain as the defense-in-depth backstop for residual cases the pre-merge wait can't cover. The platform-level complement (adding the CI workflows as required status checks on the `main` ruleset, which makes `gh` block the admin-direct merge until they pass) is the maintainer's repo-config decision, noted but not dispatched.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — added step 0.5 to the "Auto-merge + snapshot-and-return pattern" section: the three-part ungated-config detection (`autoMergeAllowed == false` + `viewerPermission` ADMIN/MAINTAIN + zero required status checks) and the wait-for-checks-then-merge-when-green branch. Refined the §1.5 `merged-direct-ungated` paragraph (#457) to frame it as the now-rare defense-in-depth backstop. Updated the fix-checks-only `--watch` exception note to also name the issue-work step-0.5 wait as a sanctioned `--watch` block.
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`** — step 6 (trusted-author auto-merge) now points at the preamble step-0.5 pre-check so a worker reading only the per-mode file sees the ungated-path wait before arming auto-merge.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh`** — added a six-assertion block asserting the step-0.5 wait rule, the three-part detection, the `REQUIRED_CHECKS == 0` key, the merge-gate-re-creation rationale, and the retained `merged-direct-ungated` backstop, citing #598.

### 1.11.0 — 2026-06-15

**`/shipyard:file-issue` upgraded from a thin draft-and-file entry point to a research + refine-to-dispatch-ready workflow — the codified version of the interactive "file an issue — <description>" routine** (#593, PR #682). Previously the command took a free-form description, ran a duplicate search, drafted a title/body, and filed — but never inspected the codebase, so filed issues were often vague stubs a human had to refine before `/do-work` could pick them up. The command now inserts two passes between the duplicate search and the file step: (1) a **codebase research pass** — grep/read the surface the description implicates, confirm current behavior against ground truth (per the filing skill's "Verify before you file" gate), and sketch a plausible implementation path so `## Finding` / `## Suggested approach` cite real file paths instead of a paraphrase; (2) a **refine-to-dispatch-readiness check** — concrete verifiable acceptance criteria and a hard rule that the issue carries **no** `needs-triage` / `needs-human-review` gate by default (those exclude it from `/do-work` dispatch), applying a gate label only in the exception case where research showed the work genuinely can't be made ready, with the reason stated. The command **files only — it never starts the work** (no branch, no PR, no edits). A new leading **`--quick`** flag preserves the original thin fast-path for the "just dump a thought" case. We resolved against a separate `/issue` command: it would have duplicated this one, so the single `/file-issue` is the canonical issue-filing entry point.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/file-issue.md`** — reframed the intro/description from "thin entry point" to research + refine to dispatch-ready; added `--quick` flag parsing (step 1) and arg docs; reordered steps so the duplicate search (step 4) precedes the new research pass (step 5) and refinement check (step 7), with drafting (step 6) folding research findings in; the file step now conditionally includes a gate label only in the exception case; added "don't start the work", "don't gate by default", and "don't skip research unless --quick" to the Don't section.

### 1.10.1 — 2026-06-15

**`/shipyard:do-work` scope pre-flight Detector 2 extended — config-driven path set, `.claude/hooks/` coverage, and a deliverable-vs-mention false-positive guard** (#591, PR #682). Detector 2 (the pre-scope synthetic-defer for issues whose deliverable is an Auto-Mode-denied Claude-Code agent-config file) previously matched a hardcoded three-path set (`.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json`, `.mcp.json`) by literal body string-match. The #591 repro: a `--concurrency 4` session on `mksolutionsky.com` burned two full worker dispatches (#69 ≈103k tokens, #70 ≈112k tokens) on agent-config deliverables — a Stop hook (whose script lives under `.claude/hooks/`) and an MCP wiring — before the orchestrator pre-deferred the third by hand. Three extensions: (1) `.claude/hooks/` is now in the matched set as a trailing-slash directory prefix, so hook-script deliverables are caught even when the issue doesn't name `.claude/settings.json`; (2) the matched set is config-driven via a new `scope.self_modification_paths` knob (default the four-path set) — users *not* running under Auto Mode, where these edits succeed normally, set it to `[]` to disable the detector; (3) a deliverable-vs-mention guard fires the detector only when a configured path appears in a deliverable context (acceptance-criteria / "add"/"wire"/"create" framing), not when it's merely discussed in prose — closing the false-positive on meta-issues like #591 itself, whose body names all four paths while describing the detector. The defer class deliberately stays `human-decision-required` (declining the issue's suggested `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` + decomposition marker): the deliverable IS shippable as a single PR, blocked by a policy not by un-decomposability, so routing it to `/decompose-epic` would be wrong.

### 1.10.0 — 2026-06-15

**`/shipyard:do-work` gains a fix-main-ci attempt-cap / flake-detection circuit breaker, the analogue of the `blocked:ci` 3-attempt cap for fix-checks** (#589, PR #682). Previously, when `main` was red due to a *flaky* test (passes on a PR run, fails on the merge commit), the `fix-main-ci` divert loop had no mechanism to detect "we've tried N times and the same test keeps failing on the merge commit." Each fix PR went green on its own PR run, auto-merged, then the merge-commit CI failed again on the same test — and the orchestrator re-entered the same divert with no cap, burning a full CI suite + a Sonnet worker per cycle until a human noticed and quarantined the test (the #589 repro took 5 fix-main-ci dispatches before quarantine). The orchestrator now tracks a per-failing-workflow attempt counter (`main_ci_fix_attempts`, keyed by `earliest_red_workflow_name`); after `main_ci.max_fix_attempts` (default 3) consecutive fix PRs that each went green on their PR run but left the merge commit red on the **same** workflow, it stops auto-dispatching the divert and surfaces a structured flake escalation (recommend quarantine + tracking issue, or human CI-side investigation). The pass-on-PR/fail-on-merge pattern is itself the flake signal — a deterministic regression would fail the PR run too. A successful fix clears the counter at the next green divert-checks refresh, so only the genuinely-recurring flake accumulates toward the cap.

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh`** — added `main_ci.max_fix_attempts` (default `3`) to the built-in defaults.
- **`plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json`** — added the `main_ci.max_fix_attempts` integer property (`minimum: 1`) with a description tying it to #589.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md`** — added the session-local `main_ci_fix_attempts` orchestrator-state struct; struct count advanced fifteen → sixteen.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — step 4.5a's enqueue rule now gates `fix-main-ci` divert enqueue on the per-signature attempt cap (escalates instead of re-diverting at the cap; resets the counter when main goes green on a workflow); added the `🚩 FIX-MAIN-CI CAP HIT` escalation banner and the status-line `· flake-escalated: <sig>` suffix.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — step A's `shipped main-ci-fix` reconcile increments the attempt counter (and `noop: main already green` clears it); the fix-main-ci divert dispatch stashes the failing `signature` on the in_flight slot so the reconcile can key the counter.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md`** — added the `fix-main-ci flake escalations (#589)` end-of-session summary line (omitted when no signature escalated).
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-config.test.sh`** — added coverage for the `main_ci.max_fix_attempts` default, repo-override, deep-merge, and schema-rejection (non-integer, below-minimum) behavior.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — updated the pinned struct-count assertion from "fifteen" to "sixteen".
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.34` → `1.10.0`.

### 1.9.33 — 2026-06-15

**New `/shipyard:my-turn-and-do` command — action-taking sibling of `/my-turn` that drives the #1 human-blocked action in the user's real, logged-in Chrome via `chrome-devtools-mcp`** (#585, PR #682). `/my-turn` is and stays read-only; this new sibling keeps that contract intact while adding an execution counterpart. The command reuses `/my-turn`'s identical survey passes (A–D), ranking (P0/P1/P2 tiers, leverage-score-then-age sort), and third-party console deep-link derivation — then adds three phases: (1) a prereq check that detects a missing/misconfigured `chrome-devtools-mcp` server and falls back to read-only `/my-turn` output rather than silently acting in a logged-out isolated Chrome; (2) a plan step that prints the numbered browser steps and classifies the action (read-only navigation / mutation / judgment call) before touching the browser; (3) an execution step that drives Chrome via `--autoConnect` (Chrome 144+ — the correct post-Chrome-136 path that attaches to the user's real logged-in profile, not the `--remote-debugging-port` path which Chrome 136+ ignores for the default profile). Guardrails: confirm before any irreversible or outward-facing action (merge, post, delete, form submit), even with `--yes`; judgment-call items (PR review, nuanced replies) are teed up in the browser and handed back — never rubber-stamped; `--dry-run` for survey+plan only, no browser action; `--yes` pre-approves this run's planned mutations only. The issue's author recommended a separate command (not a `--do` flag on `/my-turn`) to preserve `/my-turn`'s read-only contract — that recommendation is what shipped. `/my-turn` now cross-references its new sibling.

### 1.9.32 — 2026-06-14

**CHANGELOG `PR #682` backfill now works on protected default branches — probes the ruleset and falls back to an auto-merged PR when a `pull_request` rule blocks direct writes** (#583, PR #686). The bug: the backfill step added in #581 (PR #582) attempted to write the corrected CHANGELOG entry via a direct GitHub Contents API PUT to the default branch. On any repo whose default branch has a `pull_request` ruleset (the most common production shape, and the shape of this very repo), that PUT is rejected with a 422 — the rule requires all changes to arrive via a merged PR. Because the prior implementation was fire-and-forget with `>/dev/null 2>&1`, the failure was silent: the `PR #692` placeholder stayed unchanged and nothing surfaced. Verified live immediately after #582 merged: the 1.9.31 entry on `main` still read `PR #TBD`. The fix adds three layers: (1) probe the branch ruleset first (`gh api repos/{repo}/rules/branches/{branch}`) so the write-path decision is explicit, not discovered via failure; (2) when the direct PUT fails AND the branch has a `pull_request` rule, fall back to a tiny auto-merged PR on a short-lived `do-work/changelog-backfill-<M>` branch using the same `gh pr create --label shipyard && gh pr merge --auto` path the workers already use successfully; (3) when both paths fail, emit a visible `WARNING` advisory — never a silent no-op. A regression test with 18 assertions guards the probe, the PR-fallback logic, the visible advisory, and the write-path ordering.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — A.1 `shipped` CHANGELOG backfill block rewritten with the three-layer write-path strategy: ruleset probe (`has_pr_rule`), direct PUT with captured exit code (`put_ok`), PR-based fallback (`do-work/changelog-backfill-<M>` branch + auto-merged PR), and visible WARNING advisory on total failure; "Scope and conditions" updated with a "Write-path selection" bullet summarizing the strategy; prose description updated to reference #583.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/changelog-backfill-protected-branch.test.sh`** — new 18-assertion regression test: ruleset probe present and references `contains(["pull_request"])`, `has_pr_rule` variable used, `put_ok` flag captured correctly, PR-fallback branch naming correct, `gh pr create` and `gh pr merge` present, `--label shipyard` applied, orphan-branch cleanup on PR-create failure, VISIBLE WARNING advisory for both failure cases, ordering of probe before PUT, and `#583` referenced in spec.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.31` → `1.9.32`.

### 1.9.31 — 2026-06-14

**Orchestrator now backfills `PR #682` in the CHANGELOG entry with the real PR number at `shipped` reconcile time** (#581, PR #686). The bug: every shipyard-authored CHANGELOG entry permanently says `PR #692` because the worker writes the entry before its PR exists and never backfills it. On direct-merge repos (admin access, no required status checks), the PR merges before the worker can push a follow-up commit to the head branch — so the placeholder is permanently stuck. The fix adds an orchestrator-side backfill step to the A.1 `shipped` reconcile handler in `steady-state.md`: the orchestrator already knows both the PR number `<M>` and the CHANGELOG path, so it reads the CHANGELOG from the default branch via the GitHub Contents API, replaces the first `PR #TBD` occurrence with `PR #<M>`, and commits the result as a small direct docs commit on the default branch. The step is guarded by `version_coordination.enabled` and `changelog_path` configuration, is idempotent (a no-op if `PR #TBD` is already absent), and fire-and-forget (failures log an advisory and never block the reconcile loop). A `sed` replacement without the `g` flag ensures only the current entry's placeholder is replaced, not any historical ones. A 5-assertion regression test in `do-work-split.test.sh` guards the behavior.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — new CHANGELOG backfill step added to the A.1 `shipped` reconcile block, between the cost-tracking section and the worktree-reap section; includes the full bash snippet (GitHub Contents API read + `sed` replace + PUT write), scope/conditions bullets, and fire-and-forget semantics.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — 5 new assertions: `PR #682` backfill step present in steady-state.md, guarded by `version_coordination.enabled`, uses the GitHub Contents API (`repos/<owner/repo>/contents/`), `sed` without `g` flag, and `[changelog-backfill]` advisory log prefix.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.30` → `1.9.31`.

### 1.9.30 — 2026-06-14

**Drain no longer dispatches fix-rebase on DIRTY-AND-red PRs — the per-poll bookkeeping now splits DIRTY PRs by rollup status and routes DIRTY+failing to fix-checks directly** (#577, PR #682). The bug: when a PR ended the session with `mergeStateStatus == DIRTY` AND a hard-failing check (e.g., a unit-test FAILURE introduced by a sibling merge), the drain's `D_dirty` classifier excluded it correctly (the spec already said "no hard-failure check"), but the orchestrator — reading only `mergeStateStatus` — dispatched fix-rebase anyway. The fix-rebase worker immediately bailed with `blocked rebase: PR has failing checks — needs fix-checks, not rebase`, wasting a Haiku dispatch plus an extra round-trip before the orchestrator finally re-routed to fix-checks. The fix introduces an explicit `D_dirty_red` set (DIRTY + hard-failure rollup) that is routed to fix-checks in per-poll action 1 alongside `R_new`, and updates the `D_dirty` definition to add a "never classify from `mergeStateStatus` alone" prohibition and an explicit `AND NOT in D_dirty_red` guard. The drain status line gains a `dirty_red=<count>` token (omitted when 0) so the routing decision is visible in drain logs.

### 1.9.29 — 2026-06-14

**Drain fix-checks/fix-rebase workers no longer bail "branch locked in another worktree" — completed issue-work agent worktrees are now force-reaped on `peer-alive` locks** (#576, PR #577). During the end-of-session drain, dispatching `fix-checks` or `fix-rebase` workers against PRs opened this session repeatedly failed because the original issue-work agent's worktree was still checked out on the `do-work/issue-<N>` branch. The A.1 `shipped` immediate-reap path and the drain pre-dispatch head-branch reap both deferred conservatively on `peer-alive` lock classifications — but a completed issue-work agent is logically done by definition (its terminal return string is in hand), so deferring caused the head branch to remain locked throughout drain, forcing every fix-checks and fix-rebase worker to bail. The fix applies the same force-reap-on-peer-alive posture A.0.5 uses for crash returns: (1) the A.1 `shipped` immediate-reap path now force-reaps even on `peer-alive`, auditing the override with classification `peer-alive-force`; (2) the drain pre-dispatch reap now distinguishes completed issue-work worktrees (branch pattern `do-work/issue-*`) from live fix-checks/fix-rebase workers (which must not be yanked) — force-reaped with classification `peer-alive-force-drain`, while other branch patterns still preserve the conservative defer. Both changes carry the `~/.shipyard/reap-audit.jsonl` classification tokens so an operator can trace the override. A regression test guards that neither path regresses to a peer-alive defer for completed issue-work worktrees.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — A.1 `shipped` immediate-reap block: `peer-alive` defer branch replaced with force-reap; prose extended with "Force-reap even on `peer-alive` here" rationale naming #576; closing "fire-and-forget" paragraph updated (peer-alive defers are no longer the fall-through path for the A.1 shipped site).
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — pre-dispatch head-branch reap: `peer-alive` defer replaced with a `case` branch that force-reaps for `do-work/issue-*` worktrees and defers for all other branch patterns; prose extended with "Force-reap even on `peer-alive` at the drain dispatch site" rationale; "Why it's safe to reap" section extended with #576 context; failure-mode prose updated to reference the #576 repro; closing commentary updated to name both classification values.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/drain-completed-worktree-reap.test.sh`** — new 22-assertion regression test: steady-state.md uses `peer-alive-force` and references #576, old defer-on-peer-alive commentary is absent, drain.md uses `do-work/issue-*` branch check and `peer-alive-force-drain` classification, drain.md explains force-reap safety and cites A.0.5 as precedent, worktree-reap.sh accepts both classification values with both phase strings and writes them to the audit log.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.28` → `1.9.29`.

### 1.9.28 — 2026-06-14

**A.0.5 crash-recovery now completes the version-coordination bump (manifest + CHANGELOG) before pushing the recovered PR** (#575, PR #576). The previous crash-recovery path (A.0.5 in `steady-state.md`) salvaged committed or dirty-worktree work by auto-committing and pushing a recovery PR — but did not add the manifest version bump or CHANGELOG entry the crashed worker had not yet reached. On version-coordinated repos (like `shipyard` itself) where the release rule mandates a bump per merged PR and admin + no-required-checks makes the direct-merge path immediate, a crash-recovered PR would auto-merge with no version bump, shipping the change invisibly to all installations. The fix adds a `a05_version_bump` helper that fires in both recovery branches (committed-but-unpushed and dirty-worktree) before the push: it reads `version_coordination.enabled`, compares the worktree manifest version to `origin/<default>`, and when they match (the worker never reached its own bump step), computes the next available version as a patch bump, rewrites the manifest file, and prepends a minimal CHANGELOG stub referencing the recovered issue. For the dirty-worktree path the bump folds into the auto-commit; for the committed-but-unpushed path it lands as a dedicated bump commit on top. The bump is fire-and-forget: when it can't be computed (manifest read fails, jq absent, coordination disabled), the recovery logs a loud `[reconcile-A.0.5-recovery] WARNING: recovered PR has no version bump — manual release bump required` advisory and pushes the PR anyway. Non-version-coordinated repos are entirely unaffected.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — A.0.5 "Pre-reap recovery check" section extended with a "Version-coordination bump during recovery" prose block and the `a05_version_bump` bash helper function; the function is called in both the committed-but-unpushed branch (step 1.5, adds a bump commit before the push) and the dirty-worktree branch (step 1.5, applies file edits before `git add -A` so the bump folds into the auto-commit); the WARNING advisory log line for the can't-compute case is specified; non-coordinated repos explicitly documented as unaffected; issue #575 referenced inline.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/crash-recovery-A05.test.sh`** — 11 new regression assertions for the #575 feature: issue reference present, section header present, gated on `version_coordination.enabled` and `manifest_path`, wt/origin version comparison, `a05_version_bump` helper defined, committed-path bump fires before push, dirty-path bump fires before `git add -A`, WARNING advisory string present, `vc_changelog` read, CHANGELOG stub marker present, fire-and-forget prose explicit, non-coordinated repos documented.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.27` → `1.9.28`.

### 1.9.27 — 2026-06-14

**The `shipyard` provenance label is now stamped on EVERY issue and PR shipyard creates, and every creation path ensures the label exists first** (#573, PR #682). Previously the stamp was applied inconsistently: `/do-work` stamped its PRs, but `/shipyard:file-issue` explicitly *declined* to stamp issues (its "Don't apply the `shipyard` label" rule), and no path guaranteed the label existed before filing — so on a repo where the label was never bootstrapped (a consumer repo that skipped `/shipyard:init`, or where the best-effort background `gh label create` raced/failed), shipyard-authored artifacts came out unlabeled and untraceable. The fix makes the rule uniform: every creation path applies `--label shipyard` and first runs an idempotent `gh label create shipyard --description "Worked on by /shipyard:do-work" --color 5319E7 2>/dev/null || true` so a missing label is created rather than silently dropped. The previously-conflicting conventions are reconciled — `/shipyard:file-issue`'s "Don't apply the `shipyard` label" rule is flipped to an affirmative stamp, and `CLAUDE.md`'s session-stamp section is broadened from "PRs only" to "every issue AND PR." The `audit:<dimension>` origin label and the `shipyard` session-stamp stay orthogonal: auditor-filed issues carry both.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/filing-github-issues/SKILL.md`** — new "shipyard provenance label" guidance documenting the ensure-then-label pattern; filing examples updated to include `--label shipyard`.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/file-issue.md`** — the `## Don't` "Don't apply the `shipyard` label" bullet flipped to an affirmative stamping rule; the step-6 `gh issue create` example updated to ensure the label first and pass `--label shipyard`.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/decompose-epic.md`** — ensures the label before creating sub-issues and passes `--label shipyard` on each sub-issue create.
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`** — workers instructed to apply `--label shipyard` on follow-up issues they file.
- **`CLAUDE.md`** — "Session-stamp label" section broadened to every issue AND PR across all creation paths, with the ensure-then-label pattern named.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-label-always-stamped.test.sh`** — new 22-assertion regression test guarding the stamping rule across every creation surface.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.26` → `1.9.27`.

### 1.9.26 — 2026-06-14

**Scope-preflight no longer re-gates issues a human has already cleared — honors label-timeline gate-clears and `<!-- do-work-decision-resolved -->` sentinel comments** (#569, PR #682). The bug: after a maintainer removed `needs-human-review` and posted a decision-resolution comment, a concurrent or subsequent `/do-work` scope pass re-read the body (which still contained "decisions needed / open questions" framing), returned `human-decision-required`, and silently re-applied the label — undoing the human's work and hiding the issue from dispatch again. Two complementary fixes close the loop: (1) the freshness check (step 6, added in #563) now treats a non-bot human's label removal OR a `<!-- do-work-decision-resolved -->` sentinel comment as a gate-clear signal that overrides the cached diagnosis, falling through to a fresh scope-agent dispatch rather than reusing a now-stale defer; (2) the scope-agent prompt instruction is extended to direct the agent to read the comment thread before judging actionability — if a resolution comment (sentinel or resolution-language match) supersedes the body's open-question framing, the agent must evaluate against the resolved context and return the ready shape rather than re-gating. A new `<!-- do-work-decision-resolved -->` sentinel convention is documented in `CLAUDE.md` so `/my-turn`-driven walkthroughs and human maintainers know to stamp it when recording decisions.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — freshness check extended with a new step 4 ("Check whether a human gate-clear has been signalled since the diagnosis comment") carrying two parallel checks: Signal A (label-timeline `unlabeled` event for `needs-human-review` by a non-bot after the diagnosis comment) and Signal B (`<!-- do-work-decision-resolved -->` sentinel comment posted after the diagnosis comment); the "When NOT to apply the freshness check" bullet list updated with both; the scope-agent prompt instruction prefixed with the comment-thread read directive and the resolution-comment instruction.
- **`CLAUDE.md`** — new "Decision-resolved sentinel (#569)" bullet in the Epic-decomposition handoff section, documenting the `<!-- do-work-decision-resolved -->` convention: recommended first line for a maintainer comment that records a decision and clears the gate, recognized by the freshness check's Signal B.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — 10 new regression assertions guarding both fixes: freshness check documents the human gate-clear signal, references #569, documents the `unlabeled` event and `needs-human-review` label, documents the `do-work-decision-resolved` sentinel (Signal B), names Signal A in the when-not-to-apply list, scope-agent prompt instructs reading the comment thread and names the sentinel; CLAUDE.md documents the sentinel convention.

### 1.9.25 — 2026-06-13

**New shared `shipyard:auditing-authenticated-surfaces` skill — the safe, framework-agnostic pattern for live-URL auditors that tour login-walled surfaces, plus wiring from the `web-ux` and `a11y` auditors** (#567, PR #682). The live-URL auditors previously carried only a thin "optionally: test credentials for authenticated surfaces" line and told the agent to type the email/password into the browser MCP itself — an unsafe model (the secret lands in transcripts/tool logs) and an unreliable one (a fresh signup can't clear email-verification / OAuth / age gates; a saved-then-reloaded SPA session comes back logged-out). The reusable kernel for doing it correctly lived only in a consumer repo's `CLAUDE.md`, so every login-walled audit re-derived it. This lifts that kernel into a shared skill (sibling to `audit-rubrics` / `filing-github-issues`) capturing four rules: (1) auditors must NOT self-authenticate by typing secrets — use a login harness that reads creds from a gitignored env file, never echoes them, and captures artifacts (screenshots / axe-core JSON / DOM probes) for the auditor to judge; (2) require a pre-provisioned account; (3) the SPA `storageState` gotcha (Firebase Web SDK et al. store the token in IndexedDB/localStorage, which Playwright `storageState` does NOT serialize — log in and tour within ONE live context); (4) assert on a protected route that 302s when unauthenticated, not on `/` (which renders the public marketing view when logged out). Project-specific wiring (which account, which env file, which seed command) stays in the consumer repo.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/auditing-authenticated-surfaces/SKILL.md`** — new skill with the four reusable rules and a "putting it together" recap; framework-agnostic, with the secret-vs-judgment division of labor (harness holds the secret, auditor holds the judgment).
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/web-ux-auditor.md`** + **`plugins/shipyard/agents/a11y-auditor.md`** — added a "surfaces behind a login wall — read `shipyard:auditing-authenticated-surfaces` first" callout next to each auditor's test-credentials input line.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/auditing-authenticated-surfaces.test.sh`** — new regression test (mirrors `worker-preamble.test.sh`) guarding skill existence + frontmatter, the four rules, and both auditors' references.
- **`README.md`** — registered the new skill in the plugin file-tree.

### 1.9.24 — 2026-06-13

**New `/shipyard:resolve-decisions` command — interactively walk a decision-gated issue's blocking decisions one-by-one (each with a recommendation), then record the answers and clear the gate; `/my-turn` stays read-only and offers the hand-off** (#566, PR #682). When `/my-turn`'s single next action is "answer the N blocking decisions on issue #X" (a decision-gated `needs-human-review` issue), the maintainer can now resolve it in one guided pass. The issue offered two designs and the maintainer-author recommended **(a)** — keep `/my-turn` read-only and put the walkthrough + record-and-unblock mutation in a dedicated sibling command — which is what shipped: `/my-turn` only *offers* (read-only) to hand off, and the new `/resolve-decisions` owns the per-decision 5-part walkthrough (restate → options → **recommendation+reasoning** → lock + carry-forward → room to clarify) plus the mutation (post a structured `<!-- shipyard-resolve-decisions -->` decisions comment + remove the gating label so `/do-work` re-admits the issue). A partial walkthrough (skipped/halted decisions) records a partial comment and leaves the gate in place; `--dry-run` previews the comment + label-removal without mutating; non-decision `needs-human-review` gates (epic-decomposition, external-dependency, external-author trust) are refused rather than walked.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/resolve-decisions.md`** — new command: trigger surface (the same answerable-decision signals `/my-turn`'s leverage-score-4 recognizes), decision-gated confirmation + untrusted-input posture, the 5-part per-decision walkthrough with mid-walkthrough controls (think-through / example / skip / stop), the record-and-unblock mutation with idempotency sentinel, partial-run and `--dry-run` handling, empty state, and a Don't list (no fabricated decisions, no neutral menu, no early gate-removal on partial runs, no non-gate label removal, no dispatch).

### 1.9.23 — 2026-06-13

**`/my-turn` ranks within each tier by a leverage score, not raw age — the single `→ Next:` directive no longer surfaces a stale undecomposable epic** (#565, PR #682). The within-tier secondary sort was a flat `createdAt` ascending (oldest-first), which in single-action mode made the *stalest* item the sole `→ Next:` directive. On a P0 tier dominated by long-lived `needs-human-review` issues, oldest-first regularly floated an auto-undecomposable epic — the *least* actionable item — to the top, directly contradicting the command's "single highest-leverage thing blocked on you" promise (repro: a 4-week-stale `epic(aso)` #540 ranked #1 over genuinely high-leverage one-decision issues). The fix replaces the flat oldest-first secondary sort with a **leverage score** (derived entirely from signals passes A–D already collect — no new `gh` calls): pure-decision items that flip to dispatch-eligible with one human call score highest; quick external/console actions next; on-device/multi-party verification below that; auto-undecomposable epics (`<!-- do-work-needs-decomposition -->` + `couldn't auto-decompose:` sentinel) score lowest and sink. `createdAt` ascending remains the final tie-breaker within a leverage score.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md`** — rewrote the "Secondary sort" section to define the 4-tier leverage score (pure-decision → quick-external → on-device → undecomposable-epic) with age as the tie-breaker; updated the Ranking intro and the "Internal fields" projection to carry the leverage score as the primary within-tier key; added a "Don't sort the within-tier order by age alone" entry to the Don't section.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh`** — 5 new assertions guarding the leverage-score contract (leverage score is the primary key, age is the tie-breaker, pure-decision scores highest, undecomposable epics sink, #565 cited).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.22` → `1.9.23`.

### 1.9.22 — 2026-06-12

**Scope-result freshness window eliminates redundant scope-agent re-dispatches when fresh marker-tagged diagnosis comments already exist on issues** (#563, PR #564). Previously, the orchestrator re-dispatched scope agents every session even when prior sessions had already documented their conclusions as marker-tagged comments (`<!-- do-work-external-dependency -->`, `<!-- do-work-human-decision-required -->`, `<!-- do-work-needs-decomposition -->`) on the issues. The repro: a maintainer bulk-cleared `needs-human-review` labels from 19 open issues; the orchestrator launched 7 scope agents (~330k tokens) that returned nearly verbatim re-derivations of 1–4 day-old comments. The fix introduces `scope.diagnosis_reuse_hours` (config knob, default 72h): before dispatching a scope agent, the orchestrator reads the issue's newest marker-tagged diagnosis comment; if it is within the window AND its mechanical evidence re-validates cheaply (cited blockers still open, body unchanged since the comment), it records the defer with provenance `cached-diagnosis` and skips the agent dispatch. Drain.md 5.b is extended to cover two tiers: the existing same-session carve-out (Tier 1, #549) and a new cross-session tier (Tier 2, #563) that re-validates `cached-diagnosis` entries within the configured window before dispatching a fresh scope agent.

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh`** — built-in default `scope.diagnosis_reuse_hours = 72`.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md`** — added `cached-diagnosis` to valid `provenance` values in the `deferred_issues` state struct.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — new "Scope-result freshness check" section (after the pre-scope detector batch, before scope-agent dispatch) with the 5-step check + `cached-diagnosis` recording path.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — 5.b extended to also cover `cached-diagnosis` entries; Tier-1 (same-session, #549) and Tier-2 (cross-session `cached-diagnosis` within window, #563) carve-outs documented; pre-drain audit banner updated to include `cached-diagnosis` reuse counts.

### 1.9.20 — 2026-06-12

**Scoping agents now MUST include shared regression-test suite files in `files` for fix-class issues, preventing drain-phase rebase bails caused by unclaimed same-file edits** (#554, PR #561). When two sibling PRs edit the same shared test accumulator file (`do-work-split.test.sh` in this repo), the one whose scope agent omitted the file from `claimed_paths` causes the orchestrator's hard-collision check to miss the overlap. The conflict only surfaces at drain-phase rebase time, costing a wasted `fix-rebase` dispatch plus a manual rebase — the most expensive outcome short of lost work. Root cause: scope-agent variance (the #546 and #548 scope agents in the same session diverged on whether to claim `do-work-split.test.sh`, despite receiving the same prompt). The fix adds an explicit `files` augmentation rule to the scoping-agent prompt instruction in `setup.md` step 6: for fix-class issues (identified by `bug`/`P0`/`P1`/`P2` labels or `fix(`/`fix:`/`bug:` title prefix) in repos with a shared regression-test accumulator file (detected by a `*.test.sh` file touched by 10+ distinct commits, or named in `CLAUDE.md`), the agent MUST include that file in `files` even when the issue body does not name it. Mirrors the coordination-managed-paths contract (`plugin.json` + `CHANGELOG.md`) that was already implicit in worker behavior but not agent-prompt-visible. Config-driven `always_claimed_paths` surface deferred to phase 2 (requires schema + orchestrator coordination changes).

### 1.9.19 — 2026-06-12

**New `changelog-monotonicity-scan.sh` CI gate + worker-side assertions close the silent released-entry deletion gap** (#555, PR #559). Previously a resolved-wrong merge conflict could silently drop released `### <version>` headings from `CHANGELOG.md` with no CI gate firing — the conflict-marker gate (#436) only catches *unresolved* markers. Session `do-work-20260612T115814Z-28614` demonstrated the failure: PR #552 and #553 resolved their CHANGELOG conflicts correctly (no markers survived) yet both dropped `### 1.9.10` and the `### 1.9.9` heading from `main`; the loss was only noticed when a human eyeballed the file during a later manual rebase of PR #551. The fix is two-layer, mirroring #436's approach: (1) a new `changelog-monotonicity-scan.sh` script that diffs the `### <version>` heading set between the merge-base and the PR head, failing when any base-side heading is absent on the head; and (2) worker-side assertions in `fix-rebase.md` (step 5.6) and `issue-work.md` (release step) that run the scan before committing or force-pushing and bail with `blocked` if any released heading was deleted. The scan is false-positive-safe: only headings present on the merge-base count, so a PR re-numbering its own unmerged entry (which was never on the base) does not trip it. A new `changelog-monotonicity-scan.test.sh` test suite unit-tests the scanner (clean pass, deletion detected, opt-out directive, false-positive guard, multi-deletion, brand-new CHANGELOG) and also runs the scanner against the working-tree CHANGELOG to pin the live-repo invariant, mirroring the #436 test pattern.

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/changelog-monotonicity-scan.sh`** — new script; accepts an optional `<merge-base-ref>` arg (defaults to auto-computed `git merge-base HEAD origin/HEAD`); extracts `### ` headings from the base and checks all are present in the head-side CHANGELOG; opts out on a `changelog-monotonicity-scan: allow` directive; exits 0 (clean), 1 (missing heading(s)), or 2 (env error); bash 3-compatible (no `declare -A`).
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/changelog-monotonicity-scan.test.sh`** — new test suite; 18 assertions covering (1) artifact existence, (2) scanner readability, (3) live-repo clean pass, (4a–4f) behavioral unit tests in synthetic git fixtures (clean add, deletion detected, PR-own-entry renumber no-false-positive, opt-out directive, multi-deletion, brand-new CHANGELOG), (5) fix-rebase.md worker assertions, (6) issue-work.md worker assertions; fixtures use `git init -b main` to pin the default branch (CI-safe).
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md`** — new step 5.6 after step 5.5 (conflict-marker assertion): runs `changelog-monotonicity-scan.sh` against `origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH`; bails `blocked rebase: deleted released CHANGELOG heading(s) after conflict resolution` if any heading is missing; links to issue #555; describes the two-layer defense and the scanner's graceful-no-op behavior when the binary isn't present.
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`** — new "CHANGELOG entry write — never delete an existing `### <version>` heading" paragraph in step 4 (after the follow-up-PRs release rule): instructs workers to run `changelog-monotonicity-scan.sh` before committing a CHANGELOG edit and bail `blocked: deleted released CHANGELOG heading(s)` if any heading is missing; links to issue #555; notes the CI gate is the load-bearing layer and the worker check is defense-in-depth.

### 1.9.18 — 2026-06-12

**Fix investigate-mode auto-close commands to use `--reason "not planned"` for noise and duplicate dispositions** (#557, PR #558). Previously the `gh issue close` commands in `investigate.md` §4c used the default state-reason `COMPLETED`, which is semantically wrong for both the noise and duplicate dispositions — nothing was fixed, so GitHub's "not planned" reason is the correct signal. Downstream consumers (issue queries filtering `reason:completed`, the GitHub UI's purple-vs-gray closed-issue icon, roadmap tooling) read `COMPLETED` as "done/fixed" rather than "acknowledged and not actionable." The fix adds `--reason "not planned"` to both auto-close commands in §4c.

- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/investigate.md`** — §4c noise close: added `--reason "not planned"` to the `gh issue close` command; §4c duplicate close: added `--reason "not planned"` to the `gh issue close` command.

### 1.9.15 — 2026-06-12

**Wire investigate-mode dispatch into orchestrator queues, prompt templates, reconcile parser, and worktree-isolation guard** (#556, PR #682). The `shipyard:investigate-worker` shim and `agents/issue-worker/investigate.md` per-mode spec shipped with #514, but the orchestrator's command specs never called them: trusted-author `needs-triage` issues were silently dropped at setup.md step 4's client-side filter, the routing table omitted the `investigate` mode row, the dispatch decision tree had no `investigate_candidates` queue step, and step A.1 had no handlers for the `investigated+*` return string family. This PR wires all four missing pieces:

- `setup.md` step 4 now routes trusted-author `needs-triage` issues to a separate `investigate_candidates` queue (gated on `triage.investigate_dispatch` config key, default `true`; set to `false` to revert to pre-#556 silent-drop behavior). The dispatch-gate drop-label set removes `needs-triage` from the enumerated drop list and the "Why each label is enumerated" rationale note is updated accordingly.
- `steady-state.md` dispatch decision tree gains step **1.5** — `investigate_candidates` non-empty → pop the front entry, read `triage.auto_close` config, dispatch `subagent_type: "shipyard:investigate-worker"` with a well-formed `mode: investigate` prompt template embedding `originating_author_trust` and `triage_auto_close`.
- `steady-state.md` routing table gains the `investigate` mode row (`shipyard:investigate-worker`, Sonnet-pinned). The hook note is updated to confirm `shipyard:investigate-worker` was already added to the guarded set when the shim shipped with #514 — no hook change needed.
- `steady-state.md` A.1 reconcile parse table gains handlers for all six investigate-mode return values: `investigated+fixed` (ships PR, appends to `session_prs`, removes `needs-triage`, reaps worktree), `investigated+needs-human-review` (records, removes `needs-triage`), `investigated+closed-noise` (records), `investigated+duplicate` (records), `reaped:` (re-enqueues into `investigate_candidates`), and `blocked` (standard classify-and-route).

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — step 4 client-side filter: removed `needs-triage` from dispatch-gate drop set; added `investigate_candidates` routing block with `triage.investigate_dispatch` config gate; updated "Why each label is enumerated" rationale note; Sort-the-survivors paragraph clarifies it applies to non-`needs-triage` survivors only; `raw_backlog` note clarifies that non-empty `investigate_candidates` does not constitute a non-empty raw_backlog.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — dispatch routing table: added `investigate` mode row; hook note updated to reference pre-existing guard; dispatch decision tree: added step 1.5 with `investigate_candidates` queue, `triage.auto_close` config read, author-trust computation, and `mode: investigate` prompt template; A.1 reconcile section: added full `investigated+*` return-string family handlers.

### 1.9.14 — 2026-06-12

**A.0 and A.1 reconcile-turn bash templates now derive the session-id cwd-independently, preventing silent token attribution loss on #477 cwd-leaks** (#548, PR #551). Previously the A.0 `bump-tokens` and A.1 `read-tokens` / `worktree-reap.sh reap` bash blocks used a literal `"<session-id>"` template placeholder that the orchestrator was expected to interpolate from `cat .shipyard-session-id` — a cwd-relative read. The reconcile turn is precisely the turn where the #477 cwd-leak fires (the harness relocates the orchestrator's Bash-tool cwd into the just-returned agent's `agent-*` worktree), so that `cat` came up empty, every `session-state.sh` call exited 64 (`--session-id is required`), and the turn silently lost: token attribution (`bump-tokens`), the cost comment (`read-tokens --format comment`), the `session_prs` append, and the immediate-reap audit-log entry — all four writes in A.0/A.1. The fix prepends the setup.md §0.55 cwd-independent `derive-session-id` preamble (using `worktree-reap.sh derive-session-id --repo-root`), updates all four `--session-id "<session-id>"` occurrences in A.0/A.5 and A.1 to use `"$SESSION_ID"` / `"${SESSION_ID:-unknown}"`, and emits a loud `[session-id-derive] empty — aborting turn writes` advisory (instead of cascading exit-64s that read as success) when both derive paths return empty. Repro: session `do-work-20260612T035752Z-44019`, reconcile turn ~04:25Z — 169k tokens unattributed, PR #1925 briefly missing from `session_prs`, cost comment failed with `Body cannot be blank`, agent worktree not immediately reaped.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — new "A.0 required preamble" subsection added before the "Strict path" heading, documenting the mandatory cwd-independent `SESSION_ID` derive block and the loud abort when both derive paths return empty; strict-path bash block updated to use `"$SESSION_ID"` with an `if [ -n "$SESSION_ID" ]` guard; degraded-path bash block updated identically; A.1 shipped cost-comment bash block updated with inline `SESSION_ID` derive + guard + loud skip advisory; A.1 green/noop cost-comment bash block updated identically; A.0.5 reap bash block and A.1 shipped-immediate-reap bash block each updated to derive `SESSION_ID` after the `STABLE_DIR` cd anchor and pass `"${SESSION_ID:-unknown}"` to `worktree-reap.sh reap`; `cwd-anchor-before-reap invariant` note extended with a new "Session-id derive in reap blocks (#548)" paragraph.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.13` → `1.9.14`.

### 1.9.13 — 2026-06-12

**Pre-drain 5.b re-validation now skips the scope-agent dispatch for same-session, evidence-verified defers — cutting 4–6 redundant ~40k-token scope-agent invocations per drain when all defers were created minutes earlier in the same session** (#549, PR #553). Previously `drain.md` §5.b dispatched a fresh scope agent for every `scope-agent`-provenance entry, unconditionally. When every defer in `deferred_issues` was created by the current session's own scope pre-flight — minutes earlier, under the same prompt, with mechanically-confirmed evidence (named blocker still OPEN, `evidence_pointer` still passes per-class shape check, issue body not amended) — the re-dispatch added cost with zero correctness gain: the scope agent would re-read the same body and reach the same conclusion. The same-session freshness carve-out skips the scope-agent dispatch and re-validates mechanically when all three conditions hold: (1) `entry.deferred_at >= session.started_at` (same session), (2) the entry's `evidence_pointer` passes the per-class shape check + cheap ground-truth probe (`gh issue view` for named blockers), and (3) the issue body's `updatedAt` precedes `deferred_at` (no mid-session amendment). Entries failing any condition still get the full scope-agent dispatch, preserving the #299 drain-on-misjudged-defers protection intact for cross-session entries. The pre-drain audit banner gains a `<Ka>/<Kb>` split to show scope-agent-dispatched vs. mechanically-confirmed entries separately.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — §5.b "Re-validate scope-agent entries": restructured to make the detector batch the first step (unchanged), then added the same-session freshness carve-out block before the scope-agent dispatch path; carve-out describes the three conditions, per-class ground-truth probes, pass/fail log lines, and the safety rationale; scope-agent dispatch path now framed as the "entries that do not qualify for the carve-out" path; cost paragraph updated from "one scope-agent invocation per defer" to "at most one, bounded by non-carve-out entries" with the repro reference to #549. §5.c pre-drain audit banner: `scope-agent-backed: <K> (re-validated)` line extended to `<Ka> scope-agent dispatch, <Kb> same-session mechanical` with a note that the short form is used when `<Kb>` is 0.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.10` → `1.9.13`.

### 1.9.12 — 2026-06-12

**Scope-agent defers with an invalid `defer_reason_class` token are now normalized rather than silently mis-recorded, and the scope-agent prompt explicitly demands the literal enum token** (#547, PR #682). Previously the setup.md step 6 recording path handled a *missing* `defer_reason_class` by defaulting to `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR`, but said nothing about a class that was *present-but-invalid* (a free-text paraphrase or invented synonym). Three of seven deferred returns in session `do-work-20260611T231002Z-91471` used out-of-enum strings (`"media_production"`, `"external_console_dependency"`, `"Umbrella / Epic requiring human discretion"`), forcing the orchestrator to improvise — risking inconsistent `deferred_issues` records. The fix adds an explicit invalid-class normalization branch in the recording path: when `defer_reason_class` is present but not one of the five valid tokens, the orchestrator infers the nearest valid class from the `evidence_pointer` shape (blocker-reference → `confirmed-blocker-still-open`; author-login → `untrusted-author`; structured prefix → `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR`; concrete named decision → `human-decision-required`; otherwise → `external-dependency`) and logs `[scope-preflight] #<N> defer_reason_class "<raw>" normalized to <class>`. If the pointer itself also fails its shape check, the rejection path fires instead of normalization. The scope-agent prompt instruction is also tightened to demand the literal token ("do NOT paraphrase, invent synonyms, or use free-text descriptions") and names concrete bad examples from the repro session.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — recording path step 3: replaced single "missing class → default" sentence with a full three-branch normalization block (missing → default, present-but-invalid → infer from evidence_pointer shape, valid → use as-is); scoping-agent prompt instruction: added explicit "EXACT LITERAL TOKEN" requirement, enumerated all five valid tokens, named concrete bad examples, and clarified that invalid tokens cost an extra reshaping pass.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — new `(N+5)` block: seven regression assertions guarding the #547 issue citation, the normalization heading, the present-but-invalid branch enumeration, the evidence_pointer shape-match log line, the EXACT LITERAL TOKEN prompt requirement, the five-token enumeration, and the concrete bad-example citation.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.10` → `1.9.12`.

### 1.9.10 — 2026-06-12

**The concurrent-session guard no longer aborts under zsh's `NOMATCH` when no `agent-*` worktrees exist, fixing a P1 silent self-assign drop** (#546, PR #547). The guard (step C, per-dispatch before self-assign) previously iterated with a bare glob: `for wt_dir in "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.git/worktrees"/agent-*; do`. Under zsh's default `nomatch` option a glob that matches no entries raises a fatal error and aborts the whole bash block — dropping the `gh issue edit <N> --add-assignee @me` self-assign that followed in the same block. Observed in session `do-work-20260611T231002Z-91471` (mattsears18/lightwork, ~23:25Z 2026-06-11) with zero `agent-*` worktrees on disk. Same class as #335 (which fixed setup.md's 3b loop); the fix replaces the bare glob with the #335 `find` idiom that exits 0 on no matches, so the loop body simply doesn't iterate when the directory is empty. The now-redundant `[ -d "$wt_dir" ] || continue` guard is also removed since `find -type d` only emits existing directories by construction.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — concurrent-session guard (step C, per-dispatch before self-assign): bare glob `"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.git/worktrees"/agent-*` replaced with `$(find "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.git/worktrees" -maxdepth 1 -type d -name 'agent-*' 2>/dev/null)`; `[ -d "$wt_dir" ] || continue` guard removed; comment updated to cite issues/546 and issues/335 as the fix source.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — new `(N+4)` block: three regression assertions guarding the #546 citation, the absence of the bare-glob form, and the presence of the find-based replacement.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bumped `1.9.9` → `1.9.10`.

### 1.9.9 — 2026-06-11

**The per-PR release rule now applies to follow-up PRs a worker opens within the same dispatch, closing a silent CHANGELOG gap when a post-merge CI hotfix ships with no version bump** (#544, PR #545). Previously `issue-work.md` step 4 specified that every primary PR must cut a release (version bump + CHANGELOG entry), but said nothing about additional PRs the worker opens in the same dispatch (e.g. a test-only CI fix after the primary PR landed as `merged-direct-ungated`). Those follow-up PRs merged with no bump and no CHANGELOG mention — invisible to the marketplace until a subsequent sibling's entry happened to name them retroactively. The fix adds a new paragraph to `issue-work.md` step 4 that extends the per-PR release rule to any same-dispatch follow-up PR, and specifies that a follow-up must compute its own version slot from `origin/<default-branch>` after the primary bump has landed (the orchestrator-supplied `next_available_version` covers only the primary PR). Regression tests guard the new rule heading, the primary-only orchestrator note, and the version-computation instruction. Repro: session `do-work-20260611T220126Z-96473` — primary PR #541 (1.9.7) shipped #537; follow-up PR #542 (test-only CI fix) merged in the same dispatch with no bump and no CHANGELOG entry.

- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`** — step 4: new "Follow-up PRs within the same dispatch must also cut a release (#544)" paragraph added after the existing "Per-PR release rule" paragraph; specifies follow-up version-slot computation from `origin/<default-branch>`, cross-references the orchestrator-supplied version as primary-PR-only, and names the #544 repro session.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — new `(N+3)` block: four regression assertions guarding the #544 issue citation, the follow-up-PR release rule heading, the primary-PR-only orchestrator note, and the version-computation instruction.
