### 1.9.8 — 2026-06-11

**Scope-agent dispatch prompt now embeds the four `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` evidence-pointer prefixes verbatim, preventing shape-validation rejections on well-evidenced defers** (#538, PR #543). Previously the scoping-agent prompt instruction told agents to populate `evidence_pointer` to match "the per-class shape table in setup.md step 6" — but that table lives in the orchestrator spec, not in the dispatch prompt the agent actually reads. A scope agent that correctly diagnosed a confirmed-non-shippable issue and wrote a natural-language evidence pointer (e.g. "docs/brand/partners-church.md line 143: 'The video itself is not yet produced'") would be rejected by the orchestrator's per-class validator for missing a structured prefix, despite the evidence being conclusive. The fix embeds the four required prefixes (`Missing dependency:` / `Multi-service coordination:` / `Multi-PR sequence:` / `Body cites <artifact>:`) and worked examples directly in the scoping-agent prompt instruction, so an agent never has to look up the shape table to know what the validator requires. No orchestrator logic changed — only the prompt instruction that prevents the gap at the source.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — scoping-agent prompt instruction (step 6, "Deferred shape" section): added one sentence naming the four `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` required prefixes and three worked examples, inserted after the existing "defers without an `evidence_pointer` … are treated as malformed" clause.

### 1.9.7 — 2026-06-11

**`/do-work` now migrates pre-fold legacy gate labels (`needs-design`, `needs-decomposition`, `tracking`, `blocked:agent-hard`) at session start, closing a silent gate-bypass window on repos that ran pre-#515/#519/#521 shipyard versions** (#537, PR #541). Step 3d.2 previously only migrated the bare `blocked:agent` legacy label (sub-sweep b) and swept `blocked:agent-soft` (sub-sweep c). The four additional labels eliminated by the #515/#519/#521 binary-backlog folds — `needs-design`, `needs-decomposition`, `tracking`, `blocked:agent-hard` — were never swept, so issues on consumer repos still carrying those labels silently passed the step-4 dispatch filter (which excludes `needs-human-review` but not the former labels), allowing epics, design-gated issues, and hard-refused issues to re-enter the workable queue. The fix adds three new sub-sweeps running in sequence after sub-sweep c: sub-sweep d (`needs-design` → `needs-human-review`); sub-sweep e (`needs-decomposition`/`tracking` → `needs-human-review` + `<!-- do-work-needs-decomposition -->` marker comment so `/decompose-epic` can find them); sub-sweep f (`blocked:agent-hard` → same refuse/dependency-wait discriminator as sub-sweep b — open `Blocked by #N` ref → no label (body-ref filter gates), else → `needs-human-review`). All three sweeps are idempotent one-shot-per-issue: removing the old label makes subsequent passes a no-op. Advisory counters and the step-2 overview block are updated to report each migration class; the `--fast` skip note adds the five new label counts.

Step 2 `--fast` note: four new `gh issue list` count lines added for the legacy labels. Step 2 overview "Auto-cleared this session" block: four new migration rows added (omit when zero). Overview description updated to trigger on any of the new counters > 0.

### 1.9.6 — 2026-06-11

**`external-dependency` and `human-decision-required` defers now exit the re-scope loop — `needs-human-review` label + distinct body markers + comment dedupe** (#536, PR #540). Previously only `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` defers applied `needs-human-review`, so `external-dependency` and `human-decision-required` defers re-entered the dispatch queue every session, spawned a redundant scope agent, re-derived the same conclusion, and posted an identical diagnosis comment. The repro (session `do-work-20260611T114039Z-43655`, `mattsears18/lightwork`) saw lightwork#1557 accumulate 5+ consecutive identical comments and 7 scope agents burn ~190k tokens re-deriving known defers in a single session. The fix has three parts: (1) the Deferred recording path in `do-work/setup.md` step 6 now applies `needs-human-review` (with ensure-then-label-then-verify per #508) for `external-dependency` and `human-decision-required` defer classes, exiting them from the dispatch queue permanently until a human clears the label; (2) each class stamps a distinct body marker on its diagnosis comment (`<!-- do-work-external-dependency -->` / `<!-- do-work-human-decision-required -->`) so the classes are distinguishable within the shared `needs-human-review` pool and `/decompose-epic` never picks them up by mistake; (3) the recording path now enforces comment dedupe — before posting, it checks for an existing comment with the same class marker and matching conclusion, and skips the post if one exists (closing the spam independently of the label fix). Label conventions in `CLAUDE.md` are updated to document all three changes.

Pre-scope detector section updated: synthetic `human-decision-required` defers now post with the `<!-- do-work-human-decision-required -->` marker via the recording-path step 2 table.

### 1.9.3 — 2026-06-11

**The dispatch loop may no longer hand a *mechanically-workable* issue to the human — attempt-then-escalate, never escalate-without-attempting** (#531, PR #532). A `/do-work` session terminated with two workable, dispatchable self-filed follow-up issues (#529, #530) still open and surfaced them to the maintainer as "left for a fresh run" / "say the word and I'll work them" — forcing an explicit re-instruction to finish work the orchestrator had itself filed and was capable of doing. Root cause: the termination assertion subtracts `deferred_issues` from the live backlog before checking net-new, and the orchestrator *judgment-excluded* its own just-filed issues from the "workable" set (rationalizing "they're meta-issues about the orchestrator" / "#530 rewrites the reconcile path I'm running" / "avoid infinite-regress"), so the loop declared termination while the mechanical step-4 filter still had candidates. None of those rationalizations matches any of the five `defer_reason_class` values, so the defer was off-book. The fix makes the bypass impossible to construct: `drain.md`'s termination-assertion step 4 now specifies the workable net-new count is computed by the **mechanical** step-4 client-side filter alone — the orchestrator may not judgment-exclude candidates — and a mechanical count > 0 forbids termination and requires dispatch. `dont.md` gains the load-bearing dispatch-loop rule, naming the four invalid rationalizations, tying the legitimate defer set to the five `defer_reason_class` values, affirming self-filed follow-ups re-enter the backlog like any other issue, and noting the correct bounded-regress guard is a soft per-session cap on self-filed-this-session issues — never a blanket refusal to work them.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — termination-assertion step 4 gains a "The workable count is MECHANICAL, never discretionary (#531)" paragraph: the post-filter list is the step-4 client-side filter alone, judgment-exclusion is forbidden, the four invalid rationalizations are named and tied to the (non-matching) five `defer_reason_class` values, and mechanical net-new count > 0 forbids termination.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md`** — new dispatch-loop bullet "Don't hand a workable issue to the human — attempt-then-escalate, never escalate-without-attempting (#531)" with the invalid-rationalization list, the `defer_reason_class` gating, the self-filed-follow-ups-re-enter-the-backlog rule, and the soft-cap bounded-regress guidance.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — nine #531 regression assertions guarding the dont.md rule (issue cite, rule heading, five-class tie, self-filed re-entry, soft-cap guard) and the drain.md mechanical-count contract (issue cite, mechanical-not-discretionary, no judgment-exclusion, count>0-forbids-termination).

### 1.9.2 — 2026-06-11

**The worker return-contract now explicitly forbids arming a background waiter and returning a non-terminal narrative — workers must run synchronously to a terminal state** (#529, PR #532). A `/do-work` issue-worker armed a `run_in_background` test-waiter and returned a progress narrative (*"I have a background waiter armed … I'll wait for that notification"*) instead of one of the documented terminal strings. The harness treats the assistant message ending the turn as a `status: completed`, so the dispatch was reported complete while the actual work was stranded mid-flight (no commit, no pushed branch, no PR — the worktree carried 0 commits + 5 dirty files). The orchestrator's A.0.5 crash-aware reap + re-dispatch recovered it, but the failed dispatch burned ~145k tokens for zero output (session `do-work-20260611T031912Z-98402`, worker `a20c…` against #522). The worker contract documented the terminal-return *vocabulary* (and a "no narrative status updates" bullet) but never explicitly prohibited *arming a background process as the wait mechanism* and returning before it resolves — this PR closes that gap. A new first bullet in `shipyard:worker-preamble` § "Return-contract discipline" names the prohibition, the failure mode, and the correct alternative (block your own turn on a foreground call); every per-mode spec's return section cross-references it. The orchestrator-side observability ask is also implemented: A.0.5 now counts a fully-wasted dispatch (a crash-like / non-terminal return that left no recoverable work) and the end-of-session summary surfaces it as a `Wasted dispatches (#529)` line (omitted when zero) so the cost is visible rather than silently absorbed.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — Return-contract discipline section: new "Run all work synchronously to a terminal state — NEVER arm a background process and return" bullet (the #529 repro, the `status: completed`-on-stranded-work failure mode, the foreground-block alternative), and the existing "No narrative status updates" bullet extended with the `"I'll wait for that notification"` exemplar and a cross-reference to the new rule.
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`**, **`fix-checks-only.md`**, **`fix-rebase.md`**, **`fix-main-ci.md`**, **`fix-failing-prs-batch.md`** — each return section now references the #529 synchronous-return clause so a worker reading only its own per-mode file still sees the prohibition.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — A.0.5 increments `wasted_narrative_dispatches` / `wasted_narrative_tokens` when the crash-aware reap fires on a non-terminal return that left no recoverable work.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md`** — new `Wasted dispatches (#529)` flat-summary line + per-line rule (silence-default when zero; the recoverable-work case is excluded from the count).

### 1.9.1 — 2026-06-11

**The `/do-work` reconcile-once gate (#317) no longer pure-silent-skips a phantom that carries a *different, still-in-flight* sibling worker's terminal outcome** (#530, PR #531). #317's A.−1 gate silently skips any `task-notification` whose `task-id` is already in `reconciled_agent_ids` — correct for genuine wind-down phantoms (`"Done."`, `"Acknowledged."`). But the harness has been observed to **cross-wire a still-in-flight sibling worker's only completion notification onto a reaped worker's `task-id`**: the phantom's id is reconciled, yet its body asserts a real terminal outcome (`shipped #<N> via PR #<M>`, `green #<M>`) for a *different* target that maps to a live `.in_flight` slot. A literal pure-skip there **strands the in-flight sibling** — its real completion arrived only as the phantom, so the slot hangs unreconciled until end-of-session. The fix adds a MANDATORY pre-skip body-inspection: before the silent `return`, the orchestrator parses the phantom body, and if it names a target tied to a currently in-flight slot, runs the trust-but-verify probe (issue `state` + PR `mergeStateStatus`) and reconciles the **in-flight sibling from verified ground truth** (keyed on the sibling's `agent_id`, not the reaped phantom id) rather than skipping. The body's narrative is treated as a *trigger to verify*, never authority to reconcile on its word, so #317's double-reconcile protection is preserved while the strand-the-sibling hole closes. Repro: session `do-work-20260611T031912Z-98402` — worker #522's reaped `a20c…` id re-fired twice carrying the #527 retry's and #523/#528 worker's outcomes; pure-skip would have hung both slots.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — A.−1 reconcile-once gate gains a "sibling-strand variant" subsection: the #530 repro, the pre-skip body-inspection pseudocode (parse body → match in-flight slot → ground-truth probe → reconcile sibling from verified state, else fall back to silent skip), and the verification-gate rationale.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md`** — new dispatch-loop bullet forbidding a *pure* silent-skip when the phantom body asserts a terminal outcome for a still-in-flight target, cross-linking the A.−1 variant.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — six #530 regression assertions guarding the issue citation, the still-in-flight sibling variant, the pre-skip check, the reconcile-from-verified-state directive, the preserved wind-down silent-skip, and the dont.md bullet.

### 1.9.0 — 2026-06-11

**`/my-turn` now renders third-party console deep links in operator directives** (#523, PR #524). When a surfaced next-action's actual work lives in a provider console — create a Test User in the Meta App Dashboard, enable an auth provider in the Firebase Console, paste a CI secret into a GitHub repo's Actions settings, submit a build in App Store Connect, etc. — the rendered directive now appends a clickable deep link to the **most-specific reachable page**, derived from identifiers already in hand (app ID, project ID, owner/repo slug). The information was already present in the surveyed issue/PR context; the feature turns it into a URL so the user skips a manual, error-prone provider-UI navigation across many nested pages. The concrete motivating case: `/my-turn` surfaced "Create a Test User on the test Meta App `982594884358918`" with the app ID in prose but no link — the correct `https://developers.facebook.com/apps/982594884358918/roles/test-users/` deep link was trivially constructible from the ID. A provider URL-template table (Meta / Firebase / Vercel / App Store Connect / Apple Developer / Play Console / GCP / GitHub) drives the substitution; when a required identifier is missing, the render falls back to the provider's top-level console rather than emitting no link. Scope is kept tight to `/my-turn`'s directive-rendering path — the read-only contract is preserved (link derivation is pure string construction over already-surveyed data, no new `gh` round-trip, no provider-API discovery of missing identifiers). The shared filing/worker preambles were intentionally left untouched: they don't render third-party-console operator directives, so wiring the table there would have been speculative scope creep.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md`** — new "Third-party console deep links" section under Output (provider URL-template table, top-level-console fallback table, and derivation rules: use-identifiers-in-hand / never-fabricate, read-only constraint, one-link-per-action). Wired into **both** render modes: the single-action `→ Next:` directive gains a second example + a URL-line rule for the deep link; the list-mode Rendering rules gain a per-item deep-link bullet with an example row.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh`** — new #523 regression assertions guarding the deep-link section, the most-specific-reachable-page contract, the Meta / Firebase / App Store Connect / GitHub-secrets templates, the top-level fallback, and the issue citation.

### 1.8.70 — 2026-06-10

Phase-2 slice of the **binary-backlog label-taxonomy collapse** (#519, follow-up to #515). Folds the `needs-decomposition` / `tracking` epic-decomposition label pair into `needs-human-review`. Unlike the phase-1 `needs-design` fold (#515 — an inert label with no machinery), this pair carries **live machinery**: `/decompose-epic` keys its candidate fetch on `--label needs-decomposition` and applies `tracking` as a post-shard parent marker. A naïve label-only fold would silently break auto-sharding (the command would find zero candidates among the now-broad `needs-human-review` pool). The fold therefore **re-keys the machinery onto `needs-human-review` + a body marker**: `do-work/setup.md` step 6's Deferred recording path now applies `needs-human-review` and stamps a `<!-- do-work-needs-decomposition -->` HTML-comment sentinel on the scope-agent diagnosis comment, and `/decompose-epic`'s candidate fetch becomes `--label needs-human-review` filtered to issues carrying that trigger marker — reproducing exactly the candidate set the old `--label needs-decomposition` fetch produced while never picking up the *other* `needs-human-review` issues (refined user-feedback, external-author, design-gated). A sharded parent now **keeps** `needs-human-review` (a human-gated tracking umbrella — there is no separate `tracking` label) and the `<!-- do-work-decompose-agent -->` idempotency comment, not a label swap, is what prevents re-processing. The load-bearing acceptance criterion — `/decompose-epic` still finds epic candidates after the fold — is verified concretely (the candidate-fetch `select(...)` jq selects only marker-carrying epics, never unrelated `needs-human-review` issues). Scope is deliberately tight to this fold; `needs-refinement` (#520), `blocked:agent-hard` (#521), and the `needs-human` reconciliation (#522) remain deferred to human review.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — step 6's Deferred recording path applies `needs-human-review` (re-keyed from `needs-decomposition`, preserving the #508 ensure-then-label-then-verify hardening) and prepends the `<!-- do-work-needs-decomposition -->` marker to the diagnosis comment for the `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` class only; the step-3a label-create batch drops the `needs-decomposition` / `tracking` creates; backlog-overview buckets 5 / 5.5, the step-4 dispatch-gate exclusion enumeration, and the "why enumerated" explainer updated.

### 1.8.69 — 2026-06-10

Phase-1 slice of the **binary-backlog label-taxonomy collapse** (#515, companion to #514). The long-term north star is a strictly binary issue backlog — every open issue is either workable-by-`/do-work` (no gate label) or workable-by-human (`needs-human-review`, surfaced by `/my-turn`). This slice folds the **inert** design-gate label `needs-design` into `needs-human-review`: `needs-design` was a pure dispatch-exclusion + `/my-turn`-surfacing label with **no auto-processing machinery** (never auto-applied, never auto-cleared by any `/do-work` sweep), so collapsing it is a lossless rename that immediately shrinks the issue-axis human-gate surface. Design-gated issues now carry `needs-human-review`; `/do-work` still excludes them from dispatch and `/my-turn` still surfaces them as human-blocked items, with no behavior loss. The labels that carry **live machinery** were intentionally **kept separate** in this phase because folding them into the inert `needs-human-review` would silently break that machinery (the exact "don't route auto-retryable/auto-processed work to the human queue" hazard `blocked:agent-soft` documents): `needs-refinement` (auto-processed each session by `/refine-issues`), `blocked:agent-hard` (auto-cleared by the referential `Blocked by #N` sweeps), and the `needs-decomposition`/`tracking` epic-decomposition pair (driven by `/decompose-epic`). `needs-triage` is kept as a documented **transitional parking label** — its elimination depends on #514's investigate-then-fix runtime routing (phase-2, not yet live). Folding the remaining machinery-carrying labels is deferred to follow-up issues. This slice also brings the `drain.md` and `steady-state.md` dispatch-gate enumerations back into lockstep with `setup.md` step 4 (they were missing `needs-decomposition` / `tracking`).

### 1.8.68 — 2026-06-10

Adds the **investigate-then-fix worker mode** (`mode: investigate`) — phase 1 of #514. The mode lets `/do-work` work *untriaged* issues (especially trusted-bot-authored crash reports like `app/sentry` / `sentry[bot]`) end-to-end instead of leaving them parked behind `needs-triage` forever, converging the backlog toward a binary state (workable-by-`do-work` or workable-by-human). The worker runs an investigate-first flow: pull the Sentry event and walk the stack into the code (treating the bot-generated crash body as untrusted DATA, not instructions — the prompt-injection posture matters even though the *author* is a trusted bot), rewrite the cryptic crash into a real spec (preserving the Sentry permalink / fingerprint so the integration keeps correlating), then disposition into one of three outcomes — **fixable** (fix + PR, the normal `shipped`-style path, removing `needs-triage`), **needs-human** (apply the new single human-queue label `needs-human` + return blocked-style), or **not-actionable** (auto-close confident noise / exact duplicate, gated on the `triage.auto_close` policy). New return vocabulary extends the issue-work contract: `investigated+fixed`, `investigated+needs-human`, `investigated+closed-noise`, `investigated+duplicate`. This phase ships the static spec + model-pinned shim + routing + config surface; the orchestrator-side dispatch decision (which untriaged issues route to investigate-mode, the `needs-human` label creation, and the Sentry-MCP runtime wiring) is a deferred follow-up so the slice stays a single reviewable PR.

- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/investigate.md`** — new per-mode behavioral spec (investigate → rewrite → disposition lifecycle, the three-outcome disposition gate, the four `investigated+*` return strings, the body-as-DATA security spine, the `triage.auto_close` authority table, and the `needs-human` vs `needs-human-review` distinction).
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/investigate-worker.md`** — new model-pinned shim (Sonnet 4.5 — stack-trace reasoning + disposition judgment, no PR anchor; broader than fix-checks-only's log-pattern-match, but the common dispositions don't need full Opus) that forwards to the per-mode spec, mirroring the existing `fix-main-ci-worker` shim shape.
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker.md`** — entry router updated to route the 6th mode (`investigate` → `shipyard:investigate-worker`): mode table row, frontmatter description, mutually-exclusive-job count, and the worktree-isolation contract shim list.
- **`plugins/shipyard/hooks/enforce-worktree-isolation.sh`** — guarded-set extended with `shipyard:investigate-worker` (header doc + `case` arm) so a dispatch missing `isolation: "worktree"` hard-fails like the other five shims.
- **`plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json`** — `models.investigate` added; new `triage` block (`auto_close` enum, `max_investigations_per_session` cost cap).
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh`** — built-in defaults for `models.investigate` (`claude-sonnet-4-6`) and the `triage` block (`auto_close: confident-only`, `max_investigations_per_session: null`).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump to 1.8.68.

### 1.8.67 — 2026-06-10

Fixes `/do-work`'s step-0.55 session-id derive grabbing the **oldest orphan** orchestrator worktree instead of the live session's, silently misattributing every `session-state.sh` write to a dead session's file (#513). When multiple `orchestrator-*` worktrees coexist in `.claude/worktrees/` (orphans from prior crashed/un-reaped sessions), the old inline `awk '... {print p; exit}'` derive returned the *first* match in `git worktree list --porcelain` order — which is the oldest orphan when orphans accumulate. The recovered `$SESSION_ID` then pointed at a dead orphan's `.shipyard-session-id` stash, so this session's `update` / `bump-tokens` / drain bookkeeping all landed in the orphan's session file while the real file stayed at init defaults. Same-repo, so the `--expected-repo` guard never tripped; the result is a corrupted cost ledger, a broken `/shipyard:status`, and a `--resume` that loads the wrong state (the repro misattributed 245k tokens + 11 deferred issues + `session_prs += [1897]` to a 6-day-old orphan). The fix adds a new `worktree-reap.sh derive-session-id` subcommand that selects the **newest-by-mtime** `orchestrator-*` worktree — the live session's worktree was just created this run, so it has the newest dir mtime among any coexisting set — and reads its stash; strictly better than first-by-listing-order and a no-op in the common single-worktree case. The subcommand globs `<repo-root>/.claude/worktrees/orchestrator-*` directly (cwd-independent given the explicit `--repo-root`, so it stays immune to the #477 cwd-leak), skips candidates lacking a readable/non-empty stash, and whitespace-trims the result.

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/worktree-reap.sh`** — new `derive-session-id` subcommand (header docstring, usage line + description, `derive_session_id` function, `main` dispatch case). Portable mtime via `stat -c %Y` / `stat -f %m` fallback; requires a non-empty stash so a half-set-up/cleaned worktree can't shadow an older valid one.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — step 0.55 derive snippet now calls `worktree-reap.sh derive-session-id` (carrying the canonical `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` preamble) instead of the inline `awk ...; exit` first-match walk; prose documents the newest-vs-first rationale and cross-links the deeper #280 step-1.6.5 orphan-reap fix.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-reap.test.sh`** — new test group (67–75) covering the empty/single-worktree cases, the #513 repro (live wins over 5 older orphans), recency-beats-lexical-order, stash-absent/empty skip, agent-* exclusion, whitespace-trim, and bad-usage exits.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump to 1.8.67.

### 1.8.66 — 2026-06-09

Fixes `/do-work`'s end-of-session agent-worktree reap stalling on a busy checkout and stranding *this session's own* shipped worktrees (#509). The cleanup-summary step-3 sweep iterated every `.git/worktrees/agent-*` and ran `classify-lock` per worktree; on a checkout with many accumulated cross-session leftovers (17+ in the repro) the loop was slow enough that it didn't finish within the orchestrator's bash window — reaping a handful then going quiet before the post-loop counters printed, leaving this session's merged-work worktrees (whose branches are `[gone]`) un-reaped. The fix adds a **targeted this-session pass that runs first**: a new `worktree-reap.sh reap-session-worktrees` subcommand takes the orchestrator's own agent-ids (the union of `reconciled_agent_ids` and the live `in_flight.<slot>.agent_id` values, which the orchestrator already tracks) and reaps them directly — bounded by that small known set, so it always completes regardless of how many unrelated worktrees the checkout has accumulated. The generic sweep then runs second as the cross-session straggler / safety-net pass (its running totals seeded from the targeted pass so counts compose), and a stall in it can no longer strand this session's own work. The new subcommand classifies the lock per the existing #138/#263 semantics (reaps on `no-lock`/`dead`/`self-ancestor`, defers only on `peer-alive`), routes the actual remove + audit write through the existing `reap` transaction (audit `phase: "cleanup-session-targeted"`), unions `--agent-id` flags with one-id-per-line stdin, de-dupes, and silently skips worktrees the steady-state immediate-reap (#282) already removed mid-session. Repro: lightwork session `do-work-20260609T034015Z-47977` — 17 agent worktrees, ~6 reaped, then the loop stalled, leaving `agent-add8ef31…` (#1877, merged) and `agent-a67e506f…` (#1663, merged) for manual `git worktree remove --force`.

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/worktree-reap.sh`** — new `reap-session-worktrees` subcommand (docstring, usage, `reap_session_worktrees` function, `main` dispatch). Anchors cwd to `--repo-root` before routing through `reap_action` (whose `git worktree remove` is cwd-dependent), short-circuits on `SHIPYARD_ORCHESTRATOR_PID`, defers on `peer-alive`, emits `reaped: agent-<id>` / `deferred: agent-<id>` per acted-upon id, supports `--dry-run`.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md`** — step 3 split into a targeted this-session pass (3.0, runs first by explicit agent-id) and the generic straggler sweep (3.1, seeds its `reaped_worktrees`/`deferred_live` totals from the targeted pass so counts compose).
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-reap.test.sh`** — new test group (58–66) covering dry-run, real reap + audit `phase`, silent-skip of already-reaped ids, flag+stdin union, dedup, peer-alive defer, `SHIPYARD_ORCHESTRATOR_PID` self-ancestor short-circuit, and bad-usage / empty-input exits.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump to 1.8.66.

### 1.8.65 — 2026-06-09

Documents the soft-collision tier's same-section limitation and gives the orchestrator a named drain branch for it, instead of leaving the `blocked rebase` outcome an ad-hoc surprise (#507). The soft-collision tier lets up to `--soft-collision-concurrency` workers claim the same additive-docs path on the premise that PR-land conflicts are trivially auto-resolvable — true when claimers edit *independent sections*, but false when two+ siblings edit the **same section** of the same soft-collision file. That produces a real prose-content conflict, not the coordinated manifest `.version` row + CHANGELOG top-of-file insert that `fix-rebase.md` §4.6 resolves deterministically, so `fix-rebase` correctly bails `blocked rebase #<M>: conflict extends beyond coordinated manifest+CHANGELOG rows` — but nothing documented that this is *expected*, so the DIRTY PR stranded ad hoc. This is option 1 (document + accept) of the three #507 offered: it removes the silent-strand surprise without making the soft cap section-aware (the deliberately-rejected option 2, which costs scope-agent return-shape changes for little real throughput). The existing blocked-rebase reconcile path (add to `rebase_blocked_prs`, comment `Needs manual rebase.`, surface still-DIRTY for hand-resolution) is now the named drain branch for the case. Repro: session `do-work-20260609T025704Z-59825` ran #499/#500/#501 as a C=5 batch all editing the same `my-turn.md` Pass-B section; #504 and #506 went DIRTY and were hand-resolved (all five still shipped green — recoverable, P2).

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — soft-collision rules gain a "same-section content conflicts are an accepted limitation" callout (premise holds for independent sections, not the same section; §4.6 does not cover it; the bail is expected, not a worker failure); the blocked-rebase reconcile bullet now names the `conflict extends beyond coordinated manifest+CHANGELOG rows` reason as the expected soft-collision sub-case and points back to the soft-collision premise boundary.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md`** — soft-collision-tier section records why same-section edits are the accepted residual cost and why option 2 (section-granularity) was rejected, with the #507 repro.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md`** — new Don't entry: a `blocked rebase … conflict extends beyond coordinated manifest+CHANGELOG rows` on a soft-collision PR is expected, not a worker failure; don't re-dispatch (same conflict re-bails) and don't make the soft cap section-aware to "fix" it.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — new test group (29) asserting the #507 citation, the expected bail-string documentation, and the same-section premise-boundary prose across steady-state.md, RATIONALE, and dont.md.

### 1.8.64 — 2026-06-09

Hardens `/do-work`'s defer-labeling path so the `needs-decomposition` surfacing label can't silently no-op when the label is absent (#508). `gh issue edit … --add-label needs-decomposition` is atomic: if step 3a's backgrounded `gh label create … &` group was skipped, raced, or its subshell errored under `2>/dev/null || true`, the label may not exist, and the whole edit exits non-zero — the apply silently fails and (on any repo whose defer path also clears the `@me` self-assign in the same combined edit) the `--remove-assignee` is dropped too. Net effect without the fix: a confirmed-non-shippable epic is re-scoped every future session (the waste the label exists to prevent) and may be left assigned. The setup.md step-6 Deferred recording path now **ensures-then-labels-then-verifies**: an idempotent `gh label create needs-decomposition` runs immediately before `--add-label` (so the apply never depends on 3a), any `--remove-assignee @me` is split into its own `gh issue edit` (so a label failure can't drop the unassign), and a read-back of `.labels` warns loudly if the label still didn't land. Repro: lightwork session `do-work-20260609T034015Z-47977` — #1673 and #1769 both failed the atomic edit; #1673 also kept its `@me` assignment, caught only by a post-cleanup label audit.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — step-6 Deferred recording path rewritten from a bare `--add-label needs-decomposition` to the ensure-then-label-then-verify pattern (idempotent label-create + split mutations + `.labels` read-back warning); corrected the stale "13 idempotent labels" comment on the step-3a batch to 14.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — new test group (28) asserting the `#508` citation, the idempotent `gh label create needs-decomposition` before `--add-label`, the `ensure-then-label-then-verify` discipline, the read-back warning string, and the split-mutations requirement.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump to 1.8.64.

### 1.8.63 — 2026-06-09

Adds the `/shipyard:decompose-epic` command — the automation layer on top of #498's `needs-decomposition` surfacing label (#501). Where #498 makes a confirmed-non-shippable epic *visible* (it exits both the `/do-work` re-scope loop and surfaces via `/my-turn`), a human still had to split the epic, sequence the pieces, and re-feed them. `/decompose-epic` removes that manual step for the mechanically-decomposable cases: for a scope agent's `Multi-PR sequence:` or `Missing dependency:` evidence class it auto-shards the epic into dispatch-ready GitHub sub-issues chained via `Blocked by #<sibling>` (so `/do-work`'s existing bucket-7 blocker-state gating sequences them automatically), links them to the parent via native `addSubIssue` + `Part of #<N>` references, inherits the parent's priority, then swaps the parent's `needs-decomposition` label for a new `tracking` marker. Non-mechanical classes (`Multi-service coordination:`, `Body cites <artifact>:`) and any low-confidence breakdown fall through to the existing human handoff with a `couldn't auto-decompose:` comment — the same "fix it, or kick it to a human" posture as `/refine-issues`' escalate-to-triage branch. The command is explicit/human-invoked for v1 (an opt-in auto path inside `/do-work` is deliberately deferred), mirrors `/refine-issues`' sentinel-keyed (`<!-- do-work-decompose-agent -->`) parallel-worker shape, applies the same untrusted-input discipline, and caps fan-out at `--max-subissues` (default 8).

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/decompose-epic.md`** — new command: setup (label-ensure for `needs-decomposition` + `tracking`, candidate fetch, sentinel filter, `--issue` restriction), the decomposition-worker prompt template (Step A read evidence class → Step B ordered breakdown + confidence gate → Step C create sub-issues + native `addSubIssue` link + parent label-swap → Step D escalate fall-through), `--dry-run` mode, end-of-run summary, idempotency invariants, the deferred auto-path note, and a Don't section.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — wired the `tracking` parent-epic marker into the step-3a idempotent label-create batch, the secondary label-create block, the step-4 dispatch-exclusion enumeration + its "why each label is enumerated" rationale, and the step-2 backlog bucket-5 criteria (so a decomposed parent surfaces as non-workable, not as a workable leaf).
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md`** — Pass B now surfaces `needs-decomposition` epics as human-blocked items (P1), distinguishing "not yet decomposed — run `/decompose-epic`" from a `couldn't auto-decompose:` genuine human-handoff via the sentinel comment.
- **`CLAUDE.md`** — new "Epic-decomposition labels" subsection documenting the `needs-decomposition` → `tracking` label pair and the `/decompose-epic` flow.
- **`README.md`** — registered `/decompose-epic` in the quick-start example, the slash-command reference list, and the repo-layout tree.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/decompose-epic.test.sh`** — new regression guard (command existence + frontmatter + args, the two decomposable + two escalate evidence classes, the `Blocked by` / `Part of` / `addSubIssue` sequencing+linking, the sentinel + `tracking` swap, the `/do-work` exclusion wiring, `/my-turn` surfacing, and the README / CLAUDE.md registration).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump to 1.8.63.
### 1.8.62 — 2026-06-09

Surfaces non-clearable `blocked:agent-hard` refuses as a `/my-turn` human-review item (#500). Before this change, `/my-turn` Pass B only surfaced a `blocked:agent-hard` issue when it was *auto-clearable* — i.e. when every `Blocked by #N` reference in the body was already CLOSED/MERGED (a P2 "housekeeping: remove the stale label" item). The common case — a genuine security / scope / prompt-injection *refuse* that `steady-state.md`'s bail-classification table stamps `blocked:agent-hard` precisely because it "genuinely needs human review" — has no `Blocked by` references at all (or has an open one), so it never surfaced. The result: `/do-work` hard-blocks the issue out of dispatch across sessions while `/my-turn` stays silent, and real refuses stack up with no handoff. The fix splits the signal by clearability: a non-clearable hard-block (≥1 referenced blocker still open, OR zero `Blocked by` refs) now ranks **P1 — decisions** with the action "review the refuse reason in the comment; clear, re-scope, or close"; the clearable subset stays **P2 — housekeeping**.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md`** — Pass B's `blocked:agent-hard` bullet split into non-clearable (P1) and clearable (P2) signals with the `Blocked by #N` reference-state decision rule (zero refs ⇒ refuse ⇒ non-clearable); the P1 — decisions and P2 — housekeeping ranking tiers updated to carry the two cases; Rendering-rules action-verb examples extended with both the non-clearable ("review the refuse reason…") and clearable ("remove the blocked:agent-hard label…") actions.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh`** — four new assertions (non-clearable split documented, clearable-vs-non-clearable distinction, the `Blocked by` keying, and the `#500` citation), bringing the suite to 29 assertions.

### 1.8.61 — 2026-06-09

Gives `/shipyard:my-turn` a surfacing signal for the `needs-design` label so design-gated issues stop falling through both loops (#499). `/shipyard:do-work` correctly *excludes* `needs-design` from dispatch (it's not agent-workable until the design call is made — the client-side filter in `do-work/setup.md` step 4, `drain.md`, and `steady-state.md`), but `my-turn.md` had no Pass B signal or ranking entry for it, so a `needs-design` issue was dispatch-excluded by `/do-work` *and* invisible to `/my-turn` — stacking up in the backlog with no path to a human. Since `needs-design` is by definition "blocked on a human decision," it's exactly the kind of item `/my-turn` exists to surface.

Pairs with #498 — if a do-work non-shippable-defer surfacing label lands on `needs-design`, this is what makes those defers visible.

### 1.8.60 — 2026-06-08

Applies a `needs-decomposition` surfacing label when a `/do-work` scope agent confirms an issue is non-shippable as a single PR (the "epic" case), closing the human-handoff gap (#498). Previously, when the scope pre-flight reached `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` it posted a diagnosis comment but applied *no label*, so the issue fell through the cracks of both automated loops: `/do-work` re-scoped it every single session (a scope agent burning tokens to re-derive the same "too big" conclusion), and `/my-turn` — being entirely label-driven — never surfaced it as a human decision. The intended diagnosis-comment handoff never reached the human queue. The fix adds a dedicated `needs-decomposition` label (semantically distinct from `needs-design` / `needs-triage`), applies it only for the `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` defer class (the other four defer classes are not decomposition handoffs and keep the no-label behavior), and adds it to `/do-work`'s dispatch-exclusion set so the same epic stops being re-scoped. The drain-phase scope-agent re-validation removes the label again if a fresh pass finds the issue ready (a slicing-miss defer can still recover). The companion `/my-turn` surfacing side is tracked separately in #499.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — registers `needs-decomposition` in both idempotent label-ensure blocks (the step 0.7 background group, comment bumped from "12" to "13 idempotent labels", and the canonical step 3a block plus its prose intro); the step 6 Deferred recording path now applies the label via `gh issue edit --add-label needs-decomposition` gated on `defer_reason_class == "confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR"`, replacing the old "Do not add a label" instruction (other classes keep the no-label behavior); adds `needs-decomposition` to the step 4 dispatch-exclusion set and to the "enumerate each literal label name" rationale note.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — the 5.b scope-agent re-validation ready-shape branch now removes `needs-decomposition` (gated on the original `confirmed-non-shippable-as-single-PR` class) when a re-validated defer flips to ready, with a `(removed needs-decomposition)` log suffix, so a slicing-miss epic isn't left hidden behind the new exclusion label.

### 1.8.59 — 2026-06-08

Hardens every worktree-reap block in the steady-state loop against a cwd-leak hazard distinct from the derivation-leak fixed in #452/#477 (#497). When the harness leaks the orchestrator's Bash-tool cwd into a just-returned agent's `agent-*` isolation worktree, and the orchestrator then reaps *that* worktree on the reconcile turn, `git worktree remove --force` deletes the directory the shell is standing in — and every subsequent bare git command in the same block (the `git worktree prune`, any follow-up `fetch`/`log`) dies with `fatal: Unable to read current working directory`. The reap silently half-fails, and on the reconcile turn it can skip the post-merge CI watch on the exact `merged-direct-ungated` path where that watch matters most (repro: session `do-work-20260608T204506Z-36559` reconciling PR #496). The spec's old recovery (`cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"`) could not rescue the block — git resolves its own deleted cwd before reading anything, so the rev-parse fails first; empirical testing also showed `git worktree list --porcelain` (the idiom the issue's suggested fix assumed would survive) fails the same way. The fix anchors cwd to a stable directory derived *cwd-independently* via the #477 porcelain idiom (orchestrator worktree first, primary as fallback) and `cd`s to it **before** the reap, while cwd is still valid — so the remove/prune never run with cwd inside the doomed directory.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — added a `STABLE_DIR` porcelain-anchor preamble (`cd "${STABLE_DIR:-/}"`) to all four reap blocks: A.0.5 crash reap, the A.1 `shipped`-immediate reap (#282), step B's per-completion reap (#334), and step C 2d's pre-dispatch reap (#368). The A.1 / step B / step C blocks additionally re-derive their `.git/worktrees` walk root from the porcelain-derived primary instead of the leaked cwd. A new "cwd-anchor-before-reap invariant" prose section documents the hazard, the cwd-independence requirement, and why `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` cannot recover a deleted cwd.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/reconcile-reap-cwd-anchor.test.sh`** — new 10-assertion regression guard: spec assertions that all four reap blocks carry the porcelain anchor and name #497, plus a runtime proof (negative control: the naive `cd $(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)` recovery fails on a deleted cwd; positive: anchor-before-reap lets `git worktree prune` and follow-up git commands survive).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump to 1.8.59.

### 1.8.58 — 2026-06-08

Extends the A.0.5 pre-reap crash-recovery (introduced in 1.8.57 / #493) to also salvage UNCOMMITTED working-tree edits, not just committed-but-unpushed commits (#495). The 1.8.57 trigger (`rev-list --count origin/<default>..HEAD > 0`) fires only when the worker managed to commit before crashing; a worker that stalled *before* committing — e.g. killed by the stream watchdog mid-version-bump, or hung in a pre-commit hook — has `rev-list --count == 0` and the previous recovery skipped, discarding the edits. This session's own #493 implementation was dogfooded: the worker stalled after writing ~80 lines of code + a 20-assertion test (both uncommitted), and the orchestrator had to go off-spec to hand-recover the files. The fix adds a `elif git status --porcelain` branch: when `rev-list` finds nothing but the working tree is dirty, the orchestrator stages all changes and commits with `--no-verify` (the pre-commit gate may be exactly what hung the worker; CI is the real safety net), then falls through to the existing push → PR-create → arm-auto-merge path from #493. The recovery is gated to `kind == "issue"` dispatches, is fully fire-and-forget, and always proceeds to the reap afterward.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — "Pre-reap recovery check" header and issue link updated to include #495; step 1 of "Recovery semantics" extended with the dirty-working-tree case; the code block gains a `elif [ -n "$(git status --porcelain)" ]` branch that runs `git add -A` + `git commit --no-verify`, sets `push_ok`, and falls through; the shared push+PR-create+auto-merge block is extracted outside the `if/elif` and guarded with `push_ok="${push_ok:-false}"` so the clean-worktree (no recovery) case can't trip a `set -u` unbound-variable error.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/crash-recovery-A05.test.sh`** — extended from 20 to 29 assertions: the new #495 traceability check, `status --porcelain` detection, `commit --no-verify`, `git add -A`, the dirty-worktree log marker, the `elif` → shared-block document ordering, the `push_ok` default guard, the `slot_kind == "issue"` scope coverage, and the `#495` prose reference.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump to 1.8.58.

### 1.8.57 — 2026-06-08

Adds a pre-reap recovery check to the `/shipyard:do-work` orchestrator's A.0.5 crash handler so a worker that crashes *after* committing locally but *before* pushing no longer has its work discarded (#493). The previous spec reaped a crashed worker's `agent-*` worktree and dropped its `do-work/issue-<N>` branch unconditionally, then re-enqueued the issue for a from-scratch redo — throwing away a hook-validated commit (typecheck/lint/format/secrets already passed) and burning another full dispatch plus the user's wall-clock. The new check inspects the crashed branch for commits ahead of `origin/<default>` *before* the reap: when found, it pushes the branch, opens (or reuses) a PR with the standard `Closes #N` + `--label shipyard` template, arms auto-merge, and appends the recovered PR to `session_prs` so drain and the end-of-session summary see it — converting a mid-session network blip from "lose N committed PRs + redo from scratch" into "push + open PR for the work already done." The recovery is gated to issue-work dispatches (slot `kind == "issue"`), is fully fire-and-forget (a failed push or PR-create logs but never aborts the reconcile turn), and always proceeds to the reap afterward. This very session dogfooded the gap: the #493 worker stalled at the version-bump step with the implementation uncommitted, and the work was hand-recovered to ship this PR.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — A.0.5 gains a "Pre-reap recovery check" prose section (rev-list signal, push → PR-create → arm auto-merge → `session_prs` append ordering, issue-work-only scope, fire-and-forget posture) plus the corresponding recovery code block ahead of the existing reap; the scope guard reads the slot's documented `kind`/`target` fields (`kind == "issue"`); fire-and-forget and audit-log-shape notes extended to cover the recovery steps and the `[reconcile-A.0.5-recovery]` stdout log prefix.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/crash-recovery-A05.test.sh`** — new regression guard (20 assertions): A.0.5/#493 traceability, the rev-list detection signal, the `kind == "issue"` scope gate, the push/PR-create/auto-merge/`session_prs` recovery sequence, the recovery-before-reap document ordering, and the zero-commits-ahead skip.

### 1.8.56 — 2026-06-06

Redirects the `/shipyard:do-work` end-of-session HTML report to a persistent, hook-permitted location so it actually gets produced (#488). The report-writer wrote `<YYYY-MM-DD>-do-work-session.html` to the in-repo `.shipyard/do-work/`, resolved against the orchestrator's cwd — which lands on the user's **primary checkout**. On any machine carrying the global `enforce-worktree.sh` PreToolUse hook (the exact environment shipyard's worktree-isolation contract assumes — and the same friction as #486), that `Write` is hard-blocked ("targets the PRIMARY checkout"); writing to the orchestrator's own linked worktree instead is futile because that worktree is force-removed at end-of-session cleanup. The net effect was that the documented HTML report could **never** persist in its intended environment. The fix points the writer at `${SHIPYARD_HOME:-$HOME/.shipyard}/do-work-reports/<owner>-<repo>/` — outside any git checkout, alongside the cost-history ledger and session-state files — so it's never hook-blocked and survives worktree reap, with a single shared stylesheet at `~/.shipyard/do-work-reports/styles.css`.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md`** — "Write the consolidated report to disk" now resolves a `REPORTS_DIR` under `${SHIPYARD_HOME:-$HOME/.shipyard}/do-work-reports/<owner>-<repo>/` (repo slug `/`→`-`), writes the shared stylesheet one level up, surfaces the resolved absolute path in chat, and scopes the graceful-degrade clause to a genuine shipyard-home filesystem failure. Adds a "Why shipyard-home, not in-repo" rationale block citing the reaped-worktree + read-only-primary constraints; the wind-down-trio intro line updated to match.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md`** — phase-routing table's report-writer description updated to the new `~/.shipyard/do-work-reports/<owner>-<repo>/` location.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/audit.md`** — stylesheet step no longer claims `/shipyard:do-work` shares `.shipyard/styles.css`; notes do-work's separate out-of-checkout report root and its own stylesheet copy.

### 1.8.55 — 2026-06-06

Adds a step-0 cwd fail-fast guard to the shared worker preamble so a misrouted `isolation: "worktree"` dispatch bails immediately instead of burning its entire run and dying at the final commit (#486). A Claude Code *harness*-level misroute can land an `isolation: "worktree"` dispatch with its process cwd pinned to the **primary checkout root** rather than the created worktree; on any repo carrying the global `enforce-worktree.sh` PreToolUse hook (the exact environment shipyard's worktree-isolation contract assumes), every git-mutating command then targets the user's primary checkout and the hook hard-blocks the worker's final `git commit` — but only after the worker has implemented, validated, and staged the whole task (the issue's repro: ~94 min of Opus dying at the last step, with no in-session path to recover the staged work). The harness owns the cwd, so AC items (1) and (2) of #486 — forcing the dispatch/orchestrator cwd to the correct worktree — are **out of shipyard's control**; the in-repo mitigation is AC items (3) and (4): a pre-flight assertion that detects a primary-checkout cwd via git's own truth (`git rev-parse --git-dir` == `--git-common-dir`, which holds only for a primary checkout and is false for every linked worktree) and returns `blocked: dispatch-isolation cwd override is wrong` at step 0, plus a behavioral regression guard over a real primary-checkout + linked-worktree fixture. The guard runs in every worker mode and is a cheap no-op on the normal (correctly-pinned) path.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — new § "Step-0 cwd fail-fast — assert you're actually in your worktree" after the worktree-discipline rules: explains the harness-level mispin, prescribes the `git-dir == git-common-dir` primary-checkout detection predicate, emits the `dispatch-isolation cwd override is wrong` bail (`blocked:`, not `reaped:` — the mispin is deterministic for this dispatch), and notes it applies to every worker mode.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-cwd-fail-fast.test.sh`** — new behavioral regression suite (AC 4): builds a real primary checkout + a `git worktree add` linked worktree and asserts the detection predicate classifies primary (incl. nested subdir) as `primary` and the linked worktree (incl. nested subdir) as `worktree`, path-independently; also pins the SKILL.md wiring of the predicate.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh`** — six new doc-grep assertions guarding the #486 section's presence (the bail message, the git-common-dir predicate, the cwd-pinned-to-primary failure-mode framing, and the every-mode applicability statement).

### 1.8.54 — 2026-06-03

Fixes the `guard-primary-checkout` hook to fail-open when `cwd` is absent from the hook payload, restoring green CI on `main` (PR #484). The hook's fail-open contract (issue #482: "fail-open on any uncertainty") requires exit 0 when the input payload is missing fields needed to determine whether we're in the primary checkout. The `missing tool_input/cwd → allowed` regression test shipped in 1.8.53 correctly captured this contract, but the hook itself was falling back to `${PWD:-}` when `cwd` was absent from the JSON payload — if the hook process's `$PWD` happened to be the primary checkout (e.g. when the CI runner's shell cwd was not inside a linked worktree), the guard would fire and exit 2 even though the payload lacked a `cwd` field entirely. The fix removes the `$PWD` fallback and exits 0 immediately when `cwd` is empty after JSON extraction, consistent with the other fail-open cases (`not-a-repo`, `malformed JSON`, `empty object`). 25/26 tests passed before; all 26/26 pass after.

- **`plugins/shipyard/hooks/guard-primary-checkout.sh`** — removed `cwd="${PWD:-}"` fallback; `[[ -z "$cwd" ]] && exit 0` now fires immediately when the payload omits `cwd`, matching the fail-open contract.

### 1.8.53 — 2026-06-02

Adds an opt-in primary-checkout guard offered during `/shipyard:init` (#482). `/do-work` isolates *background* workers in their own worktrees and the native `worktree.bgIsolation` setting isolates *background* sessions, but nothing protected a user's *interactive* sessions from colliding in the repo's primary checkout — two sessions sharing one working tree can switch the branch / dirty the index out from under each other (the issue's real-world repro: a second session switched the shared checkout to a feature branch mid-task while the first believed it was on a clean `main`). The guard is a `PreToolUse` hook that fires when an `Edit`/`Write`/`git commit` runs in the primary checkout rather than a linked worktree, nudging each editing session into its own `git worktree`. It ships **off by default** and is wired into the user's own `settings.json` (never the plugin's always-on `hooks.json`), with three modes (`off`/`warn`/`block`) and a scope choice (repo vs global). Per the issue's carrot+stick refinement, `/shipyard:init` offers the hook (stick) alongside a paired CLAUDE.md "enter a worktree first" rule (carrot) and documents the `ExitWorktree` cleanup lifecycle (tied to merge state, not "worker finished"); the block message leads with the `EnterWorktree` tool rather than a bare `git worktree add`.

- **`plugins/shipyard/hooks/guard-primary-checkout.sh`** — new reference hook. Detects the primary checkout via git's own truth (`--absolute-git-dir` == `--git-common-dir`, both realpath-normalized so a `/tmp`↔`/private/tmp` symlink doesn't false-positive); reads `SHIPYARD_PRIMARY_GUARD` (`off`/`warn`/`block`) so one script serves all policies; scopes to `Edit`/`Write`/`MultiEdit`/`NotebookEdit` + `git commit` Bash calls; fails open on any uncertainty (not-a-repo / malformed input / detection error → allow). Not registered in `hooks.json` — init wires it into the user's `settings.json` via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`.
- **`plugins/shipyard/hooks/tests/guard-primary-checkout.test.sh`** — new regression suite (26 assertions) over a real git primary-checkout + linked-worktree fixture: block/warn/off modes, the linked-worktree allow path, the `git commit`-only Bash scoping, the `EnterWorktree`-first block message, and the fail-open cases.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/init.md`** — new interactive prompt + `--primary-guard`/`--primary-guard-scope` flags + step 9.5 (install logic: settings.json merge, paired CLAUDE.md rule, idempotent re-run), plus Related links to the issue and the hook script.

### 1.8.52 — 2026-06-02

Hardens the issue-worker's end-to-end close guarantee so a resolving PR actually closes its dispatched issue on merge (#481). The worker contract implicitly promises the issue gets closed when its PR lands, but nothing enforced that the PR used a GitHub **closing keyword** (`Closes`/`Fixes`/`Resolves #N`) rather than a bare reference (`Refs #N`, `Related to #N`, plain `#N`). A bare reference does NOT register a closing link, so the issue silently lingers OPEN forever after merge — the work ships, the issue stays open, and `/do-work` can re-pick an already-resolved issue (polluting the "zero matching issues remain" termination signal). The real-world repro: `lightwork#1581` merged with `Refs #1580`, leaving #1580 OPEN. Likely cause: a worker over-applied a repo-local "don't auto-close" convention (correct for *incidental* references) into "always use `Refs #N`" — but the resolving PR for a dispatched issue is the intended-close case and must use the keyword. The fix (a) makes the step-5 keyword requirement explicit about closing-keyword-vs-bare-reference and carves out the repo-local-convention exemption for the dispatched issue, (b) adds a new post-create verification step that asserts GitHub registered `#N` as a closing reference and auto-patches the PR body with `Closes #N` (then re-verifies, bailing to `needs-human-review` if the link still won't register), and (c) reinforces the shared worker-preamble's PR-creation contract with the same distinction.

- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`** — step 5 gains an explicit closing-keyword-vs-bare-reference paragraph plus the repo-local "don't auto-close" exemption for the resolving PR; new § "5.8 Post-PR-create closing-link verification" asserts `closingIssuesReferences` contains `#N` and patches the body + re-verifies before arming auto-merge; a new "Don't" bullet pins the bare-reference prohibition.
- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — the PR-creation contract's closing-line one-liner is expanded to spell out the closing-keyword requirement, the bare-reference failure mode, and the repo-local-convention exemption, pointing at the new issue-work.md sections.

### 1.8.51 — 2026-06-01

Fixes the step 1.3 #465 admin-direct-merge detector (#479), which was silently suppressing its own warning on precisely the repos it targets — admin + an unprotected default branch (the shipyard repo itself). The required-status-checks read `gh api .../required_status_checks --jq '(.checks // []) | length' 2>/dev/null || echo 0` is not 404-safe: `gh api` does NOT apply `--jq` to error responses, so on a 404 (`Branch not protected` — exactly the zero-required-checks shape #465 warns about) it writes the raw error JSON to *stdout* and exits non-zero, and the `|| echo 0` fallback then *appends* `0` to that body rather than replacing it (`required_checks_count=[{"message":"Branch not protected",...}0]`). That non-numeric value never equals `"0"`, so shape-2 of the detector (`admin && required_checks_count == 0`) never matched. The fix adds a one-line numeric-shape normalize (`case "$required_checks_count" in ''|*[!0-9]*) required_checks_count=0 ;; esac`) right after the read — option 1 from the issue — collapsing any non-digit value (404 body, empty, transient failure) to `0`, which is the correct semantic (a 404 means zero required checks) and matches the warn-on-doubt direction the step-1.3 comment already documents. The trailing prose paragraph is corrected to describe the actual (now-working) behavior.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — step 1.3's required-checks read gains the `case` numeric-shape normalize plus a comment explaining the `gh api` 404 append-not-replace trap; the closing paragraph's claim about the `|| echo 0` fallback is rewritten to reflect the fix.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/required-checks-404-normalize.test.sh`** — new regression suite (10 assertions): pins the normalize block's presence and ordering in setup.md, reproduces the pre-fix bug (bare `|| echo 0` on a 404 ≠ `"0"`), and proves the normalize collapses the 404 body / empty string to `0` while preserving genuine counts, so shape-2 fires on admin+404 and stays quiet when required checks exist. Full `*.test.sh` sweep passes; shellcheck clean.

### 1.8.50 — 2026-06-01

Hardens the step 0.55 session-id stash read-back (#477) against the `isolation: "worktree"` cwd-leak class so it stops breaking when the harness relocates the orchestrator's Bash-tool cwd into a just-returned agent worktree on a reconcile turn. The fallback read-back derived the orchestrator worktree from `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (cwd-dependent); when cwd leaked into an `agent-*` worktree, that resolved to the agent worktree — which has no `.shipyard-session-id` stash — so the `cat` came up empty and every downstream `session-state.sh` call exited 64 (`--session-id is required`), silently losing the turn's cost attribution and `session_prs` append. The fix mirrors A.0.6's porcelain-first idiom (#452): walk `git worktree list --porcelain` for the `orchestrator-*` entry (always present regardless of cwd, since all linked worktrees share one worktree list), with the cwd-derived read retained only as a last-resort fallback for a non-worktree layout. Implements option 1 from #477; relates to #365 (introduced the stash file) and #452 (the cwd-leak class A.0.6 already hardens against).

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — step 0.55's `$ORCH_WT`-not-in-scope read-back snippet now porcelain-derives the orchestrator worktree instead of reading `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, with prose documenting the #477 cwd-leak failure mode and cross-linking A.0.6's primary-leak guard.

Full `*.test.sh` sweep passes; shellcheck clean.

### 1.8.48 — 2026-06-01

Broadens the step 1.3 silent-direct-merge detector (#465) so it catches the **admin + zero-required-status-checks** repo shape, not just the original #438 `allow_auto_merge: false` + admin case. The real trigger for an ungated immediate admin direct-merge is "admin AND the default branch has no required checks for `--auto` to wait on" — which fires *even when `allow_auto_merge` is `true`*. The prior gate stayed silent on exactly that shape (this repo: `allow_auto_merge=true`, dispatcher admin, no required checks), so a session direct-merged issue PRs out of version order, leapfrogging a pre-allocated version and forcing a manual rebase. The detector now reads `repos/<owner/repo>/branches/<default>/protection/required_status_checks` and warns (recommending `--concurrency 1` or adding a required check) when admin + zero required checks holds, independent of `allow_auto_merge`. Warning-only — no behavior change to merge config.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — step 1.3 heading + prose document both triggering shapes; the embedded shell reads `required_status_checks` count and ORs the new `admin + 0 required checks` condition into the existing gate, with an `|| echo 0` fallback (warn-on-doubt) for a transient read failure. Warning text broadened to name both #438 and #465.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — three new assertions pin the broadened detector (reads `required_status_checks`, gates on `required_checks_count`, cites `issues/465`). Full `*.test.sh` sweep passes; shellcheck clean.

### 1.8.46 — 2026-06-01

Closes [#466](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/466) (P2, `fix`) — **teaches fix-rebase mode to deterministically resolve the version-coordinated `plugin.json`/CHANGELOG re-number conflict instead of bailing `blocked rebase` and forcing a manual rebase.** On a `version_coordination.enabled` repo, when a sibling PR merges first and advances the manifest version while this PR carries an earlier pre-allocated slot, the rebase conflicts on the manifest `.version` row + the top-of-file CHANGELOG entry. fix-rebase.md's trivial-or-bail policy classified the single-value `.version` collision as "both sides edited the same JSON key" → non-trivial → bail, and even the CHANGELOG "take both" rule would have left a bogus out-of-order entry — so a version-leapfrogged PR was a guaranteed `blocked rebase` even though the resolution is fully mechanical. Repro: session `do-work-20260601T013917Z-76896`, PR #462 (pre-allocated 1.8.38, main advanced to 1.8.41) — the orchestrator resolved it by hand to 1.8.42. The fix adds a narrow §4.6 carve-out: when coordination is enabled AND every conflicted hunk is on the manifest `.version` row + the CHANGELOG top-of-file insert, take main's version, bump to the next free patch, re-number this PR's CHANGELOG heading to that slot, and place it newest-first. Bails when the conflict touches source/spec content beyond those two rows, and pairs with the existing post-resolution conflict-marker assertion (#436).

- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md`** — step 4 trivial-list gains a version-coordinated-manifest bullet (and the non-trivial JSON-key example now carves out the coordinated `.version` row); new §4.6 section with the four-gate eligibility check (coordination enabled, conflicted files ⊆ {manifest, CHANGELOG}, manifest conflict only on the `.version` row, CHANGELOG conflict only on the top-of-file insert) + the deterministic resolution recipe, explicitly chained to the #436 conflict-marker assertion.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — the fix-rebase dispatch prompt template appends an authoritative version-coordination context paragraph (only when `version_coordination.enabled`) surfacing `manifest_path` / `manifest_version_jq` / `changelog_path` so the worker doesn't have to discover the carve-out's applicability blind.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/fix-rebase-version-coordination.test.sh`** — new regression suite: spec assertions on both edited files + a behavioral git fixture that reconstructs the #466 conflict and proves the resolution yields main-floor+1-patch (1.8.42, not the stale 1.8.38), strictly-descending/no-duplicate CHANGELOG headings, and a conflict-marker-clean tree the #436 assertion passes.
