### 1.8.42 — 2026-06-01

Closes [#458](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/458) (P2, `fix`) — **teaches the worker-preamble `node_modules` bootstrap to detect Next 16 / Turbopack and skip the link strategies deterministically, instead of letting each worker rediscover the `Symlink points out of the filesystem root` failure.** Next.js 16's Turbopack refuses a `node_modules` that resolves outside the worktree's filesystem root, so the `../../../node_modules` symlink (and even the `cp -al` hard-link copy, whose real inodes still live under the primary checkout's tree) fails the build/dev/test. In session `do-work-20260601T004608Z` against `mattsears18/mattsears18.com`, issue-work workers #169 / #140 / #170 each attempted the symlink, hit the root-check failure, and self-recovered to `npm ci` — burning a turn per worker on a predictable failure. The fix adds a Turbopack/Next-16 detection snippet (a `next >=16` dep, a `--turbopack` script flag, or a `turbopack` config key) to the bootstrap remediation; on a hit, the worker skips both link strategies and falls through straight to `npm ci`, mirroring the existing Auto Mode wasted-turn logic.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — "Dependency-bootstrap check" section: new "Next 16 / Turbopack constraint" block under remediation path 1 with the detection snippet + repro, and the `npm ci` fallback (path 2) now lists Turbopack among its trigger conditions.

### 1.8.41 — 2026-06-01

Closes [#457](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/457) (P2, `bug`) — **splits the `merged-direct` auto-merge outcome into `merged-direct` (CI green at merge) vs `merged-direct-ungated` (PR landed before CI completed), and makes the ungated case force an unconditional main-CI refresh so a post-merge red is caught.** On a repo where the dispatching user is an admin AND no *required* status checks are configured, the issue-work step-6 `gh pr merge --auto` silently falls through to an immediate admin direct-merge — landing the PR while its `build` check is still `IN_PROGRESS`. The "auto-merge waits for green" guarantee does not hold in that configuration: if the post-merge build then fails on the default branch, `main` goes red with no PR-level gate having caught it. Repro: session `do-work-20260601T004608Z` against `mattsears18/mattsears18.com` landed all 25 issue-work PRs (#182–#210) as `merged-direct` with `checks: pending`. The fix surfaces the precondition (admin + no required checks ⇒ ungated immediate merge) explicitly, refines the worker's return suffix off the check rollup, and exempts the `merged-direct-ungated` case from the step-D adaptive-skip carve-out so the main-CI divert reliably watches the merge commit.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — step 1.5 auto-merge categorization gains the `merged-direct` ⇒ `merged-direct-ungated` refinement keyed on the step-2 check rollup, plus the admin/no-required-checks precondition; step-2 note corrected (a direct-merge is not green "by definition").
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`** — step 7 refines the suffix off the rollup; step 8 adds the `auto-merge: merged-direct-ungated` return shape.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — step-D refresh trigger 1 fires unconditionally for the `merged-direct-ungated` sub-case (exempt from the rule-4 adaptive-skip carve-out).
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/inline-trivial.md`** — inline path mirrors the refinement.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — 7-assertion regression block pinning the new outcome + precondition language (164 tests green, shellcheck clean).

### 1.8.40 — 2026-06-01

Closes [#459](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/459) (P2) — **closes a third silent-quality-gate-bypass in fresh agent worktrees: husky / `core.hooksPath` pre-commit hooks that git silently skips because the hook file lacks the executable bit.** A `git worktree add` checks out hook files with their committed mode and runs no npm `prepare` lifecycle script, so a repo whose `.husky/pre-commit` was committed `100644` (or whose hooks are provisioned only via `husky install`) ends up with inert hooks in the worker's worktree — git prints an advisory `hint:` to stderr and commits anyway (exit 0), so lint-staged / prettier / eslint never run and no `--no-verify` was passed. Repro: `mattsears18.com` session `do-work-20260601T004608Z`, the #170 issue-work worker. The fix adds a worker-preamble section with a detect-and-`chmod`-or-`npm ci` remediation, sitting alongside the existing missing-`node_modules` and worktree-path-ignore silent-pass sections.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — new "Husky / `core.hooksPath` hooks silently skipped on a missing exec bit" section: mechanism (two ways a worktree gets non-executable hooks), the detect one-liner (resolve hooks dir, `find` non-executable extensionless hook files), remediation order (`chmod +x` the worktree copy → `npm ci` to re-run `prepare` → never `--no-verify`), and a when-NOT-to-worry scope note (shipyard's own repo has no husky setup; this guards target repos like lightwork / mattsears18.com).

### 1.8.37 — 2026-06-01

Closes [#460](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/460) (P2, `bug`) — **gives issue-work workers a deterministic contract for repos that carry a per-PR release-process rule: bump the manifest version + add the CHANGELOG entry in the worker's own PR, never defer it to a post-merge main-direct action.** In session `do-work-20260601T011144Z-60939`, two sibling issue-work workers handled this repo's release rule (`CLAUDE.md` → "ALWAYS cut a release when a PR merges") inconsistently — #452's worker deferred the bump (PR #454 merged-direct leaving `main` at 1.8.34 undocumented, forcing the orchestrator to cut a separate catch-up release PR #455 that nearly collided on the manifest version row against #456's concurrent bump), while #453's worker correctly included it. The bump-in-PR-vs-defer choice was left to per-worker LLM judgment because `issue-work.md` had no contract for it. The fix adds a "Per-PR release rule" block to step 4 that makes the in-PR bump mandatory when such a rule is present, documents how it composes with the existing `next_available_version` coordination hint (the release rule decides *whether* to bump; the coordination value decides *which slot*), and pins the contract with a 4-assertion regression block.

- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`** — step 4: added the "Per-PR release rule — bump in your own PR, never defer" block with the #460 repro and the composes-with-coordination guidance.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — block (26): 4-assertion regression pinning the new contract language.

### 1.8.36 — 2026-06-01

Closes [#453](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/453) (P2, `bug`) — **makes the issue-work worker's pre-push verification a superset of CI's required checks instead of a hand-picked subset, so "passes locally" implies "passes CI."** The issue-work spec told the worker to "run the test suite locally" without pinning *which* suites; in practice a worker would run an ad-hoc subset (e.g. do-work-split / config / init-config / shellcheck) and ship a change that reddened a CI gate it skipped. On this repo — where PRs frequently direct-merge (admin on an `allow_auto_merge: false` repo) — that surfaced as broken `main` rather than a held-back red PR: PR #441 direct-merged green-locally yet reddened main because the worker never ran `claude-plugin-root-preamble.test.sh`, the one suite guarding the `drain.md` `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` block it added. The fix rewrites step 4's verification bullet to require the local gate be a superset of CI's checks and to discover suites the way CI discovers them (mirror `.github/workflows/tests.yml`'s `find plugins -type f -name '*.test.sh'` / `shellcheck.yml`'s glob) — running at minimum every discovered suite whose guarded paths intersect the changed files, preferring the full discovered set when it's cheap.

- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/issue-work.md`** — step 4 verification: added the superset-of-CI contract plus the "discover the suites the way CI discovers them" guidance with the #441 repro.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — 4-assertion regression block pinning the new contract language.

### 1.8.35 — 2026-06-01

Closes [#452](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/452) (P1, `bug`) via [#454](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/pull/454) — **makes the primary-checkout leak guard derive `PRIMARY_CHECKOUT` independent of the orchestrator's Bash cwd, so a harness-leaked `agent-*` cwd can't make the guard mutate the wrong worktree.** The Claude Code `isolation: "worktree"` dispatch path can silently relocate the orchestrator's own Bash-tool cwd into a just-returned agent's worktree (same harness-env leak class as #387 / #322 / #354). The A.0.6 primary-leak guard derived `PRIMARY_CHECKOUT` from `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` with an `orchestrator-*`-only suffix strip, so when cwd had leaked into an `agent-*` worktree the strip didn't match — the guard read the agent worktree's branch (`do-work/issue-NNN`), saw it `!= main`, and ran `git checkout main && git pull --ff-only` against the **agent** worktree while emitting a phantom `[primary-leak] restored primary` line, never inspecting the real primary. The fix derives the primary cwd-independently from `git worktree list --porcelain`'s first entry (always the primary), retaining the suffix-strip — now covering `agent-*` as well as `orchestrator-*` — as a fallback.

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — A.0.6 primary-leak guard now derives `PRIMARY_CHECKOUT` from `git worktree list --porcelain`'s first entry; suffix-strip fallback extended to `agent-*`.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — drain-entry primary-leak guard given the identical cwd-independent derivation.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — 6-assertion regression block asserting the primary derivation is correct when invoked from inside an `agent-*` worktree (149 tests green, shellcheck clean).

### 1.8.34 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#434](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/434) (P1, `bug`) via [#451](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/pull/451) — **adds a hard verify-before-file gate so audit agents can't file findings against unverified command output.** During a full `/shipyard:audit all` run against `mattsears18.com`, 13 of 44 filed issues were fabricated false positives that had to be retracted — four auditors ran reconnaissance reads in the *same* parallel tool-call batch as `gh issue create` and acted on unverified (sometimes empty/garbled) output (e.g. "`tailwind.config.ts` doesn't exist" against a repo where it does; "remove unused `@tailwindcss/typography`" against a package that isn't a dependency), then self-corrected and retracted *after* filing. The skills didn't hard-gate filing behind a verification step. The fix is structural and three-sided: the filing skill now requires every finding to cite a freshly-read evidence artifact captured in a step that *completed before* the create (never batched with it), plus a self-review re-read pass immediately before each create and a documented false-positive retraction path; the rubrics Evidence bar requires the evidence be freshly observed (not assumed) and observed before the create; and `/shipyard:audit`'s agent prompt carries the verify-before-file instruction while its reconciliation step closes any issue an agent flags as filed-in-error.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/filing-github-issues/SKILL.md`** — new "Verify before you file (REQUIRED — hard gate)" section (sequence recon strictly before the create, confirm each claim against ground truth, self-review re-read pass, drop inconclusive findings) plus the "If you filed a false positive anyway: the retraction path" subsection (self-close with a retraction comment, or surface under a "Filed in error (needs retraction)" summary line when the close is classifier-denied).
- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/audit-rubrics/SKILL.md`** — Evidence bar now requires evidence freshly observed this session (not assumed) and observed *before* the create, cross-referencing the filing-skill gate.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/audit.md`** — agent prompt template now carries the verify-before-file instruction and asks agents to surface filed-in-error issues; reconciliation step gains a "close any issue an agent flagged as filed in error" rule.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/audit-verify-before-file.test.sh`** (new) — 17 regression assertions guarding the verify-before-file gate, the before-create sequencing, the self-review pass, the retraction path + in-summary fallback, the rubrics cross-reference, and the audit.md prompt + reconciliation wiring.

### 1.8.33 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#435](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/435) (P1, `bug`) via [#TBD](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/pull/TBD) — **stops parallel auditors from reporting guessed/colliding issue numbers and gives the orchestrator a trustworthy agent→issue mapping to reconcile against.** When `/shipyard:audit all` dispatches 12+ auditors at once, GitHub assigns issue numbers sequentially across the concurrent `gh issue create` calls, so an agent that predicts its number or reads it back with a separate post-filing `gh issue list` can report a colliding or stale value — and a genuine finding can be silently lost (a 15-auditor `mattsears18.com` run had two auditors both report #150/#152 and one real finding never traced to its actual number). The fix is two-sided: the filing skill now requires every auditor to capture the real issue URL from `gh issue create`'s stdout and report *that* (never a guess, never a read-back), plus a per-run `audit-run=<run-id>` body marker; and `/shipyard:audit` generates the run id, injects it into every agent prompt, and reconciles the agents' reported URLs against a run-id-scoped enumeration before writing the end-of-run summary, flagging any unresolved/colliding URL in Process notes.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/filing-github-issues/SKILL.md`** — new "Capture the real issue number — never guess or read it back stale" section (captures `gh issue create` stdout into a variable), "Per-run attribution marker" section (the conditional `audit-run=<run-id>` body comment, volatile and NOT used for dedup), and "Return summary — report captured URLs" section; body template now shows the conditional `audit-run` comment alongside the mandatory `audit-key`.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/audit.md`** — new "Generate the audit run id" pre-dispatch step; agent prompt template now passes the run id and the capture-from-stdout instruction; new "Reconcile filed issues before summarizing" step cross-checks reported URLs against a `gh issue list --search "audit-run=<id>"` enumeration; end-of-run summary now lists captured URLs and a Process-notes mismatch surface.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/audit-issue-number-reconciliation.test.sh`** (new) — 18 regression assertions guarding the capture-from-stdout requirement, the predicted/read-back prohibitions, the `audit-run` marker + its not-for-dedup warning, and the orchestrator's run-id generation, prompt injection, and reconciliation step.

### 1.8.32 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#439](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/439) (P2, `shipyard`) via [#TBD](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/pull/TBD) — **adds the root shared docs to the soft-collision path set so concurrent worker edits don't hard-collide → conflict at merge.** Workers routinely edit root docs (`CLAUDE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `README.md`) alongside their fix, but `CONTRIBUTING.md` was missing from the built-in default soft-collision glob set entirely, and this repo's `shipyard.config.json` didn't register any of the root docs in `concurrency.soft_collision_paths` — so the orchestrator treated them as hard paths and dispatched colliding workers whose PRs then conflicted at merge time (the #403-vs-#409 CLAUDE.md conflict and the #398-vs-#399 CONTRIBUTING.md conflict in session `do-work-20260531T172554Z-8676`). With `CONTRIBUTING.md` now in the built-in default set and `CLAUDE.md`/`CONTRIBUTING.md`/`README.md` registered in the repo config, the orchestrator soft-tiers these paths (≤3 simultaneous claimers per path, trivial PR-land conflicts handled by fix-rebase) instead of serializing or producing post-merge conflicts.

### 1.8.31 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#440](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/440) (P2, `shipyard`) via [#447](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/pull/447) — **adds worker-preamble guidance for when GitHub push-protection blocks a synthetic test-fixture secret.** A worker that adds a NEW test fixture with a realistic-shaped secret (Slack `xoxb-`, Stripe `sk_live_`/`sk_test_`, GitHub `ghp_`/`github_pat_`, AWS `AKIA…`) — to exercise a scrubber, secret-scan rule, or redaction regex — gets its `git push` bounced by GitHub's server-side push-protection, even when the fixture is already allowlisted in `.gitleaks.toml`. The #402 and #408 scrubber-fixture workers both hit this and worked around it ad hoc. The new section names the load-bearing distinction (push-protection is a *separate* scanner from the `.gitleaks.toml` committed-content scanner, so a gitleaks path-allowlist does not exempt the blob) and prescribes the safe recovery mirroring the existing classifier-denial guidance: never click the server-side unblock URL, rewrite the fixture to an obviously-synthetic value that still matches the pattern under test, rebuild the commit so the flagged blob never enters pushed history, and mirror a new fixture file's path into `.gitleaks.toml`. Bails with `blocked:` for the rare checksum-validating-detector case.

- **`plugins/shipyard/skills/worker-preamble/SKILL.md`** — new "GitHub push-protection blocking a synthetic test-fixture secret" section, placed between the escape-symlink hygiene and the no-`--no-verify` rule.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worker-preamble.test.sh`** — 5 new regression assertions covering the section header, the never-click-unblock / rewrite-synthetic / rebuild-commit behaviors, and the `.gitleaks.toml`-distinction callout.

### 1.8.30 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#404](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/404) (P1, `audit:testing`) — **ships a branch-protection enablement helper + contributor docs so the maintainer can make the CI gates *required* with one reviewable command.** The repo's `tests.yml` / `shellcheck.yml` / `secret-scan.yml` workflows run on every PR but `main` has no branch protection, so they're advisory only — a red-tests PR can still merge (9 of the last 100 `tests.yml` runs on `main` were failures). Enabling required status checks is a GitHub admin action with no committable file, so a `/shipyard:do-work` worker can't ship it directly; the decision (enable, or keep the single-maintainer direct-push model) remains the maintainer's. This PR ships the *preparation*: a helper that wraps the exact `gh api` enablement call with the correct check-context names (which are easy to mistype — a wrong context waits forever on a check that never reports), defaulting to a non-mutating dry-run and gating the mutation behind an explicit `--apply`. The enablement leaves `enforce_admins=false` so the documented push-direct-to-`main` workflow keeps working; only PR merges with red checks get blocked.

- **`scripts/enable-branch-protection.sh`** (new) — wraps the `gh api -X PUT .../branches/main/protection` call. `--dry-run` (default) prints current state + the payload and mutates nothing; `--apply` enables required checks; `--show` prints current protection state. Required contexts: `bash test suites`, `shellcheck`, `shell tests`, `gitleaks`. `REPO` / `BRANCH` env overrides for reuse.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/enable-branch-protection-script.test.sh`** (new) — 13 assertions: existence / executability / shebang, every required-context name is referenced, `--help` / unknown-flag behavior, the default mode is a non-mutating dry-run (asserts the apply path does NOT run, pointed at a non-existent repo so it can't touch live settings), and shellcheck-clean.
- **`CONTRIBUTING.md`** — new "CI gates and branch protection" section documenting the workflows, their check names, the advisory-vs-required gap, the admin-bypass coexistence with direct-push, and the helper-script usage.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump → 1.8.30 (patch — additive contributor tooling + docs; no plugin runtime behavior change).

### 1.8.29 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#408](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/408) (P1, `bug` / `audit:privacy`) — **the auto-report secret scrubber now redacts JWTs, Stripe keys, GitLab PATs, npm tokens, and credential-bearing database URLs**. With `SHIPYARD_AUTOREPORT=1`, a failing session whose transcript touched any of these secret shapes would have uploaded them verbatim into a public GitHub issue, because `PY_SCRUB` in `report-plugin-error.sh` only covered eight shapes (the private-key + Slack shapes flagged in the original finding were already added by #402, so this PR closes the remaining gap). The fix extends `PY_SCRUB` with the missing patterns, ordered so the database-URL rule runs ahead of the email rule (the email pattern would otherwise match `pass@host` inside a connection string and redact only part of it) and the JWT rule runs ahead of the 40+ char hex rule. The database-URL rule only redacts the credential-bearing form (`scheme://user:pass@host`), leaving credential-free URLs intact to avoid over-redaction. Patch bump (additive scrubbing patterns; no behavior change when no secret is present).

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/report-plugin-error.sh`** — `PY_SCRUB` gains six patterns: Stripe live/test keys (`sk_live_…` / `sk_test_…`), GitLab PATs (`glpat-…`), npm tokens (`npm_…`), standalone JWTs (three-segment base64url, complementing the existing Bearer-prefixed catch), and credential-bearing database connection URLs (`postgres`/`mysql`/`mongodb`/`mongodb+srv`/`redis`/`amqp` schemes with inline `user:pass@`).
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/report-plugin-error.test.sh`** — 11 new assertions: one per new shape plus a `mongodb+srv` variant, a credential-free-URL no-over-redaction guard, and redaction-marker presence checks.

### 1.8.28 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#436](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/436) (P1, `shipyard`) — **conflict markers can no longer ride a green CI run onto `main`**. During a high-concurrency `/shipyard:do-work` session a fix-rebase/merge force-pushed a `CHANGELOG.md` carrying unresolved Git conflict markers (`=======` / `>>>>>>> <sha>`) which then merged to the default branch; none of the existing gates (shellcheck, bash tests, secret-scan) grep for markers, so the corruption merged silently and every subsequent PR branched from a poisoned base (repro: session `do-work-20260531T172554Z-8676`). The fix is two-layer: a repo-side CI gate that fails on any conflict marker in a tracked file, plus a worker-side assertion in the fix-rebase spec that bails `blocked rebase` before force-pushing if a marker survived conflict resolution. The detection regex is the anchored exactly-seven-chars-then-space-or-EOL pattern shared by the CI gate, the worker assertion, and the existing `check-merge-conflict` pre-commit hook, so all three agree on what counts as a marker and benign inline prose (a doc writing `` `=======` `` in backticks) doesn't false-positive. Patch bump (new CI gate + worker-spec assertion; no behavior change to non-rebase paths).

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/conflict-marker-scan.sh`** — new reusable scanner: `git grep`s tracked files for the anchored marker pattern, prints offending `file:line`, exits 1 on any match. Honors a `conflict-marker-scan: allow` opt-out directive for fixtures that legitimately construct conflicted files.
- **`.github/workflows/conflict-markers.yml`** — new read-only CI gate running the scanner on every push to `main` + PR; separate-concern / separate-file convention matching the other gates, checkout action pinned by SHA, no event-payload interpolation.
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-rebase.md`** — new step 5.5: assert no conflict markers remain (same regex) before the force-push, bail `blocked rebase #<M>: conflict markers remain after resolution` if any survive — the worker-side half of the defense so a fix-rebase dispatch isn't the thing that needs catching.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/conflict-marker-scan.test.sh`** — 13 regression assertions: artifact presence, workflow wiring + least-privilege, fix-rebase.md assertion + bail string, and behavioral scanner checks (clean pass / marker fail / allow-directive opt-out / no false-positive on wrong-length or inline marker-like prose).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump → 1.8.28 (patch — conflict-marker gate).

### 1.8.27 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#437](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/437) (P1, `shipyard`) — **batch dispatch now pre-allocates distinct manifest versions via a session-local `version_cursor`, killing the C≥2 pool-fill version-row collision storm**. The #339 next-available-version computation walks `session_prs` for **open** PRs to find the highest manifest version any in-flight PR has claimed. At the initial pool fill (setup step 7) and any step C multi-fill, the N sibling workers' PRs are not open yet, so every worker in the batch reads the same floor (main's version) and the per-dispatch walk hands them all the same next slot — the first C=4 batch all claimed 1.8.2, and 3 of 4 went DIRTY on the version row, eating a drain-rebase storm (repro: session `do-work-20260531T172554Z-8676`). The fix adds a session-local `version_cursor` high-water mark tracking the highest version slot **claimed by dispatch** (not just by open PRs): the computation seeds from `max(version_cursor, session_prs-walk)` and **advances the cursor to the value it hands out**, so running the block once per slot while composing a batch's prompts yields distinct monotonic slots (`main+1`, `main+2`, … `main+N`). The `Agent` calls still fire simultaneously; only the per-slot version assignment is computed serially against the shared cursor. Session-local working memory (not mirrored to the session-state file), consulted only when `version_coordination.enabled` + a `manifest_path` is configured — a no-op on non-coordinated repos. Patch bump (orchestrator dispatch-spec behavior, default-safe on non-coordinated repos).

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md`** — new `version_cursor` orchestrator-state struct (15th entry; opening-sentence count word advanced thirteen → fifteen) documenting the high-water-mark semantics, the batch-blindness gap it closes, and its session-local lifecycle.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — the #339 next-available-version computation now folds `version_cursor` into `max_inflight_version` and advances the cursor to each handed-out value; a new paragraph explains why the cursor (not the `session_prs` walk alone) is what makes a *batch* of simultaneous dispatches monotonic.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — step 7 (initial pool fill) documents running the version computation once per issue-work slot in sequence, so each batch sibling's prompt carries a distinct "Next-available version" paragraph.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — six `#437` regression assertions (struct-list naming + issue citation in do-work.md, steady-state seed/advance, setup batch pre-allocation) plus the updated "fifteen mental data structures" count-word assertion.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump → 1.8.27 (patch — batch version pre-allocation fix).

### 1.8.26 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#438](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/438) (P1, `shipyard`) — **serializes drain-phase rebases on CHANGELOG-coordinated repos and warns when `allow_auto_merge=false` + admin silently direct-merges**. On a repo where every PR appends a top-of-file `### <version>` CHANGELOG entry (the canonical version-coordinated shape — `version_coordination.enabled` + a non-empty `changelog_path`, the shipyard plugin itself), parallel drain rebases cannot converge: each merge moves the CHANGELOG insert point, re-DIRTYing every sibling that just rebased onto the previous head. The #438 repro saw 6 distinctly-versioned PRs (1.8.18–1.8.23) all re-DIRTY on the CHANGELOG append after the first merge; only serial rebase (rebase one → let it merge → rebase the next) converged. A new `version_coordination.serialize_drain_rebase` key (default `true`) caps effective drain-rebase concurrency to 1 when the gate is engaged — one DIRTY PR rebased per poll, the next deferred until the in-flight one merges. The cap applies only to fix-rebase dispatch; fix-checks against red PRs stays urgent. Separately, a setup-time warning fires when the repo has `allow_auto_merge=false` and the dispatching user has admin (the silent-direct-merge shape from #340), explaining why C≥2 version coordination can't hold without serialized merges. Patch bump (additive config key + drain-spec behavior, default-safe on non-coordinated repos).

- **`plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json`** — registers `version_coordination.serialize_drain_rebase` (boolean, default `true`) with the convergence rationale and the engage condition (enabled + non-empty `changelog_path`).
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh`** — adds `serialize_drain_rebase: true` to the built-in `version_coordination` defaults so `get` never trips a key-not-present fallback.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — new CHANGELOG-serialization gate in per-poll action 2 (after the #323 CI-minute truncation): when engaged, caps the `D_dirty` dispatch set to one PR per poll and defers the rest, with a no-op path for non-coordinated repos.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — new step 1.3 detects the `allow_auto_merge=false` + admin shape via a single REST read folded into the setup parallelization batch and emits a one-line operator warning (no behavior change — the orchestrator never flips repo auto-merge config).
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — seven `#438` regression assertions pinning the schema key, the config default, the drain gate (issue citation + key read + section heading), and the setup warning (silent-direct-merge naming + `allow_auto_merge` read).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump → 1.8.26 (patch — drain-serialization + setup-warning fix).

### 1.8.25 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#403](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/403) (P1, `audit:api`) — **the user-global config schema no longer declares keys the merge layer silently ignores**. The deep-merge (`shipyard-config.sh deep_merge_layers`) is a flat last-wins union with no key remapping, so six user-global keys (`default_auto_merge_policy`, `default_models`, `cost_tracking_enabled`, `exclude_repos_from_cost_tracking`, `pricing_override`, `currency`) validated cleanly but landed under their own names — never under the canonical paths consumers read (`auto_merge.policy`, `models.*`, `cost_tracking.enabled`). A user who set `default_auto_merge_policy: never` in `~/.shipyard/config.json` got a green `validate` and zero effect; `exclude_repos_from_cost_tracking` was the worst case (docs presented it as a privacy opt-out while tracking continued). The fix splits the six keys by whether a canonical consumer exists: the three that do are **remapped on load** so a user-global value actually overrides the built-in default (repo/local still win per last-wins); the three that don't (`pricing_override`, `currency`, `exclude_repos_from_cost_tracking` — none read by any script) are **removed from the schema and docs** so the contract stops over-promising. Patch bump (config-contract fix — no orchestrator runtime behavior changed for existing valid configs).

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh`** — new `normalize_user_layer()` translates `default_auto_merge_policy` → `auto_merge.policy`, `default_models.*` → `models.*`, `cost_tracking_enabled` → `cost_tracking.enabled` and drops the aliases; new `load_user_merged()` feeds the normalized layer into the merge/source-annotation paths (`cmd_load`, `cmd_show` effective + source map, `cmd_get` value + source). The raw `load_user()` is preserved for `show --layer user` / `validate --layer user` so the on-disk file stays inspectable verbatim.
- **`plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.user-config.schema.json`** — removed the three unconsumed keys (`pricing_override`, `currency`, `exclude_repos_from_cost_tracking`); clarified the three remapped keys' descriptions to name their canonical target.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-config.test.sh`** — new "user-global aliases remap onto canonical paths" section asserts each remap (value + `user` source), the unset-mode fall-through, that the alias names don't leak into the effective config, that repo-level canonical paths still win, and that the three removed keys are now unknown-field validation errors.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-init-config.test.sh`** — asserts `default_models.issue_work` is declared and the three removed keys are absent from the user schema.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/cost.md`**, **`plugins/shipyard/commands/config.md`**, **`CLAUDE.md`** — doc surfaces updated to point users at the `cost_tracking_enabled` alias / canonical-path remap instead of the removed `exclude_repos_from_cost_tracking` and `pricing_override` keys.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump → 1.8.25 (patch — config-contract fix; coordination-managed slot above this session's merged siblings).

A recurring, self-inflicted CI red: a worker adds a user-facing string to a centralized strings module (`lib/strings.ts` etc.) but forgets the matching key in every locale file, reddening the repo's key-parity test (`i18n.test.ts` etc.) and costing a fix-checks cycle to add the one missing key. Lives in the shared preamble (not per-mode) because the failure mode is identical across every worker that pushes. Patch bump (docs/spec guidance, no runtime behavior change).

### 1.8.21 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#407](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/407) (P2, `audit:api`) — **`worktree-reap.sh` now uses exit/return `64` for bad-usage, matching the shared error envelope every sibling script already honors**. An `api` script-contract audit found `worktree-reap.sh` was the lone divergence: where `shipyard-config.sh`, `session-state.sh`, `cost-history.sh`, `flake-registry.sh`, `gh-batch.sh`, `gh-cached.sh`, and six others map "called the helper wrong" (unknown flag, missing required flag, malformed flag value, unknown subcommand/action, unexpected positional) to `64` (`sysexits.h`'s `EX_USAGE`), `worktree-reap.sh` returned `2` for the identical conditions and contained zero `64`. The inconsistency defeats the whole point of a shared envelope — a caller branching on `64`-means-usage across the plugin's internal CLI surface silently got `2` from one script. Every one of the 28 `return 2` paths in `worktree-reap.sh` was a bad-usage error (no runtime-control returns used `2`), so all 28 renumber cleanly to `64`. No `do-work` spec call site branched on the numeric exit (they capture the stdout classification token), so no caller change was needed. Patch-class change (error-envelope alignment — no behavior change for well-formed invocations).

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/worktree-reap.sh`** — all 28 bad-usage `return 2` → `return 64` across `classify_lock`, `find_orphan_orchestrators`, `reap_action`, `reap_orphan_branches`, and `main`'s subcommand dispatch; the five `Exit codes:` documentation blocks (the four per-subcommand headers plus the `usage()` summary) updated `2` → `64`.

### 1.8.17 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#402](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/402) (P1, `audit:security`) — **the `SHIPYARD_AUTOREPORT` runtime secret-scrubber now redacts Slack tokens, fine-grained GitHub PATs, Google API keys, and PEM private-key blocks before public auto-filing**. The opt-in auto-report pipeline scrubs error excerpts, invocation prompts, and the last 80 transcript lines before posting them into a public GitHub issue, but `PY_SCRUB`'s pattern set only covered classic GitHub tokens (`gh[pousr]_`), `sk-ant-`/`sk-` keys, `AKIA` AWS keys, Bearer/Authorization headers, emails, and 40+ char hex. Several common live-credential shapes passed through unredacted — verified empirically: `xoxb-`/`xoxp-` Slack tokens, `github_pat_` fine-grained PATs (which do NOT match the classic `gh[pousr]_` shape), `AIza…` Google API keys, and `-----BEGIN … PRIVATE KEY-----` blocks all leaked into the public issue body. The fix adds the four missing pattern shapes to `PY_SCRUB`, with the multiline PEM pattern compiled `re.DOTALL` and run first so the whole block collapses before any inner base64 line can trip a narrower rule. Patch bump (security fix to an opt-in helper — no change to default behavior).

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/report-plugin-error.sh`** — `PY_SCRUB` gains a DOTALL multiline `-----BEGIN … PRIVATE KEY-----` → `<REDACTED_PRIVATE_KEY>` pattern (ordered first), a `github_pat_` → `<REDACTED_GH_TOKEN>` pattern, a `xox[baprs]-` → `<REDACTED_SLACK_TOKEN>` pattern, and an `AIza…` → `<REDACTED_GOOGLE_API_KEY>` pattern.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/report-plugin-error.test.sh`** — six new scrubbing assertions covering `xoxb-`/`xoxp-` Slack tokens, `github_pat_` PATs, `AIza…` Google keys, and PEM blocks (body redacted, markers redacted, collapses to a single `<REDACTED_PRIVATE_KEY>` token). 37/37 pass.
- **`.gitleaks.toml`** — reconciled the header comment: it previously implied the runtime scrubber covered `xox[bp]-*` and PEM keys (it did not). The comment now explicitly distinguishes the two scanners — gitleaks (committed-content, upstream default ruleset) vs. the narrower `PY_SCRUB` runtime scrubber — and enumerates the runtime scrubber's actual coverage so no shape is claimed-but-not-covered.

### 1.8.4 — 2026-05-31

Closes [#405](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/405) (P2, `audit:security`) — **the `worktree-reap.sh` audit ledger no longer builds JSONL via unescaped string interpolation**. A `security` audit found that `reap`'s three `emit_line` records hand-constructed JSON with `printf`-style interpolation, so any field value (`--reason`, `--worktree-name`, `--session-id`, `--classification`, `--reaped-session-id`, `--phase`) containing a `"`, `\`, or newline produced malformed JSONL — or, with a crafted value, forged additional record fields (a `--reason` of `x","action":"reaped","classification":"forged` rewrites the record's semantics). Those values derive from branch / worktree / session identifiers that are only validated non-empty at the call site, so the injection surface is real. Rather than introduce the `jq` dependency the script header explicitly disclaims ("Pure bash + `ps` + `date` + `git`. No jq, no python — the helper has to be cheap to call from the cleanup loop"), the fix adds a pure-bash `json_str` escaper (handles `"`, `\`, the C0 short escapes, and a `\u00XX` fallback for any surviving control byte) and routes every interpolated string value through it; the validated numeric/literal fields (`actor_pid`, `lock_pid`) stay unquoted. Patch bump (security hardening — no behavior change for well-formed inputs).

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/worktree-reap.sh`** — new `json_str` helper in the `reap` action; all three `emit_line` call sites (reaped / deferred / reaped-orphan-orchestrator) and the `phase_suffix` construction now JSON-escape their string fields.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/worktree-reap.test.sh`** — new test (44b) drives a reap with adversarial `--reason` (injection payload), `--worktree-name` (quote + backslash + tab), and `--session-id` (embedded newline), asserting a single ledger line, no forged `classification` field, and a successful `jq`/`python3` JSON parse.

Closes [#394](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/394) (P2, gap 1) — **the `do-work-split` thin-entry line cap now auto-derives from the orchestrator-state struct count instead of being a hardcoded magic number**. The `thin entry stays <= N lines` assertion in `do-work-split.test.sh` had to be manually re-baselined on every PR that adds an orchestrator-state struct to `commands/do-work.md` (200 → 220 → 222 → 223 across #195/#233/#246/#323/#387). Each bump was a separate edit a struct-addition PR could forget — and when it did, CI went red; if that PR merged anyway, `main` went red and a `fix-main-ci` divert burned a recovery cycle (the exact 1.7.2 sequence). The fix counts the documented top-level struct bullets (`- **\`name\`**`) in the `## Orchestrator state` section and asserts a derived cap of `197 base + 2 per struct` (currently 14 structs → 225, actual 223), so adding a 15th struct raises the cap by 2 automatically with no manual edit. The #154 intent is preserved: the base does NOT grow with struct count, so the cap stays fixed against non-struct prose — a thin entry that balloons with non-struct content still fails the assertion ("the split is over-engineered, we'd rather know"). Patch bump (test-tooling change — no plugin runtime behavior changed). Gap 2 from the issue (making `Shell scripts (lint + tests)` a required status check on the default-branch ruleset) is a GitHub branch-protection change, not a code change, and remains a human follow-up.

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — section (2) replaces the hardcoded `assert_line_count_at_most "$do_work_path" 223` with a struct-derived cap computed from `STRUCT_BASE (197) + STRUCT_PER_BUDGET (2) × <struct_count>`, where `struct_count` is the count of top-level `- **\`name\`**` bullets scoped to the `## Orchestrator state` section; new section (2b) pins a `struct_count >= 10` floor so a heading rename or struct-format change can't silently collapse the derivation back to a de-facto magic number; top-of-file comment + the new derivation comment explain the mechanism and cite #394.
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump 1.8.0 → 1.8.1 (patch — test tooling).

### 1.8.0 — 2026-05-30

Closes [#391](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/391) (P2, feat) — **`/shipyard:my-turn` now leads with the single next action instead of a full ranked list**. The command's tagline promises *focus* ("what's the ONE thing blocking the most, that I should do next") but its v1 output rendered a 20-line ranked backlog by default, reintroducing exactly the prioritization burden the command was meant to remove — dogfooding on `mattsears18/lightwork` (2026-05-30) surfaced this as reading like an "issue dump" rather than "your next step." This adopts Proposal A from the issue: the default render is now just the #1-ranked item as a prominent `→ Next:` directive (with its URL and any dependency/unblocks context, since "what this unblocks" is part of "what to do next"); the full ranked list is opt-in via a new `--all` flag (or an explicit `--limit N > 1`). The 0-items empty-state one-liner is unchanged — it's identical across modes. Minor bump (new default behavior + new `--all` flag, additive).

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/my-turn.md`** — frontmatter description + `argument-hint` updated to advertise the single-next-action framing and the new `--all` flag; new **Mode resolution** subsection under Args defining single-action mode (default) vs. list mode (`--all` or `--limit N > 1`, with `--limit 1` equivalent to the default); Output section restructured to lead with the **Single-action mode** render (the `→ Next:` directive, its URL, an optional dependency/unblocks line, and a `<K> more — rerun with --all` remainder footer) followed by the existing list render as **List mode**; empty-state marked unchanged-across-modes; limit-overflow scoped to list mode; new **Don't** entry forbidding the default full-list dump.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/my-turn.test.sh`** — five new regression assertions guarding the single-action default (`→ Next:` directive present, `--all` flag accepted, single-action + list modes documented, empty-state one-liner preserved).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump 1.7.2 → 1.8.0 (minor — new feature).

The test's own contract explicitly allows the thin entry to grow when a new orchestrator-state struct lands, so the fix is the missing re-baseline that should have shipped with 1.7.1. ### 1.7.1 — 2026-05-29

Closes [#387](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/387) (P1, bug) — the **harness `isolation: "worktree"` dispatch path leaks `do-work/*` branch checkouts into the user's PRIMARY working tree**, stranding the drain. The orchestrator runs exclusively in its own `.claude/worktrees/orchestrator-<id>` worktree and never issues `git checkout do-work/*` against the primary, yet the primary HEAD reflog shows leaked checkouts (`moving from main to do-work/issue-378`, etc.) originating from the harness and/or a dispatched agent operating against the shared `.git`. The damage surfaces at drain: a leaked `do-work/*` checkout holds git's per-branch lock on a PR head branch, so a drain-phase `fix-rebase` worker can't `git switch <head>` ("already checked out at <primary>") and bails `blocked rebase` — defeating drain's whole purpose. Root cause is harness behavior shipyard can't change, so the fix is an orchestrator-side defensive guard. Patch bump (spec/docs change — the orchestrator loop is interpreted prose, no helper script touched).

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/steady-state.md`** — new step **A.0.6** (primary-checkout branch-leak guard), fired every reconcile turn before A.1: resolves the primary checkout from the orchestrator-worktree path, and if the primary is off the default branch, restores it (`git -C <primary> checkout <default> && pull --ff-only`) when the tree is clean (lossless) or surfaces a loud `[primary-leak] WARNING` and skips when the tree is dirty (possible real edits — never auto-restore). Increments `primary_leak_restores` / `primary_leak_dirty_skips`. This is the single source of truth for the guard.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — runs the A.0.6 guard once at **drain entry** (where the leak does its damage), and extends the [pre-dispatch head-branch reap](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/370) to detect the **primary** holding the head branch (restore-if-clean / warn-if-dirty — never reap the primary), as a belt-and-suspenders for a leak that lands mid-drain.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md`** — new `primary_leak_counters` orchestrator-state struct (`{ primary_leak_restores, primary_leak_dirty_skips }`), session-local, initialized all-zero, on the `ci_session_counters` precedent.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/dont.md`** — documents the one sanctioned exception to "never write to the primary checkout": the corrective `git -C <primary> checkout <default>` the guard runs only on the leak-detected-and-clean path.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md`** — new `Primary-checkout leaks (#387)` end-of-session friction line (omitted when both counters are zero); a non-zero dirty-skip is the actionable signal (primary still parked on a `do-work/*` branch with uncommitted work).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump 1.7.0 → 1.7.1 (patch — bug fix).

### 1.7.0 — 2026-05-29

Closes [#385](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/385) (P1, feat) — **phase 2 of the cross-PR flake registry: enforce the three escalation actions**. [Phase 1](#160--2026-05-29) (#378) shipped the data layer — the `~/.shipyard/flake-registry.jsonl` ledger, the `fix-checks-only` write path, the `flake_registry` config schema, and the `flake-registry.sh crossed` reader that names which (workflow, job, test) flakes have crossed the escalation bar — but deliberately stopped before *acting* on the crossed rows. This phase is the enforcement consumer: the orchestrator reads `crossed` at setup time and performs the three configured actions per crossed row, each idempotent so re-running `/do-work` across sessions doesn't duplicate side effects. This is the half of #378 that actually enforces the "fix the root cause" rule rather than just surfacing the pattern. Gated on `flake_registry.enabled` (default off — pre-#378 behavior preserved). Minor bump (new additive feature).

Also includes [#388](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/388) (P2, bug) — **backfilled here per [#397](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/397)**. PR [#389](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/pull/389) (merge commit `820191d`, 2026-05-29 13:12) re-anchored the `shipyard-init-config.test.sh` CHANGELOG assertion to the version floor but merged between the v1.7.0 and v1.7.1 release cuts (~7 minutes before #390) without its own version bump or CHANGELOG entry — an invisible release that violated the "ALWAYS cut a release when a PR merges" invariant. Since #388 merged immediately after #385's v1.7.0 cut and before v1.7.1, it's grouped into the v1.7.0 release batch here (the repo's "one release per PR" rule is the steady-state aspiration, not a hard requirement — catch-up situations bundle). The plugin version (now 1.8.1) already subsumes all of #385/#387/#388, so no retroactive version bump is needed; this is a CHANGELOG-only history backfill.

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/flake-enforce.sh`** (new) — the enforcement helper. `enforce` reads `flake-registry.sh crossed` (computed itself or fed on `--from-stdin`) and performs each crossed row's own `actions`: `file-tracking-issue` opens a chronic-flake tracking issue stamped with an `audit:test-stability` label + a stable `flake-key=<base64>` HTML dedupe marker (skips when an OPEN issue already carries the marker), `stop-auto-rerunning` appends the pipe-joined `<workflow>|<job>|<test>` key to a per-repo `.shipyard/flake-suspects.txt` (skips a key already present; `.shipyard/` is gitignored, so this is a LOCAL human-signoff gate), and `apply-blocked-ci` labels each still-open affected PR `blocked:ci` with a comment referencing the tracking issue (skips a PR already labeled). `--dry-run` prints planned actions with no side effects; `suspects-list` / `is-suspect` are the consumer-side reads for fix-checks-only. `gh` is injectable via `$GH` for testing.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/flake-enforce.test.sh`** (new) — 31 assertions covering dry-run (no side effects), all-three-actions-fire against a mock `gh`, cross-session dedupe (no duplicate issue / no re-label / suspect-key-once), per-row `actions` honoring (a row omitting an action skips it), empty-crossed no-op, `suspects-list` comment/blank stripping, and `is-suspect` exit codes.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/setup.md`** — new step 5.8 ("Enforce the flake registry"): gated on `flake_registry.enabled`, shells out to `flake-enforce.sh enforce` once per session after the failing-PR snapshots. Documents the setup-vs-per-dispatch read-site decision (setup, because the `stop-auto-rerunning` consumer re-reads the suspects file per dispatch so mid-session escalations are still honored) and the idempotence contract.
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md`** — new fix-loop step 1.5 (pre-rerun flake-suspects check, the `stop-auto-rerunning` consumer): before re-running a failing check, probe `flake-enforce.sh is-suspect`; on a match, return `blocked #<M> at fix-checks: chronic flake ...` without burning a fix attempt. Updated the phase-1 recording section to reflect that enforcement has landed.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-init-config.test.sh`** (#388, PR #389) — re-anchored the `shipyard-init-config` CHANGELOG-presence assertion to the version *floor* rather than an exact-version match, so the check keeps passing as the plugin version advances past the version that first shipped the feature. Bundled into this v1.7.0 batch via the [#397](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/397) backfill (the PR merged un-released between v1.7.0 and v1.7.1).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump 1.6.0 → 1.7.0 (minor — new additive feature).

### 1.6.0 — 2026-05-29

Closes [#378](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/378) (P1, feat) — **phase 1 of the cross-PR flake registry**. Today's `fix-checks-only` worker handles flakes per-PR in isolation: none of the independent workers sees that the SAME test has flaked on N other PRs this week, so chronic flakes go uninvestigated and the user's repo-level rule ("Flaky CI is a real failure — fix the root cause") is silently violated. This phase ships the **data layer + threshold tooling**: a session-spanning `~/.shipyard/flake-registry.jsonl` ledger, a `fix-checks-only` write path that records one event per flake conclusion (gated on `flake_registry.enabled`, default off), the `shipyard.config.json::flake_registry` config schema (window / rerun-threshold / distinct-PRs-threshold / actions), and a `flake-registry.sh crossed` reader that aggregates per-(workflow,job,test) flake rates over the window and names which flakes have crossed the escalation bar. Per issue-work scope discipline the *enforcement* of the three escalation actions (`file-tracking-issue`, `stop-auto-rerunning`, `apply-blocked-ci`) is deliberately deferred to a follow-up phase ([#385](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/385)) — `crossed` emits the configured action set on each crossed row so the future enforcement phase can act on it, but this phase files no issues, writes no flake-suspects file, and labels no PRs. Minor bump (new feature, additive — defaults preserve pre-#378 behavior).

- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/flake-registry.sh`** (new) — the ledger helper, modeled on `cost-history.sh`. Subcommands: `record` (append one event: repo/pr/workflow/job + optional test/action/session, append-only JSONL safe under concurrent writers), `report` (windowed per-(workflow,job,test) aggregation: event count, distinct-PR count, PR list, first/last timestamps), `crossed` (threshold filter — event count ≥ `rerun_threshold` AND distinct-PR count ≥ `distinct_prs_threshold` within `window_days`; echoes the configured action set per row), `read` / `prune` / `reset` housekeeping. GNU+BSD `date` fallback for the cutoff math; corrupt lines dropped defensively at read.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/flake-registry.test.sh`** (new) — 24 assertions covering record arg-validation + dry-run + optional-field omission, windowed aggregation (in/out-of-window, repo isolation), the threshold + distinct-PRs guard (5 events on 1 PR does NOT cross), custom `--actions` echo, prune, and reset-to-.bak.
- **`plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json`** + **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh`** — new `flake_registry` config block (`enabled` default `false`, `window_days` 7, `rerun_threshold` 3, `distinct_prs_threshold` 2, `actions` enum-constrained to the three escalation actions). Added to both the schema (with `additionalProperties: false` + per-field descriptions) and the built-in defaults so every consumer sees the value on a bare repo.
- **`plugins/shipyard/agents/issue-worker/fix-checks-only.md`** — new "Record a flake event in the cross-PR flake registry" section after the root-cause-context step: gates on `flake_registry.enabled`, documents when to record (genuine flakes only — not real-bug fixes), the `flake-registry.sh record` call shape, and the explicit phase-1 scope boundary (record only; no enforcement yet).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump 1.5.28 → 1.6.0 (minor — new additive feature).

### 1.5.29 — 2026-05-29

Closes [#374](https://github.com/mattsears18/shipyard/issues/374) (P2, bug) — the **end-of-session drain's 120-min wall-clock ceiling exited prematurely on healthy-but-slow merge trains**. The old ceiling fired on the wrong signal: a multi-PR session with full E2E sharding (3 shards × ~10-15 min), Lighthouse (~9 min), and release-please cascades (each merge re-DIRTY-ing siblings, then a 15-25 min rebase + re-CI round-trip) can legitimately take >2h to land even when nothing is wrong, and the cap cut those sessions off mid-flight — forcing the user to re-invoke `/do-work` to resume the drain (which also resets cost tracking). The fix replaces wall-clock with a **per-PR progress signal**: drain tracks each open PR's `headRefOid` and the timestamp it last moved (`head_unchanged_since`), and a PR counts as "settled" once its head has been unchanged for `ci.settled_minutes` (default 20) — CI grinding but the branch not being actively reworked. Drain exits the moment every session_pr is settled (merged/closed, `blocked:ci`, rebase-blocked, rate-limit-capped, or pending-head-quiet), with no fixed-poll no-progress window. A new `ci.max_drain_hours` (default 8h) survives as the belt-and-braces outer bound so a degenerate session (runaway CI loop, infinite rebase whose head moves every poll) still can't run forever. The per-poll status line now surfaces the progress clock per still-pending PR (`PR #N: head unchanged 12m / 20m settled-threshold`) so the operator can watch the signal that determines settle. Docs/spec + config-schema + test change — the drain loop is orchestrator-interpreted prose, so the behavior change lands in `drain.md` rather than a helper script. Companion to #373 (cross-session DIRTY adoption into `session_prs`, shipped earlier this session).

- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work/drain.md`** — replaced the "Hard ceiling: 120 min" rule with the progress-based termination criterion + `max_drain_hours` ceiling; added the `head_unchanged_since` per-session structure and the `settled_minutes` / `max_drain_hours` config reads at drain entry; redefined `P_settled` and the settled definition around the per-PR head-movement threshold; added the per-PR progress-detail status line. Updated the intro, soft-drain step 5, and per-poll bookkeeping step 3 to match.
- **`plugins/shipyard/schemas/shipyard.config.schema.json`** — new `ci.settled_minutes` (integer, min 1, default 20) and `ci.max_drain_hours` (number, min 0.1, default 8) keys; `ci` block description notes the #374 keys ship with active non-zero defaults (they replace a hardcoded ceiling) vs the #323 opt-in gates.
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/shipyard-config.sh`** — built-in defaults for the two new `ci` keys.
- **`plugins/shipyard/commands/do-work.md`**, **`commands/do-work/setup.md`**, **`commands/do-work/cleanup-summary.md`**, **`commands/do-work-RATIONALE.md`** — updated the cross-references to the drain ceiling (struct-list settle note, C=1 drain-semantics line, `Drain phase` summary reason strings, and the RATIONALE's why-progress-based-not-wall-clock paragraph).
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/shipyard-config.test.sh`** — default-value + round-trip + below-minimum-rejection assertions for the two new keys (115 assertions).
- **`plugins/shipyard/scripts/tests/do-work-split.test.sh`** — asserts drain.md carries `head_unchanged_since` / `settled_minutes` / `max_drain_hours` and no longer carries the "Hard ceiling: 120 min" behavioral rule (129 tests).
- **`plugins/shipyard/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`** — version bump 1.5.28 → 1.5.29 (patch — spec/docs + config + test change closing a drain-duration friction gap).
